Tidebinder and Wasp

Dimir Midrange interaction guide vs the 16 most-played Standard decks · Sept 2026 · every rules claim adversarially checked
How to read this: each deck has a ranked list of abilities to counter with Tidebinder and creatures to tap-and-blank with Wasp, the exact stack moment, what the permanent loses and keeps afterward, and the traps. Each entry was drafted, then attacked by three independent reviewers (rules lawyer, strategy skeptic, oracle-grounding checker), then revised; corrections were accepted only when backed by oracle text or the rules. Decks ordered by metagame share.

The two cards

Tishana's Tidebinder{2}{U} · 3/2 · Creature · Merfolk Wizard
Flash When this creature enters, counter up to one target activated or triggered ability. If an ability of an artifact, creature, or planeswalker is countered this way, that permanent loses all abilities for as long as this creature remains on the battlefield. (Mana abilities can't be targeted.)
If Tishana's Tidebinder leaves the battlefield before its triggered ability resolves, the target activated or triggered ability will still be countered, but the source of the ability won't lose its abilities at all.
If the affected permanent gains an ability after Tishana's Tidebinder's effect begins to apply to it, it will keep that ability.
Tishana's Tidebinder's triggered ability checks to see if the source of the ability is an artifact, creature, or planeswalker as it resolves. If it isn't, it won't lose all abilities, even if it was an artifact, creature, or planeswalker when the ability was activated or when it triggered.
The Wondrous Wasp{1}{U} · 2/1 · Legendary Creature · Human Hero
Flash Flying Wasp's Sting, When The Wondrous Wasp enters, tap up to one target creature. It loses all abilities for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield.

Rules you must know

  • Tidebinder counters only activated and triggered abilities: static abilities (lords, cost reducers, Voice of Victory, Clarion Conqueror, Explorer's land permissions), replacement effects (enters with counters, shock-land life payments, enters as a copy) and anything that is casting a spell (flashback, adventure, plot, warp, evoke, harmonize, kicker, spree, Mayhem, Sneak) can never be targeted; a 'when you cast' trigger (Sage of the Skies' copy) is a triggered ability and can be countered, though its source is a spell so nothing is blanked.
  • Mana abilities cannot be targeted (printed on Tidebinder), so Llanowar Elves, Treasures, Springleaf Drum, Tablet of Discovery's tap abilities and every land's mana ability are off limits; blank the artifact or creature through a different trigger and the mana ability goes with it. A trigger that adds mana off a lore counter (Summon: Esper Maduin's chapter II) is not a mana ability and can be countered (CR 605.1b).
  • Tidebinder checks the source's type as its trigger resolves (ruling 3): only an artifact, creature or planeswalker on the battlefield loses its abilities, so a trigger from an enchantment or Aura (Sheltered by Ghosts' exile, Seam Rip's exile, Meltstrider's Resolve's fight, Earthbender Ascension's ETB, Political Triumph), a land that has not finished animating, a card in a graveyard or hand, a sacrificed source or a spell on the stack is countered but nothing is blanked.
  • Countering an equip, crew or other Equipment activation blanks the Equipment itself (it is an artifact): the equip stays paid, nothing attaches, and Leyline Axe stops granting double strike and trample, Dragonfire Blade stops granting hexproof from monocolored, and Basilisk Collar stops granting deathtouch and lifelink for as long as Tidebinder lives, including to a creature it is already attached to (the grant has the earlier timestamp).
  • Costs already paid stay paid: a sacrificed Headliner, Fabled Passage or Farm Animals stays sacrificed, discarded cards stay discarded, life and mana stay spent, a ninjutsu'd attacker stays in hand, and a once-per-turn or once-ever activation (Prowler's pump, Captain Marvel's power-up) counts as used.
  • Ruling 1: if Tidebinder leaves the battlefield before its trigger resolves, the target is still countered but the source keeps its abilities, so with removal mana open (Burst Lightning, Erode, Sear, Lesson, Sheriff, Triumph) price the counter alone and expect no blank.
  • The same logic applies to Wasp (CR 611.2b): if Wasp is killed in response to its Sting, the tap still happens but the 'for as long as' blank never begins.
  • An ability already on the stack exists independently of its source (CR 113.7a): flashing Wasp at a creature whose trigger is on the stack does nothing to that trigger, and only Tidebinder (or making the trigger's target illegal) stops it.
  • Both blanks last 'for as long as this creature remains on the battlefield', so tapping, stunning or blanking our Tidebinder or Wasp does not end them (CR 611.2a); only killing, bouncing, exiling, blinking or ninjutsu-returning it does, and the effect ends instantly then.
  • Countering an ETB never removes the permanent: a Sunderflock, Thor, Deceit, Excruciator or Hellkite whose trigger is countered is still a body that needs Drowner, Kaito -2, Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff.
  • Ward (CR 702.21) triggers only when the permanent itself becomes a target, so Tidebinder is never taxed by ward, Wasp always is, and the ward trigger itself is a legal Tidebinder target whose source is the creature (counter it and the creature is blanked with no tax paid while our original spell resolves).
  • Hexproof, Snakeskin Veil, Dawn's Truce, Shore Up and 'this spell can't be countered' (Cavern of Souls, Surrak, Gigantic Big Bear) protect the permanent or the spell, never an ability on the stack, so Tidebinder works straight through them while Wasp cannot target a hexproof creature at all.
  • Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat, so Wasp only stops an attack when it resolves in their beginning of combat step or earlier (or at our end step for a blocker); after attackers are declared it is a blank, not a fog.
  • Abilities gained after the blank began are kept (ruling 2) and abilities granted before it are stripped by timestamp in layer 6, so Wasp at their beginning of combat after Practiced Offense strips the double strike, Wasp before Offense does not, and Equipment attached after a blank grants normally.
  • Loses all abilities leaves power, toughness and counters intact (Hydra, Chocobo, Mako, Wan Shi Tong, Dyadrine keep their size), except a characteristic-defining P/T ability is itself lost, so Lumbering Worldwagon becomes 0/4.
  • A permanent that had no abilities immediately before it died has no death trigger (CR 603.10a), which is why blank-then-kill removes Enduring Curiosity, Enduring Innocence and creature-form Enduring Tenacity for good, and why Day of Black Sun kills them permanently on its own.
  • Delayed triggers are never stopped by blanking the permanent (they already exist) but are always counterable; whether the counter blanks anything depends on who created them. A delayed trigger made by a permanent's own triggered ability has that permanent as its source (CR 603.7e): Casey Jones's upkeep discard, Dragonhawk's end-step damage, Mobilize's end-step token sacrifice and Zoraline's reflexive return all blank the creature if it is still on the battlefield when Tidebinder resolves. A delayed trigger made by a spell or by an enchantment or land effect (warp's end-step exile, earthbend's return from Ascension or Ba Sing Se, The Eagles Are Coming!'s upkeep Birds) blanks nothing.
  • Kaito is a creature only during our turn (hexproof while he has loyalty) and a plain planeswalker on theirs, so he can never block, can never be a Wasp, Drowner or airbend target, and dies to Erode, Sear, Bitter Triumph and Thor triggers on their turn; the same is true in reverse for an opposing Kaito, who is a plain planeswalker during OUR turn.
  • Kaito's ninjutsu is an activated ability of a card in hand: countering it leaves Kaito in hand with all abilities and blanks nothing, Clarion Conqueror does not stop it, High Noon and Voice of Victory do not count it as a spell, and returning a Wasp or Tidebinder that is holding a blank ends that blank at once.
  • Voice of Victory and Jennifer Walters ('Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn') shut off both cards on their turn entirely; the lock is static, so kill it on our turn (Requiting Hex, Sheriff, Bitter Triumph) or Wasp-blank it on our turn, and only our-turn triggers (Lorehold's upkeep loot, Amalia's ward trigger, Queen's recruit off our spell, Farm Animals' sac) remain Tidebinder windows.
  • Torpor Orb and Doorkeeper Thrull ('creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger') switch off Tidebinder's and Wasp's own ETBs (and Drowner, Siren, Beavers, Wan Shi Tong), so answer them on the stack with Annul, Spell Snare, Spell Pierce or We Say Thee Nay!, or kill Thrull with Hex before deploying.
  • X is 0 for a permanent on the battlefield (CR 202.3), so Wan Shi Tong is mana value 2 there and Requiting Hex kills it at any size; save Tidebinder on its ETB for X of 4 or more.
  • The Wondrous Wasp is legendary: a second copy forces the legend rule before its trigger goes on the stack, so only one Wasp blank can exist at a time and the second copy is a rebuy, never a second lock.
  • Countering a fetch-land activation (Fabled Passage, Escape Tunnel) denies every trigger that land entry would have produced at once, the sacrificed land stays sacrificed, and Wan Shi Tong gets no search draw; a tutor activation (Maelstrom of the Spirit Dragon's Dragon search) is the same kind of target with no land involved, and none of these blank anything because the source is a land.
  • A Saga that loses all abilities is NOT sacrificed (CR 714.4 applies only to a Saga with one or more chapter abilities, the post-2024 Blood Moon and Urza's Saga rule), but lore counters are still added after each of their draw steps because that is a turn-based action, not an ability; so a Wasp on Summon: Esper Maduin, or Tidebinder on any chapter, freezes the Saga as a body with no chapters, the missed chapters never trigger, and the moment the blanker leaves with three or more lore counters on it the Saga is sacrificed and, having a finality counter, exiled.
  • Our own triggers can be answered: Spider-Sense (counter target triggered ability) and Return the Favor (copy or redirect an activated or triggered ability) both hit Tidebinder's and Wasp's ETB on the stack, so against Azorius Momo with {U} open or Jeskai with {R}{R} open the counter itself is not guaranteed.

Universal traps

  • Flashing Wasp in response to a trigger to stop it: the trigger resolves regardless (CR 113.7a); Wasp only prevents future triggers, Tidebinder handles the one on the stack.
  • Countering a manland's animation ability (Restless Reef, Cottage, Anchorage, Soulstone Sanctuary, Great Hall of the Biblioplex) to blank the land: at resolution it is still a land, so nothing is lost and it re-animates later. Restless Reef, Cottage and Anchorage: counter the ATTACK trigger while animated, or Wasp the animated body, and the blank then sticks to the permanent after it reverts. Soulstone Sanctuary and Great Hall animate permanently with no attack trigger, so Wasp them any time after animation; Great Hall's granted cast trigger is also a Tidebinder hook once it is a creature.
  • Countering a warped creature's end-step exile: whatever its source, it gifts them a permanent Harmonizer, Riddler, Shepherd or Nova, and the exile trigger was going to remove the body for you; likewise never expect a Wasp or Tidebinder blank to keep a warped body on the battlefield.
  • Countering the evoke sacrifice (Deceit, Emptiness) or a Mobilize token sacrifice: legal, but it hands them a 5/5 for two mana or permanent 1/1s while the ETB they actually wanted still resolves (and Voice's Mobilize sacrifice happens on their turn, when Voice forbids casting); target the discard, bounce or reanimate trigger instead.
  • Casting Tidebinder while the creature SPELL is on the stack (Excruciator, Spider-Man, Sunderflock, Thor): a spell is not an ability, the ETB does not exist yet, and Tidebinder becomes a 3/2 with nothing to hit; let it resolve and counter the trigger.
  • Trying to Tidebinder a static (Voice's lock, Clarion Conqueror, Momo's discount, Icetill Explorer's land permissions, Kaito's creature clause, Sunderflock's cost reduction, Corpses' anthem, Torpor Orb): none of these ever use the stack; reach a creature's statics only by countering one of its triggers or non-mana activations so it loses everything.
  • Countering 'enters with a counter' or 'enters as a copy' (Mossborn Hydra, Dyadrine, Glen Elendra Guardian, Superior Spider-Man's Mind Swap): replacement effects, not triggers.
  • Firing for a blank into open removal mana: Burst Lightning, Erode, Sear, Firebending Lesson, Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph or a Headliner ping kills the 3/2 or 2/1 with its trigger on the stack, Prismari Charm's 1 damage kills only the Wasp while its bounce mode returns either one; the counter still happens, the blank never does (ruling 1, CR 611.2b), and an unblanked Thor pings off that response spell.
  • Ninjutsu-ing Kaito off the Wasp or Tidebinder that is holding a blank, or blinking it into a Sunderflock bounce: the blank ends the moment it leaves the battlefield; return Siren or Beavers instead.
  • Blanking THEIR Wasp or Tidebinder to free our locked creature: their effect lasts as long as their creature remains on the battlefield, not as long as it has abilities; kill, bounce or Drowner-shuffle the lock piece.
  • Paying or fearing ward when Tidebinder counters a warded creature's trigger (Amalia, Emeritus, Stallion, Vren, Raubahn, Saboteur, Sheltered by Ghosts): ward triggers only when the permanent is targeted, so Tidebinder is never taxed; Wasp is.
  • Countering a 'Discard a card:' or cycling activation (Iron-Shield Elf, Hardened Academic, Marauding Mako, Intimidation Tactics) to stop the discard: the discard is a cost already paid and every discard payoff already triggered; you deny only the indestructible, lifelink or drawn card.
  • Blanking an earthbended land and expecting it to stay dead: the return is a delayed trigger created by earthbend, so a Wasp-blanked land still comes back tapped and fires landfall; only Tidebinder on the return trigger keeps it in the graveyard.
  • Casting a second Wasp while the first is alive to hold two blanks: the legend rule bins one before the new trigger resolves, so you keep exactly one blank.
  • Trying to Wasp or blank Enduring Curiosity, Innocence or Tenacity after it has returned as an enchantment: it is no longer a creature, Wasp has no target, and Tidebinder can only counter single triggers with no blank; every permanent answer works only on the creature version.
  • Wasp on a warped body (Riddler, Shepherd, Nova, Harmonizer): the body leaves at end step anyway and the tap is wasted on their untap. Gollum is a different case: he stays, so Wasp shuts him off for good while Wasp lives, at the price of one Gollum trigger off the Wasp cast itself (MV 2) if they chose even.
  • Countering the Carnage-returned creature's 'sacrifice it' trigger or blanking it: you remove drawbacks they were stuck with; counter Carnage's ETB so nothing returns.
  • Holding Tidebinder to counter Sapling Nursery's indestructible activation in response to Day of Black Sun: Black Sun's later-timestamped 'loses all abilities' strips the indestructible before the destroy, so the Treefolk die regardless.
  • Countering Political Triumph's scry trigger or one Monument to Endurance trigger instead of the payoff: the scry trigger that places the fourth plan counter only delays the payoff (counter the 'when the fourth plan counter is put' trigger instead and Triumph sits dead), and with a mass discard each Monument trigger is separate so Tidebinder counters exactly one. Casey Jones is NOT this pattern: his ETB is the best target because countering it denies the three cards and means the upkeep discard is never created.
  • Expecting the freeze to outlive Tidebinder: countering one Hydra doubling, one Harmonizer doubling or one loyalty activation is permanent, but the blank on the source ends the moment Tidebinder leaves, and the Hydra resumes doubling from its current counters; kill the blanked permanent in the same turn cycle.
  • Deploying Tidebinder or Wasp under a resolved Torpor Orb or into two open mana with Doorkeeper Thrull plausible: they enter with no trigger and are plain 3/2 and 2/1 bodies.

Top Tidebinder targets in the format

#1Mightform Harmonizer Mono-Green Landfall (18.4%), Selesnya Landfall (3.5%)
Landfall: Whenever a land you control enters, double the power of target creature you control until end of turn.
Roughly 22% of the field runs the card that turns one land entry into lethal, and one Tidebinder both counters the doubling on the stack and blanks Harmonizer so every later land that turn (fetch crack, Explorer's extra drop, an Erode basic) produces nothing; Veil, Warg Tactics, Surrak and 'can't be countered' do nothing against it. The one hole is an untapped fetch: if they crack Passage in response to Tidebinder's trigger, the new land and its doubling resolve before the counter, and countering the fetch activation instead lets the original doubling resolve and blanks nothing (the source is a land). So with a lethal doubling on the stack and a live fetch, Tidebinder cannot stop both; kill or bounce the doubling's target so both triggers fizzle (Hex, Sheriff, Bitter Triumph; a Drowner tap does not fizzle it), or fire when their fetches are tapped or gone.
#2Sunderflock Izzet Spellementals (12.2%)
When this creature enters, if you cast it, return all non-Elemental creatures to their owners' hands.
The second-largest deck's one-sided board reset, castable for {U}{U} once a Crab (MV 7) or another Sunderflock is out and for {1}{U}{U} off a lone Hearth Elemental (MV 6); countering the trigger keeps Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong and every Wasp/Drowner lock on the battlefield and strips flying so our fliers go over it. Every later copy still gets the full discount because a blanked Sunderflock is still an MV 9 Elemental, so the hold continues for copies two to four.
#3Doomsday Excruciator (hardcast or Superior Spider-Man copying it) Dimir Excruciator (5.7%)
When this creature enters, if it was cast, each player exiles all but the bottom six cards of their library face down.
Only 5.7% of the field but the impact is the whole game: Tidebinder is the only card in our 75 that stops the ETB through Cavern of Souls, and killing Tidebinder in response does not save it (ruling 1). On Spider-Man two triggers appear at once; target the library exile, not Mind Swap's 'exile that card'. Check their graveyard and land count before every turn to know when it is live.
#4Enduring Curiosity (dies-return trigger) Dimir Midrange mirror, Azorius Tempo (1.5%)
When Enduring Curiosity dies, if it was a creature, return it to the battlefield under its owner's control. It's an enchantment.
The mirror is one of the most-played matchups for a Dimir pilot and Curiosity is their highest-throughput engine; countering the return after Sheriff or Bitter Triumph kills the creature version is the one permanent answer that does not depend on Tidebinder surviving (the source is already in the graveyard, ruling 1 cuts in our favor). It also works when they trade Curiosity in combat.
#5Kaito, Bane of Nightmares (any loyalty activation) Dimir Midrange mirror
+1: You get an emblem with 'Ninjas you control get +1/+1.' / 0: Surveil 2. Then draw a card for each opponent who lost life this turn. / -2: Tap target creature. Put two stun counters on it.
Countering one loyalty ability strips Kaito of everything while Tidebinder lives, including the static that makes him a hexproof 3/4 on their turn, so on their turn he stops attacking, stops triggering Curiosity, cannot activate again and is a targetable planeswalker for Bitter Triumph at instant speed (on our turn he was always a plain planeswalker anyway); loyalty already paid stays paid. Fire only when they are tapped out (main 2 after a ninjutsu), because removal on Tidebinder in response leaves the activation countered but Kaito unblanked.
#6Icetill Explorer (mill trigger, to blank the statics) Mono-Green Landfall (18.4%), Selesnya Landfall (3.5%)
Landfall: Whenever a land you control enters, mill a card. Blanking it removes 'You may play an additional land on each of your turns' and 'You may play lands from your graveyard'.
Same 22% share as Harmonizer; the worthless mill is the only hook into the two statics that halve their landfall count and let them replay every cracked fetch. Best on their first land drop of a turn, since the second drop becomes illegal the moment the blank lands (CR 305.2a). With the lone main copy prefer Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph on Explorer (Hex misses, MV 4) and keep Tidebinder for Harmonizer; postboard with three copies fire freely.
#7Mossborn Hydra Selesnya Landfall (3.5%), Mono-Green Landfall sideboard (18.4%)
Landfall: Whenever a land you control enters, double the number of +1/+1 counters on this creature.
Exponential and permanent growth (1, 2, 4, 8, 16 across fetch drops) with trample; countering a doubling at two or more counters denies that doubling permanently, and while Tidebinder lives the blank strips trample and all further growth, leaving an X/X that Beavers, Siren, Drowner or Kaito -2 handle. The freeze ends and doubling resumes from the current count the moment Tidebinder leaves, so kill the Hydra while it is blanked. Weighted by Mono-Green's share it appears in most postboard landfall games. Hex misses it (MV 3); Sheriff and Bitter Triumph kill it at any size.
#8Casey Jones, Vigilante (ETB) Mardu Discard (4.7%)
When Casey Jones enters, draw three cards. At the beginning of your next upkeep, discard three cards at random.
Only Tidebinder stops this: Sheriff in response kills the 4/3 but the draw three resolves and the upkeep discard is still created. Countering the ETB denies the dig, means the upkeep discard is never created at all, and deletes their best discard turn (three Mako counters, six Cool but Rude damage at level 2, three Monument triggers, an Inti card), so its value scales with payoffs on board. The upkeep discard trigger is the fallback window when the mana was not there on their turn: its source is Casey (CR 603.7e), so countering it blanks him too if he is still on the battlefield.
#9Thorin, Mountain-king (ETB) Boros Dwarves (4.1%)
When Thorin enters, attach any number of target Equipment you control to target creature you control. When one or more Equipment become attached to that creature this way, that creature deals damage equal to its power to up to one target creature.
One trigger is a mass re-equip at instant speed plus their only main-deck removal, usually onto a Dragonfire Blade carrier that our mono-black spells and Wasp cannot target; Tidebinder is the cleanest answer and Cavern does not protect the trigger. Counter the first trigger by default; counter the reflexive damage half when the ping (at our Curiosity or Tidebinder) is the real threat, since its source is still Thorin and he is blanked either way. Cheaper alternative when the creature target is not Blade-protected: kill it in response, and the ETB resolves doing nothing to its illegal target (CR 608.2b), so no Equipment moves and the reflexive trigger never happens.
#10Amalia Benavides Aguirre (first explore of a gain chain) Orzhov Lifegain (4.0%)
Whenever you gain life, Amalia Benavides Aguirre explores. Then destroy all other creatures if its power is exactly 20.
Every creature they cast is one to three gain events and each one fires Amalia; countering the first explore of a turn blanks her before the rest of the chain resolves, strips Ward: Pay 3 life so Hex, Sheriff, Kaito -2 and Drowner hit her untaxed, and Tidebinder itself is never taxed because it targets the ability. Under an unblanked Voice of Victory the only Tidebinder line is countering her ward trigger on our turn when we target her, which still blanks her.

Top Wasp targets in the format

#1Mightform Harmonizer Mono-Green Landfall (18.4%), Selesnya Landfall (3.5%)
A resolved Harmonizer blanked at their upkeep, before the land drop, means no land this turn or any later turn doubles anything while Wasp lives, and Wasp also fires in response to a fetch activation, Shared Roots or Worldwagon trigger so the payoff is blanked before the basic enters. It never stops the first doubling turn when the land comes from hand, and Veil, Warg Tactics and Surrak all punish it, so aim it when green mana is tapped.
#2Mossborn Hydra Selesnya Landfall (3.5%), Mono-Green Landfall sideboard (18.4%)
Hydra is a 0/0 body that is worth exactly its counters and its growth; a 2-mana blank at their upkeep freezes it at its current size and strips trample, and on the draw against turn-1 Elves it is the only play that reaches their turn-3 Harmonizer turn. Kill it while blank (Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Drowner shuffle) before Erode or Sheltered by Ghosts undoes the Wasp; Sheltered's exile is an Aura ETB trigger, so a second Tidebinder can counter it with no blank and the Wasp stays.
#3Voice of Victory Mardu Discard (4.7%), Orzhov Lifegain (4.0%), Boros Tokens (2.1%), Boros Dragons (1.6%), Azorius Tempo (1.5%)
Across roughly 14% of the field this static shuts off both our cards and every counterspell on their turn, and Wasp is the only card in our 75 that removes a static without needing a trigger or activation to hook, cast on OUR turn since we cannot cast on theirs. It is the backup, not the default: Requiting Hex (MV 2, one mana, permanent), Sheriff and Bitter Triumph answer Voice without dying to a {W} Erode or Torch in response, so use removal on Voice and send Wasp at the payoff; Wasp the Voice only when no removal is in hand.
#4Enduring Curiosity (creature version) Dimir Midrange mirror, Azorius Tempo (1.5%)
Blanked, it loses both the draw trigger and the dies-return clause, so Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph in the same turn removes it permanently for four total mana; Requiting Hex cannot reach MV 4, which makes this Wasp's unique job. Do it when they are tapped below instant-speed removal, because Hex on Wasp before the kill resolves restores the return.
#5Icetill Explorer Mono-Green Landfall (18.4%), Selesnya Landfall (3.5%)
No trigger window needed: Wasp strips the extra land drop and graveyard land plays for as long as it lives, roughly halving their landfall count, and even blanking it in response to their first landfall stack still denies the second drop that turn. Prefer Wasp here and save Tidebinder for Harmonizer. The 2/4 has no reach and cannot block the flying Wasp; its tap only matters to clear a blocker for Tidebinder, Drowner or a ninjutsu attacker.
#6Marauding Mako Mardu Discard (4.7%)
Their win condition is a 1-drop that only matters because it grows; Wasp at their beginning of combat freezes it (counters stay) and taps it so a Practiced Offense double strike is wasted, and because Offense is a sorcery Wasp always has the later timestamp and strips the granted double strike too. Shoot the Sheriff cannot target Mako (Shark Pirate is an outlaw), so Hex and Wasp are the answers.
#7Clarion Conqueror Orzhov Lifegain (4.0%), Boros Dragons (1.6%), Azorius Tempo sideboard (1.5%)
'Activated abilities of artifacts, creatures, and planeswalkers can't be activated' turns off Kaito's loyalty abilities, Drowner's shuffle and Map tokens, Hex cannot reach MV 3, it has no trigger for Tidebinder to hook, and it is usually Cavern-cast so no counterspell touches it; Wasp on our main phase blanks it and Kaito comes back online the same turn. It does not stop Tidebinder's or Wasp's triggers or Kaito's ninjutsu. Kill it with Sheriff or Bitter Triumph when you can; Wasp when you cannot.
#8Sunderflock Izzet Spellementals (12.2%)
The only main-deck creature in the second-largest deck where the blank matters: it loses flying, so Beavers, Siren, Wan Shi Tong and Wasp attack over the 5/5 and our ground bodies can block it, and a beginning-of-combat tap skips one flying attack. Never in response to its ETB (the bounce resolves and returns Wasp to hand); if Tidebinder already blanked it, keep Wasp as a tempo body.

Hold or fire

  • Count their open mana before every Tidebinder or Wasp: the counter is guaranteed (except into Spider-Sense or Return the Favor mana), the blank is not, so fire for the blank only when they are tapped below their cheapest instant, and fire into open mana only when the countered ability alone is worth the card (a lethal double, an Excruciator or Sunderflock ETB, a Thor trigger of 5+, a Wan Shi Tong ETB at X of 4 or more).
  • Hold Tidebinder only for a named trip-wire that is on the battlefield or castable next turn (Harmonizer, Hydra or Explorer out; a Crab, Hearth or Sunderflock out; an Excruciator in their graveyard with four lands or six lands; Thor at five lands; a Casey or Tersa turn with payoffs down); with no trip-wire live, develop and stop holding.
  • Holding {2}{U} on their turn is nearly free because Drowner, Wasp, Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong and all our removal have flash or instant speed: if no window appears, spend the mana at their end step on Curiosity, Drowner or Wan Shi Tong, not on Tidebinder as a bare body while the trip-wire is still live.
  • The Kaito tension is the one real conflict: do not tap out for a sorcery-speed Kaito on turn 4 while a live engine trigger is coming next turn; delay him a turn or ninjutsu him off Siren or Beavers, never off a Wasp or Tidebinder that is holding a blank.
  • Prefer cheap permanent removal to a temporary blank whenever both are legal: Requiting Hex kills every MV 2-or-less engine (Voice of Victory, Belladonna Took, Kili, Momo, Inti, Mako, their Drowner, their Wasp, Chocobo, Wan Shi Tong at any size), so blank only what Hex cannot reach (MV 3+, hexproof carriers, Cavern-cast Dragons) or when the trigger already on the stack is the problem.
  • A blank is a window, not a lock: the same turn cycle you blank something, kill it (Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Drowner stun then shuffle, Kaito -2), because Erode, Burst Lightning, Sear, Lesson, Sheriff, Triumph, Emptiness, Daydream, Conductor blinks and Sunderflock bounces all end it for one to two mana; when the answer is Sheltered by Ghosts or Seam Rip, their exile is an ETB trigger, so a second Tidebinder saves the lock piece.
  • Wasp fires early, Tidebinder holds: Wasp's best moments are their upkeep before a land drop (landfall payoffs), their beginning of combat before attackers (the biggest attacker, the Practiced Offense target, an animated Restless Reef), our main phase before our own spell (Voice, Clarion, Guardian, Queen of Dale), and our end step only when Voice forbids casting on their turn.
  • Play/draw math: on the play our third land is untapped during their turn 3, so hold for their turn-3 engine (Harmonizer warp, Tersa or Casey, Tablet, Corpses); on the draw we have two lands then, so Tidebinder is live from their turn 4 and turn 3 belongs to Wasp on Elves or Hydra, Hex on Chocobo or Voice, or killing the doubling's target so the trigger fizzles.
  • Under an unblanked Voice of Victory or Jennifer Walters, flip the plan: kill or Wasp-blank the lock on our main phase first, and until then Tidebinder is castable only on our turn at our-turn triggers (Lorehold's upkeep loot, Amalia's ward trigger when we target her, Queen's recruit off our spell, Farm Animals' sac at Curiosity) or as a 3/2 body.
  • Under a resolved Torpor Orb or Doorkeeper Thrull, both cards are flash bodies: deploy them at end of turn, do not cast Wan Shi Tong for X above 0, and lean on Bitter Triumph, Sheriff, Hex, Kaito -2 and Day of Black Sun.
  • Against control shells with few permanents (4c Control, Jeskai Control, Golgari Midrange), default to firing both as flash bodies at their end step to start the clock, and hold Tidebinder only for windows you can see coming (Thor at five lands, Deceit's discard, Fel's loyalty, a Ritual Chamber Demon when Hex and Drowner are not in hand).
  • Against cheap aggro (Mono-Black, Boros Tokens, Azorius Momo), Tidebinder is body-first: take the on-curve counter that wastes their turn (Corpses of the Lost's token, Saboteur's attack trigger, Headliner's sac-ping at a lock piece) and otherwise flash it in as a blocker by their turn-3 or turn-4 end step rather than letting it rot.
  • Never Wasp a warped body and never Tidebinder a warped creature's ETB unless the trigger itself is the threat (a Harmonizer doubling); the body leaves at end step anyway and countering the exile trigger only hands them a permanent body.
  • Countering an ETB leaves the body, so when the body is the problem and the trigger is not (Hellkite, hardcast Deceit, Thor on a tapped-out turn), We Say Thee Nay! on a non-Cavern spell or Bitter Triumph is usually better than a blank; when the trigger is the problem (Excruciator, Sunderflock, Casey, Thorin, Aven Interrupter's exile of our spell, Aang's airbend of our Curiosity or Kaito), Tidebinder is the answer.

Sideboarding the extra Tidebinders

Tidebinder count across the field. Bring both sideboard copies in (3 total) against Mono-Green Landfall (cut Spell Snare or We Say Thee Nay!, never Shoot the Sheriff; also add Flashfreeze x2 and Annul for Torpor Orb), Izzet Spellementals (cut 3 to 4 Requiting Hex, whose only target is Hydro-Man; add Spell Pierce), the Dimir mirror (cut 2 Spell Snare, or 1 Snare and 1 Siren; keep all 4 Hex, which kills a summoning-sick Drowner, their Wasp and Wan Shi Tong), 4c Control (as Duress- and Negate-proof flash bodies with the Thor counter as upside; cut 4 Hex, 2 Bitter Triumph, 1 Sheriff, 2 Drowner for Duress x3, Tidebinder x2, Flashfreeze x2, Spell Pierce x2), Dimir Excruciator (the only card that beats a Cavern-protected Excruciator ETB and two in hand is the real safety against Deceit and Gearhulk; cut 4 Hex, 2 Snare, 2 Wan Shi Tong for 2 Tidebinder, 3 Duress, 2 Spell Pierce, 1 Ghost Vacuum), Mardu Discard (every copy has live targets and nothing has hexproof or ward; add Flashfreeze x2, Annul, Day of Black Sun cast before Wasp; leave Duress out), Boros Dwarves (Thorin's ETB on a Blade carrier, Blade and Axe equips which blank the Equipment itself, Chainsaw's ETB; cut 2 Wan Shi Tong, keep 23 lands and Spell Snare), Golgari Midrange (Deceit, Fel, Chamber; cut 2 Hex, keep both Sheriff) and Selesnya Landfall if your 75 carries them. Bring one in (2 total) against Orzhov Lifegain (three Voice plus Erode and Emptiness make a third copy dead too often, and Ancient Vendetta punishes stacking one 3-drop; add 2 Day of Black Sun at X=2, Vren, Strategic Betrayal; cut 2 We Say Thee Nay!, 2 Wan Shi Tong, 1 Beavers), Jeskai Control (12 instant burn spells make the blank fragile and Return the Favor can redirect the trigger; bring Duress x3, Spell Pierce x2, Flashfreeze x2, Annul, 1 Tidebinder; cut 4 Hex, 1 Sheriff, 3 to 4 Drowner) and Azorius Tempo (Duress, Annul and Spell Pierce first, then one Tidebinder, which also counters Aven Interrupter's exile of our spell and Aang's airbend; cut Shoot the Sheriff x2 since Aven Interrupter is a Rogue). Leave it at 1 against Azorius Momo (most of their engine is static, spell, mana ability or warp body, and Spider-Sense answers the trigger), Boros Tokens (their triggers land behind Voice, and Hex on Took beats the blank), Mono-Black Aggro (turns 1 to 4 are decided by one- and two-mana interaction; all ten of their instants, 4 Shoot the Sheriff, 4 Dissection Practice and 2 Requiting Hex, end a Wasp blank, while only the four Sheriffs end a Tidebinder blank) and Boros Dragons (a countered Hellkite ETB still leaves a 4/5; Burst Lightning and Erode end either blank and Charring Bite ends only Tidebinder's, since it cannot target a creature with flying; spend slots on Sheriff and Bitter Triumph instead). Two structural cautions on going to 3: exile-by-name effects hit every copy at once, and each needs a different hook, Deadly Cover-Up with evidence needs a Tidebinder already in our graveyard, The End needs one on the battlefield to target, and Ancient Vendetta can name it blind (Golgari, Orzhov, 4c); and the third copy competes for the same {2}{U} as Spell Snare, Spell Pierce and Curiosity on their turn. Wasp count. Keep 2 in every matchup and never go to zero: it is the only card in our 75 that removes a static with no trigger or activation to hook (Voice, Clarion Conqueror, Momo's discount, Riddler's draw replacement; Glen Elendra Guardian is different, its counter is an activated ability Tidebinder can counter to blank it, and Day of Black Sun is the sweeper version), the only answer besides removal to Enduring Curiosity and Innocence that makes the kill permanent, and a 2-mana flash flier that enables ninjutsu and Curiosity draws against decks with no fliers or reach (Mardu, Mono-Black, Boros Tokens, game-1 Selesnya). Do not add a third from outside the 75 except arguably against Boros Dragons, where it blanks both statics that beat us; the legend rule means two on the battlefield is only a rebuy. The only places a Wasp comes out are Golgari Midrange (first creature cut if a slot is needed for Duress or Spell Pierce, since its Sting has almost no targets and it dies to Hex, Sheriff and Nowhere to Run) and Jeskai Control (optionally 1 Wasp for the fourth Drowner's shuffle redundancy); against Azorius Momo the second copy is redundancy against 3 to 4 Erode, not a second lock. Cross-deck pairings that protect the blanks: Spell Pierce covers Erode, Sheltered by Ghosts, Seam Rip and Torpor Orb; Spell Snare hits Get Out, Sear, Sheriff, Nowhere to Run, Voice, Took, High Noon, Bilbo's Gambit and the Stoke Genius adventure; Flashfreeze counters the whole green and red threat suite (Harmonizer including the warp cast, Hydra, Explorer, Ascension, Origins flashback, Thor, Revelation, Chainsaw, Casey, Tersa, Song of Totentanz) but never Surrak or Big Bear; Annul is the hard counter for Torpor Orb, Cool but Rude, Tablet of Discovery, Dragonfire Blade, Rest in Peace and Monument to Endurance; and a spare Tidebinder counters the Sheltered by Ghosts or Seam Rip exile trigger itself. Day of Black Sun sizing: X=2 kills our own Wasp, Drowner, Siren, Beavers and Wan Shi Tong, X=3 kills Tidebinder, X=4 kills Kaito on our turn and Curiosity (blanked, so it stays dead); sweep first and deploy the blankers afterward, and never sweep at X of 3 or more with a live Tidebinder blank holding the game.
vs. Mono-Green Landfall18.4% TB: premiumWasp: good▼
Every land that enters is a payday: Sazh's Chocobo ("Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature") grows, Mightform Harmonizer ("Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, double the power of target creature you control until end of turn") turns one land entry into lethal, and Icetill Explorer ("You may play an additional land on each of your turns. You may play lands from your graveyard.") plus 8 fetch lands (Fabled Passage, Escape Tunnel), Shared Roots and Earthbender Ascension manufacture two to four land entries a turn. Their earliest real Harmonizer turn is turn 3 with Llanowar Elves (Forest + Forest + Elves: warp for {2}{G} precombat, then play or crack the third land), turn 4 without Elves; a doubled-twice Chocobo or earthbended land swings with trample from Ascension or Summon: Esper Maduin's chapter III. They also kill with no Tidebinder target ever exposed: Chocobo + Ascension quest counters + Sapling Nursery Treefolk, which is why Requiting Hex on Chocobo on sight matters as much as anything on this page. Post-board they add Mossborn Hydra (counter doubling), Torpor Orb (turns off our ETB triggers, including Tidebinder's and Wasp's) and Gigantic Big Bear (hexproof, can't be countered).
Tishana's Tidebinder
The deck's engine is landfall TRIGGERS on CREATURES (4 Harmonizer, 4 Explorer, 4 Hydra post-board), so one Tidebinder both counters the trigger that would kill you and blanks the engine creature while it lives. Their protection suite is irrelevant to it: Snakeskin Veil and Warg Tactics give a creature hexproof, but Tidebinder targets the ABILITY on the stack, not the creature, and Surrak's draw trigger ('Whenever a creature you control ... becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls') never fires off Tidebinder. It does not touch the Chocobo/Ascension/Nursery half of their kills; Hex, Shoot the Sheriff and Bitter Triumph cover those. Two things beat it: Meltstrider's Resolve making the blanked 4/4 fight your 3/2 (the blank ends when Tidebinder dies), and post-board Torpor Orb ('Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger'), a static ability you must Annul on the stack.
#1Mightform Harmonizer
Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, double the power of target creature you control until end of turn.
WhyThis is the card that turns a 4/5 Chocobo into a 16/5 with two land entries. Countering the trigger stops the doubling on the stack AND blanks Harmonizer, so every later land that turn (Explorer's second drop, a Passage crack) produces no doubling at all. Killing the doubling's target instead (Requiting Hex on Chocobo, 1 mana) makes the trigger fizzle but leaves Harmonizer alive for the next land.
WhenTheir turn, immediately after a land enters: all landfall triggers go on the stack at once (Chocobo counter, Explorer mill, Harmonizer double, Nursery token, Ascension quest). They order them; you get priority with everything on the stack. Flash Tidebinder, target Harmonizer's trigger wherever they stacked it. If they warped it ({2}{G}) that turn, this is still the window: the end-step exile is a delayed trigger created by the warp SPELL (CR 702.185a, 603.7g), it still exiles the blanked Harmonizer, and they may recast it from exile on a later turn as a fresh 4-mana creature with its abilities back. On the play you have 3 lands during their turn 3 (the first realistic warp turn), so hold {2}{U} on their turn 3 and 4. On the draw you have only 2 lands during their turn 3: Tidebinder is live from their turn 4, and the turn-3 warp is answered by Hex/Shoot on the doubling's target in response (fizzle).
AfterHarmonizer stays a 4/4 body with no landfall trigger. Any doubling that already resolved this turn stays until end of turn. Abilities come back the moment Tidebinder leaves the battlefield.
RiskThey cannot respond by playing a land from hand (CR 305.1: a special action, stack must be empty), but they CAN respond by cracking an untapped Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel. The fetched basic makes a NEW Harmonizer trigger that goes on the stack above Tidebinder's trigger and resolves first, while Harmonizer is still unblanked (the blank only begins when Tidebinder's trigger resolves). They get one full doubling, then the original is countered and Harmonizer is blanked. If they hold an untapped Passage/Tunnel and one doubling is lethal, counter the fetch activation instead (target 4) or wait for the fetch crack rather than the land from hand. Also: Chocobo, Nursery and Explorer triggers on the same stack still resolve.
#2Icetill Explorer
Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, mill a card. (The two statics 'You may play an additional land on each of your turns' and 'You may play lands from your graveyard' cannot be targeted; you reach them by countering the mill.)
WhyCounter a worthless mill trigger and Explorer loses both static abilities: no second land drop, no replaying Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel from the graveyard. This halves their landfall count for as long as Tidebinder lives. But Explorer is slow value that Shoot the Sheriff and Bitter Triumph kill outright (Requiting Hex cannot: mana value 4), and only Tidebinder or a fizzle beats a Harmonizer trigger already on the stack. With your lone main-deck Tidebinder, remove Explorer with Shoot/Triumph and keep Tidebinder for Harmonizer; fire it on Explorer only when you have no other Explorer answer and hold Hex/Shoot as the Harmonizer backup. Post-board with 3 Tidebinder, fire on Explorer freely.
WhenAny land entering while Explorer is out puts the mill trigger on the stack; counter it. Best moment is their FIRST land drop of a turn: once Explorer is blanked they cannot make the second land drop that turn (CR 305.2a: the number of lands they may play is checked when they try to play it). Countering the trigger does not undo the land already played.
AfterExplorer is a 2/4 with no text. Lands already on the battlefield stay; cards already milled stay in the graveyard (Esper Origins in the yard is still flashback-castable, that is a spell, not an ability). Regains everything if Tidebinder dies.
RiskLow. Explorer is 2/4, so your 3/2 cannot attack into it; the value is entirely in the blank.
#3Mossborn Hydra (sideboard, 4 copies)
Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, double the number of +1/+1 counters on this creature.
WhyPost-board this is the second Harmonizer: 1 counter becomes 2, 4, 8, 16 with two land drops a turn. Counter a doubling and the Hydra is frozen at its current counters and loses trample. Shoot the Sheriff and Bitter Triumph kill it at any size; Requiting Hex does not (mana value 3). Prefer Shoot on Hydra and save Tidebinder for Harmonizer unless the doubling on the stack would take it to 8+.
WhenHydra is cast precombat for 3 and the land follows the same turn (their turn 2 with Elves, turn 3 otherwise), usually before you have 3 mana, so expect to catch the 2-to-4 or 4-to-8 doubling, not the first. 'This creature enters with a +1/+1 counter on it' is a replacement effect and cannot be countered, so it always starts at 1/1.
AfterKeeps its existing +1/+1 counters (counters are not abilities), loses trample and future doubling. A 2/2 or 4/4 forever while Tidebinder lives. If Tidebinder dies the doubling resumes from wherever the counters are.
RiskIf Hydra already has 8+ counters, blanking it does not solve the body; Bitter Triumph or Drowner it and save Tidebinder for Harmonizer.
#4Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel (land fetch activations)
Fabled Passage: '{T}, Sacrifice this land: Search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle. Then if you control four or more lands, untap that land.' Escape Tunnel: '{T}, Sacrifice this land: Search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle.'
WhyCountering the fetch means the land never enters, so EVERY landfall trigger for that fetch is denied at once (Chocobo, Harmonizer, Explorer, Nursery, Ascension), and the sacrificed Passage stays sacrificed (costs stay paid): they are down a land. This beats countering a single landfall trigger when three or more payoffs are on the table and the lethal math needs zero triggers, and it is the answer to the 'crack a fetch in response to Tidebinder' play in target 1. Neither ability is a mana ability (it adds no mana), so it is a legal target. Nothing gets blanked: at resolution the source is a land card in their graveyard, not an artifact, creature or planeswalker.
WhenWhen they activate it, in their main phase (a landfall deck cracks fetches on its own turn to bank Chocobo counters and Harmonizer doublings). Early Passages (turns 1-2) are cracked before you have 3 mana, so this is a mid-game play (turn 4+) against a board of 3+ payoffs, when a fetch is played from hand or replayed from the graveyard. Only one ability can be countered; if the land already entered, this window is gone.
AfterPassage/Tunnel is in their graveyard. If Icetill Explorer is alive and unblanked they can replay it from the graveyard as a land drop and crack it again, so this is a full Stone Rain only once Explorer is handled. Side effect for us: no search happened, so Wan Shi Tong does not trigger.
RiskSpends your Tidebinder on a one-shot with no lasting blank. Correct choice only when the immediate landfall stack would be lethal or near-lethal and Harmonizer is not the single point of failure.
#5Meltstrider's Resolve (2 main, 2 side)
When this Aura enters, enchanted creature fights up to one target creature an opponent controls.
WhyThis is their only way to kill the blanker and switch the engine back on: a blanked Harmonizer is still a 4/4 and a blanked Explorer a 2/4, so Resolve on it fights your Tidebinder (3/2) or Wasp (2/1), kills it, and every blanked permanent regains its text at once. A second Tidebinder countering the fight trigger protects the first. Secondary use: countering the fight saves Enduring Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong or a Drowner. The Aura is an enchantment, so nothing is blanked and it keeps '+0/+2 and can't be blocked by more than one creature'.
WhenIn response to the ETB trigger, on their turn. Cheaper answers when a blank is load-bearing: We Say Thee Nay! or Spell Pierce on the Aura spell itself, or kill the enchanted creature while the Aura or its trigger is on the stack (the Aura fizzles or the fight has no fighter).
AfterAura stays on their creature; no fight happens.
RiskLow, but only fire if the creature being fought matters.
#6Lumbering Worldwagon (1 main)
'Whenever this Vehicle enters or attacks, you may search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle.' Also counterable: 'Crew 4' (an activated ability, CR 702.122a).
WhyCountering the ETB or attack trigger denies a landfall AND, since Wagon is an artifact, it loses all abilities: the characteristic-defining 'This Vehicle's power is equal to the number of lands you control' is gone so its power is 0 (printed */4 becomes 0/4), and it has no crew, so it can never become a creature again while Tidebinder lives. Countering a crew activation does the same: the creatures they tapped stay tapped and Wagon does not animate.
WhenETB trigger the turn it is cast (3 mana, so turn 2-3), or the attack trigger in their declare-attackers step: if it was crewed this turn it stays a creature until end of turn but attacks as a 0/4.
AfterA 0/4 artifact with no text. Lands it already fetched stay.
RiskIt is a one-of; do not hold Tidebinder for it.
#7Earthbender Ascension
When this enchantment enters, earthbend 2. Then search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle.
WhyCountering the ETB denies a land (so no landfall triggers from it that turn) and the 2-counter haste land creature. But Ascension is an enchantment: it keeps its landfall ability and the quest counters keep coming.
WhenWhen the ETB trigger is on the stack, after the Ascension spell resolves and it enters (their main phase, typically turn 3). Annul or Flashfreeze on the spell is the cheaper answer.
AfterAscension stays with all abilities; they simply did not get the land and the animated land. If allowed to resolve, the search triggers Wan Shi Tong.
RiskSpends your best card on ramp denial while Harmonizer is still in their deck.
#8Summon: Esper Maduin (back face of Esper Origins, Enchantment Creature, Saga Elemental 4/4)
III, Other creatures you control get +2/+2 and gain trample until end of turn. (Also counterable: I, Reveal the top card of your library. If it's a permanent card, put it into your hand. II, Add {G}{G}. Chapter II is NOT a mana ability, CR 605.1b and the card's own Scryfall ruling: it uses the stack.)
WhyChapter III is a team pump with trample on their attack turn and it comes from a CREATURE, so countering it blanks the Saga. A Saga with no chapter abilities is not sacrificed (CR 714.4 requires 'one or more chapter abilities') and gets no further lore counters (714.3c), so it sits as a 4/4 body. The moment Tidebinder leaves, its abilities return, it has 3 lore counters, and it is sacrificed at once (exiled by the finality counter, which works even on an ability-less permanent per the card's ruling). Late-game only: it needs Esper Origins in the graveyard, 4 mana to flash back, then two more turns; worth Tidebinder only when their board is 3+ creatures and the pump is lethal.
WhenChapter III triggers as their precombat main phase begins on the second turn after the Saga entered (lore counter 3). Counter it there, before attackers. The Saga only enters via the flashback cast from the graveyard ({3}{G}) with a finality counter; the front face is a sorcery and cannot be countered by Tidebinder (Spell Snare can: mana value 2 even when flashed back).
After4/4 body with no chapters, keeps its finality counter and lore counters. Other creatures do not get +2/+2 or trample this turn.
RiskLow value if their board is one creature; the pump is what matters. Chapters I and II are counterable but almost never worth the card.
#9Keen-Eyed Curator (1 main, 1 side)
{1}: Exile target card from a graveyard. (The +4/+4 clause 'As long as there are four or more card types among cards exiled with this creature, it gets +4/+4 and has trample' is static and cannot be targeted; countering one activation switches it off.)
WhyOnce switched on it is a 7/7 trampler. Counter any {1} activation and Curator loses the static: back to 3/3, no trample, no more exiling. Cards already exiled stay exiled but no longer count for anything.
WhenAny time they pay {1} for it, often at your end step. It is not a mana ability (it has a target).
After3/3 with no text.
RiskThey can activate cheaply and repeatedly, so wait for the activation that would turn on the +4/+4, or just kill it with Requiting Hex (mana value 2) or Shoot the Sheriff (Raccoon Scout is not an outlaw).
#10Earthbend return trigger ('When that land dies or is put into exile, return it to the battlefield tapped')
The delayed triggered ability created by earthbend (CR 701.66a), fired when you kill or exile the earthbended land creature.
WhyAfter you Shoot / Bitter Triumph an earthbended Forest, this trigger puts it straight back. Countering it is the only way to keep that land dead: a real Stone Rain against a deck that lives on land count. Its source is Ba Sing Se (a land) or Earthbender Ascension (an enchantment) per CR 603.7e, so nothing is blanked. Best with Vren, the Relentless ('If a creature an opponent controls would die, exile it instead') or Strategic Betrayal: the land goes to exile, the return still triggers ('dies or is put into exile'), and if you counter it the land is gone for good, and Icetill Explorer cannot play it from exile.
WhenWhen the return trigger is on the stack after the land creature leaves the battlefield, on either player's turn.
AfterThe land stays in the graveyard (an unblanked Explorer can replay it as a land drop) or in exile (gone).
RiskOnly a 3/2 body plus a land for a Tidebinder; take it only when nothing bigger is live and land count is what they are short on.
Traps
Llanowar Elves / Forest / Ba Sing Se ({T}: Add {G}): Counter the mana ability to blank Elves. Mana abilities cannot be targeted, Tidebinder's own reminder text says so. Wasp can tap-and-blank Elves; Tidebinder cannot touch it.
Icetill Explorer: Target 'You may play an additional land' or 'You may play lands from your graveyard'. Both are static abilities and never go on the stack. You get them only by countering the landfall mill trigger, which blanks the creature.
Mightform Harmonizer (warp): Counter the warp cast, or counter the end-step exile to 'keep it blanked forever'. Warp is a casting permission (CR 702.185a), not an ability on the stack; use We Say Thee Nay!, Flashfreeze or Spell Pierce for the spell. The exile IS a delayed triggered ability and is technically counterable, but its source is the warp SPELL (CR 603.7g; the spell object no longer exists, CR 400.7), so countering it blanks nothing: they keep a permanent 4/4 Harmonizer with a live landfall trigger. Never do this. Likewise, blanking Harmonizer does NOT stop the exile: the trigger is not an ability of the permanent.
Esper Origins (front face) and its Flashback: Tidebinder the 'Surveil 2. You gain 2 life...' spell or the flashback. Both are casting a spell, not abilities. Spell Snare answers it (mana value 2 even when cast from the graveyard). Only the back face's chapter abilities are Tidebinder targets.
Mossborn Hydra: Counter 'This creature enters with a +1/+1 counter on it' so it dies as a 0/0. That is a replacement effect, not a trigger. It always enters as a 1/1. Hit a landfall doubling instead.
Sapling Nursery / Earthbender Ascension landfall triggers: Counter the token or quest-counter trigger to blank the enchantment. Both sources are enchantments; Tidebinder's blank only applies to an artifact, creature or planeswalker (ruling 3). You counter exactly one token or one counter and the enchantment keeps working. Nursery's 'Affinity for Forests' is a static cost reducer and untouchable.
Sapling Nursery indestructible activation: Save Tidebinder to counter '{1}{G}, Exile this enchantment: Treefolk and Forests you control gain indestructible until end of turn' in response to Day of Black Sun. Unnecessary: Day of Black Sun says 'Each creature with mana value X or less loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures.' Its later-timestamped ability loss strips the granted indestructible before the destroy, so the Treefolk die regardless. Countering the activation only matters against your single-target removal, and the Nursery is already exiled as a cost.
Surrak, Elusive Hunter / Gigantic Big Bear ('This spell can't be countered'): Tidebinder is turned off by 'can't be countered'. That clause protects the spell, not abilities. Surrak's draw trigger is a legal target. Big Bear simply has no activated or triggered abilities; nothing to hit, and Wasp cannot target it (hexproof).
Surrak, Elusive Hunter: Tidebinder on a Harmonizer trigger gives them a Surrak draw. Surrak needs 'a creature you control or a creature spell you control' to become the target. Tidebinder targets the ability on the stack, so Surrak does not trigger. Wasp, Drowner, Kaito's -2 and Bitter Triumph all do trigger him.
Snakeskin Veil / Warg Tactics (hexproof): They can Veil in response to protect Harmonizer from Tidebinder. Tidebinder targets neither the creature nor its controller; hexproof on the creature is irrelevant, and the 'loses all abilities' part is not targeted. Veil only beats Wasp and Drowner.
Torpor Orb (sideboard, 2): Counter the Orb's effect. 'Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger' is static. With Orb on the battlefield Tidebinder, Wasp, Drowner, Siren, Beavers and Wan Shi Tong (his X counters and draw are an ETB) have none of their ETBs; Faebloom Trick and Kaito's -2 still work. Answer the spell with Annul, Spell Pierce or We Say Thee Nay!; once it resolves, Tidebinder and Wasp are only flash bodies.
Earthbended land ('When that land dies or is put into exile, return it to the battlefield tapped'): Counter the return trigger to blank the land, or expect Wasp's blank to switch the return off. The return is a delayed trigger created by earthbend (CR 701.66a), not an ability of the land. Its source is Ba Sing Se or Earthbender Ascension (CR 603.7e), so countering it blanks nothing, and a Wasp-blanked land still returns when it dies. It IS worth countering as a Stone Rain (target 10); just do not expect a blank.
Shared Roots: Counter the land search. It is a sorcery; Tidebinder cannot counter spells. Spell Snare (mana value 2), Flashfreeze or Spell Pierce.
Wasp in response to a landfall trigger: Flashing Wasp on Harmonizer while its doubling trigger is on the stack counters the doubling. An ability on the stack exists independently of its source (CR 113.7a). Blanking Harmonizer only stops FUTURE triggers; the one on the stack resolves. Only Tidebinder (or killing the doubling's target so it fizzles) stops the current one.
Not worth it
Sazh's Chocobo landfall: freezing a 1-drop is poor value when Requiting Hex (destroys a creature with mana value 2 or less: Chocobo, Elves, Curator), Shoot the Sheriff or Drowner just remove it; save Tidebinder for the engine.Sapling Nursery landfall: you deny one 3/4 Treefolk and the enchantment keeps making more.Earthbender Ascension quest-counter trigger and its reflexive 'When you do': one +1/+1 counter and trample, enchantment keeps working.Ba Sing Se '{2}{G}, {T}: Earthbend 2': they lose 3 mana and a land tap, but a land is not blanked and Tidebinder is worth more later.Escape Tunnel's '{T}, Sacrifice this land: Target creature with power 2 or less can't be blocked this turn': usually a Chocobo-sized nuisance. The one exception: power is checked only on targeting and resolution, so an unblockable Chocobo that then gets doubled by Harmonizer is the lethal line; counter the activation (Tunnel stays sacrificed) only when Harmonizer is out and you cannot hit its trigger.Demolition Field '{2}, {T}, Sacrifice this land: Destroy target nonbasic land an opponent controls...' is a land swap plus a landfall for them (they may put a basic onto the battlefield), not a Stone Rain on themselves; countering it denies the landfall, the destroy and their basic, and letting it resolve gives Wan Shi Tong a draw. Still a 1-of aimed at Gloomlake Verge, Watery Grave, Hidden Lair, Restless Reef or Soulstone Sanctuary; fire only if the landfall stack it would create is the problem.Surrak's draw trigger: one card denied and a 4/3 blanked, but Surrak is a 1-of main (a second copy comes in post-board) and Bitter Triumph handles him.Summon: Esper Maduin chapters I and II: a card and two mana; wait for III.The warp end-step exile trigger: never counter it, you would be gifting them a permanent Harmonizer with its trigger intact.
The Wondrous Wasp
Wasp is a reliable blank for the two engine creatures (Harmonizer, Explorer) on the turns AFTER they land, and a tempo tap for a lethal Chocobo, but it is the card their sideboard is aimed at: Snakeskin Veil and Warg Tactics fizzle its trigger (hexproof), Warg Tactics' first mode 'Destroy target creature with flying' kills it outright, Meltstrider's Resolve makes the blanked 4/4 fight it, Surrak draws them a card the moment Wasp targets anything, 3/4 reach Treefolk block and kill it, and Torpor Orb switches off its ETB. It never stops an engine creature's FIRST landfall turn when the land comes from hand (no priority window between the creature resolving and the land drop), but it does when the land comes from a Passage/Tunnel crack, Shared Roots or Worldwagon: respond to that and the payoff is blanked before the land enters.
#1Mightform Harmonizer
WhyBlank it and no land this turn or any later turn doubles anything while Wasp lives. Tapping is irrelevant; the blank is the whole point.
WhenTheir upkeep or draw step of the turn AFTER Harmonizer resolves, before they can play a land. Wasp never stops the first doubling turn itself, warped or hard-cast, when the land comes from hand: they cast Harmonizer, get priority as it resolves, and play the land before you can act; and Wasp in response to a trigger already on the stack does nothing to it (CR 113.7a). Exception: if that turn's land comes from a Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel activation, Shared Roots or Worldwagon's trigger, flash Wasp in response; Wasp resolves first, Harmonizer is blanked before the basic enters, and no landfall trigger is ever created. Against a warped Harmonizer the upkeep window never comes (it is exiled at end step), so the fetch-response is the only Wasp line.
AfterA 4/4 with no text; any doubling that already resolved stays until end of turn. Everything returns if Wasp dies, is bounced, or is ninjutsu'd back to hand for Kaito.
#2Icetill Explorer
WhyBlank removes the extra land drop and graveyard land plays for as long as Wasp lives, cutting their landfall count roughly in half. 2 mana for that is a fine trade.
WhenTheir upkeep before land drops. If you are late and they already played one land, blanking Explorer in response to the first landfall stack still denies the second land drop this turn, because the permission is checked when they try to play it (CR 305.2a). Explorer is a 2/4 that blocks and kills Wasp if untapped, so tap it in your own beginning of combat only if you need the attack.
After2/4 with no text. Lands already played stay.
#3Sazh's Chocobo
WhyTwo jobs: tap it in their beginning of combat so it cannot attack this turn, and blank it so it stops growing. It keeps every +1/+1 counter it already has (counters are not abilities), so this is damage control, not a kill; Requiting Hex is the kill.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, AFTER main-phase land drops have resolved (the counters it would have gotten are already there and no more come) and BEFORE attackers are declared. Tapping an already-attacking Chocobo does nothing; a tapped attacker still deals damage.
AfterAn X/X+1 with no landfall trigger, tapped this turn only; it untaps normally next turn but stays frozen at that size while Wasp lives.
#4Mossborn Hydra (post-board)
WhyBlank freezes the counter count and removes trample. Same logic as Chocobo but the growth is exponential, so the earlier the better. Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph kill it at any size; Hex cannot (mana value 3).
WhenTheir upkeep the turn after it resolves (it will already have 1 counter from the replacement effect, or 2 after its first land). If it already doubled twice, tapping it before attackers is still fine tempo.
AfterKeeps its counters, loses trample and doubling.
#5Lumbering Worldwagon (crewed)
WhyWhile crewed it is an artifact creature, so Wasp can target it: it is tapped (no attack, no attack-trigger land), loses the characteristic-defining power (0/4), the land-fetch trigger and Crew 4, and because Wasp's effect applies to the object regardless of type it stays blanked after it stops being a creature at end of turn: a 1-of engine piece dead for as long as Wasp lives, and the 4 power they tapped to crew stays tapped.
WhenTheir beginning of combat, after Crew 4 resolves and before attackers are declared. Only then is it a creature; an uncrewed Wagon is not a legal Wasp target.
AfterA 0/4 artifact with no text that cannot crew again while Wasp lives.
#6Summon: Esper Maduin (4/4 creature Saga)
WhyBlanked Sagas lose their chapters, are not sacrificed (CR 714.4) and stop getting lore counters (714.3c). Blanked in their upkeep at 1 or 2 lore counters it sits as a plain 4/4; when Wasp leaves it resumes from the same lore count and the remaining chapters happen later. It is sacrificed on unblank (exiled via the finality counter) only if it already had 3 lore counters when blanked, which is the Tidebinder-countered-chapter-III case, not this one.
WhenTheir upkeep before the precombat-main lore counter that would trigger chapter II or III.
After4/4 body, no chapters, keeps its finality counter and current lore counters; chapters resume when Wasp leaves.
#7Keen-Eyed Curator
WhyBlank removes the +4/+4 static (7/7 becomes 3/3) and the graveyard-exile ability.
WhenAny time; beginning of combat if it is the attacker.
After3/3 with no text; cards already exiled stay exiled but no longer count.
#8Treefolk token (3/4 reach) or Surrak, Elusive Hunter
WhyBlanking a Treefolk removes reach so Wasp, Spyglass Siren, Dream Beavers and Wan Shi Tong fly past it into Enduring Curiosity draws; tapping it removes a blocker on your turn. Surrak: blank removes trample and the draw trigger, but targeting him with Wasp lets him draw one card first (his trigger resolves before Wasp's).
WhenYour own beginning of combat (remove the blocker) or their beginning of combat (remove an attacker).
AfterTreefolk: 3/4 with no reach. Surrak: 4/3 with no text.
#9Llanowar Elves
WhyTap and blank Elves in their upkeep and their turn-3 warp (Forest + Forest + Elves) does not happen: after their land drop they have 3 mana but no land left to trigger Harmonizer. On the draw this is your only proactive answer to the turn-3 warp, since Tidebinder mana is not up yet.
WhenTheir upkeep on turn 2 (on the play) or turn 3 (on the draw, the turn that matters).
AfterA 1/1 with no mana ability while Wasp lives; tapped this turn.
#10Earthbended land (0/0 land creature with two +1/+1 counters and haste)
WhyBlanking it strips its intrinsic mana ability (CR 305.6) and haste while Wasp lives; it stays a 2/2 land creature (the creature status and counters are effects, not abilities). It does NOT switch off the return: that is a delayed trigger created by earthbend (CR 701.66a, Scryfall ruling), so if the blanked land dies or is exiled it still returns tapped. Only Tidebinder countering the return trigger keeps it dead.
WhenAfter the earthbend resolves (Ba Sing Se's activation is sorcery-speed; Ascension's earthbend is an ETB trigger that resolves after the enchantment enters in their main phase), before attackers.
AfterA 2/2 land creature with no mana ability and no haste; keeps its two +1/+1 counters, stays a land, and still returns if it dies.
Traps
Gigantic Big Bear: Wasp it to blank hexproof/haste. It has hexproof; Wasp cannot target it at all. Nothing in either card interacts with the Bear; you need blockers or Day of Black Sun (X=7).
Snakeskin Veil / Warg Tactics mode 2: Wasp the Harmonizer into open green mana. 'It gains hexproof until end of turn' in response makes Wasp's trigger fizzle for having no legal target; you keep a 2/1 flyer and nothing is blanked. Two Veil and one Warg Tactics in the 75, mostly post-board. Warg Tactics mode 1 'Destroy target creature with flying' also kills Wasp itself, which ends the blank.
Meltstrider's Resolve: Once Harmonizer or Explorer is blanked by Wasp the job is done. The blanked creature is still a 4/4 or 2/4 body. Resolve on it ('enchanted creature fights up to one target creature an opponent controls') kills your 2/1 Wasp and every blank ends at once. Keep We Say Thee Nay! / Spell Pierce for the Aura, or Tidebinder for the fight trigger, when the blank is holding the game together.
Surrak, Elusive Hunter: Wasp's target is free. 'Whenever a creature you control ... becomes the target of ... an ability an opponent controls, draw a card' triggers off Wasp (and Drowner, Kaito -2, Bitter Triumph). They draw before Wasp's trigger resolves.
Any attacking creature: Flash Wasp after attackers are declared to tap the attacker out of combat. Tapping does not remove a creature from combat; it still deals damage. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step or earlier.
Mightform Harmonizer with a landfall trigger on the stack: Wasp counters the doubling. The trigger exists independently of Harmonizer (CR 113.7a). Only Tidebinder counters it. Wasp works one step earlier: in response to the fetch ACTIVATION, before the land enters.
Kaito ninjutsu returning Wasp: Ninjutsu Kaito in off an unblocked Wasp for free. Returning Wasp to hand ends 'for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield': the Harmonizer or Explorer gets all its text back at once. Return Siren, Beavers or Drowner instead.
Torpor Orb on the battlefield: Wasp still taps something. 'Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger': Wasp's Sting never triggers. It is a 2/1 flash flyer and nothing else.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Landfall stack with Harmonizer + Chocobo: Tidebinder counters Harmonizer's trigger (blanks it), then Kaito's -2 ('Tap target creature. Put two stun counters on it') or Floodpits Drowner's ETB stuns the now-frozen Chocobo. Cheaper alternative, and the ONLY answer to a turn-3 warp on the draw when you have 2 lands: Requiting Hex or Shoot the Sheriff on the creature Harmonizer's trigger targets; with no legal target the doubling fizzles, but Harmonizer stays live for the next land.
  • Fetch-response line: when they crack Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel (or cast Shared Roots, or Worldwagon's trigger is on the stack) with Harmonizer, Hydra, Chocobo or Explorer out, flash Wasp in response at the payoff. Wasp resolves first, the payoff is blanked, the basic enters into nothing. Tidebinder in response to the fetch instead denies the land itself (and Passage stays sacrificed). Either way Wan Shi Tong loses the search draw.
  • Beware the reverse: with Tidebinder's trigger on the stack aimed at a Harmonizer trigger, they can crack an untapped Passage/Tunnel; the new doubling resolves before Harmonizer is blanked (CR 113.7a). If that doubling is lethal, target the fetch activation instead.
  • Turn plan on the play: hold {2}{U} on their turns 3 and 4 (cast nothing sorcery-speed on your turn 3; Drowner, Hex, Wasp, Curiosity and Wan Shi Tong are all instant speed). On the draw: their turn-3 warp is Hex/Shoot-on-the-target territory, Wasp on Elves in their turn-3 upkeep blunts it, and Tidebinder mana is live from their turn 4.
  • Kaito tension: Kaito is sorcery-speed and taps 4, so casting him on turn 4 means no Tidebinder on their turn 4. With Tidebinder in hand and Harmonizer/Explorer/Hydra unanswered, delay Kaito a turn or ninjutsu him in off Siren or Beavers (never off Wasp or Tidebinder while their blank matters). If you hold mana on their turn and no target appears, spend it at their end step on Drowner, Wan Shi Tong (X=1) or, with 4 lands, Curiosity, not on Tidebinder as a body.
  • Protect the blank: once Tidebinder or Wasp is holding Harmonizer/Explorer off, Meltstrider's Resolve is their out (the blanked 4/4 fights your 3/2). Keep We Say Thee Nay! / Spell Pierce for the Aura spell or a second Tidebinder for the fight trigger; killing the enchanted creature while the Aura or its trigger is on the stack also works.
  • Blank Explorer (via the mill trigger), then every Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel they crack is a real Stone Rain target for a later Tidebinder, because they can no longer replay it from the graveyard. Wan Shi Tong ('Whenever an opponent searches their library, put a +1/+1 counter on Wan Shi Tong and draw a card') feeds on every fetch, Shared Roots, Ascension ETB, Demolition Field and Worldwagon trigger you let resolve.
  • Land kill: Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph the earthbended Forest, then Tidebinder the earthbend return trigger; with Vren, the Relentless out the land is exiled instead of dying, the return still triggers, and countering it removes the land for good (Explorer only plays lands from the graveyard).
  • Day of Black Sun with X=4 kills Chocobo, Harmonizer (mana value 4 even if warped), Explorer, Curator, Hydra and Treefolk tokens, and its own 'loses all abilities until end of turn' strips any indestructible Sapling Nursery granted in response, so keep Tidebinder for the Harmonizer trigger instead of the Nursery activation. Post-DoBS, Tidebinder and Wasp mop up the rebuild.
  • Post-board they bring Torpor Orb: keep Annul / Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay! for the Orb on the stack. If it resolves, stop holding Tidebinder and Wasp as answers; run them out as 3/2 and 2/1 flash bodies at end of turn, do not cast Wan Shi Tong for X>0, and lean on Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff, Requiting Hex, Faebloom Trick, Kaito -2 and Day of Black Sun (Drowner's ETB is also off).
  • Wasp blanks one Treefolk's reach or taps it in your beginning of combat so Wasp, Spyglass Siren, Dream Beavers and Wan Shi Tong connect for Enduring Curiosity draws ('Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card').
  • Snakeskin Veil and Warg Tactics cannot protect their creature from Tidebinder, so vs a Veil-heavy opponent prefer Tidebinder over Wasp/Drowner for the engine pieces. Spell Snare counters Esper Origins whether cast from hand or flashed back (mana value 2); Flashfreeze counters any green spell except Surrak and Gigantic Big Bear ('This spell can't be countered').
Hold or fire
Hold Tidebinder whenever Mightform Harmonizer, Icetill Explorer or (post-board) Mossborn Hydra is on their battlefield or could arrive this turn, and accept the cost: that means Kaito waits a turn or comes in by ninjutsu off Siren/Beavers, and unused mana on their turn goes to Drowner / Wan Shi Tong / Curiosity at their end step, never to Tidebinder as a 3/2 body (it does nothing against 3/4 reach Treefolk and a growing Chocobo). Fire on the first landfall stack that contains a Harmonizer or Hydra doubling. If only Explorer is out and you hold your lone Tidebinder, kill Explorer with Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph and keep Tidebinder for Harmonizer; fire it on the mill trigger only when you have no other Explorer answer and still hold Hex/Shoot as the Harmonizer backup (kill the doubling's target in response so it fizzles). Post-board with 3 copies, fire on Explorer freely. Counter a Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel activation instead when three or more payoffs would trigger off that land, or when they hold an untapped fetch that would let a new doubling resolve ahead of your Tidebinder trigger. Wasp: fire in their upkeep at a Harmonizer or Explorer that resolved last turn (before land drops), in response to a fetch activation / Shared Roots / Worldwagon trigger at any landfall payoff, or in their beginning of combat at the one attacker that is lethal; never in response to a landfall trigger expecting to stop it, and never into open green mana once you have seen Snakeskin Veil or Warg Tactics. Turn math: their first real Harmonizer turn is turn 3 with Elves (warp, then land), turn 4 without. On the play your third land is down during their turn 3, so hold {2}{U} on their turns 3 and 4. On the draw you have 2 lands during their turn 3: Tidebinder is live from their turn 4; use Wasp on Elves in their turn-3 upkeep and Requiting Hex / Shoot on Chocobo (or on the doubling's target in response) to survive turn 3. Once their engine is dead (Harmonizer/Explorer/Hydra removed, or none seen by turn 6), Tidebinder as an end-step body is fine. Post-board, if Torpor Orb resolves, flip the rule: both cards become flash bodies, deploy them at end of turn and stop holding.
How many to run
Tidebinder: bring in both sideboard copies for 3 post-board, and this deck's 18.4% share is the strongest argument for a second main-deck copy. Cut a Spell Snare (main-deck hits here are Esper Origins, Shared Roots and Curator, all low impact) or a We Say Thee Nay!, never Shoot the Sheriff: nothing in their 75 is an outlaw and only Gigantic Big Bear is hexproof, so Shoot kills Harmonizer, Explorer, Hydra (mana value 3, out of Hex range), Curator and Surrak for 2 mana at instant speed, and that answer is not undone by Meltstrider's Resolve or Warg Tactics killing a 3/2. Tidebinder's edge over Shoot is the trigger already on the stack plus immunity to Veil/Warg Tactics/Surrak. Wasp: keep 2, do not add; it is good against a Harmonizer/Explorer on the turns after they land and in response to fetch cracks, but it fizzles to Veil/Warg Tactics, dies to Warg Tactics mode 1, Resolve fights and 3/4 reach Treefolk, draws Surrak a card, and cannot preempt a land played from hand. Also bring in Flashfreeze x2 and Annul (Torpor Orb, Ascension, Nursery); if they show Torpor Orb, the third Tidebinder is still fine as a body but stop adding more.
Review trail: confidence high, 1 disputed, 25 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Strategy reviewer: downgrade the Tidebinder verdict from 'premium' because the Chocobo/Ascension/Nursery kill exposes no target. Kept 'premium' (the reviewer's suggested 'strong' is not a valid value): 12 of their creature engine cards post-board (4 Harmonizer, 4 Explorer, 4 Hydra) plus Worldwagon, Curator and the Saga are artifacts/creatures with triggered or activated abilities, all 8 fetch lands are counterable activations that deny every landfall at once, and their entire protection suite (Snakeskin Veil, Warg Tactics, Surrak, 'can't be countered') does nothing against an ability-targeting counter.
