I accepted the underlying point by deleting 'best matchup in the format' (not defensible without a cross-deck comparison), adding the target-free kill path to how_they_win, and leading with 'Hex the Chocobo on sight' in the summary.
Changes after review
  • Fixed FATAL: Wasp target on the earthbended land no longer claims the return trigger is removed. Verified CR 701.66a (earthbend creates a delayed trigger 'When that land dies or is put into exile, return it') and the Scryfall ruling on Ba Sing Se / Earthbender Ascension ('the delayed triggered ability created by earthbend'); a Wasp-blanked land still returns.
  • Fixed MAJOR: Tidebinder target 1 risk now warns that they can crack Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel in response to Tidebinder's trigger and the new Harmonizer doubling resolves before the blank begins (CR 113.7a, blank starts on resolution); added the counter-the-fetch mitigation and a combined line.
  • Fixed MAJOR: warp exile trap. The delayed trigger's source is the warp spell (CR 702.185a with 603.7g, spell object gone per 400.7), so countering it blanks nothing; page is now consistent with target 1. Changed the citation from 603.7d to 603.7g (warp is a static ability of the spell that allows an action and creates the delayed trigger; either way the source is the spell, not the permanent).
  • Fixed MAJOR: Wasp on Summon: Esper Maduin at 1-2 lore counters resumes chapters when Wasp leaves; only a Saga blanked at 3 counters is sacrificed on unblank (CR 714.3c, 714.4). Added the Scryfall ruling that a finality counter still exiles an ability-less permanent.
  • Fixed MAJOR: turn math. Turn-2 warp with a payoff does not exist (Elves + 2 Forests uses the turn-2 land before Harmonizer resolves); earliest real Harmonizer turn is 3 with Elves, 4 without. On the draw Tidebinder is live from their turn 4; on the play hold on their turn 3. Removed the impossible 'keep {2}{U} open from turn 3 on the draw' instruction.
  • Fixed MAJOR: hold_or_fire now states the Kaito tension (sorcery-speed 4-drop vs holding 3), what to do with unused held mana (Drowner / Wan Shi Tong / Curiosity at end step), and when Tidebinder-as-body becomes acceptable.

  • Fixed MINOR: Surrak is 1 main + 1 side (deck file), not a plain 1-of.

vs. Izzet Spellementals12.2% TB: goodWasp: situational▼
Turns 1-3 they cycle cheap cantrips and burn (Opt, Sleight of Hand, Prismari Charm, Burst Lightning, Traumatic Critique) to fill the graveyard, which makes their Elementals nearly free: Eddymurk Crab ("This spell costs {1} less to cast for each instant and sorcery card in your graveyard", Flash, 5/5, ETB taps up to two creatures) and Hearth Elemental ("costs {X} less... where X is the number of cards in your graveyard that are instant cards, sorcery cards, and/or have an Adventure", 4/5). Once any Crab, Hearth or Sunderflock is on board, Sunderflock ({7}{U}{U}, "costs {X} less... X is the greatest mana value among Elementals you control") costs {U}{U} to {1}{U}{U}, and its ETB "return all non-Elemental creatures to their owners' hands" is one-sided because every creature they run is an Elemental. Engine: spells into free 5/5s on turns 3-5, then a 5/5 flyer that resets our board, protected by Get Out, Spell Pierce and Spell Snare. Tidebinder and Wasp cannot touch the 28-spell half of the deck; their whole value here is the Sunderflock and Crab ETBs (plus Stallion post-board).
Tishana's Tidebinder
Four targets that decide games (the 4 Sunderflock bounce triggers) and four conditional ones (Crab taps, worth countering only when a second answer to Sunderflock is in hand or the taps cost you lethal-relevant damage or a Curiosity connection). Countering the bounce keeps our board, keeps any Wasp/Drowner lock alive, and leaves them a ground 5/5 with no flying. Not premium: 53 of their 61 main-deck cards (28 spells, 21 lands, 4 Hearth Elemental) give Tidebinder nothing to target, the 3/2 dies to Burst Lightning and Sear, and Get Out (2 main) counters the Tidebinder spell itself.
#1Sunderflock
"When this creature enters, if you cast it, return all non-Elemental creatures to their owners' hands."
WhyTheir one-sided board reset: all their creatures are Elementals, so it bounces Enduring Curiosity (to hand, not 'dies', so no enchantment rebuy), Wan Shi Tong (loses all counters), Beavers, Siren, Drowner, Wasp and Tidebinder, and bouncing Wasp/Drowner ends their 'loses all abilities' locks. Kaito is a planeswalker on their turn and is never bounced; an animated Soulstone Sanctuary has all creature types, so it is an Elemental and survives. Countering the trigger keeps everything, and Sunderflock (a creature as the trigger resolves) loses all abilities: Flying goes, so Beavers, Siren and Wan Shi Tong fly over it and our ground bodies can block it. Note that a blanked Sunderflock is still an Elemental with mana value 9, so every later Sunderflock copy costs {U}{U}; the fight is not over after copy one.
WhenTheir main phase only (Sunderflock has no flash), typically their turn 4-6 once a Crab or Hearth is out (Sunderflock then costs {U}{U} or {1}{U}{U}; a Stallion alone only gets it to {4}{U}{U}, Hydro-Man alone to {5}{U}{U}). Either fight the spell with We Say Thee Nay! or let it resolve; when it enters, the bounce trigger goes on the stack and you get priority. Cast Tidebinder (a creature spell, no target). When it resolves and enters, its ETB goes on the stack above the bounce trigger; choose the bounce trigger as its target. Tidebinder's ETB resolves first and counters the bounce. On the draw their turn 4 comes while you have 3 lands: it is Tidebinder or nothing, so hold {2}{U} from your turn 3 once an Elemental is out. On the play you have 4 lands at their turn 4, enough for Tidebinder plus Spell Snare.
AfterLoses: Flying, the cost-reduction static, the ETB. Keeps: 5/5 body and any counters. It still must be answered (Floodpits Drowner stun then shuffle, Kaito -2, Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph). Blank ends if Tidebinder leaves. A Sunderflock they bounce with Get Out mode 2 or Prismari Charm and recast is a new object with all abilities and a fresh bounce trigger (recast costs {U}{U} only if a Crab or another Sunderflock is still out); rare, do not hold Spell Snare for it.
RiskBurst Lightning ({R}, 2) or Sear ({1}{R}, 4) on Tidebinder in response to its ETB: the bounce is still countered (ruling 1) but Sunderflock keeps Flying. Prismari Charm's 1 damage does not kill the 3/2; Traumatic Critique needs X=2 ({2}{U}{R}, 4 mana), unrealistic the same turn they cast Sunderflock. Impractical Joke is a sorcery: it cannot respond, it can only kill Tidebinder on their next turn, which restores Sunderflock's Flying then. Get Out mode 1 ('Counter target creature or enchantment spell') on the Tidebinder spell lets the bounce happen; Spell Snare (MV 2) answers Get Out but Tidebinder plus Snare is 4 open mana on their turn. If they hold {U}{U} after casting Sunderflock and you have only 3 mana, weigh letting the bounce resolve and re-buying your ETBs instead of walking Tidebinder into Get Out.
#2Eddymurk Crab
"When this creature enters, tap up to two target creatures."
WhyThe Crab's only on-board value is the two taps, and they time them: flashed at our end step to tap two potential blockers for their turn, at our beginning of combat to tap Enduring Curiosity plus a body (costing combat-damage draws), or in their precombat main to tap our blockers before a 5/5 swing. Countering the trigger keeps our creatures untapped and leaves a vanilla 5/5. Kaito can never be a Crab target (hexproof creature on our turn, not a creature on theirs). Warning: this Crab is itself an Elemental, so once it is on the battlefield Sunderflock costs {U}{U} on their very next main phase; a Crab ETB is never a free Tidebinder target.
WhenWhenever they flash it: the ETB goes on the stack with its targets chosen; respond by casting Tidebinder and targeting the trigger with its ETB. On our turn (their Crab at our beginning of combat) Tidebinder competes for the same {3} as Kaito's ninjutsu ({1}{U}{B}) off an unblocked flier; with 5 or fewer lands prefer the ninjutsu Kaito (hexproof on our turn, immune to Sunderflock) and let the taps resolve unless they hit Curiosity plus your only flier. A Crab cast on our turn enters tapped regardless; Tidebinder does not untap it.
AfterLoses: nothing that still matters (Flash, the cost reduction and the enters-tapped replacement all only work on the stack or on entry). Keeps: 5/5 body, still an Elemental with mana value 7 for Sunderflock's discount. The win is the untapped creatures, not the blank.
RiskCounter a Crab ETB only if (a) a second Tidebinder or a teamwork We Say Thee Nay! is in hand for Sunderflock, or (b) the taps cost lethal-relevant damage or a Curiosity connection you cannot afford to lose. Otherwise you have spent the Sunderflock answer at exactly the moment Sunderflock turns on.
#3Colorstorm Stallion (sideboard, 4)
"Opus, Whenever you cast an instant or sorcery spell, this creature gets +1/+1 until end of turn. If five or more mana was spent to cast that spell, create a token that's a copy of this creature."
WhyPost-board they bring 4 hasty 3/3s that grow with every spell and clone themselves off a 5-mana spell (Traumatic Critique X=3, kicked Burst Lightning, unreduced harmonize on Winternight Stories at {4}{U}). Countering one Opus trigger blanks the Stallion while Tidebinder lives: Ward {1}, haste and Opus all go, so Drowner, Kaito -2 and Sheriff hit it untaxed. Ward does not tax Tidebinder here because Tidebinder targets the trigger on the stack, not the Stallion. Token copies are their own Elementals with full Ward {1}, haste and Opus: a second Tidebinder can counter a token's Opus trigger the same way. Stallion is an Elemental: it survives the Sunderflock bounce and makes Sunderflock cost {4}{U}{U} on its own.
WhenThe Opus trigger goes on the stack above the instant or sorcery they just cast; counter it while their spell is still on the stack (their spell still resolves). Best moment: a trigger from a spell they spent 5+ mana on, denying the token copy and blanking the Stallion in one shot.
AfterLoses: Ward {1}, haste, Opus. Keeps: 3/3 body plus any +1/+1 from Opus triggers that already resolved this turn. Copy tokens already created keep all abilities.
RiskDo not fire on a +1/+1 pump from a 1-mana cantrip while Sunderflock is uncast and you hold no other answer; a blanked Stallion is still a 3/3 attacker.
#4Hydro-Man, Fluid Felon (sideboard, 1)
"At the beginning of your end step, untap Hydro-Man. Until your next turn, he becomes a land and gains '{T}: Add {U}.'" (also: "Whenever you cast a blue spell, if Hydro-Man is a creature, he gets +1/+1 until end of turn.")
WhyHe is a creature when the end-step trigger goes on the stack (the previous land effect ended when their turn began), so countering it blanks him while Tidebinder lives: he stays a 2/2 with no abilities, does not become a mana source, and the blue-spell pump is gone too. Countering the pump trigger instead gives the same blank. Low value: a one-of sideboard 2/2, and an Izzet pilot is unlikely to bring him in against us.
WhenTheir end step, with the untap/become-a-land trigger on the stack; or their turn, with a pump trigger on the stack above a blue spell.
AfterLoses both triggered abilities while Tidebinder lives (they return the moment Tidebinder leaves). Keeps: 2/2 body; stays a creature on our turn, so Wasp, Drowner, Kaito -2 and Requiting Hex (MV 2) can now hit him.
RiskOnly fire if Tidebinder has no better job left in the game and you want the 3/2 body now.
Traps
Hearth Elemental: Hold Tidebinder to counter Hearth Elemental or its 'Discard your hand, then draw two cards' adventure. Hearth Elemental has no activated or triggered ability. Its cost reduction is static, the adventure (Stoke Genius) is a spell being cast, and 'You may cast the creature later from exile' is casting permission. Tidebinder does nothing. Spell Snare does: the adventure half is a mana value 2 spell on the stack, and a countered adventure goes to the graveyard, not exile, so they lose the 4/5 too. We Say Thee Nay! also works.
Eddymurk Crab / Sunderflock / Hearth Elemental: Tidebinder can stop the cost reduction so Sunderflock costs nine again. 'This spell costs {X} less to cast' is a static ability applied while the spell is being cast. It is never on the stack and cannot be targeted. Blanking a Crab or Sunderflock also does not change its mana value or Elemental type, so the discount persists.
Eddymurk Crab: Counter the 'enters tapped if it's not your turn' so the Crab comes in on our terms. That is a replacement effect, not a triggered ability; it has already applied by the time the Crab is on the battlefield. Tidebinder can only hit the 'tap up to two target creatures' trigger.
Eddymurk Crab: Counter the Crab ETB after we have already declared attackers. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat; it still deals damage. If they flash Crab after attackers are declared, its taps are nearly worthless and Tidebinder should be saved.
Eddymurk Crab (first Elemental): No Elemental is on their board yet, so this Crab ETB is a free Tidebinder target. The Crab whose ETB is on the stack is already on the battlefield and is an Elemental with mana value 7: Sunderflock costs {U}{U} on their next main phase. Countering it spends your Sunderflock answer at the exact moment Sunderflock turns on.
Winternight Stories: Counter the harmonize cast from the graveyard, or the 'tap a creature to reduce the cost' part. Harmonize is casting a spell from the graveyard; tapping a creature is part of paying its cost. Neither is an ability on the stack. Counter the spell with We Say Thee Nay!, not Tidebinder.
Burst Lightning: Counter the kicker so it only deals 2. Kicker is an additional cost paid while casting; the spell's effect checks whether it was kicked. Nothing to target.
Traumatic Critique / Prismari Charm / Get Out / Opt / Sleight of Hand: Counter the 'draw two, then discard' or a Charm mode with Tidebinder. These are spell effects, not abilities. 28 of their 61 main-deck cards are instants and sorceries and Tidebinder cannot interact with any of them; that is why Tidebinder is 'good' here and not 'premium'.
Riverpyre Verge / Steam Vents / Multiversal Passage: Deny mana by countering a land ability. The Verge's conditional tap is a mana ability (untargetable); 'As this land enters, you may pay 2 life' and 'choose a basic land type' are replacement/static effects with nothing on the stack.
Colorstorm Stallion: Pay Ward {1} when Tidebinder counters the Opus trigger. Ward triggers only when the permanent itself becomes the target of a spell or ability. Tidebinder targets the Opus trigger on the stack, not the Stallion, so no ward trigger and no tax. (Wasp targeting the Stallion does get taxed.)
Sunderflock: Countering the ETB removes Sunderflock or bounces it back. Countering an ETB never removes the permanent. You still face a 5/5 (now without flying); it needs Drowner, Kaito -2, Sheriff or Bitter Triumph.
Sunderflock (later copies): Once the first Sunderflock is handled, later copies cost nine again. Any Sunderflock on their battlefield, blanked or not, is an Elemental with mana value 9, so every further copy costs {U}{U}. With 4 copies in the deck, the second Sunderflock (often cast alongside Get Out) is the one that usually ends the game; keep holding for it.
Not worth it
Hearth Elemental: nothing to target at all; treat it as a vanilla 4/5 and answer it with Drowner, Sheriff or Bitter Triumph.Hydro-Man (1 sideboard copy): never hold a card for him. If you ever see him, Tidebinder his end-step trigger or Wasp him on THEIR turn (he is a land on ours); both blank him into a 2/2 that Requiting Hex kills.Stallion's Ward {1} trigger when our Drowner, Kaito -2 or Sheriff targets it: countering the ward trigger with Tidebinder does blank the Stallion and lets the removal resolve untaxed, but paying {1} is cheaper than 3 mana and a card. Only do it when the Sunderflock fight is over and you want the 3/2 body anyway.Crab ETBs that tap creatures you did not need untapped (e.g. a Siren and a Beaver at our end step, which would not block a 5/5 anyway): let it resolve, keep Tidebinder for Sunderflock.Get Out / Prismari Charm bouncing their own blanked Sunderflock to recast it: rules-legal (new object, fresh bounce trigger) but a line Izzet pilots almost never take; do not hold Spell Snare for it.
The Wondrous Wasp
Their creatures are big bodies whose abilities are spent once they hit the battlefield, so the blank does nothing to Crab or Hearth Elemental; its real ability-value is stripping Flying from Sunderflock (the only creature in their 75 that can block our fliers) and shutting off Stallion's Opus engine post-board (through Ward {1}). The tap half is the everyday use: tap the lone untapped 5/5 in our precombat so Enduring Curiosity (4/3) and other ground attackers connect, or tap a 5/5 in their beginning of combat to skip one attack. If you also hold Floodpits Drowner, Drowner is usually the better tempo tap on a 5/5 (tap plus stun plus shuffle setup); Wasp is for the Flying strip or when Drowner is not in hand. Fragile: Prismari Charm's 1 damage kills Wasp (often alongside a Siren or Beaver) and releases whatever it blanked; Spell Snare and Get Out counter the Wasp spell; Shore Up gives hexproof in response.
#1Sunderflock
WhyThe only main-deck creature where the blank matters: it loses Flying, so Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren, Wan Shi Tong and Wasp itself attack over it freely and our ground bodies can block it. Tapping it in their beginning of combat also stops one 5/5 flying attack.
WhenBlank value: any time after it is on the battlefield; if Tidebinder already countered its ETB it is already blanked, so save Wasp. Tap value: their beginning of combat (before attackers are declared) to stop the swing, or our precombat main to remove the blocker. Never in response to its ETB trigger: the trigger on the stack resolves independently of its source, the bounce still happens, and Wasp itself is returned to hand.
AfterLoses: Flying and the (irrelevant) cost-reduction static. Keeps: 5/5 body, Elemental type and mana value 9 (later Sunderflocks still cost {U}{U}). Effect ends the moment Wasp leaves; Prismari Charm's 1 damage or a later Sunderflock bounce of Wasp restores Flying.
#2Eddymurk Crab
WhyPure tap value for our GROUND attackers: with the lone untapped 5/5 tapped, Enduring Curiosity (4/3), Tidebinder and Drowner attack safely and every attacker is unblocked, which also gives Kaito's ninjutsu more candidates. Crab and Hearth have neither flying nor reach, so Beavers, Siren and Wan Shi Tong were never blocked by them; do not spend Wasp to 'clear the way' for fliers unless the blocker is Sunderflock. In their turn, a Crab cast in their precombat main enters untapped, and Wasp tapping it before attackers are declared denies one attack. The blank removes nothing relevant (Flash, cost reduction, enters-tapped all already spent).
WhenOur precombat main or beginning of combat (any time before blockers are declared removes a blocker); their beginning of combat, before attackers are declared, to prevent its attack. A Crab flashed in on our turn entered tapped anyway, so it is not a blocker that turn and Wasp's tap on it is wasted.
AfterLoses: nothing that matters. Keeps: 5/5 body. Wasp does not undo taps the Crab's ETB already put on our creatures.
#3Colorstorm Stallion (sideboard)
WhyBlanking it turns off Opus (the +1/+1 per spell and the 5-mana token copy) and Ward {1}, leaving a 3/3 our removal hits untaxed; tapping it in their beginning of combat stops a hasty attack. Costs {1}{U} plus the {1} ward tax, so budget 3 mana. Token copies can be Wasped the same way, each through its own Ward {1}.
WhenTheir beginning of combat if you need to stop the attack; otherwise their end step or our main phase for the blank. Pay the Ward {1} when it triggers, or Wasp's trigger is countered and Wasp is just a 2/1 flyer.
AfterLoses: Ward {1}, haste, Opus. Keeps: 3/3 body and any pump already applied this turn. Copy tokens made earlier are unaffected.
#4Hearth Elemental
WhyTap only. 4/5 with no on-battlefield abilities and no flying/reach; blank value is zero. Tap it to remove a blocker for Curiosity or to skip an attack when it is their only relevant body.
WhenOur precombat (blocker) or their beginning of combat (attacker).
AfterUnchanged 4/5.
#5Hydro-Man, Fluid Felon (sideboard, 1)
WhyBlanking him on their turn removes both his triggers, so at their end step he does not untap or become a land, and he stays a targetable 2/2 creature into our turn. One-of, opportunistic only.
WhenWasp must RESOLVE on their turn before their end step begins (their main phase or combat), while he is a creature. Do not wait for the end-step trigger: once it is on the stack it resolves regardless of the blank (he untaps, becomes a land, and the '{T}: Add {U}' he gains afterwards is kept per ruling 2). On our turn he is a land and not a creature, so he is not a legal Wasp target at all.
AfterLoses both triggers while Wasp lives; stays a 2/2 creature that Requiting Hex (MV 2) now kills.
Traps
Sunderflock: Flash Wasp in response to Sunderflock's ETB to blank it and stop the bounce. An ability already on the stack is independent of its source; removing the source's abilities does not counter it. The bounce resolves, returns Wasp (a Human, not an Elemental) to our hand, and the blank ends. Only Tidebinder (counter the trigger) or a counterspell on the Sunderflock spell stops the bounce.
Eddymurk Crab: Wasp in response to the Crab ETB so our creatures are not tapped. The taps on our creatures still resolve either way. Wasp's own tap on the Crab is irrelevant on our turn (it entered tapped) and is only a one-turn attack denial when they cast Crab in their own precombat main.
Any attacker: Tap an attacking Crab/Sunderflock after attackers are declared to stop the damage. Tapping does not remove a creature from combat. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared.
Colorstorm Stallion: Wasp targets Stallion for 2 mana. Ward {1} triggers because Wasp's ability targets the permanent; pay {1} or the tap-and-blank is countered and you have a 2/1 flyer for 2.
Eddymurk Crab / Hearth Elemental: Tap the 5/5 so Beavers and Siren can attack. Neither Crab nor Hearth has flying or reach; our fliers already connect over them. Tapping them only helps ground attackers (Curiosity, Tidebinder, Drowner). Only Sunderflock blocks fliers.
Any creature (Shore Up in sideboard): The blank is a lock you can lean on as removal. Shore Up ('gains hexproof until end of turn') in response makes Wasp's trigger fizzle. Prismari Charm's 1 damage to two targets kills Wasp and a Siren/Beaver and restores the creature's abilities; Burst Lightning, Sear and (on their turn) Impractical Joke do the same. Plan for the lock to end; use Drowner/Sheriff/Bitter Triumph for actual removal.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Sunderflock enters, bounce trigger on the stack: cast Tidebinder, its ETB targets and counters the bounce. Sunderflock is now a ground 5/5 with no abilities. To delete it: flash Floodpits Drowner on THEIR turn (their end step), so the stun counter is still on Sunderflock during our turn and Drowner, untapped and under our control since our turn began, pays {1}{U},{T} to shuffle both away. A Drowner cast on OUR turn does not work for this: the stun counter is removed at their untap step instead of Sunderflock untapping, and by our next turn there is no counter to target.
  • Blanked Sunderflock, Drowner already on our board and untapped: Kaito -2 ('Tap target creature. Put two stun counters on it') then Drowner {1}{U},{T} shuffles it away the same turn. Kaito -2 is also the fix when Drowner was cast on our turn: two counters means one survives their untap step.
  • Sunderflock on the stack and they are tapped low: We Say Thee Nay! with teamwork (tap any creatures with total power 2 or more: Wasp, Drowner, Tidebinder, Curiosity, or Siren plus Beaver together) forces them to pay {4}. Sunderflock is usually cast for {U}{U}, so count their open mana after casting: 3 or fewer untapped means Nay is a hard counter and saves Tidebinder; 4+ means let it resolve and Tidebinder the trigger instead.
  • They hold {U}{U} up with Get Out: when Get Out mode 1 targets your Tidebinder spell, Spell Snare the Get Out (mana value 2). This needs 4 open mana on their turn (Tidebinder 3 + Snare 1): on the play from their turn 4, on the draw from their turn 5. Spell Snare also counters Prismari Charm, Sear, Hydro-Man, Flashfreeze, Disdainful Stroke and the Stoke Genius adventure half of Hearth Elemental (a countered adventure goes to the graveyard, no 4/5 later).
  • Enduring Curiosity has flash. From the turn an Elemental is on their board, pass with mana up: if Sunderflock comes, Tidebinder (3) its trigger; if their turn ends without it, flash Curiosity (4 lands) or Drowner/Wasp (2-3 lands) in their end step. Flashing Curiosity after Sunderflock has resolved or been answered is how you keep it out of the bounce entirely.
  • Our turn, they have one untapped 5/5 (Crab or Hearth): Wasp in precombat main taps it, then Enduring Curiosity (4/3) attacks alongside the fliers (which were never blockable by Crab/Hearth); each connection draws, and an unblocked attacker ninjutsus into Kaito ({1}{U}{B}), who is hexproof on our turn and cannot be Crab-tapped.
  • Wasp on Sunderflock strips Flying, so Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren and Wan Shi Tong swing over it every turn while Curiosity converts each connection into a card. If Tidebinder already blanked Sunderflock, skip Wasp and keep it as a tempo body.
  • Post-board, Stallion Opus trigger from a 5-mana Traumatic Critique/kicked Burst Lightning/harmonized Winternight Stories on the stack: Tidebinder counters it (no ward tax), no token copy, Stallion is a vanilla 3/3; Shoot the Sheriff or Drowner then answers it with no Ward {1}. A second Tidebinder does the same to a token copy's Opus trigger.
  • Kaito is your Sunderflock-proof threat: on their turn he is a planeswalker, so the bounce never touches him, and Drowner, Wasp, Tidebinder, Beavers and Siren can be recast after a bounce while he keeps ticking. Keep him out of Sear/Impractical Joke/Traumatic Critique range where you can (all three hit planeswalkers).
  • Their Crab flashed in at our beginning of combat targeting Curiosity plus a body: Tidebinder counters the taps and we attack as planned (Tidebinder itself is summoning sick), Curiosity draws. Do this only with a second Tidebinder or a teamwork Nay in hand for Sunderflock, and only if the {3} is not better spent on Kaito's ninjutsu this combat.
  • Letting the bounce happen on purpose: when our board is only Beavers/Siren/Drowner/Wasp and Tidebinder is not in hand (or Get Out is up), develop normally, take the bounce, and re-buy the ETBs; the replayed Drowner (flash, on their turn if possible) stuns the Sunderflock itself, and stun counters already on their creatures stay. The bounce hurts only when Curiosity (4 mana, no rebuy) or Wan Shi Tong (loses counters) is on board, or when you lack mana to replay next turn.
Hold or fire
Tidebinder is the Sunderflock answer first and everything else second, but hold it by reading two boards. THEIR board: the trip-wire is a Crab, Hearth or Sunderflock on their battlefield (Sunderflock then costs {U}{U} to {1}{U}{U} on their next main phase); a Stallion or Hydro-Man alone only gets it to 6-7 mana, not yet live. After any Sunderflock resolves, blanked or not, every later copy costs {U}{U}, so the hold continues for copies 2-4. OUR board: hold Tidebinder and keep {2}{U} open on their turn when Enduring Curiosity or Wan Shi Tong is on the battlefield, when a Wasp/Drowner lock is doing work, or when you could not replay what would be bounced next turn. When your board is only cheap ETB creatures (Beavers, Siren, Drowner, Wasp), develop normally and let a bounce happen; re-buying those ETBs and re-stunning the Sunderflock with a replayed Drowner is fine, and holding 3 mana every turn against a deck with 9 counters and 12 flash-speed threats gives away the tempo game. A Crab ETB is never a free Tidebinder target: the Crab itself turns Sunderflock on for their next turn. Counter a Crab ETB only if (a) a second Tidebinder or a teamwork We Say Thee Nay! is in hand, or (b) the taps cost lethal-relevant damage or a Curiosity connection you cannot afford to lose; on our own turn also weigh that Tidebinder's {3} is Kaito's ninjutsu mana. Never fire Tidebinder as a vanilla body while Sunderflock is castable and your board would hurt from the bounce. Wasp is the opposite: fire it freely as a tempo body whenever tapping a 5/5 blocker gets Curiosity or a ground attacker through, or tapping a 5/5 attacker in their beginning of combat buys a turn; if Drowner is also in hand, Drowner is the better tempo tap on a 5/5 (tap plus stun plus shuffle setup). Hold Wasp only when a Sunderflock with Flying is on the battlefield (Wasp blanks it so our fliers connect) or, post-board, for a Stallion with 3 mana available. Never flash either card in response to a Sunderflock ETB expecting the bounce to stop: only Tidebinder countering the trigger does that. Play/draw: on the draw their turn 4 arrives while you have 3 lands, so the cost of holding is a blank turn 3 on your side (Tidebinder or nothing) and no Spell Snare backup until their turn 5; on the play you have 4 lands at their turn 4, enough for Tidebinder plus Snare against Get Out, and Curiosity's flash means you never have to tap out on your own turn to deploy it.
How many to run
Against this deck we want all 3 Tidebinders in the 75 (1 main + 2 side, stock) in the deck post-board, and Wasp stays at 2. Post-board: +2 Tishana's Tidebinder, -3 or -4 Requiting Hex (Hex 'destroys target creature with mana value 2 or less': Crab is MV 7, Hearth MV 6, Sunderflock MV 9, Stallion MV 3; only sideboard Hydro-Man qualifies, and only while he is a creature), plus Spell Pierce for their 28 spells (outside these two cards). Keep The Wondrous Wasp at 2 main and do not add: it is a fine flash flyer that taps a blocker for Curiosity and strips Sunderflock's Flying, but it does not answer 5/5s, dies to Prismari Charm's 1 damage, gets Spell Snared and Get Out'd, and is ward-taxed by Stallion. Moving to 2 Tidebinder in the main (cutting a Hex) would be right for this 12.2% matchup alone, but Hex is in the stock main for the other 88% of the field; that is a whole-meta call, not a conclusion of this page.
Review trail: confidence high, 5 disputed, 19 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Strategy skeptic: 'Enduring Curiosity (4/4)'. Rejected on oracle: Enduring Curiosity is {2}{U}{U} 4/3 (deck file cards['Enduring Curiosity'].pt). The point about it needing the 5/5 tapped to attack safely stands; the P/T is corrected.
  • Strategy skeptic: collapse Hydro-Man entirely into a dont_bother bullet and drop him from targets. Partially rejected: Wasp's timing against Hydro-Man is a genuine rules trap (the end-step trigger resolves independently of its source per CR 113.7a, and the '{T}: Add {U}' gained afterwards is kept per Tidebinder ruling 2), so one compact Tidebinder entry and one compact Wasp entry with the correct window are kept; the two separate Hydro-Man traps and the duplicate Tidebinder target were removed and the 'never hold a card for him' verdict is in dont_bother.
  • Strategy skeptic missed-target: Stallion's Ward {1} trigger as an active Tidebinder target. Mostly rejected: with Shoot the Sheriff the blank is moot (the Stallion dies), and with Drowner or Kaito -2 paying {1} is cheaper than {2}{U} and a card; CR 702.21a makes ward a triggered ability so the line is legal and it is recorded in dont_bother as a leftover-Tidebinder play only.
  • Strategy skeptic: 'countering the bounce saves our whole board' should be replaced by a hold rule keyed only to our board. Partially accepted: the board-keyed hold rule is adopted, but Sunderflock's ETB remains the top Tidebinder target regardless because countering it also strips Flying (Sunderflock loses all abilities as a creature source, Tidebinder oracle) and keeps any Wasp/Drowner lock alive; the disagreement is about when to hold mana, not what to hit.
  • Pre-emptive rejections kept, with corrected citations: (a) 'Ward {1} taxes Tidebinder when it counters Stallion's Opus trigger': CR 702.21a, ward triggers when 'this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability'; Tidebinder targets the ability on the stack, not the permanent. (b) 'Wasp in response to Sunderflock's ETB stops the bounce': CR 113.7a, an ability on the stack exists independently of its source. (c) 'Tidebinder can counter Hearth Elemental's cost reduction or the Stoke Genius adventure': cost reduction is a static ability (CR 113.3d), casting an adventure is casting a spell; a countered adventure spell goes to the graveyard like any countered spell (CR 701.5a), only a resolving Adventure is exiled (CR 715.4). (d) 'A Tidebinder-blanked Sunderflock stays blanked after Get Out and recast': CR 400.7, a permanent that leaves and returns is a new object. (e) 'Kaito gets bounced by Sunderflock': Kaito is a creature only 'During your turn'; Sunderflock has no flash and is cast on their turn.
Changes after review
  • Fixed Drowner removal line: Drowner must be flashed on THEIR turn (or Kaito -2's two stun counters used) so a stun counter is still on Sunderflock when Drowner's {1}{U},{T} shuffle is activated; added same-turn Kaito -2 + Drowner line.
  • Removed Impractical Joke from every instant-speed response list (it is a Sorcery); moved it to 'plan for the lock to end'. Added mana-reality note on Traumatic Critique X=2 (4 mana).
  • Corrected Wasp vs Hydro-Man timing: Wasp must resolve before their end step begins; responding to the end-step trigger does not stop the land conversion (CR 113.7a) and the granted mana ability is kept (Tidebinder ruling 2). Folded Hydro-Man's two Tidebinder entries into one, removed both Hydro-Man traps, added a dont_bother bullet.
  • Deleted the 'no Elemental on board yet = free Crab counter' escape hatch everywhere; added a trap explaining the Crab whose ETB is on the stack is itself the first Elemental.
  • Rewrote hold rule around Enduring Curiosity's Flash and correct play/draw land counts (their turn 4 on the draw = our 3 lands); added a board-keyed hold rule (hold when Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong/locks are out; otherwise develop and re-buy ETBs).
  • Fixed Wasp Crab entry and combined lines: Crab and Hearth have no flying or reach, so tapping them only helps ground attackers (Curiosity 4/3, Tidebinder, Drowner); added a trap for 'tap the 5/5 so fliers can attack'.
  • Corrected Wasp-in-response-to-Crab trap: a Crab cast in their precombat main enters untapped, so Wasp tapping it before attackers does deny an attack.
  • Reworded Sunderflock timing: Tidebinder spell has no target; the bounce trigger is chosen as the target of Tidebinder's ETB.
  • Teamwork 2 corrected to any creatures with total power 2 or more (Wasp, Drowner, Siren+Beaver all qualify).
  • Removed Requiting Hex from 'replayed after a bounce' (it is an instant).
  • Sunderflock cost table corrected: {U}{U} with Crab or another Sunderflock, {1}{U}{U} with Hearth, {4}{U}{U} with Stallion, {5}{U}{U} with Hydro-Man; a recast after Get Out is {U}{U} only if a Crab/Sunderflock remains. Added that a blanked Sunderflock still counts (MV 9, Elemental) so copies 2-4 cost {U}{U}, plus a trap for it.
  • Added Stallion notes: Elemental (survives the bounce, enables a 6-mana Sunderflock); token copies are targetable by a second Tidebinder (Opus trigger, no ward tax) and by Wasp through Ward {1}.
  • Added Get Out mana-reality note: Snare-protected Tidebinder needs 4 open mana; before that, weigh letting the bounce resolve.
  • Compressed the Get Out self-bounce/recast line to one sentence and a dont_bother bullet; removed its trap and disputed entries.
  • Added Tidebinder-vs-ninjutsu mana clash to the Crab timing and combined lines; added Drowner-over-Wasp as the preferred tempo tap on a 5/5.
  • Fixed summary arithmetic: 61-card main, 28 instants/sorceries, 53 main-deck cards with nothing to target; rewrote Tidebinder summary to 'four decisive + four conditional' targets, removing the internal contradiction with the Crab entry.
  • Added duration caveat to the Hydro-Man blank (only while Tidebinder/Wasp lives).
  • Counts recommendation reframed: post-board +2 Tidebinder / -Hex for 3 total; 2 Tidebinder main is a whole-meta call, not this page's conclusion.
  • Corrected CR citations: ward is CR 702.21a, static abilities CR 113.3d; added CR 701.5a / 715.4 for the countered-adventure claim.
vs. Dimir Midrange (mirror)10.8% TB: premiumWasp: good▼
Same 75 as ours. Cheap evasive 1-drops (Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren) connect on turns 2-3 and turn on two card engines: Kaito via ninjutsu (turn 3-4 off an unblocked flier) followed by his 0 ("Surveil 2. Then draw a card for each opponent who lost life this turn") every turn, and Enduring Curiosity ("Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card"), which returns as an unremovable enchantment when it dies. Wan Shi Tong at X=3-4 is the late haymaker, and a stack of cheap interaction (4 Requiting Hex, 2 Shoot the Sheriff, 2 Bitter Triumph, 2 Spell Snare, 2 We Say Thee Nay!) protects the lead. Because they run our cards, every answer we have exists against us: their Bitter Triumph kills our Kaito on their turn and our Wan Shi Tong; their Requiting Hex kills our Wan Shi Tong at any size (X is 0 off the stack, so it is mana value 2 on the battlefield); and their permanent answers to our Curiosity are Floodpits Drowner stun-then-shuffle, Wasp blank plus Sheriff/Triumph, Tidebinder on the dies-return trigger, and post-board Annul on the spell, Vren's exile replacement, or Ghost Vacuum on the graveyard. The mirror is decided by who keeps a card engine online while the other's is turned off.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Excellent Tidebinder matchup: every engine they have is an activated or triggered ability on an artifact, creature or planeswalker, and Tidebinder at mana value 3 dodges their 4 Requiting Hex and 2 Spell Snare. It also plays defense: countering their Wasp's Sting or their Tidebinder's ETB blanks the lock piece and keeps our engine online. It is not the best card in the mirror (Kaito, Curiosity and Requiting Hex are); it is a 3-mana flash body with three windows worth holding for: Enduring Curiosity's dies-return trigger (permanent, works even if Tidebinder then dies), a Kaito loyalty activation while they are tapped out, and a Wan Shi Tong ETB at X>=4. Their answers to Tidebinder: Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Drowner stun+shuffle, Day of Black Sun X>=3, Strategic Betrayal (only if we choose it or it is our only creature), their own Tidebinder countering our ETB trigger; Kaito's -2 only taps and stuns it (the lock holds).
#1Enduring Curiosity
'When Enduring Curiosity dies, if it was a creature, return it to the battlefield under its owner's control. It's an enchantment.'
WhyThe cleanest permanent answer to their highest-throughput engine (it draws per connecting creature, often 2-3 a turn, and the returned enchantment can never be blanked or removed by anything in our 75). The trigger's source is already in the graveyard, so nothing is blanked, but the result does NOT depend on Tidebinder surviving (ruling 1): even if they Sheriff our Tidebinder in response, the return is still countered.
WhenOur Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph resolves on the creature version, the return trigger goes on the stack, flash Tidebinder targeting it. Needs 2+3 mana in the same stack, so turn 5 on curve. Also fires when THEY trade Curiosity in combat (into our Curiosity or a Tidebinder block: 3 damage kills the 4/3).
AfterCuriosity stays in the graveyard for good. Nothing on the battlefield loses abilities. Tidebinder is a free 3/2.
RiskGhost Vacuum (SB) can exile it from the graveyard in response, which suits us. Their own Tidebinder can counter our ETB trigger (then the return resolves); hold a second copy or We Say Thee Nay! if they have 3 open.
#2Kaito, Bane of Nightmares
Any loyalty activation: '+1: You get an emblem with "Ninjas you control get +1/+1."' / '0: Surveil 2. Then draw a card for each opponent who lost life this turn.' / '-2: Tap target creature. Put two stun counters on it.'
WhyCountering one loyalty ability makes Kaito lose ALL abilities while Tidebinder lives: no more activations and he loses the static 'During your turn, as long as Kaito has one or more loyalty counters on him, he's a 3/4 Ninja creature and has hexproof', so he stops attacking and stops triggering their Curiosity. Loyalty cost is already paid: he sits at 5 (if the +1 was countered), 4 (the 0) or 2 (the -2), assuming he entered at 4. The highest-value activation to counter is a -2 aimed at our Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong while they control a Floodpits Drowner: two stun counters survive our untap step and set up the shuffle at leisure.
WhenTheir main phase with the loyalty ability on the stack, ideally while they are TAPPED OUT (the classic window is main 2 after a 3-mana turn-3/4 ninjutsu). Into 2+ open mana expect Sheriff/Triumph on Tidebinder with our trigger on the stack: per ruling 1 the activation is still countered but Kaito keeps his abilities, so the lock only exists if Tidebinder survives to resolution. Hexproof is irrelevant (we target the ability). On the draw their turn-3 ninjutsu-then-0 is unanswerable (we have 2 lands); their turn-4 activation is the first counterable one. On the play we have 3 lands on their turn 3.
AfterKaito keeps his loyalty counters and stays a planeswalker: attackable by our fliers, and Bitter Triumph destroys him on our turn (he is only hexproof on theirs). Loses all three loyalty abilities and the creature/hexproof static, so he is never a creature again while Tidebinder lives. An emblem they already made keeps working.
RiskThey unlock him by removing Tidebinder (Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Drowner stun+shuffle, Day of Black Sun X>=3) or by the legend rule: a second Kaito lets them keep the fresh one and bin the blank. Fix: attack the blank Kaito down or Triumph him on our turn; then Tidebinder is just a 3/2. When they have removal mana open, Bitter Triumph on our turn is the better Kaito answer and Tidebinder should be held for Curiosity's return.
#3The Wondrous Wasp (theirs)
Wasp's Sting: 'When The Wondrous Wasp enters, tap up to one target creature. It loses all abilities for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield.'
WhyTheir cheapest permanent-Curiosity line is Wasp blank + Sheriff/Triumph on our Enduring Curiosity (no abilities when it dies means no return trigger). Countering the Sting stops the whole line: our creature is not tapped (the tap is part of the countered trigger) and keeps every ability, and their Wasp, a creature at resolution, loses flying and flash while our Tidebinder lives, a 2/1 that no longer blocks or attacks in the air. Same when it is aimed at our Wan Shi Tong or our Tidebinder.
WhenTheir Wasp ETB on the stack targeting our Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong or Tidebinder. Usually their main phase (so they can kill our blanked Curiosity the same turn) or our beginning of combat. Respond only when the target matters; a Sting on a Beaver is not worth 3 mana.
AfterOur creature: untapped, all abilities intact. Their Wasp: 2/1 with no flying and no flash for as long as Tidebinder lives.
RiskThey still have the kill spell in hand; our Curiosity is now only protected by its return trigger, which their Tidebinder could counter next. Keep We Say Thee Nay! mana if possible.
#4Tishana's Tidebinder (theirs)
'When this creature enters, counter up to one target activated or triggered ability. If an ability of an artifact, creature, or planeswalker is countered this way, that permanent loses all abilities...'
WhyThe real counter-war of the mirror. When they flash Tidebinder at our Kaito loyalty activation, our Curiosity's dies-return trigger, our Wan Shi Tong ETB, our Drowner shuffle or our own Tidebinder's ETB, our (second) Tidebinder counters THEIR trigger: their Tidebinder is a creature at resolution, so it becomes a blank 3/2, and our ability resolves normally. No lock is ever created against us.
WhenTheir Tidebinder's ETB trigger on the stack, before it resolves. Requires {2}{U} open on top of whatever we were doing, so it mostly happens on turn 6+ or when they answer our end-step play.
AfterTheir Tidebinder: blank 3/2 body. Our ability resolves as if nothing happened.
RiskThey can respond again with Sheriff/Triumph on our Tidebinder (ruling 1: their trigger is still countered, theirs just is not blanked). A third layer (their second Tidebinder) is rare with only 1 in the stock main.
#5Floodpits Drowner
'{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries.'
WhyThis is their one-card permanent answer to our creature Curiosity, and it also hits Wan Shi Tong or our lock Tidebinder. Counter it: our creature stays, they lose 2 mana, Drowner stays tapped (costs paid), and Drowner loses all abilities so it can never try again while Tidebinder lives.
WhenOnly on THEIR turn. The shuffle has {T} in its cost, so a Drowner that entered on our turn cannot activate until their next turn begins (summoning sickness, CR 302.6): our first and cheaper window is to kill it on our own turn with Hex/Sheriff or blank it with Wasp. A Drowner flashed on their own turn never gets a shuffle off its own stun (the counter comes off at our untap step before it can activate); the shuffle is real only when Drowner entered on our turn or when Kaito's -2 (two stun counters) set it up. Keep {2}{U} up on their turn if we could not answer it.
AfterKeeps: 2/1 body, still tapped. Loses: vigilance, shuffle ability, ETB (already used). Our creature keeps its stun counter but is still ours.
RiskIf the stunned creature is our only Tidebinder, we need a second copy in hand.
#6Wan Shi Tong, Librarian
'When Wan Shi Tong enters, put X +1/+1 counters on him. Then draw half X cards, rounded down.'
WhyOne trigger carries both the counters and the cards. Counter it and their X+2 mana bought a 1/1 that also loses flying and vigilance. But the body is cheap to remove regardless: on the battlefield his mana value is 2 (X is 0 for an object not on the stack, CR 202.3), so Requiting Hex kills him at any size for {B}. That means countering the ETB is really about the cards: at X=2-3 it saves one card, at X>=4 two. Fire at X>=4, or at X=3 when Curiosity/Kaito are already handled and we lack Hex/Sheriff.
WhenETB trigger on the stack, whenever they cast it. It has flash, so expect it at our end step or precombat main to attack with it.
AfterKeeps: 1/1 body, Bird Spirit types (Sheriff and Hex both kill it). Loses: flying, vigilance, the search trigger (we never search).
RiskThey can bait by casting it into our open mana with a small X. Their own Tidebinder can counter ours in response.
#7Kaito, Bane of Nightmares
Ninjutsu '{1}{U}{B}, Return an unblocked attacker you control to hand: Put this card onto the battlefield from your hand tapped and attacking.'
WhyPure tempo, but it is the most realistic on-curve window on the play (their turn 3 ninjutsu spends their whole turn; our only sorcery-speed 3-drop is Tidebinder itself, so passing with {2}{U} is natural). Costs are paid, so the attacker is already back in hand, no damage comes through, Kaito stays in hand, and they cannot 0 for a card this turn. Nothing is blanked (a card in hand is not a permanent). Decision rule: if they would be TAPPED OUT after ninjutsu, let Kaito land and counter the main-2 activation instead (a safe blank beats a tempo counter for the same {2}{U}); if they would still have Sheriff/Triumph mana, counter the ninjutsu itself.
WhenDeclare blockers step, after blocks, ninjutsu ability on the stack.
AfterKaito in hand with all abilities; they can hard-cast him for 4 next turn (into We Say Thee Nay!). The returned attacker is in hand, re-castable.
RiskYou have spent the Tidebinder and Kaito is still coming.
#8Enduring Curiosity
'Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card.'
WhyCountering one draw trigger blanks the creature version: no more draws AND no dies-return trigger while Tidebinder lives. Then Sheriff/Triumph it and it is gone forever (a permanent with no abilities immediately before it died has no leaves-the-battlefield trigger to look back on, CR 603.10a, exactly how their Day of Black Sun kills it). Ranked below the return-trigger line because it needs Tidebinder to survive until our kill spell resolves.
WhenTheir combat damage step, trigger on the stack. If two creatures connect there are two separate triggers already on the stack and Tidebinder counters only one; the other still draws. Best when exactly one creature connects.
AfterKeeps: 4/3 Enchantment Creature body, can attack and block. Loses: draw trigger, dies-return trigger, flash (irrelevant).
RiskIf they kill Tidebinder before our removal resolves, Curiosity regains its abilities and returns when it dies. Hold the kill spell for the same turn.
#9Vren, the Relentless (SB)
Either 'At the beginning of each end step, create X 1/1 black Rat creature tokens ...' or the ward trigger 'Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter it unless that player pays {2}.'
WhyVren comes in for the mirror because 'If a creature an opponent controls would die, exile it instead' turns their Sheriff/Triumph into permanent answers to our Enduring Curiosity (exiled, so no return). Blanking Vren removes that replacement, the ward and the Rats. Two windows: the end-step trigger has no intervening-if (X may be 0), so it is on the stack every end step; or when our Bitter Triumph or Wasp targets Vren, the ward trigger goes on the stack with Vren as its source, Tidebinder counters it, we pay no {2}, our spell/trigger resolves, and Vren (a creature at resolution) loses all abilities anyway. Shoot the Sheriff never kills Vren (Rat Rogue is an outlaw; types are not abilities).
WhenEnd step (ours or theirs) with the Rat trigger on the stack, ideally BEFORE they can point removal at our Curiosity with Vren live; or the ward trigger when we target Vren with Triumph/Wasp.
AfterKeeps: 3/4 body, existing Rat tokens (their pump is the token's own ability). Loses: ward, exile replacement, token trigger.
RiskSideboard 1-of; only relevant post-board.
#10Floodpits Drowner
'When this creature enters, tap target creature an opponent controls and put a stun counter on it.'
WhySaves our Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong from losing two untaps and pre-empts the shuffle: a blank Drowner has no shuffle ability. Lower than the shuffle because we spend 3 to answer their 2, they may have another Drowner, and if it entered on our turn a 1-mana Hex on the sick Drowner does the job.
WhenETB trigger on the stack, usually flashed in at our beginning of combat or end step. Our Kaito is never a legal target for their Drowner (not a creature on their turn, hexproof on ours), so this only matters when Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong or Tidebinder is on our board.
AfterKeeps: 2/1 body untapped. Loses: vigilance and the shuffle ability for as long as Tidebinder lives.
RiskThey still got a flash 2/1; fine only if our creature is a real engine.
#11Restless Reef
'Whenever this land attacks, target player mills four cards.' (or the activation '{2}{U}{B}: Until end of turn, this land becomes a 4/4 blue and black Shark creature with deathtouch.')
WhyCounter the ATTACK trigger: Reef is a creature at resolution, so it loses all abilities: deathtouch gone (our blocker survives), the mill stops, and it loses its mana ability and animation for as long as Tidebinder lives, a dead land. Counter the ANIMATION instead: 4 of their mana wasted and no attack, but Reef is a land at resolution and nothing is blanked (ruling 3).
WhenAttack trigger: declare attackers step. Animation: whenever they pay {2}{U}{B}, usually precombat or beginning of combat.
AfterAttack-trigger version: 4/4 Shark until end of turn without deathtouch, then a land that cannot tap for mana or animate. Animation version: untouched land, 4 mana lost.
RiskLate-game only; usually the Tidebinder is better held for Curiosity/Kaito.
#12Ghost Vacuum (SB)
'{T}: Exile target card from a graveyard.'
WhyTheir Vacuum's job in the mirror is to exile OUR Curiosity from the graveyard while its return trigger is on the stack. Counter the tap ability: our Curiosity returns, Vacuum stays tapped and loses all abilities (artifact) while Tidebinder lives.
WhenIn response to their Vacuum activation, typically stacked above our Curiosity's dies-return trigger.
AfterVacuum: blank artifact, both abilities gone. Our Curiosity comes back as an enchantment.
RiskSideboard 1-of. The {6} mode sacrifices Vacuum as a cost, so countering it blanks nothing.
#13Faebloom Trick (SB)
Reflexive trigger 'When you do, tap target creature an opponent controls.'
WhyCounterable (reflexive triggers are triggered abilities) but the source is an instant, so nothing is blanked and they keep two 1/1 fliers. Only if the tap would cost us a lethal or Curiosity-drawing attack.
WhenTrigger on the stack after the tokens are created, usually our beginning of combat.
AfterOur creature stays untapped; their two Faeries stay.
Risk3 mana to save one attack; almost always hold.
Traps
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares: Target 'he's a 3/4 Ninja creature and has hexproof' or the emblem to turn Kaito off. The creature/hexproof clause is a static ability and an emblem is not a permanent; neither can be targeted. The only way to strip Kaito is to counter a loyalty activation; countering ninjutsu blanks nothing because Kaito is still a card in hand when the trigger resolves.
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares: Flash Tidebinder at a loyalty activation into their open Sheriff/Triumph mana and expect a lock. If they kill Tidebinder with our trigger on the stack, the activation is still countered but Kaito loses nothing (ruling 1). The blank only exists if Tidebinder survives to resolution; fire when they are tapped out, otherwise Bitter Triumph him on our turn.
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares: Countering ninjutsu keeps their attacker attacking or makes them lose it. Returning the unblocked attacker is a cost; it is already in hand when we respond. The countered ninjutsu just means Kaito stays in hand too. Net: they lose 3 mana and this turn's damage, keep both cards.
Wan Shi Tong, Librarian: A big Wan Shi Tong is out of Requiting Hex range, so Tidebinder must stop the ETB at any X. X is 0 for a permanent on the battlefield (CR 202.3), so Wan Shi Tong is mana value 2 there: Hex kills him at any size, and Day of Black Sun X=2 sweeps him. Only the drawn cards are at stake, so hold Tidebinder for X>=4. Remember their Hex kills OUR Wan Shi Tong the same way.
Enduring Curiosity (returned enchantment): Blank it, Wasp it, or Drowner-shuffle it once it has come back as an enchantment. After it returns 'It's an enchantment. (It's not a creature.)': Tidebinder can only counter its draw triggers one at a time (no blank, not an artifact/creature/planeswalker), Wasp and Drowner cannot target a non-creature, and nothing in our 75 removes it. Every permanent answer works only on the creature version: counter the dies-return trigger, blank it (Wasp or draw-trigger Tidebinder) and kill it, or stun-then-shuffle it with our Drowner.
Enduring Curiosity: One Tidebinder stops all the draws when several creatures connect. Each connecting creature makes a separate trigger; triggers already on the stack exist independently of their source, so blanking Curiosity only removes the one you targeted and future triggers.
Vren, the Relentless: Counter 'If a creature an opponent controls would die, exile it instead.' Replacement effect (static), not targetable. Reach it by countering the end-step Rat trigger or the ward trigger, either of which blanks Vren.
Restless Reef / Soulstone Sanctuary: Countering the animation ability turns the land off for good. At resolution the land has not become a creature yet, so per ruling 3 it loses nothing; you only fizzle that activation. Reef is blanked only via its attack trigger while animated. Sanctuary stays a creature once animated and has no trigger, so it is blanked by Wasp, or by Tidebinder only if they redundantly activate {4} again while it is already a creature (they have no reason to).
Watery Grave / We Say Thee Nay! / Requiting Hex / Bitter Triumph: Counter 'you may pay 2 life', Teamwork, blight, or the discard/3-life cost. Watery Grave's clause is a replacement effect; Teamwork, blight and Triumph's cost are additional costs of casting a spell. None are abilities on the stack. All their counterspells, removal, Duress, Annul and Day of Black Sun are spells, which Tidebinder cannot touch.
Spyglass Siren's Map token / Ghost Vacuum {6} mode: Counter the activation and blank the artifact. Both sacrifice the artifact as a cost; the source is gone when the trigger resolves, so nothing is blanked. The Map's explore is not worth 3 mana anyway.
Tishana's Tidebinder / The Wondrous Wasp (theirs, on ours): If they Wasp or Tidebinder-blank OUR Tidebinder, their Kaito/Curiosity wake up. Our blank is a continuous effect created by a resolved ability with duration 'for as long as this creature remains on the battlefield' (CR 611.2a). Our Tidebinder losing its own abilities does not end it. Only removing our Tidebinder from the battlefield ends it. What their Tidebinder CAN do is counter our ETB trigger while it is on the stack, so no lock is ever created; answer that with a second Tidebinder on their trigger.
Not worth it
Dream Beavers ETB ('each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life. Scry 1'): a life point and a scry; the only edge case is when it is the sole life loss enabling a Kaito 0 draw, still just one card.Spyglass Siren ETB (create a Map token): a land or a +1/+1 counter at best.Soulstone Sanctuary '{4}: This land becomes a 3/3 creature': a land at resolution, nothing blanked; wasting their 4 mana is not worth our 3 and the Tidebinder.Wan Shi Tong at X<=3 when we hold Requiting Hex or Sheriff: the body dies to 1-2 mana either way and the ETB is worth at most one card; at X<=1 never.Wan Shi Tong's 'Whenever an opponent searches their library': our deck never searches.Their Wasp's Sting aimed at a Beaver/Siren: the tap and blank on a 1/1 are not worth 3 mana; only respond when it targets Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong or Tidebinder.Faebloom Trick's tap: they keep the two fliers; nothing blanked.Ghost Vacuum {6} sacrifice mode: counters the return but blanks nothing and is rarely activated in the mirror.Their Annul (SB) on our Curiosity: a spell, untouchable by Tidebinder; the fix is We Say Thee Nay!/Spell Pierce backup, not Tidebinder.
The Wondrous Wasp
Wasp can never touch Kaito (a hexproof 3/4 creature on their turn, not a creature at all on ours), which caps it. What it does well: tap-and-blank Enduring Curiosity (loses both the draw trigger and the dies-return trigger, so a follow-up Shoot the Sheriff/Bitter Triumph kills it for good), tap their lone evasive 1-drop at their beginning of combat (no unblocked attacker means no ninjutsu on turn 3; later the same tap denies the Curiosity draw), blank a Floodpits Drowner on our turn while it is summoning sick, and tap a big Wan Shi Tong or animated land before attackers. Its weakness is fragility: Requiting Hex, Spell Snare, Sheriff, Triumph and Drowner all answer a 2-mana Wasp, and the blank ends the moment it leaves. Also a fine flash flier for our own Curiosity draws.
#1Enduring Curiosity (creature version)
WhyBlank = no 'Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card' and no 'When Enduring Curiosity dies ... return it', so Sheriff/Triumph then removes it permanently for 4 total mana. Tapping it in their beginning of combat also stops the 4-power attack that turn.
WhenBlank value: our main phase when they are tapped below instant-speed removal (Hex on Wasp with its trigger on the stack still taps but the blank never starts, and Hex on Wasp later ends it), then kill it the same turn. Tap value: their beginning of combat, before attackers.
AfterKeeps: 4/3 body, can block. Loses: draw trigger, return trigger, flash. Both come back if Wasp leaves before the kill resolves.
#2Dream Beavers / Spyglass Siren (their only untapped evasive attacker)
WhyNinjutsu needs an unblocked attacker and Curiosity draws are made by whichever creature connects; in the mirror that is the 1/1 fliers, not the 4/3 that gets blocked. Tapping their lone flier at their beginning of combat on turn 3 delays Kaito a full turn; later it denies the Curiosity draw. This is our on-curve turn-2/3 flash play (Wasp also blocks the second flier).
WhenTheir beginning of combat, before attackers are declared. Not after attacks (tapping does not remove an attacker from combat).
After1/1 tapped, no flying while Wasp lives; no attack this turn, so no ninjutsu and no Curiosity trigger from it.
#3Floodpits Drowner
WhyA blank Drowner cannot activate '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it', saving our stunned Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong/Tidebinder. Must land BEFORE they activate; Wasp in response to an activation on the stack does nothing to it.
WhenOUR turn, right after their Drowner's ETB resolves (including our end step): the shuffle has {T} in its cost, so a Drowner that entered on our turn is summoning sick and cannot activate until their next turn begins (CR 302.6), so they cannot respond by shuffling. Their upkeep is too late: they have untapped and the Drowner is live. If we hold Hex/Sheriff, killing it in the same window is cheaper.
AfterKeeps: 2/1 body. Loses: vigilance and the shuffle ability while Wasp lives.
#4Wan Shi Tong, Librarian (after its ETB resolved)
WhyCounters stay, so it remains a 3/3 to 5/5, but it loses flying and vigilance: it can no longer swing over our team and must be blocked or traded on the ground. Tapping it pre-attack buys a turn. Note Requiting Hex simply kills it (mana value 2 on the battlefield), so Wasp here is for when we lack Hex/Sheriff.
WhenTheir beginning of combat, before attackers, or our main phase to remove a blocker for a lethal flying attack.
AfterKeeps: body and +1/+1 counters, Bird Spirit types. Loses: flying, vigilance, the search trigger (irrelevant).
#5Vren, the Relentless (SB)
WhyWard {2} makes it a 4-mana play, but a blank Vren has no ward, no exile-replacement (which was making their removal exile our Curiosity) and no Rat trigger; Bitter Triumph then kills it cleanly. With Tidebinder available, counter the ward trigger instead and pay nothing.
WhenAny time we have {1}{U} plus {2} for ward; if we skip the ward payment the trigger is countered and Wasp is just a 2/1 flier.
AfterKeeps: 3/4, existing Rats. Loses: all three abilities.
#6Restless Reef / Soulstone Sanctuary once animated
WhyOnly while they are creatures. Wasp taps the animated land before attackers (Reef animated precombat cannot attack) and it loses all abilities for as long as Wasp lives: no mana ability, no re-animation, Reef loses deathtouch, Sanctuary loses vigilance. A dead land is real tempo.
WhenReef: their beginning of combat after they animate precombat; Sanctuary: any time after it has been animated (it stays a creature).
AfterKeeps: creature status and P/T from the resolved animation (types and P/T are not abilities). Loses: mana ability, animation ability, deathtouch/vigilance.
#7Any flying blocker (Beavers, Siren, Faerie tokens)
WhyRemove flying from a blocker so our fliers connect for a Curiosity draw or lethal.
WhenOur beginning of combat, before attackers.
After1/1 without flying, tapped.
Traps
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares: Wasp him on either turn. On their turn he is a creature with hexproof; on our turn he is not a creature. Wasp never has a legal target. (Same reason their Drowner and Kaito -2 can never hit our Kaito.)
Enduring Curiosity (returned enchantment): Wasp it after it has come back. It is no longer a creature, so it cannot be targeted; the Wasp window is only the creature version.
Floodpits Drowner / Wan Shi Tong: Flash Wasp in response to the ETB or shuffle activation to stop it. Blanking a creature does not counter an ability already on the stack (CR 113.7a). Only Tidebinder does that. Wasp must land before the activation.
Floodpits Drowner: Wait for their upkeep to Wasp the Drowner that stunned our engine. By their upkeep the Drowner is no longer summoning sick and they have all their mana: they shuffle in response. The safe window is our own turn, when the {T} cost is unpayable (CR 302.6).
Any attacker: Wasp after attackers are declared to stop the hit. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat. Tap in their beginning of combat step.
Their Tidebinder / their Wasp: Wasp their lock piece to free our Kaito or Curiosity. Their effect already exists with duration 'remains on the battlefield'; stripping abilities does not end it (CR 611.2a). Kill, bounce or shuffle the lock piece instead (Sheriff, Triumph, Hex on their Wasp, our Drowner).
Our own Kaito ninjutsu: Return the Wasp that is holding Curiosity blank to ninjutsu Kaito. Wasp leaving the battlefield ends its effect immediately; Curiosity's draws and return trigger come back. Ninjutsu off a Beaver or Siren instead.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Permanent Curiosity kill A: Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph on the creature Curiosity, then Tidebinder counters the 'When Enduring Curiosity dies ... return it' trigger. Tidebinder-independent (ruling 1); also works when they trade Curiosity into our 4/3 or a Tidebinder block.
  • Permanent Curiosity kill B: Wasp taps and blanks Curiosity, same turn Sheriff/Triumph kills it; no abilities immediately before death means no return trigger (CR 603.10a). Do it when they are tapped out of Hex/Snare/Sheriff mana, because removal on Wasp before the kill resolves restores the return.
  • Permanent Curiosity kill C: our Floodpits Drowner stuns the creature Curiosity on THEIR turn is useless (counter falls off at their untap); do it on their end step or our turn only if Kaito's -2 has already put two stun counters on it, otherwise stun on our turn is not possible (Drowner's ETB must target an opponent's creature and the Drowner must survive to their turn). Practical version: Kaito -2 on their Curiosity (two stun counters), then Drowner's {1}{U},{T} shuffle any time before both counters are gone.
  • Kaito shutdown: Tidebinder counters a loyalty activation while they are tapped out (main 2 after ninjutsu); Kaito is a blank planeswalker at 5/4/2 loyalty (after +1/0/-2) who is never a creature. Attack him with Beavers/Siren/Wasp or Bitter Triumph him on our turn so the legend-rule reset (second Kaito) is not an out, then Tidebinder is a free 3/2.
  • Drowner defense, cheap version: when their Drowner enters on our turn and stuns Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong, it is summoning sick until their turn begins, so Requiting Hex (1 mana) or Sheriff kills it, or Wasp blanks it, before the shuffle is ever available. Expensive version if we were tapped out: Tidebinder counters the shuffle on their turn (Drowner blank, our creature stays).
  • Counter-war for the lock: their Tidebinder targets our Tidebinder/Wasp ETB (or our Kaito activation / Curiosity return); our second Tidebinder counters THEIR trigger, theirs becomes a blank 3/2 and ours resolves. Before it lands: We Say Thee Nay! taxes their Tidebinder spell by {2} ({4} with Teamwork), a real counter only when they cannot pay; Spell Snare hard-counters their Wasp or Drowner (mana value 2). Spell Pierce never hits either.
  • Their Wasp on our Curiosity: Tidebinder counters Wasp's Sting; Curiosity stays untapped with all abilities and their Wasp is a blank 2/1. Or, if we have no Tidebinder, Requiting Hex their Wasp before their kill spell resolves (the blank ends when Wasp leaves, so the return trigger is back).
  • Our Curiosity's return vs their Ghost Vacuum: when they tap Vacuum at our return trigger, Tidebinder counters the Vacuum activation; Curiosity returns and Vacuum is a blank artifact while Tidebinder lives.
  • We Say Thee Nay! Teamwork: tap Tidebinder (power 3) as the additional cost to make Nay 'counter unless pays {4}' against their hard-cast Kaito or Wan Shi Tong. Tidebinder tapped still holds its lock.
  • Wasp as Curiosity fuel: flash Wasp at their end step, attack over their ground with Wasp + Beavers + Siren, each connection draws off our Curiosity; ninjutsu Kaito by returning a Beaver/Siren, never the Wasp that holds a blank.
  • Wan Shi Tong denied: Tidebinder counters its ETB at X>=4 (a 1/1 with no flying, and no cards), then Requiting Hex (1 mana, mana value 2 on the battlefield) or Shoot the Sheriff (Bird Spirit) removes the body. At smaller X skip the Tidebinder and just Hex the resolved bird; Kaito -2 only taps and stuns it.
  • Vren (post-board): Bitter Triumph targets Vren, the ward trigger goes on the stack, Tidebinder counters it: no {2} tax, Triumph resolves, and Vren is blank anyway. Or counter the end-step Rat trigger the turn Vren lands so their Sheriff/Triumph stop exiling our Curiosity.
Hold or fire
Hold Tidebinder for one of three things, in this order: (1) Enduring Curiosity's dies-return trigger when we have Sheriff/Triumph and 5 total mana in the same turn (turn 5 on curve), the only Tidebinder-independent permanent answer to their best engine; (2) a Kaito loyalty activation while they are TAPPED OUT (main 2 after a turn-3/4 ninjutsu is the classic window); into open Sheriff/Triumph mana the activation is still countered but the blank dies with Tidebinder (ruling 1), so prefer Bitter Triumph on Kaito during our turn instead; (3) a Wan Shi Tong ETB at X>=4 (at X=2-3 the body dies to a 1-mana Requiting Hex anyway and the ETB is worth one card). Fire defensively without hesitation when their Wasp's Sting or their Tidebinder's ETB is aimed at our Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong, Kaito activation or Tidebinder: countering it keeps our engine online and blanks their lock piece. Fire on a Floodpits Drowner shuffle aimed at Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong only if we failed to Hex/Sheriff/Wasp the Drowner on our own turn while it was summoning sick. Against ninjutsu: if they will be tapped out afterwards, let Kaito land and counter the main-2 activation for a safe blank; if they would still hold 2+ mana, counter the ninjutsu itself (they lose the whole turn and Kaito stays in hand). Deploy Tidebinder as a bare 3/2 at their end step only when Kaito is dead or blanked, Curiosity is handled, Wan Shi Tong is out of range, and we hold a second copy or Bitter Triumph. On the play: pass with mana up on their turns 3-4 by default; our deck is almost all flash and instants (Drowner, Wasp, Tidebinder, Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong, all removal), so if no window appears we flash Curiosity or Drowner at their end step; hard-cast Kaito only when they are tapped out or we have Nay backup. On the draw: their turn-3 ninjutsu-then-0 is unanswerable (we have 2 lands); the first counterable activation is their turn 4, so hold {2}{U} on their turn 4 if our turn-3 play can be a flash creature at their end step. Wasp: it never hits Kaito, so its jobs are (1) blank Curiosity on our turn when they are tapped out, kill it the same turn; (2) tap their lone evasive 1-drop at their beginning of combat (turn 3 to deny ninjutsu, later to deny Curiosity draws); (3) blank a freshly flashed Drowner on our turn while it is summoning sick; (4) tap Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong/animated Reef at their beginning of combat before attackers; (5) evasive flash body for our Curiosity draws at their end step. Never cast Wasp for the blank into open Requiting Hex or Spell Snare mana if the blank is the point; as a body it is fine.
How many to run
Post-board 3 Tidebinder and 2 Wasp. Note the featured MTGGoldfish sideboard in the deck file (Duress 3, Day of Black Sun 2, Annul 1, Ghost Vacuum 1, Spell Pierce 2, Strategic Betrayal 1, Vren 1, Flashfreeze 2, Faebloom Trick 2) contains no Tidebinder; the 2 sideboard copies come from the Karsten Sept 3 aggregate cited in the file's note, so a mirror opponent on the featured list may have only 1 total. Tidebinder is the third-best card in the mirror behind Kaito and Curiosity: it answers all four of their engines (Kaito loyalty in the tapped-out window, Curiosity's return, Wan Shi Tong's ETB, Drowner's shuffle), plays defense against their Wasp/Tidebinder, and dodges 4 Hex and 2 Spell Snare. KEEP all 4 Requiting Hex (reversing the draft): for one mana it kills Floodpits Drowner during its summoning-sick window (before the shuffle is ever available), their Wasp (their cheapest permanent-Curiosity line), a resolved Wan Shi Tong at any size (mana value 2 on the battlefield, X is 0 off the stack), the evasive 1-drops that enable ninjutsu and Curiosity draws, and Faerie/Rat tokens; only Tidebinder (3), Curiosity (4), Vren (4) and Kaito (never a targetable creature for us) are out of range. Make room for the 2 Tidebinder by cutting 2 Spell Snare: it hits Drowner/Wasp/Sheriff/Triumph/Nay/Strategic Betrayal but never Kaito or Curiosity, needs mana held on their turn (competing with Tidebinder, Drowner and Wasp for the same mana), and Hex already covers the Drowner/Wasp bodies on our turn. If you want to keep one Snare for their Drowner, cut 1 Snare + 1 Spyglass Siren instead. Keep both Bitter Triumph (kills Kaito on our turn, Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong, Vren through ward) and both Shoot the Sheriff (kills Curiosity, Wan Shi Tong, Drowner, Wasp, their Tidebinder; not Vren). Wasp stays at 2: the Curiosity blank-and-kill, the beginning-of-combat tap on their lone flier, and the flash flier for our own Curiosity earn it despite Kaito immunity and Hex/Snare exposure; a third is legendary and has no Kaito to hit.
Review trail: confidence high, 6 disputed, 19 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Brief says 'Ward taxes ... Tidebinder's trigger.' Rejected for Vren, and reviewer 3 independently confirms: ward is 'Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter it unless that player pays [cost]' (CR 702.21a). Tidebinder's ETB targets an activated or triggered ability on the stack, never the permanent, so Vren never becomes a target and ward does not trigger. Ward does tax Wasp, which targets the creature. Better still, when something of ours does target Vren, the ward trigger itself is a targetable triggered ability whose source is a creature.
  • Possible objection: 'once their Wasp/Tidebinder blanks our Tidebinder, our lock ends.' Rejected: the lock is a continuous effect from a resolved ability with the stated duration 'for as long as this creature remains on the battlefield' (CR 611.2a), and the affected object set is fixed when it begins (611.2c); the source losing its abilities does not end it, only leaving the battlefield does.
  • Possible objection: 'a blanked Enduring Curiosity still returns when it dies.' Rejected: leaves-the-battlefield triggers look back at the object as it last existed on the battlefield (CR 603.10a); with no abilities at that moment there is no trigger. This is the exact mechanism of their own Day of Black Sun ('loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures').
  • Possible objection: 'Vren's end-step trigger only happens when X>0, so there is no free window.' Rejected: the ability has no intervening-if clause; it triggers at every end step and X is computed on resolution, so it is on the stack (and targetable) every turn.
  • Possible objection: 'Wasp can hit Kaito on our turn since he is not hexproof then.' Rejected: on our turn Kaito is not a creature ('During your turn, as long as Kaito has one or more loyalty counters on him, he's a 3/4 Ninja creature and has hexproof' applies only on his controller's turn), and Wasp targets a creature.
  • No reviewer finding was rejected outright. Reviewer 2's 'priority order' and 'best card' critiques were accepted as strategy judgments, not rules matters; the verdict stays 'premium' because the matchup quality (every engine is a counterable ability on a permanent, MV 3 dodges Hex/Snare) is what the verdict measures, while the summary no longer calls Tidebinder the best card in the mirror.
Changes after review
  • Fixed the major rules error: Wan Shi Tong's mana value on the battlefield is 2 (X is 0 for an object not on the stack, CR 202.3), so Requiting Hex kills it at any size and Day of Black Sun X=2 sweeps it. Rewrote the Wan Shi Tong target, the 'Wan Shi Tong denied' combined line (Hex handles the body; Kaito -2 only taps and stuns), added a Wan Shi Tong trap, raised the Tidebinder threshold to X>=4, and noted their Hex kills our Wan Shi Tong.
  • Reversed the counts recommendation: keep 4 Requiting Hex (kills sick Drowner, their Wasp, Wan Shi Tong, 1-drops, tokens); cut 2 Spell Snare (or 1 Snare + 1 Siren) for the 2 sideboard Tidebinder. Noted the featured sideboard has zero Tidebinder and the extra copies come from the Karsten aggregate.
  • Reordered Tidebinder priorities: Curiosity dies-return trigger is now #1 (permanent, Tidebinder-independent), Kaito loyalty #2 with an explicit tapped-out condition, then the two new defensive targets, Drowner shuffle, Wan Shi Tong at X>=4, ninjutsu moved up to #7 as a conditional line.
  • Added missed target: their The Wondrous Wasp's Sting ETB (counter it: our engine stays untapped with abilities, their Wasp loses flying/flash).
  • Added missed target: their Tishana's Tidebinder ETB (counter it with our second copy: theirs becomes a blank 3/2, our ability resolves).
  • Added the Kaito lock fragility (ruling 1: removal on Tidebinder in response leaves the activation countered but Kaito unblanked) to the Kaito target's timing and risk, plus a new trap entry, and made Bitter Triumph on our turn the default Kaito answer into open removal mana.
  • Corrected Kaito loyalty math after the counter: 5 (if +1), 4 (if 0), 2 (if -2).
  • Added the Kaito -2 + Drowner interaction (two stun counters survive our untap) as the highest-value activation to counter.
  • Fixed the Floodpits Drowner timing: the shuffle has {T} in its cost, so a Drowner that entered on our turn is summoning sick until their turn (CR 302.6); our first window is our own turn with Hex/Sheriff/Wasp. Noted a Drowner flashed on their turn never shuffles off its own stun. Replaced Wasp's wrong 'their upkeep' window with 'our turn while sick' and added a Wasp trap for it.
  • Fixed the returned-enchantment Curiosity trap: Drowner can only stun/shuffle the creature version; nothing in our 75 removes the enchantment version; Wasp and Drowner cannot target it.
  • Rewrote how_they_win: Drowner is no longer described as their only permanent answer; listed Bitter Triumph on Kaito/Wan Shi Tong, Hex/Sheriff on Wan Shi Tong, and all their permanent Curiosity answers (Drowner, Wasp+kill, Tidebinder on the return, Annul, Vren exile, Vacuum).
  • Fixed We Say Thee Nay! wording everywhere: it taxes {2} ({4} with Teamwork), a real counter only when they cannot pay; Spell Snare is the hard counter on their Wasp and Drowner.
  • Vren: added the ward-trigger window (counter the ward trigger when our Triumph/Wasp targets Vren; no tax and Vren is blanked) and reframed why Vren matters (its exile replacement makes their removal permanent against our Curiosity), moving it above the Drowner ETB.
