  • Added Wasp target #2: tap their lone evasive 1-drop at their beginning of combat to deny turn-3 ninjutsu and later Curiosity draws.
  • Expanded the list of their answers to Tidebinder (Strategic Betrayal, Kaito -2 stun that does not end the lock) in the summary.
  • Softened 'best card in the mirror' to 'excellent matchup'; summary now names the three hold windows explicitly.
  • Soulstone Sanctuary trap now notes the redundant-second-activation edge case where Tidebinder could blank it.
  • Rewrote hold_or_fire and on-play/on-draw sequencing: our deck is flash-heavy so holding mana on their turns 3-4 is free on the play; ninjutsu, not hard cast, is how Kaito usually arrives; added defensive fire rules for their Wasp/Tidebinder and the Drowner summoning-sick window.
  • Added dont_bother entries for their Wasp's Sting on a 1-drop, small-X Wan Shi Tong when we hold Hex/Sheriff, and their Annul (a spell).
vs. 4c Control5.9% TB: situationalWasp: weak▼
A near-creatureless control deck: turn 3 Tablet of Discovery (ramp plus a free mill-and-play), dig with Stock Up / Consult the Star Charts, kill every creature 1-for-1 (3 Firebending Lesson, 2 Sear, 3 Shoot the Sheriff, 4 Inevitable Defeat) or sweep with Deadly Cover-Up, then take over from 5-7 mana with Jeskai Revelation (bounce + 4 damage + two Monks + draw 2 + gain 4) recycled through Flashback and Thor's ETB. Thor, God of Thunder is the only real creature: a 5/5 flier whose "Whenever you cast a noncreature spell, Thor deals damage equal to that spell's mana value to any target" turns Revelation into 11 damage, and on their turn that trigger can point at Kaito (no hexproof off our turn). Great Hall of the Biblioplex is a permanent-animation manland ({5}: 2/4 Wizard, no duration in the oracle text) and Mistrise Village makes the big spell uncounterable. They have 17 instant-speed ways to end a Tidebinder/Wasp blank (12 removal spells, Nowhere to Run, 4 Revelation bounces); only Deadly Cover-Up is sorcery speed. Value the counter itself, not the lock.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Almost everything in this deck is a spell, which Tidebinder cannot touch. The windows that are worth a card: Thor's two triggers (either one blanks Thor: no Flying, no more pings, and the ETB version also denies a Revelation / Deadly Cover-Up replay), Tablet of Discovery's ETB (blanking the artifact switches off both mana abilities until they spend a removal spell on the 3/2), and, opportunistically, Petrified Hamlet's ETB postboard (no name chosen = both name-dependent statics inert forever; Hamlet still taps for {C}) and a Mistrise Village activation when you hold a counter. Everything else is a land or an enchantment, so the ability is countered but nothing is blanked. The variable that decides whether you get the blank is THEIR OPEN MANA when you cast Tidebinder, not the size of the trigger: if they can Sear / Lesson / Shoot the Tidebinder while its trigger is still on the stack, the target ability is still countered (ruling 1) but the permanent never loses its abilities, and Thor (if unblanked) pings off that removal spell as normal. Fire into open mana only when the counter alone is worth a card.
#1Thor, God of Thunder
When Thor enters, exile target Equipment, instant, or sorcery card from your graveyard. Until the end of your next turn, you may play that card.
WhyThe earliest and cleanest Thor window: on curve they just spent 5 on Thor and are tapped out, so the blank is guaranteed, Thor never deals cast-trigger damage, and they lose the replay of whatever they targeted. Highest-value graveyard target is Deadly Cover-Up: a replayed Cover-Up with collect evidence 6 is how this deck permanently strips all four Enduring Curiosity or all four Kaito from our hand, graveyard and library. Next: Jeskai Revelation (a free 7-mana spell), Together as One, Inevitable Defeat. Even if the target is junk (Stock Up, Sear, Consult), take the ETB whenever they are tapped out: the blank (no Flying, no pings on every later spell) is what you are buying, and waiting for a bigger trigger means eating 5 flying damage a turn and giving them a chance to hold Revelation for the end step you tap out on Kaito or Curiosity.
WhenThor resolves and enters; the ETB goes on the stack targeting a graveyard card. Respond with Tidebinder before it resolves. If their graveyard has no instant, sorcery or Equipment, the trigger is removed from the stack for lack of a legal target (CR 603.3d) and there is no window; the cast trigger is then your only shot. Turn 5+ (turn 4 with Tablet), their main phase. Check their open mana: 0 open = guaranteed blank; 1 red open = expect Firebending Lesson in response (counter still happens, no blank, Thor pings 1).
AfterThe targeted card stays in the graveyard with no permission to play it. Thor loses Flying, the ETB and the cast trigger while Tidebinder remains; keeps a 5/5 body, legendary, God Warrior Hero types (not an outlaw, so our Shoot the Sheriff still kills it). Regains everything the moment Tidebinder leaves.
RiskThey will point removal at Tidebinder to unlock Thor. Follow up on the body with Floodpits Drowner (stun, then shuffle it away), Kaito -2 or Shoot the Sheriff rather than trusting the blank.
#2Thor, God of Thunder
Whenever you cast a noncreature spell, Thor deals damage equal to that spell's mana value to any target.
WhyThis is their kill mechanism: Revelation with Thor out is 7 to the face plus the spell's own 4, or 7 at Kaito (loyalty 4, no hexproof on their turn), which kills our card engine outright; Together as One is 6, Deadly Cover-Up 5, Inevitable Defeat 4. Countering the trigger stops that damage and blanks Thor. Rule: take the FIRST Thor trigger that comes while they cannot pay for removal in response (a 7-mana Revelation usually means tapped out). If they have Lesson/Sear mana open, fire only when the counter alone is worth a card: a trigger of 4+ aimed at Kaito or your face.
WhenThey cast a noncreature spell with Thor on the battlefield; the spell goes on the stack, Thor's trigger goes on top of it with its target chosen. You get priority with both on the stack: flash Tidebinder targeting Thor's trigger. Tidebinder's ETB resolves first, counters the trigger, Thor is blanked; the spell then resolves normally (Tidebinder does NOT stop the spell). Usually their main phase, but Revelation is an instant so keep 3 open on your own end step too. Turn 5+ at the earliest.
AfterThor loses Flying, the ETB and the cast trigger while Tidebinder remains; keeps the 5/5 body and its types. Damage already dealt by earlier resolved triggers stays dealt. If they kill Tidebinder AFTER its trigger has resolved, Thor gets no ping off that removal spell (Thor has no trigger at that moment, and a missed trigger does not come back); when Tidebinder dies Thor regains everything at once. If they kill Tidebinder IN RESPONSE to its trigger, Thor is still live: the removal spell pings as normal (Sear 2, Shoot 2, Lesson 1, any target including Kaito), the original trigger is still countered (ruling 1), but Thor never loses its abilities.
RiskYou can only counter one trigger; Thor still hits for 5 on the ground until you deal with the body.
#3Tablet of Discovery
When this artifact enters, mill a card. You may play that card this turn.
WhyTablet is an artifact, so countering its ETB makes it lose ALL abilities, including "{T}: Add {R}." and "{T}: Add {R}{R}. Spend this mana only to cast instant and sorcery spells." They lose the Tablet mana until they spend a removal spell on a 3/2, usually one turn, and you get a free 3-power attacker on an empty board. This is a tempo play, not a lock: it is right because there is nothing better to do with flash mana on their turn 3, and in game 1 it is the play your single Tidebinder actually makes most games (Thor windows are 1-of rarities). The mill/play denial itself is minor.
WhenTablet resolves and enters; the mill trigger goes on the stack; respond with Tidebinder. On the play you have 3 untapped lands on their turn 3 if you passed with mana up, which is exactly their Tablet turn. On the draw their turn 3 comes before your third land, so the window is their turn 4 Tablet or a second copy (they run 4). NO Tablet window is a guaranteed blank, even when their lands are all tapped: Tablet enters untapped and its mana abilities still work while Tidebinder's trigger is on the stack, so "{T}: Add {R}{R}" pays for Sear or Firebending Lesson in response (mana abilities can be activated whenever a player has priority, CR 605.3a). If they do that, the ETB is still countered, Tidebinder dies, Tablet keeps its abilities; you traded a card for a card and their mill. Their turn-4 Tablet with a land open is the same story with one more mana.
AfterTablet loses the ETB and both mana abilities while Tidebinder remains; it is a blank artifact that still counts as a permanent. Any card they already milled/played from an earlier Tablet is unaffected. If Tidebinder dies, the mana abilities come back immediately (the ETB does not re-trigger).
#4Petrified Hamlet (sideboard)
When this land enters, choose a land card name.
WhyHamlet comes in against us to name a manland (Restless Reef or Soulstone Sanctuary): "Activated abilities of sources with the chosen name can't be activated unless they're mana abilities" and "Lands with the chosen name have '{T}: Add {C}.'" If the ETB is countered, no name is ever chosen, so both name-dependent statics refer to nothing and do nothing for the rest of the game; the ETB does not trigger again. Opportunistic only: never hold 3 mana waiting for a 1-of land drop. If you happen to have Tidebinder plus 3 open when they play it (typically late, when your manlands are the plan against sweepers), counter it; otherwise let it resolve.
WhenThey play Hamlet as their land drop; the choose-a-name trigger goes on the stack (the name is chosen on resolution, not when it triggers). Respond with Tidebinder before it resolves. Any postboard turn, their main phase. Once it has resolved and named a land, Tidebinder cannot undo it: the lock is static.
AfterHamlet is a land, so it loses nothing: it still taps for {C} via its own printed mana ability. Its two name-dependent statics are permanently inert because there is no chosen name.
#5Mistrise Village
{U}, {T}: The next spell you cast this turn can't be countered.
WhyRare, late, opportunistic. They activate this to force through Jeskai Revelation, Deadly Cover-Up or Stock Up past our counters (Revelation needs Village plus 7 other lands, so turn 8+). Counter the activation and the spell they cast next is counterable by We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Pierce or sideboard Flashfreeze (Revelation and Flashback are red spells; Deadly Cover-Up and Stock Up are not). Costs stay paid: the Village stays tapped and the {U} is gone. Realistic payoff: a competent pilot simply holds the spell that turn rather than casting into your counter, so what you usually buy is one wasted {U} plus a land tap and a 3/2 on the table. Only do it when you wanted the body anyway and hold a counter.
WhenThey activate Village (must be before casting the spell, since it protects "the next spell you cast"); the ability is on the stack; respond with Tidebinder. Their main phase for sorceries; your end step for an instant-speed Revelation.
AfterVillage is a land: nothing is blanked, it still taps for {U} and can activate again next turn. Their next spell this turn has no protection.
#6Great Hall of the Biblioplex
{5}: If this land isn't a creature, it becomes a 2/4 Wizard creature with "Whenever you cast an instant or sorcery spell, this creature gets +1/+0 until end of turn." It's still a land.
WhyPure tempo: they sink 5 mana and get nothing. By ruling 3 the land is not a creature when Tidebinder's trigger resolves, so it does not lose abilities and they can animate again next turn. Take this only when you want the 3/2 body on the table anyway and nothing better is coming.
WhenThey activate for {5}; the ability is on the stack; respond with Tidebinder. Almost always their own turn, since it needs 5 spare mana.
AfterNothing is lost. Great Hall keeps "{T}: Add {C}.", the pay-1-life colored mana ability and the {5} animation. The 5 mana is simply wasted.
#7Nowhere to Run
When this enchantment enters, target creature an opponent controls gets -3/-3 until end of turn.
WhySaves the creature (Enduring Curiosity 4/3, a Drowner, Dream Beavers) for a 1-for-1 that leaves you a 3/2. Only a 1-of, so it is a fallback, not a plan.
WhenNowhere to Run has flash, so this can come on your turn: it resolves, enters, the -3/-3 trigger targets your creature; respond with Tidebinder before it resolves.
AfterNowhere to Run is an enchantment: nothing is blanked. Its static ("Creatures your opponents control can be the targets of spells and abilities as though they didn't have hexproof. Ward abilities of those creatures don't trigger.") stays on, so Kaito is targetable on our turn from then on.
Traps
Deadly Cover-Up: Counter the "collect evidence 6" or the "Destroy all creatures" with Tidebinder. Collect evidence is an additional cost paid while casting a spell, and the destroy is the spell's own effect. Neither is an activated or triggered ability; Tidebinder has no target. Only a spell counter stops it, and Mistrise Village turns that off too.
Inevitable Defeat: Tidebinder can blank or stop the exile. It is a spell, not an ability, and its "This spell can't be countered" is a static ability of a spell. Nothing for Tidebinder to target. Worse: Inevitable Defeat on Tidebinder ends every blank instantly.
Consult the Star Charts / Firebending Lesson: Counter the kicker to stop the second card or the 5 damage. Kicker is an additional cost, part of casting the spell; not an ability on the stack. (Kicked Firebending Lesson is still mana value 1 for Thor's trigger.)
Flashback (and Thor's "you may play that card"): Counter the flashback cast from the graveyard. Granting flashback is a spell effect and casting a card from the graveyard is casting, not an ability. Same for a card Thor's resolved ETB let them play: the permission came from an ability that already resolved; the play itself cannot be countered by Tidebinder.
Blood Crypt / Sacred Foundry / Steam Vents / Watery Grave: Counter "As this land enters, you may pay 2 life" to make it enter tapped. That is a replacement effect, not a triggered ability; it never uses the stack. Same for "enters tapped unless" on Shattered Sanctum, Starting Town and Mistrise Village.
Great Hall of the Biblioplex: Countering the {5} animation blanks the land so it can never animate again. Ruling 3: Tidebinder checks the source's type as its trigger resolves; the land is not a creature yet, so it loses nothing and can be activated again. Also its "{T}: Add {C}" and "{T}, Pay 1 life: Add one mana of any color" are mana abilities and cannot be targeted at all.
Tablet of Discovery: Target "{T}: Add {R}{R}" directly to shut off their instant-and-sorcery mana, or assume a tapped-out opponent cannot respond to Tidebinder on their Tablet turn. Both tap abilities are mana abilities and cannot be targeted; the only way to switch them off is to counter the ETB so the artifact loses all abilities. And until that trigger resolves Tablet still works: they can tap it for {R}{R} in response and Sear or Lesson the Tidebinder.
Thor, God of Thunder: Countering the cast trigger also stops the spell that caused it, or removes Flying by itself, or means the removal spell they fire at Tidebinder never pings. The spell resolves normally; only the damage trigger is gone. Flying is static and cannot be countered; it is lost only as a side effect of Thor being blanked, and it returns when Tidebinder leaves. And removal cast IN RESPONSE to Tidebinder's trigger (Thor not yet blanked) triggers Thor as normal; only removal cast after the trigger resolves gets no ping.
Nowhere to Run: Countering its ETB switches off the anti-hexproof static. It is an enchantment, so it does not lose abilities (only artifacts, creatures and planeswalkers do). The static stays.
Day of Black Sun / Day of Judgment / Jeskai Revelation: Something on these cards is a trigger Tidebinder can hit. All spell effects (including Revelation's token creation and bounce). Revelation reads "Return target spell or permanent": bouncing Tidebinder from the battlefield ends the blank; bouncing it with the ETB still on the stack means the ability is still countered but nothing is blanked (ruling 1); bouncing Tidebinder WHILE IT IS STILL A SPELL on the stack returns it to hand before it ever enters, so nothing triggers and nothing is countered. With 7 open (or 5 plus Tablet's {R}{R}) that is a real response to count.
Not worth it
Undercity Sewers "When this land enters, surveil 1": a land, nothing blanked, and surveil 1 is worth nothing.Monk tokens' prowess (from Jeskai Revelation): countering one +1/+1 trigger and blanking a 1/1 is a waste of a 3-mana card.Animated Great Hall's granted "Whenever you cast an instant or sorcery spell, this creature gets +1/+0 until end of turn": it is counterable and the land-creature would lose all abilities including its mana abilities (it stays a 2/4 creature because the animation already resolved), but you are spending 3 mana to stop 1 damage; Wasp does the same blank for 2 and taps it.Outrageous Robbery (sideboard 1-of): lets them cast OUR cards from exile, so a Robbery-cast Floodpits Drowner ETB, Enduring Curiosity draw trigger or Kaito loyalty ability on their side becomes a legal Tidebinder target and their stolen creature a legal Wasp target. Real but too rare to plan around: treat it like any other opposing creature if it ever happens.Strategic Betrayal, Ancient Vendetta, Duress, Negate, Flashfreeze, Stock Up, Together as One, Consult, Flashback: pure spells, no ability ever hits the stack. Their Flashfreeze counters only red or green spells and is dead against our blue/black deck.
The Wondrous Wasp
Nothing in this 75 has hexproof or ward, but there is almost nothing for Wasp's Sting to hit: the only creatures they ever present are Thor (1 main, 2 postboard), an animated Great Hall, and 1/1 Monk tokens. Play Wasp as what it is here, a 2-mana flash flier cast at the end of their turn that dodges Duress (creature card), Negate (creature spell), and sorcery-speed sweepers for a turn cycle, and connects for Enduring Curiosity draws against a deck with no blockers. The Sting is a bonus for a Thor that is already on the table; the blank lasts only until their next {R} (Firebending Lesson), so the real Thor answers are Drowner's stun-and-shuffle and Shoot the Sheriff.
#1Thor, God of Thunder
WhyBlanking Thor removes Flying and "Whenever you cast a noncreature spell, Thor deals damage equal to that spell's mana value to any target" for as long as Wasp lives: a 5/5 vanilla on the ground that our creatures can block and Drowner/Kaito -2 can stun. Tapping it in their beginning of combat also stops a 5-power flying attack that turn. Cheapest possible answer to their only threat, and a 2/1 body is a fine thing to trade for a removal spell they wanted to point at Kaito.
WhenBest window: in response to Thor's ETB trigger (the ETB still resolves, but Thor is blanked before any spell is cast), or at their beginning of combat before attackers are declared. Wasp does NOT stop a trigger already on the stack (the ETB or a cast trigger exists independently of Thor once it triggers), so if a Revelation and its Thor trigger are already on the stack you need Tidebinder, not Wasp. Wasp's job is to make sure the NEXT spell triggers nothing.
AfterThor loses Flying, ETB and cast trigger while Wasp remains; keeps the 5/5 body and its types (still a legal Shoot the Sheriff target). Any damage from triggers that already resolved stays. Sear, Firebending Lesson, Nowhere to Run, or a Revelation bounce on Wasp restores everything instantly; expect the blank to last about one turn.
#2Great Hall of the Biblioplex (after animation)
WhyOnce the {5} has resolved it is a 2/4 Wizard creature land permanently (no duration in the oracle text). Wasp can target it because it is a creature; it loses ALL abilities including "{T}: Add {C}." and "{T}, Pay 1 life: Add one mana of any color", so 2 mana turns their 5-mana investment into a mana-less 2/4. Tap it in their beginning of combat to stop an attack, or on your turn to remove a blocker for Kaito's ninjutsu attacker.
WhenOnly after the animation ability has resolved (before that it is not a creature and Wasp has no legal target). Their beginning of combat to stop the attack; your own precombat main to clear the blocker; any time to shut off the mana.
AfterStays a 2/4 Wizard creature land (the animation effect already applied) but with no mana abilities, no +1/+0 trigger and no {5} ability while Wasp remains. Can still block once it untaps. Note it is a creature with mana value 0, so our sideboard Day of Black Sun at X=0 destroys it, and so does our Requiting Hex.
#3Monk token (Jeskai Revelation)
WhyOnly as a free tap to remove a blocker or stop a 1-power attack; blanking prowess is worth nothing.
WhenYour precombat main to clear the blocker, or their beginning of combat.
AfterA 1/1 with no prowess while Wasp remains. Not worth holding Wasp for.
Traps
Thor, God of Thunder: Flash Wasp in response to Thor's ETB or cast trigger to stop that trigger. An ability on the stack exists independently of its source (CR 113.7a); blanking Thor does not remove or counter a trigger that has already triggered. Wasp only prevents future triggers. Use Tidebinder for the one on the stack.
Thor, God of Thunder: Tap the attacking Thor with Wasp after attackers are declared to stop the damage. Tapping does not remove a creature from combat. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step (or earlier) to stop the attack.
Great Hall of the Biblioplex: Wasp in response to the {5} activation stops the land from becoming a creature. Wasp needs a creature target and the land is not one yet; Wasp cannot counter abilities. Wait until the animation resolves, then tap and blank it.
Everything else in the 75: Hold Wasp for a target, or for a Thor you have not seen. About 90% of the deck is instants, sorceries and lands, and Thor is a 1-of (2 postboard). A Wasp held for a Sting target that never comes is 2 damage a turn you did not deal to a deck that wins by stabilizing.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Tidebinder on turn-3 Tablet (on the play), attack with the 3/2 next turn into an empty board, then ninjutsu Kaito in swapping Tidebinder back to hand. Know the cost: Tidebinder leaving the battlefield unblanks Tablet immediately. That trade is usually right because Kaito's card flow beats a temporary mana denial, and the returned Tidebinder is a fresh flash trigger for Thor later.
  • Never ninjutsu away or bounce a Wasp/Tidebinder that is holding Thor blanked; that is the one lock worth keeping. Ninjutsu Kaito in off a different attacker (Spyglass Siren, Drowner, Dream Beavers).
  • Thor answer that does not depend on the lock: Wasp or Tidebinder blanks Thor (no Flying, no cast damage), then Floodpits Drowner's stun and {1}{U},{T} shuffle-away or Kaito -2 (tap + two stun counters) handles the 5/5 body. Shoot the Sheriff also kills Thor outright (God Warrior Hero is not an outlaw), blanked or not.
  • Protect the lock with Spell Snare: Sear, Shoot the Sheriff and Nowhere to Run, their three cleanest answers to Tidebinder/Wasp, all have mana value 2. Firebending Lesson (MV 1 even when kicked) and Inevitable Defeat (MV 4, uncounterable) get past Snare. Postboard Flashfreeze covers the red half: Firebending Lesson, Sear, Flashback, Tablet of Discovery, Thor himself and Jeskai Revelation (red, unless Mistrise resolved); it cannot hit Shoot the Sheriff, Nowhere to Run, Deadly Cover-Up or Inevitable Defeat. Annul counters Tablet and Nowhere to Run as spells.
  • Mistrise Village line: they activate {U},{T}; Tidebinder counters the activation; the Revelation or Deadly Cover-Up they cast next is now counterable by We Say Thee Nay! or Spell Pierce (Flashfreeze too if it is Revelation). Needs 3 mana plus the counter on their turn or your end step, and the usual result is that they hold the spell a turn; do it only when you wanted the 3/2 anyway.
  • Wasp plus Enduring Curiosity: a 2/1 flier connecting into an empty board draws a card every turn. Flash both at the end of their turn so Deadly Cover-Up / Day of Judgment cannot answer them before they attack once. Curiosity comes back as an enchantment after Day of Judgment or an evidence-less Cover-Up, but NOT after Deadly Cover-Up with evidence collected (it destroys, then exiles a card from our graveyard in the same resolution, before the dies trigger resolves, so they exile Curiosity and then every other copy from hand, graveyard and library) and not after Day of Black Sun at X=4 (removes the trigger before destroying).
  • Postboard, our Day of Black Sun at X=0 destroys their animated Great Halls and Monk tokens (both mana value 0) without touching our Kaito; Requiting Hex kills the same two things at instant speed.
  • Duress (ours) takes Jeskai Revelation, Deadly Cover-Up or Inevitable Defeat; their Duress cannot take Tidebinder or Wasp (creature cards), their Negate cannot counter either creature spell, and their Flashfreeze counters only red or green spells so it is dead against our whole deck. Our flash creatures are the threats their sideboard is worst against.
  • Thor trigger at Kaito: on their turn Kaito is a plain planeswalker (loyalty 4, hexproof only during our turn), so a Revelation trigger (7) or Together as One trigger (up to 6) kills him. If Kaito is your engine and Thor is out, keep 3 open on their turn for Tidebinder on exactly that trigger; it saves Kaito and blanks Thor in one card.
Hold or fire
Default: fire both as flash bodies at the end of their turn and start the clock. This deck wins by stabilizing, and a held 3/2 or 2/1 that waits for a target is damage you did not deal; flashing in at their end step also dodges the sorcery-speed sweepers for one attack. Hold Tidebinder only for windows you can actually see coming: (1) Thor is on the battlefield or about to be (they are at 5 lands and have shown Thor): counter the ETB as it enters (they are usually tapped out, guaranteed blank; best when it targets Deadly Cover-Up or Revelation), otherwise the first cast trigger that comes while they are tapped out, or any trigger of 4+ aimed at Kaito or your face even into open mana; (2) their turn-3 Tablet on the play when you have 3 lands untapped and nothing else to do at flash speed (on the draw the window is their turn-4 Tablet or the second copy). Never hold for Petrified Hamlet or Mistrise Village; take those only if Tidebinder happens to be in hand with 3 spare. Before firing, count their open mana AND Tablet: if they can Sear / Lesson / Shoot the Tidebinder while its trigger is on the stack, the target is still countered (ruling 1) but nothing gets blanked and an unblanked Thor pings off that removal spell; if they have 7 open (or 5 plus Tablet), Revelation can bounce Tidebinder as a spell and nothing is countered at all. So only fire into open mana when the counter alone is worth a card (Thor triggers, a Cover-Up/Revelation replay, Hamlet ETB). Wasp: it is a 2-power flying clock here. Sting Thor only if Thor is already on the battlefield when you draw or hold Wasp (in response to the ETB or in their beginning of combat); never hold Wasp for a Thor you have not seen. Use the tap to buy one turn while Drowner or Shoot the Sheriff handles the body. Postboard with 2-3 Tidebinder, every copy beyond the first is a body at end of turn unless a Thor is on the table.
How many to run
Main 1 Tidebinder + 2 Wasp is right for game 1. Postboard, bring in both sideboard Tidebinders (to 3) and keep 2 Wasp. Reasons: their sideboard (Duress, Negate; Flashfreeze cannot come in against a blue/black deck) cannot touch a creature spell, so flash bodies are exactly the threats they handle worst, and our main is full of near-dead cards against a one-creature deck to cut from: 4 Requiting Hex (destroys MV 2 or less; their only creatures at that size are Monk tokens and an animated Great Hall), 2 Bitter Triumph, and Shoot the Sheriff and Floodpits Drowner beyond the copies you keep for Thor. A sample board: +3 Duress, +2 Tidebinder, +2 Flashfreeze, +2 Spell Pierce; -4 Requiting Hex, -2 Bitter Triumph, -1 Shoot the Sheriff, -2 Floodpits Drowner. Keep 2 Drowner and 1 Shoot the Sheriff for Thor (Drowner's stun-and-shuffle is the cleanest permanent answer to a 5/5 God). Do not judge the Tidebinder count by target density; its job here is a 3-mana flash 3/2 that dodges Duress and Negate, with the Thor counter as upside. The real cost of the third copy is that 3 open mana on their turn competes with holding Spell Snare / Spell Pierce mana, so on turns where you hold both a counter and Tidebinder, the counter for Revelation/Cover-Up usually wins.
Review trail: confidence high, 8 disputed, 16 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Reviewer 2: "Their turn-3 Tablet is the only guaranteed blank in the matchup (they are tapped out)." Rejected: Tablet of Discovery is an artifact that enters untapped with "{T}: Add {R}." and "{T}: Add {R}{R}. Spend this mana only to cast instant and sorcery spells." While Tidebinder's trigger is on the stack Tablet still has those abilities, and a player may activate a mana ability whenever they have priority (CR 605.3a), so they can tap Tablet in response for Sear or Firebending Lesson. The ETB is still countered (ruling 1) but Tablet is not blanked. No Tablet window is guaranteed; it depends on whether they hold Lesson/Sear.
  • "Tablet of Discovery's ETB is tied to a mana source, so it is a mana ability and untargetable." Rejected: a triggered ability is a mana ability only if it triggers from activating a mana ability and could add mana (CR 605.1b); Tablet's ETB triggers on entering and mills a card. It is targetable, and countering it makes the artifact lose all abilities, including its two mana abilities, while Tidebinder remains.
  • "Wasp in response to Thor's ETB saves the graveyard card." Rejected: an activated or triggered ability on the stack exists independently of its source (CR 113.7a); blanking Thor does not remove the trigger. Only Tidebinder counters it.
  • "Countering Great Hall's {5} blanks the land so it can never animate again." Rejected: Tidebinder ruling 3, the source must be an artifact, creature or planeswalker as Tidebinder's trigger resolves; the land is not a creature yet, so nothing is lost and it can be activated again.
  • "Countering Thor's cast trigger also counters the spell that caused it." Rejected: the spell and the trigger are separate stack objects; Tidebinder targets only the ability, and the spell resolves.
  • "Deadly Cover-Up's collect evidence is an activated ability you can counter." Rejected: collect evidence here is an additional cost paid as the spell is cast; costs are not abilities on the stack. Only a spell counter interacts, and Mistrise Village turns that off.
  • "Nowhere to Run's ETB being countered removes its hexproof-piercing static." Rejected: Tidebinder's loses-all-abilities clause applies only to artifacts, creatures and planeswalkers; an enchantment keeps everything. The static stays and Kaito remains targetable on our turn.
  • "Great Hall's animation wears off at end of turn." Rejected on the oracle text in the deck file: "{5}: If this land isn't a creature, it becomes a 2/4 Wizard creature with ... It's still a land." carries no duration, so the creature status is permanent; this is why Wasp can target it afterward and why Day of Black Sun at X=0 kills it.
Changes after review
  • Removed every reference to Negate as one of our counters (it is only in THEIR sideboard); replaced with We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Pierce, and sideboard Flashfreeze/Annul with the exact spells each can hit, per our_stock_list_sept_2026 in the deck file.
  • Fixed the Thor cast-trigger claim that the removal spell aimed at Tidebinder never pings: now distinguishes removal cast in response to Tidebinder's trigger (Thor still live, pings as normal, no blank, ruling 1) from removal cast after it resolves (no ping).
  • Reordered Thor entries: ETB is now priority 1 (earliest, usually tapped out, guaranteed blank, denies replay) and the cast trigger priority 2; replaced the MV-4+ rule with the open-mana rule (take the first trigger that comes while they cannot pay for removal in response).
  • Named Deadly Cover-Up as the highest-value Thor ETB target (replay with collect evidence strips all copies of Curiosity/Kaito) and added Thor's trigger at Kaito on their turn (no hexproof off our turn, loyalty 4) as a save-the-engine window.
  • Moved Petrified Hamlet and Mistrise Village out of the hold list into opportunistic-only; rewrote the Hamlet summary line (only the two name-dependent statics go inert; Hamlet still taps for {C}).
  • Corrected the instant-speed answer count from 'roughly 14' to 17 (12 removal spells + Nowhere to Run + 4 Revelation bounces), with Deadly Cover-Up as the only sorcery-speed answer.
  • Reframed the Tablet entry from 'Stone Rain lock' to tempo; added that Tablet's own mana abilities work in response so no Tablet window is a guaranteed blank (and disputed reviewer 2's contrary claim with CR 605.3a).
  • Added the Revelation 'target spell' response: bouncing Tidebinder/Wasp while still on the stack means nothing enters and nothing is countered; folded into the Revelation trap and the open-mana check.
  • Fixed the Curiosity line: Deadly Cover-Up with evidence exiles Curiosity from the graveyard before the dies trigger resolves, then exiles all other copies; only Day of Judgment / evidence-less Cover-Up let it return.
  • Corrected the Thor-ETB-no-target wording to CR 603.3d (trigger goes on the stack and is removed for lack of a legal target).
  • Kicked Firebending Lesson noted as still MV 1 for Thor; added a Consult/Lesson kicker trap.
  • Simplified Wasp hold rule: never hold for an unseen Thor; Sting only a Thor already on the battlefield; noted the blank realistically lasts one turn.
  • Added Flashfreeze coverage (red spells: Lesson, Sear, Flashback, Tablet, Thor, Revelation) to the protect-the-lock line; noted their own Flashfreeze is dead against our blue/black spells and removed it from their relevant sideboard cards.
  • Rewrote counts: go to 3 Tidebinder postboard with a concrete in/out plan built on cuts verified in the deck file (Requiting Hex is MV-2-or-less removal with no real target; Bitter Triumph; excess Shoot the Sheriff and Drowner), keeping 2 Drowner + 1 Shoot for Thor.
  • Added Outrageous Robbery and the remaining pure-spell sideboard cards to dont_bother with the reason; noted Requiting Hex and Day of Black Sun X=0 both kill an animated Great Hall.
  • Added Dream Beavers as a ninjutsu carrier and the exact Drowner shuffle cost in the Thor-answer line.
vs. Dimir Excruciator5.7% TB: premiumWasp: situational▼
A removal-and-discard control shell (3 Duress, 4 Requiting Hex, 3 Bitter Triumph, 2 Archenemy's Charm, 2 Day of Black Sun, Deadly Cover-Up, 4 Deceit) buys time while Winternight Stories ("discard two cards unless you discard a creature card"), Bitter Triumph's additional cost ("discard a card or pay 3 life", instant speed, so it can happen on OUR turn), Undercity Sewers surveil and (postboard) M.O.D.O.K. connive put Doomsday Excruciator into their graveyard. The engine is Superior Spider-Man ({2}{U}{B}, Mind Swap: "enter as a copy of any creature card in a graveyard"); Scryfall ruling: "Any ... 'When this creature enters' abilities of the chosen card will work", so a turn-4 Spider-Man copying Excruciator IS a cast Excruciator: "each player exiles all but the bottom six cards of their library face down" (turn-3 Stories into turn-4 Spider-Man is their fastest line; the {B}{B}{B}{B}{B}{B} hardcast is the backup, often through Cavern of Souls naming Demon or Human). Spider-Man has two other modes to know: copying Harvester of Misery (Harvester bins itself with "{1}{B}, Discard this card") for a second -2/-2 sweep, and copying Emeritus of Ideation for a second Ancestral Recall copy. With both libraries at six they close by casting Emeritus's exiled Ancestral Recall ("Target player draws three cards", {U}) at us, attacking with Restless Reef ("Whenever this land attacks, target player mills four cards"), and swinging 6/6 flyers, racing their own Excruciator upkeep draw, which decks them on the fourth upkeep after the ETB.
Tishana's Tidebinder
The best Tidebinder matchup in the format. Their whole game is one triggered ability, the Excruciator ETB, and Tidebinder is the only card in our 75 that stops it through Cavern of Souls (Cavern makes the creature SPELL uncounterable; the trigger is not a spell). Reserve copies for that trigger while it is live, and learn the cheap public check that tells you when it is live (an Excruciator card in their graveyard, or six lands). Spare copies have real secondary jobs: Restless Reef's mill trigger once libraries are at six (also turns the Reef into a dead land), Spider-Man's copied ETBs (Harvester sweep, Deceit modes), the ward trigger on Emeritus/Gearhulk when our removal targets them, Deceit's ETB, Harvester's ETB.
#1Doomsday Excruciator (hardcast, or Superior Spider-Man entering as a copy of it)
"When this creature enters, if it was cast, each player exiles all but the bottom six cards of their library face down." On Spider-Man the copied ETB works because Spider-Man was cast (Scryfall ruling 2025-09-19: 'Any ... "When this creature enters" abilities of the chosen card will work').
WhyIt is their entire win condition. Countering it keeps our library intact; their Recall and Reef mills become harmless and their flyer becomes a body we can Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff (Demon and Spider Human Hero are not outlaws). Cavern of Souls does not protect the trigger, and killing Tidebinder in response does not save it (ruling 1: the ability is still countered).
WhenLet the creature spell resolve. When it enters, the ETB trigger goes on the stack and they get priority first; when they pass, cast Tidebinder with the trigger on the stack; Tidebinder resolves, its own trigger targets the Excruciator trigger and resolves first. NEVER cast Tidebinder while the Excruciator/Spider-Man SPELL is on the stack: a spell is not an ability, the trigger does not exist yet, and Tidebinder's trigger will have nothing to hit. On Spider-Man two triggers appear at once (the library exile and Mind Swap's 'When you do, exile that card'); make them identify which is which and target the library one. Mana: hold {2}{U} through their main phases on any turn the ETB is live (see hold_or_fire), which on the draw can mean passing our turn 3 with three lands untapped; on the play we have four lands on their turn 4 and can spend one on Siren/Beavers and still hold {2}{U}.
AfterLoses: Flying and 'At the beginning of your upkeep, draw a card' for as long as Tidebinder is on the battlefield. Keeps: the body (6/6 hardcast; 4/4 for the Spider-Man copy, since 'he's a 4/4 Spider Human Hero' is part of the copy exception) and its creature types. The library exile is gone forever: killing or bouncing Tidebinder later restores flying and the upkeep draw but does not re-trigger the ETB. Watch for Deceit's {U}{U} mode bouncing THEIR OWN Excruciator to recast it, or Archenemy's Charm mode two returning it from the graveyard to hand; each recast is a fresh ETB that needs a fresh answer.
RiskThey respond with Bitter Triumph or Archenemy's Charm on Tidebinder: the trigger is still countered (ruling 1), Excruciator just keeps its abilities. Day of Black Sun X=3 later kills Tidebinder but changes nothing about the library. The real risk is a second live ETB (second Spider-Man, recast) when we hold no second Tidebinder. We Say Thee Nay! is a backup only if they are not paying with Cavern mana AND cannot pay the {2} tax ({4} with teamwork): a turn-4 Spider-Man off exactly four lands cannot pay, later turns usually can. The held Tidebinder is also exposed to Deceit's {B}{B} mode and Oildeep Gearhulk (both take a creature card from hand; Duress cannot), one more reason two copies in hand is the real safety.
#2Restless Reef (only after an Excruciator ETB has resolved, or when the mill would otherwise be damaging)
"Whenever this land attacks, target player mills four cards."
WhyWith six cards left, mill four is lethal-adjacent and Tidebinder is the only card in our 75 that stops it (killing the animated Reef in response does not remove the trigger from the stack). Bonus: because Reef is a 4/4 Shark creature when Tidebinder's trigger resolves, it loses all abilities for as long as Tidebinder lives, including '{T}: Add {U} or {B}', the animation ability and the deathtouch its own earlier animation granted (earlier timestamp, so removed). After end of turn it is a land with no abilities: a dead land against a deck that needs {B}{B}{B}{B}{B}{B}.
WhenDeclare attackers step, with the mill trigger on the stack, after they pass priority. Do not fire on the animation activation ({2}{U}{B}) if the goal is the ability-lock: at that moment Reef is still just a land, so ruling 3 applies and nothing is blanked; countering the animation is only a 4-mana tempo hit.
AfterLoses: mana ability, animation ability, mill trigger, deathtouch. Keeps: it stays a 4/4 Shark creature until end of turn (type and P/T from the resolved animation are effects, not abilities), then reverts to a land that still cannot tap for mana while Tidebinder remains.
#3Superior Spider-Man copying something OTHER than Excruciator
Whatever ETB the copied card has, e.g. Harvester of Misery: "When this creature enters, other creatures get -2/-2 until end of turn"; Deceit: the {U}{U} bounce / {B}{B} discard ETB (live if they paid two blue or two black into Spider-Man's {2}{U}{B}; Deceit ruling: the check is on mana spent for the total cost); Quantum Riddler: "draw a card"; our Floodpits Drowner: "tap target creature an opponent controls and put a stun counter on it". If the copied card has NO enters ability (Qarsi Revenant, Emeritus whose 'enters prepared' is static, Deceit paid without UU/BB), the only trigger on the stack is Mind Swap's own "When you do, exile that card".
WhySpider-Man-as-Harvester is a second board wipe of our 1- and 2-toughness creatures and a 4/4 menace; countering it saves the board and blanks Spider-Man (a creature, so ruling 3 is satisfied). Spider-Man-as-Deceit is a 4/4 with a bounce or hand-attack ETB. In the no-ETB case, countering the Mind Swap exile trigger blanks Spider-Man AND leaves the copied card in their graveyard instead of exiled (it stays available for another Spider-Man, so this is mostly for the blank).
WhenETB on the stack after Spider-Man resolves. Only spend Tidebinder here when no Excruciator ETB is live next turn or you hold another copy: an Excruciator in their graveyard means the next Spider-Man is the one that matters.
AfterSpider-Man loses all copied abilities (and Mind Swap text) while Tidebinder lives; keeps its 4/4 body. Special case: a Spider-Man that copied Emeritus enters prepared and gets its own Ancestral Recall copy in exile (the copied 'enters prepared' static works on entering; Emeritus ruling: effects that state a creature 'enters prepared' cause it to become prepared, and the copy has the alternative characteristics). Blanking it does not touch that Recall copy (ruling: losing all abilities does not end prepared), so against that mode Tidebinder only strips flying/ward; the Recall needs We Say Thee Nay!/Spell Pierce.
#4Ward trigger of Emeritus of Ideation (ward {2}) or Oildeep Gearhulk (ward {1}) when OUR spell or ability targets it
Ward is a triggered ability (CR 702.21a): "Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter it unless that player pays [cost]." It triggers when our Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff, Kaito -2, Floodpits Drowner ETB or Wasp targets the creature.
WhyCounter the ward trigger and our removal resolves untaxed, and because the ward trigger's source is the creature, Emeritus/Gearhulk loses all abilities while Tidebinder lives (flying, ward, the re-prepare attack trigger; lifelink on Gearhulk). Mana math: Shoot the Sheriff + Tidebinder is 5 mana versus Sheriff + ward tax at 4, so this is not a discount, it is a 3/2 body plus a blank on top of the kill; it shines when the ward tax would leave us unable to also hold Tidebinder mana, or when the target is Emeritus still prepared with an uncast Recall in exile (killing it makes that copy cease to exist per the Emeritus ruling: the copy remains only 'for as long as that permanent is on the battlefield and is prepared').
WhenCast our removal targeting Emeritus/Gearhulk; the ward trigger goes on the stack; with it on the stack, flash Tidebinder and target the ward trigger. Do this only with a spare copy (no live Excruciator ETB or a second Tidebinder in hand).
AfterOur spell resolves normally. If the creature somehow survives, it stays blanked while Tidebinder lives; prepared status and any Recall copy in exile are unaffected by the blank.
#5Harvester of Misery (ETB)
"When this creature enters, other creatures get -2/-2 until end of turn."
WhyIt wipes our Spyglass Siren, Dream Beavers, Floodpits Drowner, Wasp, Faerie tokens and Tidebinder itself (3/2). Countering saves the board and turns Harvester into a 5/4 vanilla. Only worth Tidebinder #1 if we also hold Tidebinder #2 for the Excruciator, or no Excruciator ETB is live.
WhenETB trigger on the stack after Harvester resolves; Tidebinder's trigger goes on top and resolves first.
AfterLoses: menace, the ETB, and the hand-discard ability. Keeps: 5/4 body.
#6Deceit
"When this creature enters, if {U}{U} was spent to cast it, return up to one other target nonland permanent to its owner's hand." / "When this creature enters, if {B}{B} was spent to cast it, target opponent reveals their hand. You choose a nonland card from it. That player discards that card."
WhyEvoked for {U/B}{U/B} (2 mana) only one mode can be on; countering it means they paid two mana for nothing, and Deceit still dies because the evoke sacrifice trigger is already on the stack and exists independently of its source. Hardcast ({4}{U/B}{U/B}) can turn on both; one Tidebinder counters only one. Priorities: the {B}{B} discard when it would take our held Tidebinder or Kaito from hand (it also stops the hand reveal); the {U}{U} bounce when it targets the Tidebinder currently blanking an Excruciator whose ETB we countered (the bounce would restore flying and the double draw) or their own Excruciator/Emeritus (a re-entry is a fresh ETB / a fresh Recall copy). A bounce on Kaito alone is tempo, not card loss (he can re-ninjutsu), so do not spend a reserved copy on it.
WhenETB triggers on the stack. They choose the order of their own triggers and the Deceit ruling lets them resolve the bounce/discard before the sacrifice; that changes nothing for us, we target the bounce/discard trigger wherever it sits.
AfterLoses: both ETBs and evoke text (irrelevant once on the battlefield). Keeps: 5/5 body (hardcast) or is sacrificed anyway (evoked). The other, uncountered ETB still resolves.
#7Emeritus of Ideation attack trigger (situational: check their graveyard first)
"Whenever this creature attacks, you may exile eight cards from your graveyard. If you do, this creature becomes prepared."
WhyAfter they have cast the Recall copy (Emeritus ruling: 'As they cast it, that creature stops being prepared', so nothing is left in exile), this trigger is their only way to a second Recall from that Emeritus. Countering it denies Recall #2 and strips Emeritus of ward {2} and flying, so Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph (Human Wizard is not an outlaw) then kills it untaxed. BUT it needs eight cards in their graveyard: right after an Excruciator ETB (which exiles from libraries, not graveyards) they are usually short, so count the graveyard; fewer than eight means the trigger is blank and Emeritus is just a 5/5 ward-2 flyer. Ward does not tax Tidebinder here because Tidebinder targets the ability, not the permanent.
WhenDeclare attackers step with the attack trigger on the stack. If Emeritus is still prepared from entering and they have not cast Recall yet, the trigger cannot prepare it a second time (Emeritus ruling: 'can't become prepared more than once at the same time'), so skip it unless you want the flying/ward strip.
AfterLoses: flying, ward {2}, the attack trigger. Keeps: 5/5 body and, crucially, its prepared status and any uncast Recall copy in exile (ruling: 'If a prepared creature loses all abilities, it won't stop being prepared'). Tidebinder does not stop a Recall already in exile; only a counterspell on the Recall or removing Emeritus from the battlefield while prepared does.
#8Doomsday Excruciator upkeep draw (ONLY in the world where we countered its ETB and Tidebinder #1 has since died)
"At the beginning of your upkeep, draw a card."
WhyWith full libraries, a 6/6 flyer drawing two cards a turn is pure card advantage for them. If Day of Black Sun or Bitter Triumph removed the Tidebinder that was blanking it, a spare copy on the upkeep trigger re-blanks Excruciator (no flying, no extra card) while we find removal. After a RESOLVED ETB this same trigger is what decks them: never counter it then.
WhenTheir upkeep, trigger on the stack.
AfterLoses flying and the upkeep draw again while the new Tidebinder lives. Keeps the body.
#9M.O.D.O.K. (sideboard)
"Mental Organism, Pay 3 life: M.O.D.O.K. connives. Activate only during your turn."
WhyCountering any connive makes M.O.D.O.K. lose ALL abilities while Tidebinder lives, including the static 'Creatures your opponents control get -1/-1' that kills Siren, Beavers, Drowner and Wasp on sight. The 3 life stays paid.
WhenTheir turn, connive activation on the stack. Tidebinder enters as a 2/1 under the static and survives; once the counter resolves it is back to 3/2.
AfterLoses: the -1/-1 static, flying, lifelink, connive. Keeps: 2/2 body plus any +1/+1 counters already placed.
#10Oildeep Gearhulk ETB (sideboard)
"When this creature enters, look at target player's hand. You may choose a card from it. If you do, that player discards that card, then draws a card."
WhySaves a card (often the held Tidebinder) and the information; ward {1} does not tax Tidebinder since the ability, not the permanent, is targeted.
WhenETB on the stack.
AfterLoses: lifelink, ward {1}. Keeps: 4/4 artifact body.
#11Wan Shi Tong, Librarian (sideboard)
"When Wan Shi Tong enters, put X +1/+1 counters on him. Then draw half X cards, rounded down."
WhyTurns an X+2 mana flash play into a 1/1 with no flying, no vigilance and no cards.
WhenETB on the stack, usually at our end step since it has flash.
AfterLoses: flying, vigilance, both triggers. Keeps: 1/1 body with zero counters.
#12Quantum Riddler (sideboard, pre-Excruciator only)
"When this creature enters, draw a card."
WhyWarped for {1}{U} it becomes two mana for nothing; hardcast it becomes a 4/6 vanilla. Countering the ETB also blanks the static 'As long as you have one or fewer cards in hand, if you would draw one or more cards, you draw that many cards plus one instead', which cannot be targeted directly (and rarely matters in a control deck holding cards). After an Excruciator ETB has resolved, let their draws happen: they are decking themselves.
WhenETB on the stack. Warp's end-step exile is a delayed trigger created as it enters, so it still exiles the blanked Riddler.
AfterLoses: flying, the draw replacement, the ETB. Keeps: 4/6 body; still exiled at end step if warped.
#13Qarsi Revenant (sideboard, renew from graveyard)
"Renew, {2}{B}, Exile this card from your graveyard: Put a flying counter, a deathtouch counter, and a lifelink counter on target creature. Activate only as a sorcery."
WhyThree mana and the card are gone (the exile is a cost) and the target gets no counters. Nothing loses abilities because the source is a card in exile, not a permanent.
WhenTheir main phase with the renew ability on the stack.
AfterNo permanent is affected; the target creature keeps everything it had.
Traps
Doomsday Excruciator / Superior Spider-Man (the spell): Flash Tidebinder in response to the creature spell to 'stop it'. Casting a spell is not an ability. Tidebinder's trigger would have no legal ability to target, Tidebinder becomes a 3/2, and the ETB then resolves unopposed. Wait for the trigger.
Superior Spider-Man (Mind Swap) copying Excruciator: Counter 'enter as a copy' or the reflexive 'When you do, exile that card' trigger. Entering as a copy is a replacement effect and cannot be countered. The reflexive exile IS a trigger, but here it is the wrong one: if you target it, Spider-Man loses its abilities while the copied library-exile trigger, already on the stack, still resolves. Two triggers go on the stack together; target the one that says 'each player exiles all but the bottom six'. (The Mind Swap trigger is the RIGHT target only when the copied card has no enters ability at all, see target 3.)
Superior Spider-Man in OUR graveyard's image (copying our Tishana's Tidebinder): Their Spider-Man copying our Tidebinder becomes a flash counter for our Excruciator answer. The copy happens as Spider-Man ENTERS (Spider-Man ruling: 'Treat Superior Spider-Man as though he were the chosen card entering'); in hand and on the stack it is plain Superior Spider-Man with no flash, so it is cast only at sorcery speed with an empty stack, when there is normally nothing of ours to counter. It is a 4/4 body, not an interaction piece.
Deceit (evoke): Counter the evoke trigger 'When this permanent enters, if its evoke cost was paid, sacrifice it.' Legal, but it hands them a 5/5 for two mana. Counter the bounce/discard ETB instead; the sacrifice still resolves because an ability on the stack exists independently of its source even after Deceit loses all abilities.
Emeritus of Ideation (prepared / Ancestral Recall copy): Blank Emeritus with Tidebinder or Wasp to turn off the Recall; or counter 'enters prepared'; or kill Emeritus after the Recall was cast to 'remove the copy'. 'This creature enters prepared' is static and the Recall is a spell cast from exile, not an ability. Emeritus ruling: 'If a prepared creature loses all abilities, it won't stop being prepared, and nothing will happen to ... the copy of its prepare spell in exile.' Killing Emeritus only removes the copy while Emeritus is still prepared with an UNCAST copy in exile; once they cast the Recall, 'that creature stops being prepared' and nothing is in exile to remove. Only countering the Recall (We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Pierce; Spell Snare cannot, it is MV 1) or removing a still-prepared Emeritus stops it.
Cavern of Souls: Cavern-paid Spider-Man/Excruciator/Emeritus means we cannot interact. 'That spell can't be countered' is a static effect on the SPELL. Tidebinder counters the ETB trigger, which Cavern does not touch. Cavern only turns off We Say Thee Nay! on that creature spell (Spell Pierce could never target a creature spell anyway).
Restless Reef ({2}{U}{B} animation): Counter the animation to blank the land. When Tidebinder's trigger resolves, Reef has not finished animating and is a land, so ruling 3 applies: the ability is countered (4 mana wasted) but Reef keeps all its abilities. The lock only happens when you counter the attack trigger while Reef is a creature.
Winternight Stories (harmonize) / Stock Up / Day of Black Sun / Deadly Cover-Up / Duress / Ancestral Recall copy: Tidebinder can answer their sorceries and the Recall. Casting from the graveyard with harmonize, or from exile as a prepare spell, is casting a spell, not an ability; sorceries and instants are spells. Day of Black Sun's 'loses all abilities' is a spell effect. Only our real counterspells touch these: We Say Thee Nay! (main) and Spell Pierce (side). Flashfreeze hits only red/green spells and Annul only artifact/enchantment spells; Negate is in THEIR sideboard, not ours.
Harvester of Misery (hand mode) / Qarsi Revenant (renew) / Ghost Vacuum ({6},{T}, sacrifice): Countering blanks the source. The discard, graveyard exile or sacrifice is a COST and stays paid; the source is a card in the graveyard/exile or a sacrificed artifact, not an artifact, creature or planeswalker on the battlefield, so nothing loses abilities. You only deny the one effect.
Quantum Riddler (warp): Counter the warp delayed trigger 'exile it at the beginning of the next end step'. Counterable, but it leaves them a permanent 4/6 body for {1}{U}. Whether Riddler also loses flying depends on whether that delayed trigger counts as the Riddler permanent's own ability for Tidebinder's second clause, which no ruling settles; do not build a play around either reading. Countering the ETB is always the better use.
Emeritus of Ideation / Oildeep Gearhulk (ward): Ward {2}/{1} taxes Tidebinder. Ward triggers only when the permanent itself becomes a target. Tidebinder targets the ability on the stack, so no ward tax. Wasp does target the creature and IS taxed.
Watery Grave / Cavern of Souls / Undercity Sewers 'enters tapped': As-enters choices and enters-tapped are triggers. 'As this land enters, choose a creature type' and 'you may pay 2 life. If you don't, it enters tapped' are replacement effects; 'This land enters tapped' is static. Undercity Sewers' surveil IS a trigger but a land loses nothing and it is not worth a card.
Not worth it
Doomsday Excruciator's 'At the beginning of your upkeep, draw a card' after their ETB RESOLVED: this is the clock that decks them on the fourth upkeep after the ETB; countering it (and stripping flying) slows their loss more than it helps us. (In the countered-ETB world it is a fine target, see target 8.)A second Excruciator/Spider-Man ETB after the first one RESOLVED: Excruciator ruling, 'A player with six or fewer cards remaining in their library ... won't exile any cards', so it is blank; but if we countered the first, a second ETB is fully live and needs another answer.Undercity Sewers surveil 1: a land, so nothing is blanked and you trade a 3-mana card for one card of their filtering.Restless Reef animation before an Excruciator ETB: 4-mana tempo hit, no ability loss, and the mill only matters once libraries are small (pre-ETB it just feeds Spider-Man our creature cards, a minor cost).Quantum Riddler / Wan Shi Tong / Oildeep Gearhulk draws after an Excruciator ETB has resolved: every card they draw brings their loss closer.Wan Shi Tong's second trigger 'Whenever an opponent searches their library, put a +1/+1 counter on Wan Shi Tong and draw a card': counterable, but our stock 75 has no library search, so it never triggers against us.Ghost Vacuum's '{T}: Exile target card from a graveyard': we are not a graveyard deck; blanking the Vacuum is not worth a card.Harvester's hand mode aimed at a single 1/1 (Siren/Beavers): a 3-mana card to save a 1-drop, unless the -2/-2 is aimed at the Tidebinder locking their Excruciator (then judgment). Note the discarded Harvester is now Spider-Man fuel for the -2/-2 sweep (target 3).Superior Spider-Man copying Qarsi Revenant: the only trigger is Mind Swap's exile; blanking a 4/4 flying deathtouch lifelink is fine value if the copy is spare, but not while an Excruciator ETB is live.
The Wondrous Wasp
Wasp cannot touch the Excruciator ETB (blanking the creature does not remove a trigger already on the stack), and their relevant flyers are a 6/6 and a warded 5/5, so Wasp's default role is a 2-mana flash flyer that pressures for Enduring Curiosity draws before the ETB. It has one premium job: tapping an animated Restless Reef in their beginning of combat once libraries are at six, which stops the mill-four attack and kills the land as a mana source for as long as Wasp lives. In the countered-ETB world it is also a fine re-blank on an Excruciator that lost its Tidebinder. Never cast it into an active M.O.D.O.K.
#1Restless Reef (animated, after an Excruciator ETB has resolved)
WhyReef is a creature after '{2}{U}{B}: Until end of turn, this land becomes a 4/4 blue and black Shark creature with deathtouch' resolves. Wasp taps it so it cannot attack (no 'mills four cards' trigger) and it loses all abilities for as long as Wasp remains: mana ability, animation, mill trigger, deathtouch. After end of turn it is a land that cannot tap for mana. This is Wasp's only game-saving play in the matchup.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, after the animation has resolved and they pass priority (both players must pass with an empty stack before attackers are declared, so we always get this window). If they animate in main phase 1, still wait for beginning of combat. Do not respond to the animation itself: Reef is not yet a creature and is not a legal target.
AfterTapped, cannot attack this turn; loses mana ability, animation ability, mill trigger and deathtouch while Wasp lives; stays a 4/4 Shark until end of turn, then a dead land.
#2Doomsday Excruciator / Spider-Man-as-Excruciator in the COUNTERED-ETB world
WhyIf we countered the ETB and the Tidebinder blanking it has since died, Excruciator is back to a 6/6 flyer drawing two a turn with full libraries. Wasp at their beginning of combat taps it (skips the attack) and re-blanks it: no flying, no upkeep draw, while Wasp lives. Unambiguously good here, unlike the resolved-ETB world (target 7).
WhenTheir beginning of combat (to also skip the attack) or their end step.
AfterTapped; loses flying and the upkeep draw while Wasp lives. Keeps 6/6 (or 4/4) body. Their Requiting Hex/Harvester ETB/Day of Black Sun X≥2 kill Wasp and restore it.
#3Emeritus of Ideation
WhyTapping at beginning of combat stops the attack and therefore the re-prepare trigger (only relevant if they have eight cards in graveyard and have already cast the first Recall). Blanking removes flying and ward {2}, so our Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff then kills it for its printed cost. Costs {1}{U} plus {2} for ward = 4 mana, and the blank does NOT remove prepared status or an uncast Recall copy in exile (ruling). Killing a still-prepared Emeritus does remove the uncast copy; once they have cast it there is nothing in exile.
WhenTheir beginning of combat for the tap; any time for the blank. Pay the ward {2} or Wasp's trigger is countered (Wasp still enters as a 2/1 flyer). With a spare Tidebinder, counter the ward trigger instead of paying (Tidebinder target 4).
AfterLoses flying, ward, attack trigger. Keeps 5/5 body, prepared status, any uncast Ancestral Recall copy in exile.
#4Qarsi Revenant (sideboard)
Why3/3 flying, deathtouch, lifelink becomes a 3/3 vanilla our 3/2s can trade with and that no longer gains them life in the race.
WhenTheir beginning of combat to also stop an attack; end step otherwise. Keyword counters placed by a later renew AFTER Wasp's effect began have a later timestamp and grant their keywords immediately (ruling 2 logic), so fire Wasp after the counters land if that is the concern.
AfterLoses flying, deathtouch, lifelink, including keywords granted by counters placed before Wasp entered (the counters themselves stay and work again the moment Wasp leaves). Keeps 3/3 body.
#5Superior Spider-Man copying a creature with a value ability (their Harvester, their Emeritus, our Floodpits Drowner, our Enduring Curiosity)
WhyThe copied ETB is already spent by the time Wasp can target the creature, so this is blank-only value: Spider-Man-as-Harvester loses menace, Spider-Man-as-Emeritus loses flying/ward (not its Recall copy), Spider-Man-as-Drowner loses vigilance and the '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter' ability, Spider-Man-as-Curiosity loses 'Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card'. Curiosity reaches our graveyard rarely: only via Day of Black Sun X≥4 (it loses its return ability before dying) or discard from our hand (Deceit {B}{B}, Gearhulk). After an Excruciator ETB has resolved, their extra draws help us: leave a Curiosity copy alone.
WhenTheir beginning of combat if you also want the tap; otherwise their end step.
AfterLoses all copied abilities. Keeps 4/4 body.
#6Harvester of Misery
WhyMenace to vanilla 5/4; tap once to skip an attack. Its ETB is already spent by the time Wasp can target it, and Wasp itself dies if cast into that ETB.
WhenTheir beginning of combat.
AfterLoses menace and the hand-discard ability. Keeps 5/4.
#7Doomsday Excruciator / Spider-Man-as-Excruciator in the RESOLVED-ETB world (only to survive)
WhyTap it at beginning of combat to skip one 6-point attack when that turn matters in the decking race. The blank is double-edged: it removes flying (good) but also 'At the beginning of your upkeep, draw a card', which is what decks them. Use only when the skipped attack is the difference between living and dying, and let Wasp die afterward so their upkeep draw resumes.
WhenTheir beginning of combat. It cannot stop the library exile: by the time Excruciator is a targetable creature its ETB is either still on the stack (independent of the source) or already resolved.
AfterTapped; loses flying and the upkeep draw while Wasp lives. Keeps 6/6 (or 4/4) body.
#8Deceit / Oildeep Gearhulk / Quantum Riddler / Wan Shi Tong
WhyBodies whose value was their ETB. Wasp is a one-turn tap plus a minor blank: Deceit nothing, Gearhulk loses lifelink (ward {1} tax), Riddler loses flying and its conditional draw-plus-one static, Wan Shi Tong loses flying/vigilance but keeps its counters.
WhenTheir beginning of combat if you want the tap; otherwise treat Wasp as a flash flyer at their end step.
AfterAs listed; bodies remain.
#9Any lone blocker on OUR turn (pre-Excruciator)
WhyTap Deceit/Harvester/Emeritus (ward tax) in our beginning of combat so Kaito ninjutsu connects and Enduring Curiosity draws. Do not do this after an Excruciator ETB: Curiosity's draw is mandatory and our library is six.
WhenOur beginning of combat step.
AfterTapped blocker, minor blank.
Traps
M.O.D.O.K. (sideboard): Flash Wasp to tap and blank M.O.D.O.K. so our 1-toughness creatures live. 'Creatures your opponents control get -1/-1' applies the moment Wasp enters: Wasp is 1/0 and dies to state-based actions before anyone gets priority. Its ETB still taps the target, but 'loses all abilities for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield' does nothing because the duration already ended (CR 611.2b). Answer M.O.D.O.K. with Tidebinder on a connive, Shoot the Sheriff (Villain is not an outlaw), Bitter Triumph or Annul.
Doomsday Excruciator / Superior Spider-Man: Wasp the Excruciator with its ETB on the stack to stop the library exile. A triggered ability on the stack exists independently of its source; the creature losing all abilities does not remove or counter it. Only Tidebinder counters the trigger.
Emeritus of Ideation: Blank it to remove the Ancestral Recall. Prepared status and the exiled Recall copy survive 'loses all abilities' (Emeritus ruling). Wasp only removes flying/ward and the re-prepare trigger.
Any attacking creature: Wasp after attackers are declared to remove the attacker. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared.
Harvester of Misery (ETB) / Day of Black Sun X≥2 / Requiting Hex: Wasp is a safe body to leave around. Wasp is a 2/1 with mana value 2: Harvester's ETB (-2/-2) kills it, Day of Black Sun at X=2 kills it, and Requiting Hex ('Destroy target creature with mana value 2 or less') is a clean answer they run four of. Tidebinder (MV 3) dodges Hex.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Their turn-4 Spider-Man (often uncounterable via Cavern naming Human): let it resolve, wait for the 'each player exiles all but the bottom six' trigger, Tidebinder it (make them identify it versus Mind Swap's 'exile that card'). Our turn: Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph the 4/4 vanilla, Floodpits Drowner ETB (tap + stun) then '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter' to remove it for good, or Kaito -2 and keep pressuring.
  • The cheap public check before every one of our turns: is a Doomsday Excruciator in their graveyard? If yes and they untap with 4+ lands, hold {2}{U} through their turn. If no and they have fewer than six lands, the only same-turn surprises are Bitter Triumph on OUR turn (they keep 2 open and we have a creature/Kaito to kill: it bins Excruciator at instant speed) or Undercity Sewers surveil (Sewers enters tapped, so a same-turn Spider-Man needs five lands). Otherwise ninjutsu Kaito and deploy.
  • Postboard Ghost Vacuum (1 mana, our sideboard): with Vacuum untapped, '{T}: Exile target card from a graveyard' removes Excruciator the moment Stories/Triumph/surveil bins it, at instant speed, and does the same to a binned Harvester (no Spider-Man sweep). It kills the Spider-Man line outright and frees Tidebinder for other jobs. They can Duress it (noncreature artifact), so cast it early and keep it untapped on their turn.
  • Deceit evoked at our Tidebinder (via {B}{B} discard) or Kaito: with a spare Tidebinder, counter the bounce/discard ETB; the evoke sacrifice still resolves, they are down two mana and a card, we are up a 3/2. Without a spare, let a Kaito bounce happen (tempo only, he re-ninjutsus) and keep the reservation.
  • Emeritus with eight cards in their graveyard attacks after they have cast the first Recall: Tidebinder counters the re-prepare trigger, Emeritus loses ward {2} and flying, then Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph kills it for two mana. If Emeritus is still prepared with an uncast Recall in exile, kill it with removal + Tidebinder on the ward trigger (or pay the ward tax): the copy ceases to exist when the prepared permanent leaves.
  • We Say Thee Nay! with teamwork 2 (tap Wasp power 2, Tidebinder power 3, or Curiosity power 4) makes the Recall copy or Winternight Stories cost {4} more instead of {2}; keep it for the Recall once libraries are at six (it is MV 1, Spell Snare cannot hit it). Pre-ETB they usually Recall themselves for card advantage; We Say Thee Nay!/Spell Pierce is the answer to that too, not Tidebinder or Wasp.
  • If the ETB gets through: stop all optional draws (no Kaito 0; be careful with Curiosity, its draw is mandatory), then race their self-deck. Their Excruciator draws two cards a turn, so they lose on the fourth upkeep after the ETB; our own Restless Reef attack ('target player mills four cards') cuts that to about two turns. Drowner-shuffling their Excruciator here is a judgment call, not a default: it removes the 6/6 flyer but also removes the upkeep draw that is decking them and adds a card to their library; do it only if the flyer's damage clock beats their deck-out clock.
  • Post-ETB defence kit: Wasp held for their animated Reef at beginning of combat (tap plus dead land), Tidebinder #2/#3 for the Reef mill trigger or a live Emeritus re-prepare, We Say Thee Nay!/Spell Pierce for the Recall, Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff for the 6/6.
  • Harvester of Misery ETB, or a Spider-Man copying Harvester, with a spare Tidebinder in hand: counter it, saving Siren/Beavers/Drowner/Wasp/Faeries and Tidebinder itself; the body is a vanilla and Kaito -2 stuns it.
  • M.O.D.O.K. postboard: never Wasp into it; wait for a connive activation on their turn and Tidebinder it, then deploy the 1-toughness creatures while Tidebinder lives, or Annul/Shoot the Sheriff it.
  • Their end step with the Excruciator threat not live (no Excruciator in graveyard, fewer than six lands) or already spent: flash Enduring Curiosity if we have four lands, otherwise Drowner/Wasp. From seven lands we can hold Tidebinder mana and still flash Curiosity.
Hold or fire
Tidebinder is reserved for the Excruciator ETB whenever that ETB is LIVE next turn, and free otherwise. Live means: (a) a Doomsday Excruciator card is in their graveyard and they will untap with 4+ lands (Spider-Man), or (b) they will have six lands (hardcast; Cavern counts as black), or (c) they can bin one at instant speed before their turn: Bitter Triumph with 2 mana open on our turn and a target of ours, or a Sewers surveil into a fifth land. Graveyards are public: check before each of our turns. When live, hold {2}{U} through their main phases (on the draw that can mean passing our turn 3 without Kaito ninjutsu or a 2-drop; on the play we have four lands on their turn 4, so one 1-drop is affordable). When not live, ninjutsu Kaito, deploy, and flash Curiosity at their end step. Never fire Tidebinder as a bare 3/2 while the ETB is live, never at the spell (wait for the trigger), never at the upkeep draw after a RESOLVED ETB. Pre-ETB exceptions with a second Tidebinder in hand only: a Harvester ETB (or Spider-Man-as-Harvester) that would kill two or more of our creatures, or a Deceit {B}{B} discard that would take the held Tidebinder. A Deceit bounce on Kaito alone is not worth a reserved copy. Once the ETB is countered, remaining copies go: live second ETB > Reef mill trigger (post-ETB only) > Spider-Man copied ETBs / Harvester ETB > ward trigger when our removal targets Emeritus/Gearhulk > Deceit ETB > Emeritus re-prepare (only with eight cards in their graveyard) > Excruciator upkeep draw if the first Tidebinder has died, and at their end step as a 3/2 flash beater if you hold two and they are tapped low. Wasp: fire freely pre-ETB as an end-step flash flyer for pressure and Curiosity draws, but once an Excruciator has resolved keep one in hand for the Reef beginning-of-combat tap; in the countered world it is a good re-blank on an Excruciator that lost its Tidebinder; never cast it into an active M.O.D.O.K., a Harvester ETB on the stack, or before their Requiting Hex is spent if you need it to stick.
How many to run
Tidebinder: 3 in the 75 and all 3 in the main against this deck (stock 1 main + 2 side is correct for the 75; here bring both sideboard copies in). It is the only card we own that beats a Cavern-protected Spider-Man/Excruciator ETB, the held copy is exposed to Deceit {B}{B}/Gearhulk so two in hand is the real safety, and copies #2/#3 have real jobs (second ETB, Reef mill, Spider-Man copies, ward triggers, Deceit, Harvester). Wasp: keep the stock 2, do not add more; it cannot touch the win condition, dies to Harvester/Day of Black Sun/Requiting Hex, and its one premium job (Reef) needs only one copy in hand. Suggested swap (8 for 8): OUT 4 Requiting Hex (no main-deck creature of theirs has MV 2 or less except an animated Reef at MV 0 or a Spider-Man that copied one of our 1- or 2-drops; postboard Wan Shi Tong is MV 2 on the battlefield), OUT 2 Spell Snare (their MV-2 spells are Bitter Triumph main plus Negate/Strategic Betrayal/Flashfreeze side; evoked Deceit is MV 6 per its ruling, Spider-Man MV 4, the Recall copy MV 1), OUT 2 Wan Shi Tong, Librarian (MV 2 on the battlefield, so it dies to their four Requiting Hex, and its X-draw is a liability after an ETB); IN 2 Tidebinder, 3 Duress (takes Stories, Stock Up, Cover-Up, Day of Black Sun, Requiting Hex, Triumph), 2 Spell Pierce (Recall copy, Stories, Stock Up, Cover-Up, Day of Black Sun), 1 Ghost Vacuum (instant-speed exile of Excruciator or Harvester from their graveyard: kills the Spider-Man plan for one mana and is strictly better here than the one-shot, Duress-able Strategic Betrayal). Strategic Betrayal is the ninth card if you cut a Dream Beavers. Keep both Shoot the Sheriff and both Bitter Triumph: none of Demon, Human Wizard, Spider Human Hero, Elemental Incarnation, Spirit or Villain is an outlaw, and they are our clean answers to the 6/6 and to Emeritus. Leave Flashfreeze (no red/green spells) and Annul (only if M.O.D.O.K./Gearhulk are seen) out.
Review trail: confidence high, 5 disputed, 16 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Brief's rule 'Ward taxes Wasp's trigger and Tidebinder's trigger': rejected for Tidebinder. CR 702.21a defines ward as 'Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter it unless that player pays [cost].' Tidebinder targets an activated or triggered ability on the stack, never the permanent, so Emeritus's ward {2} and Gearhulk's ward {1} do not trigger against it. Wasp does target the creature and is taxed. (Reviewer 1's corollary that the ward trigger itself is a Tidebinder target was accepted and added.)
  • Brief's Spider-Man hint tag lists only 'When you do, exile that card' as its trigger: incomplete. Scryfall ruling 2025-09-19 on Superior Spider-Man (verified): 'Treat Superior Spider-Man as though he were the chosen card entering ... Any "As this creature enters," "This creature enters with," and "When this creature enters" abilities of the chosen card will work', so a Spider-Man copying Doomsday Excruciator also puts the library-exile ETB on the stack (Spider-Man was cast, satisfying 'if it was cast'). That copied trigger is the target; the Mind Swap exile is a decoy in that case.
  • Brief's hint that Emeritus's attack trigger is the interaction point for its card draw: rejected. The draw is Ancestral Recall, a copy of its prepare spell created in exile by the static 'enters prepared' and cast as a spell; Emeritus ruling 2026-03-20 (verified): 'If a prepared creature loses all abilities, it won't stop being prepared, and nothing will happen to its alternative characteristics or to the copy of its prepare spell in exile.' Tidebinder/Wasp blanks do not remove it; the attack trigger only matters for a second Recall, and only with eight cards in their graveyard.
  • Reviewer 2's claim that a Superior Spider-Man copying our Tishana's Tidebinder 'gains flash and can counter our Tidebinder trigger in the Excruciator fight': rejected. The copy is applied as Spider-Man enters (Spider-Man ruling: 'Treat Superior Spider-Man as though he were the chosen card entering'); in hand and on the stack it has only Spider-Man's own characteristics, which include no flash, so it can be cast only at sorcery speed with an empty stack, when there is nothing of ours to counter. Recorded as a trap instead of a target; the 'Ghost Vacuum our own Tidebinder' advice that depended on it was not adopted.
  • Reviewer 3's request to strip quotation marks from the rulings: not needed. Every quoted ruling (Superior Spider-Man 2025-09-19; Emeritus of Ideation 2026-03-20 x4: 'for as long as that permanent is on the battlefield and is prepared', 'As they cast it, that creature stops being prepared', 'loses all abilities, it won't stop being prepared', 'can't become prepared more than once at the same time'; Doomsday Excruciator 2024-09-20 'six or fewer cards remaining'; Deceit 2025-11-17 on evoke ordering and total-cost mana check; Quantum Riddler oracle) was re-fetched from the Scryfall API on 2026-09-11 and matches.
Changes after review
  • how_they_win: removed the false claim that Harvester's discard mode bins Excruciator (its cost is 'Discard this card', it bins Harvester itself); added Bitter Triumph's instant-speed discard cost and M.O.D.O.K. connive as enablers; added Spider-Man-as-Harvester and Spider-Man-as-Emeritus modes.
  • Tidebinder targets: added 'Superior Spider-Man copying something other than Excruciator' (Harvester sweep, Deceit modes via total-cost mana, Riddler draw, our Drowner, and the Mind Swap-only case), with the Spider-Man-as-Emeritus prepared/Recall caveat.
  • Tidebinder targets: added the ward trigger of Emeritus/Gearhulk as a target when our removal, Kaito -2, Drowner or Wasp targets them, with honest mana math.
