  • Tidebinder targets: added Excruciator's upkeep draw as a target in the countered-ETB world only; dont_bother now scopes the 'never counter the upkeep draw' rule to the resolved-ETB world.
  • Tidebinder Emeritus target: demoted to situational with the eight-cards-in-graveyard check; fixed the Recall-copy claim (after they cast it nothing is in exile; killing Emeritus removes the copy only while it is still prepared).
  • Tidebinder Excruciator risk: We Say Thee Nay! now described as a soft counter ({2}/{4} tax) that also needs non-Cavern mana; added the exposure of the held Tidebinder to Deceit {B}{B}/Gearhulk.
  • Tidebinder Deceit target: bounce-on-Kaito exception tightened (tempo only); added the own-Excruciator/own-Emeritus re-entry cases.
  • Cavern trap: Spell Pierce removed from the creature-spell sentence (it only targets noncreature spells). Sorceries trap: Negate removed from our counters (it is in their sideboard); listed our actual counters and why Flashfreeze/Annul do not apply.
  • Riddler: full conditional quote 'As long as you have one or fewer cards in hand ...' in target and Wasp entry; warp trap softened to 'permanent 4/6 body', flagging that the delayed trigger's source question is unsettled.
  • Added trap: Spider-Man copying our Tidebinder is not a flash counter (copy applies on entering).
  • dont_bother: added Wan Shi Tong's search trigger, Spider-Man-as-Revenant, and noted the discarded Harvester as Spider-Man fuel.
  • Wasp: added countered-ETB Excruciator re-blank as priority 2 and split it from the resolved-ETB survival case; Qarsi Revenant after_effect corrected (counters stay, their keywords are suppressed while Wasp lives); Spider-Man copy entry corrected (Curiosity reaches our graveyard only via Day of Black Sun X≥4 or hand discard; listed Harvester/Emeritus/Drowner copies); Emeritus entry updated for the prepared/uncast-copy distinction and the ward-trigger alternative.
  • combined_lines: added the public graveyard/land-count check; replaced the Strategic Betrayal line with Ghost Vacuum; made the post-ETB Drowner shuffle a judgment call (it removes their self-decking upkeep draw) and moved the unconditional shuffle to the countered-ETB line; added the end-step Curiosity-vs-Tidebinder mana note; added Wasp as a teamwork tapper.
  • hold_or_fire: rewritten from an unconditional reservation to a conditional one (Excruciator in graveyard + 4 lands, six lands, or instant-speed Triumph/Sewers bin), with the priority order for spare copies updated to include Spider-Man copies, ward triggers and the countered-world upkeep draw.
  • counts_recommendation: fixed 7-out/8-in arithmetic to 8-for-8 (OUT 4 Hex, 2 Snare, 2 Wan Shi Tong; IN 2 Tidebinder, 3 Duress, 2 Spell Pierce, 1 Ghost Vacuum); corrected the MV-2 spell list (Negate, Flashfreeze side) and the Hex-target list (Spider-Man copying our 1-2 drops; Wan Shi Tong MV 2 on battlefield); Ghost Vacuum preferred over Strategic Betrayal; added Villain to the non-outlaw list.
  • disputed: added Reviewer 2's flash-Spider-Man claim and Reviewer 3's unverifiable-rulings point (all rulings re-fetched from Scryfall and confirmed).
vs. Mardu Discard4.7% TB: premiumWasp: premium▼
A red-black-white aggro deck whose whole engine is discarding cards for value: Marauding Mako grows ("Whenever you discard one or more cards, put that many +1/+1 counters on this creature"), Inti draws off every discard ("Whenever you discard one or more cards, exile the top card of your library. You may play that card until your next end step"), and Cool but Rude turns discards into burn at level 2 ("Whenever you discard a card, this Class deals 2 damage to each opponent"). Outlets: Iron-Shield Elf and Hardened Academic "Discard a card:" abilities (free, instant speed), Inti's and Cool but Rude's "Whenever you attack" triggers, Tersa's ETB loot, and Casey Jones's next-upkeep random discard of three (created by his ETB). Bloodghast and Moonshadow are recursive/growing 1-2 drops, and the kill is Practiced Offense (3 copies plus flashback) giving a fat Mako double strike on turn 4-5. Removal is light: 2 Requiting Hex (MV 2 or less) and 2 Erode main; Shoot the Sheriff, Slagstorm and Strategic Betrayal in the side (Deathmark is also there but only hits green or white creatures, so it is dead against us). 27 of 38 nonland cards are creatures, every one of them an ETB/trigger/activated-ability engine, and nothing in the 75 has hexproof or ward. Cool but Rude is the one engine neither of our cards can blank (enchantment) and nothing in our 75 removes it once it resolves, so it must be answered on the stack (Spell Snare, Annul, Flashfreeze, Spell Pierce).
Tishana's Tidebinder
Almost every creature they play is a creature whose value is a triggered ability, nothing in the 75 has hexproof or ward, and their main-deck removal barely touches Tidebinder (Requiting Hex only hits MV 2 or less; only 2 Erode can kill it main). Frame it as the answer to what our 4 Requiting Hex cannot kill: Casey Jones (only Tidebinder stops the draw three and the upkeep discard it creates), Monument to Endurance (artifact, fully blanked, post-board), Carnage's reanimation, Tersa's loot-plus-haste turn. Against Hex-sized creatures (Inti, Mako, Moonshadow, Elf, Academic) it is still fine, but the pattern there is opportunistic: counter whichever discard-payoff trigger is on the stack at their declare attackers and ambush-block with the fresh 3/2, rather than holding three mana for turns. Caveat: Voice of Victory's static stops you from casting Tidebinder on their turn at all until Voice is dead or blanked.
#1Casey Jones, Vigilante (ETB)
"When Casey Jones enters, draw three cards. At the beginning of your next upkeep, discard three cards at random."
WhyOnly Tidebinder stops this: Shoot the Sheriff in response kills the 4/3 but the draw three still resolves and the upkeep discard still happens. Countering the ETB denies the draw three AND, because the delayed upkeep discard is created only when this ETB resolves, deletes their best discard turn (three discards = +3 Mako counters, 6 Cool but Rude damage at level 2, three Monument triggers, an Inti impulse card, Moonshadow counters off discarded permanents). It is card-neutral for them in raw count (draw 3, discard 3), so its value scales with payoffs on board: with Mako, a level-2 Class, Inti or Monument out it is the biggest swing in the matchup; with an empty payoff board it is still worth it (denies digging three deep for Offense/Class and three cards of graveyard fuel for Tersa, Carnage and Academic), but Shoot the Sheriff on the 4/3 is a fair alternative when you hold both.
WhenTheir main phase, turn 3+, with the ETB trigger on the stack and (usually) their mana tapped out. First reachable window: their T3 on the play; on the draw you have only 2 lands during their T3, so the ETB is out of reach and the upkeep window (target 2) is the Casey window. Not castable while Voice of Victory's static is live.
AfterCasey loses his only ability (already spent); keeps the 4/3 body. No delayed trigger ever exists, so no upkeep discard.
RiskThey can respond to Tidebinder's trigger by discarding to Iron-Shield Elf or Hardened Academic to squeeze one discard payoff, but the draw three still never happens.
#2Casey Jones, Vigilante (upkeep discard, second window)
The delayed trigger "At the beginning of your next upkeep, discard three cards at random" created by his ETB.
WhyLegal (a delayed triggered ability is a triggered ability) and it denies all three discard payoffs: +3 Mako counters, 6 Class damage at level 2, three Monument triggers, an Inti card. The cost is that they keep the three cards, so take it only when a payoff is on board (Mako, level-2 Class, Inti, Monument) or when you need the 3/2 blocker before their combat; skip it when no payoff is out and they would otherwise be discarding a flooded hand. On the draw this is the only Casey window you can reach (their T4 upkeep with your third land untapped).
WhenTheir upkeep with the discard trigger on the stack. Tidebinder is then an untapped blocker for their combat that turn.
AfterThe trigger is countered; Casey (its source, a creature) loses all abilities but has none left anyway. Their hand stays full.
RiskA 7-card hand for an aggro deck is real; only do this when the payoffs on board make the discards worse than the cards.
#3Monument to Endurance (sideboard, 2)
"Whenever you discard a card, choose one that hasn't been chosen this turn: Draw a card. / Create a Treasure token. / Each opponent loses 3 life."
WhyIt is an artifact, so countering one trigger blanks the whole Monument for as long as Tidebinder lives, and nothing else in our 75 removes it after it resolves (Annul only on the stack). Against us it is card advantage plus 3 life per discard. When it is on the table, treat it as priority 1.
WhenThe FIRST single-card discard after it lands: an Elf/Academic activation, the Inti or Cool but Rude attack loot. Avoid waiting for a Casey/Tersa mass discard: each discarded card is a separate trigger, and triggered abilities already on the stack exist independently of their source (CR 113.7a), so Tidebinder counters exactly one and the other two or three still resolve even though Monument is now blanked. Only future triggers are stopped.
AfterLoses everything going forward; Treasures already made, cards already drawn and Monument triggers already on the stack are unaffected.
RiskAnnul from our side is the cheaper answer if you see it coming; Tidebinder is the answer once it has resolved.
#4Tersa Lightshatter
"When Tersa Lightshatter enters, discard up to two cards, then draw that many cards." (also strips "Haste" and "Whenever Tersa Lightshatter attacks, if there are seven or more cards in your graveyard, exile a card at random from your graveyard. You may play that card this turn.")
WhyCountering the ETB denies two discards (no Mako counters, no Inti card, no Class damage, no Moonshadow counters), two draws, and because Tersa loses haste while summoning sick she cannot attack the turn she is cast. Her graveyard-recursion attack trigger is gone for as long as Tidebinder lives. Alternative when you hold Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph instead: kill her in response to the ETB (Orc Wizard, non-outlaw); the loot resolves but the body is gone. With payoffs on board Tidebinder is better; with none, the kill spell is.
WhenTheir precombat main phase with the ETB on the stack. Do it precombat (which is when she is always cast) so the lost haste costs them the attack.
AfterLoses haste, the ETB, and the attack trigger; keeps a 3/3 body. Cards already in their graveyard are unaffected.
RiskA 3/3 body still attacks from next turn on; you need a blocker or removal for it eventually.
#5Marauding Mako (growth trigger, at declare attackers or on a mass discard)
"Whenever you discard one or more cards, put that many +1/+1 counters on this creature."
WhyThis is the default Tidebinder in the matchup when no Casey/Tersa/Monument window has come: at their declare attackers, after the Inti or Cool but Rude attack loot discards, Mako's growth trigger is on the stack. Counter it and Mako is blanked (their win condition stops growing for as long as Tidebinder lives, existing counters stay) while the fresh untapped 3/2 blocks that same combat. Best when the trigger is for 2-3 counters at once (Tersa ETB, Casey upkeep, Cool but Rude level-3 random discard). Requiting Hex also kills Mako (MV 1) and Wasp blanks it for 2, so spend Tidebinder here only when those are not in hand or you want the body too.
WhenTheir declare attackers step with the growth trigger on the stack (Inti's and Cool but Rude's attack triggers will be there too; the discard happens on resolution of those, THEN Mako triggers). Or their main phase on the Tersa/Casey/level-3 discard.
AfterLoses the growth trigger and cycling text; keeps P/T including every counter already on it. Counters from the countered trigger are never placed.
RiskIf they respond by discarding to Elf/Academic, a new Mako trigger goes on the stack above Tidebinder's and resolves first. Accept it; Mako is blanked afterward.
#6Inti, Seneschal of the Sun
"Whenever you attack, you may discard a card. When you do, put a +1/+1 counter on target attacking creature. It gains trample until end of turn." Counter the FIRST Inti trigger you see; if it is instead the discard trigger ("Whenever you discard one or more cards, exile the top card of your library. You may play that card until your next end step."), counter that one.
WhyInti is the card-advantage engine: every discard event becomes an extra card. Countering either trigger blanks Inti for as long as Tidebinder lives, so no more impulse cards and no more attack pumps. Countering the attack trigger before they discard also denies Inti's own discard and pump that turn; they can still discard to Cool but Rude's attack loot or an Elf/Academic activation, so it is not a full stop on that turn's discards. Requiting Hex (MV 2) kills Inti for 1 mana at instant speed, so this is a Tidebinder job mainly when Hex is not in hand or you want the 3/2 blocker.
WhenTheir declare attackers step with "Whenever you attack" on the stack (Cool but Rude's attack trigger will be there too; target Inti's). Tidebinder then blocks this combat as a fresh untapped 3/2. Alternatively their main phase with the exile-top-card trigger on the stack after an Elf/Academic/Tersa discard.
AfterLoses both triggers; keeps the 2/2 body. Cards Inti already exiled stay playable until their next end step, and counters already placed stay.
RiskIf they respond to Tidebinder's trigger by discarding to Elf or Academic, Inti's discard trigger goes on the stack above Tidebinder's trigger and resolves first, netting them one last card. Accept it.
#7Carnage, Crimson Chaos
"When Carnage enters, return target creature card with mana value 3 or less from your graveyard to the battlefield. It gains 'This creature attacks each combat if able' and 'When this creature deals combat damage to a player, sacrifice it.'"
WhyDenies a free reanimated Mako/Tersa/Inti and strips Trample from a 4/3. MV 4, so Hex cannot touch it; Sheriff kills Carnage (Symbiote Villain, non-outlaw) but the ETB resolves anyway. Only one copy in the deck.
WhenTheir main phase with the ETB on the stack, whether cast normally or via Mayhem from the graveyard (Mayhem is a way of casting and cannot be countered by Tidebinder; the ETB can).
AfterLoses Trample and the ETB; keeps 4/3. Nothing is returned.
RiskLow frequency (1 copy); do not hold Tidebinder specifically for it.
#8Cool but Rude (level 3 trigger)
"When this Class becomes level 3, search your library for a card, put it into your hand, shuffle, then discard a card at random."
WhyDenies a tutor (usually Practiced Offense or a fourth Mako) and the random discard that would feed every payoff. Countering the trigger is better than countering the "{1}{R}: Level 3" activation: the Class is stuck at level 3 with the tutor spent forever, whereas a countered activation can just be paid again next turn.
WhenTheir main phase after the "{1}{R}: Level 3" activation has resolved and the level-3 trigger is on the stack.
AfterNothing is blanked (Cool but Rude is an enchantment). Level 2's "Whenever you discard a card, this Class deals 2 damage to each opponent" keeps working.
RiskThis is a one-shot counter, not a shutdown; only take it when no creature engine is on the stack.
#9Moonshadow
"Whenever one or more permanent cards are put into your graveyard from anywhere while this creature has a -1/-1 counter on it, remove a -1/-1 counter from this creature."
WhyThe counters stay, so a blanked Moonshadow is frozen at whatever size it has reached and also loses Menace. Mostly a Requiting Hex (MV 1) or Wasp job; use Tidebinder only when the trigger happens to be on the stack, nothing better is around, and you want the body.
WhenTheir turn, with the counter-removal trigger on the stack (a discarded creature/land card or a creature dying). Better the earlier it is: a Moonshadow with five or six counters is the ideal freeze.
AfterLoses Menace and the growth trigger; keeps its -1/-1 counters (stuck at current size). When Tidebinder dies it resumes growing but never gets the missed counters back.
Risk"This creature enters with six -1/-1 counters on it" is a replacement effect and is not a target; only the removal trigger is.
#10Iron-Shield Elf (only paired with our removal, or in a race)
"Discard a card: This creature gains indestructible until end of turn. Tap it."
WhyThe discard is a cost, already paid, so all their discard payoffs already triggered; countering it alone is poor. It becomes good as a kill: we target Elf with Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff / Requiting Hex, they discard for indestructible, Tidebinder counters it, Elf dies and their discarded card is gone. Countering also denies the self-tap, so Elf stays untapped (irrelevant when it is dying).
WhenTheir activation on the stack in response to our removal (either turn). Our removal must still be on the stack below it.
AfterLoses the outlet; keeps 3/1. Indestructible granted by an earlier activation this turn is also removed (Tidebinder's effect has the later timestamp).
RiskCosts 5+ mana total for one 2-drop; only take it when the game is about Elf being the last blocker/attacker.
#11Hardened Academic
"Whenever one or more cards leave your graveyard, put a +1/+1 counter on target creature you control." Also its "Discard a card: This creature gains lifelink until end of turn" activation when the lifelink decides a race.
WhyBlanking Academic removes Flying, haste, the free discard outlet and the grow trigger. Wasp does the same job for 2 mana and Hex kills it for 1, so use Tidebinder here only when the trigger happens to be on the stack and nothing better is around. Exception: in a tight race, countering the lifelink activation (discard already paid) denies the life swing and blanks the flyer at once.
WhenTheir turn when Bloodghast returns, Tersa exiles a card, Practiced Offense is flashed back, or Carnage/Mayhem leaves the graveyard, with the counter trigger on the stack. Or their lifelink activation during a racing attack.
AfterLoses Flying, haste, the outlet, the trigger; keeps 2/1 and any counters.
RiskLow; just usually a Wasp or Hex job.
#12Cool but Rude (level 2 activation)
"{1}{R}: Level 2"
WhyLevel-up is an activated ability and can be countered: they lose {1}{R} and the sorcery-speed window, but they can activate it again next turn and the Class is never blanked (enchantment). Pure tempo; take it only when they tap low on a turn you had nothing else to counter and Tidebinder wants to be a body.
WhenTheir main phase with the level-up on the stack.
AfterNothing lost; Class stays level 1.
RiskWeakest real target; two mana of theirs for three of ours.
Traps
Voice of Victory (sideboard, 3): Flash Tidebinder in on their turn to counter the Mobilize trigger or 'turn off' the can't-cast clause. "Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn" is a static ability: it cannot be countered, and it means you cannot even cast Tidebinder (or Wasp, or any instant) during their turn while it is live. Deal with Voice on OUR turn: Requiting Hex (MV 2), Shoot the Sheriff (Human Bard, non-outlaw) or Bitter Triumph kill it; Wasp blanks it but can be killed in response. Then Tidebinder is live again on their turn. Mobilize is a trigger but it fires on their turn, where you cannot cast.
Cool but Rude: Counter a Class trigger and the Class loses all abilities. It is an enchantment, not an artifact, creature or planeswalker. Every counter against it (level-ups, attack loot, 2-damage pings, the level-3 tutor) is a one-shot; the Class keeps working afterward. The real answer is on the stack: Spell Snare (MV 2), Annul (enchantment spell), Flashfreeze (red) or Spell Pierce.
Practiced Offense: Counter the double strike / the flashback with Tidebinder. It is a spell; flashback is a way of casting, not an ability. Only a counterspell (Spell Pierce, We Say Thee Nay!, both 'unless its controller pays' taxes they rarely pay on curve) stops it. Wasp's tap is the Tidebinder-deck answer (see Wasp).
Carnage, Crimson Chaos (Mayhem): Counter the Mayhem cast from the graveyard. Mayhem is casting, not an ability. Only the ETB trigger after it resolves is a target.
Creature returned by Carnage, Crimson Chaos: Counter its granted "When this creature deals combat damage to a player, sacrifice it" trigger, or blank it by countering another of its triggers. Countering the sacrifice trigger keeps THEIR creature alive. And countering any other trigger of that creature (a returned Mako's growth) makes it lose all abilities, including the granted 'attacks each combat if able' and the sacrifice drawback (both granted earlier, so removed by Tidebinder's later timestamp). Either way you help them. Leave the reanimated creature alone; counter Carnage's ETB instead so nothing comes back.
Bloodghast: Counter the Landfall return and blank Bloodghast. "Landfall: Whenever a land you control enters, you may return this card from your graveyard to the battlefield" is counterable, but the source is a card in the graveyard, not a permanent (ruling 3): nothing loses abilities and it simply triggers again on their next land. "This creature can't block" and the conditional haste are static. Three mana to delay a 2/1 one turn is not worth it.
Marauding Mako (cycling): Counter the cycling to stop the discard. "Cycling {2} ({2}, Discard this card: Draw a card.)" has the discard as a cost, already paid when you get to respond, so every discard payoff has already triggered; you only deny one drawn card, and the Mako in the graveyard is not a permanent so nothing is blanked. The on-battlefield growth trigger is the real target (target 5).
Iron-Shield Elf / Hardened Academic activations: Counter the 'Discard a card:' activation to stop the discard. The discard is the cost; it is paid before the ability goes on the stack, so Mako, Inti, Cool but Rude and Monument have already triggered. Countering Elf's denies indestructible and the self-tap (Elf stays untapped); countering Academic's denies lifelink; plus the blank. Worth it only to make our removal stick (target 10) or when the lifelink decides a race (target 11).
Inti, Seneschal of the Sun (reflexive trigger): Wait to counter the 'When you do, put a +1/+1 counter...' part. By the time the reflexive trigger exists they have already discarded and every payoff has triggered. Counter the parent "Whenever you attack" trigger before it resolves. (If you missed it, countering the reflexive trigger still blanks Inti, since it is still Inti's ability, but you ate the discard.)
Monument to Endurance (mass discard): Hold Tidebinder for a Casey/Tersa multi-card discard to counter 'all' the Monument triggers. Each discarded card is a separate trigger and triggers already on the stack exist independently of their source (CR 113.7a). Tidebinder counters one; the others resolve even though Monument is blanked. Take the first single-card discard instead.
Moonshadow (enters with counters): Counter 'enters with six -1/-1 counters' so... anything. A replacement effect, not a triggered ability; cannot be targeted (and stopping it would give them a 7/7 anyway).
Requiting Hex / Requisition Raid (blight, spree): Counter the blight -1/-1 counter or a spree mode. Additional costs of casting a spell, not abilities.
Godless Shrine / Blood Crypt / Sacred Foundry / Starting Town: Counter the 'pay 2 life or enters tapped' or the 'pay 1 life: add mana'. Shock-land text is a replacement effect ('As this land enters'); Starting Town's abilities are mana abilities and cannot be targeted.
Not worth it
Bloodghast's Landfall return: legal but blanks nothing and recurs next land (ruling 3).Marauding Mako's cycling: discard already paid, card in graveyard cannot be blanked.Cool but Rude level 1 loot ("Whenever you attack, you may discard a card. If you do, draw a card"): one-shot, enchantment never blanks; only take it if no Inti/Mako/Casey/Tersa/Monument trigger will show up and the single discard would deal lethal Class damage.Cool but Rude level 2 damage trigger: stops exactly 2 damage once.Tersa's attack trigger ("if there are seven or more cards in your graveyard, exile a card at random"): fine if it is what is on the stack, but far worse than her ETB; do not hold for it.Voice of Victory's Mobilize: unreachable, since you cannot cast spells on their turn while Voice lives.Casey Jones's ETB when Shoot the Sheriff is the alternative and no payoff is on board: Sheriff kills the 4/3 for 2 mana, the loot they get is card-neutral; Tidebinder is only clearly better once Mako/Class/Inti/Monument are out.Concealed Courtyard, Inspiring Vantage, Erode, Slagstorm, Duress, Deathmark, Strategic Betrayal, Requisition Raid: no targetable non-mana abilities.
The Wondrous Wasp
Their creatures are cheap bodies whose whole value is an ability (Mako's growth, Academic's flying/outlet, Moonshadow's counter removal, Inti's engine, Elf's free indestructible) and none of them has hexproof or ward, so a 2-mana flash blank hits every turn. The tap matters because a creature tapped at their beginning of combat cannot be declared as an attacker, and their only pump that is not sorcery-speed (Inti's attack trigger) can only target an attacking creature: Wasp at their beginning of combat on the Practiced Offense target wastes the double strike outright (the team-wide +1/+1 counters still happen). Post-board Wasp can also blank Voice of Victory's can't-cast static on our turn, but it can be killed in response, so prefer Hex/Sheriff/Bitter Triumph on Voice when you have them. Wasp dies to Requiting Hex (MV 2) and Erode, which is the main tax.
#1Marauding Mako
WhyThe main win condition. Blanking removes "Whenever you discard one or more cards, put that many +1/+1 counters on this creature" so it stops growing (existing counters stay: Wasp freezes, does not shrink). Tapping at their beginning of combat also eats an attack, which is the answer to Practiced Offense: a tapped creature cannot be declared as an attacker, so the double strike is wasted.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, after they have cast their precombat spells (including Practiced Offense, which is a sorcery and so is always before this window) and before attackers are declared. Wasp resolving after Offense has the later timestamp and also strips the granted double strike. Our end step is the fallback for pure blank value (Mako untaps on their turn, so no tap value) and is only preferred when Voice of Victory forbids casting on their turn.
AfterLoses the growth trigger and cycling text; keeps P/T including every counter already on it. Any pump granted AFTER Wasp resolved (a later Practiced Offense) is kept (ruling 2), so Wasp does not make future Offenses fizzle, it only makes the current attack not happen.
#2Hardened Academic
WhyTheir only flyer and a free repeatable outlet: "Flying, haste", "Discard a card: This creature gains lifelink until end of turn", "Whenever one or more cards leave your graveyard, put a +1/+1 counter on target creature you control." Blanked it is a ground 2/1 that cannot block Wasp, Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers, cannot race with lifelink, and cannot feed discards for free. With Enduring Curiosity out, a blanked Academic means Wasp connects every turn for a card.
WhenTheir beginning of combat (deny the haste attack the turn it is cast if it was cast precombat; deny a flying attack any later turn), or our end step for the blank alone.
AfterLoses flying, haste, the outlet, the trigger; keeps 2/1 and counters.
#3Voice of Victory (sideboard)
Why"Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn" shuts off Tidebinder, Wasp, Bitter Triumph, Requiting Hex and every counterspell on their turn. Wasp on OUR turn blanks the static (and Mobilize 2), reopening their turn for the whole flash plan. But it happens on our turn with their mana untapped: if they Requiting Hex or Erode Wasp in response to its trigger, Voice is tapped but never blanked (Wasp has already left, so the 'as long as Wasp remains' duration has ended before the effect would begin) and the lock holds all turn. So kill Voice first when you can: Requiting Hex (MV 2), Shoot the Sheriff (Human Bard, non-outlaw), Bitter Triumph. Wasp it when no removal is in hand, ideally after baiting their instant with a lesser spell.
WhenOur turn only: main phase or our end step (you cannot cast anything on theirs). Do it the turn it lands. If they kill Wasp on their turn, the static is back immediately for the rest of that turn.
AfterLoses the can't-cast static and Mobilize; keeps a 1/3 body. Warrior tokens already created are unaffected.
#4Inti, Seneschal of the Sun
WhyWhen Tidebinder or Hex is not available, Wasp shuts off both Inti triggers: no impulse card off every discard, no attack counter/trample. The blank is what stops the triggers ("Whenever you attack" fires whenever they attack with anything, so Inti itself need not attack); the tap additionally removes its own 2-power attack.
WhenTheir beginning of combat, or our end step if you just want the engine off before their next turn.
AfterLoses both triggers; keeps 2/2. Cards already exiled by Inti remain playable until their next end step.
#5Moonshadow
WhyFreezes it at its current size and removes Menace: "Whenever one or more permanent cards are put into your graveyard... remove a -1/-1 counter" is gone for as long as Wasp lives. A 1-drop that would be a 7/7 menace stays small and blockable by Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers. Requiting Hex (MV 1) kills it outright; Wasp is for when Hex is spoken for.
WhenAny time; best the turn before it would reach 5+ power. Beginning of combat if it is already big enough to matter as an attacker.
AfterLoses Menace and the growth trigger; keeps its -1/-1 counters (stuck at that size).
#6Iron-Shield Elf
WhyRemoves the free "Discard a card: This creature gains indestructible until end of turn. Tap it" so our Requiting Hex, Bitter Triumph and Shoot the Sheriff kill it cleanly (Day of Black Sun does not need this: it strips abilities itself before destroying). If they activate Elf in response to Wasp's trigger, the indestructible was granted BEFORE Wasp's effect and is removed too (layer 6 timestamp order), so the response does not save it from a follow-up kill spell.
WhenOur turn (main phase, before our removal) or their beginning of combat to also stop a 3-power attack.
AfterLoses the outlet (and any indestructible granted earlier); keeps 3/1.
#7Tersa Lightshatter
WhyWasp cannot stop the ETB loot (a trigger already on the stack resolves regardless of its source losing abilities), but Wasp in response to the ETB taps her and removes Haste, so she cannot attack the turn she is cast, and "Whenever Tersa Lightshatter attacks, if there are seven or more cards in your graveyard, exile a card at random from your graveyard. You may play that card this turn" is gone while Wasp lives.
WhenIn response to her ETB in their precombat main, or their beginning of combat on a later turn once their graveyard is at 7+.
AfterLoses haste and the attack trigger; keeps 3/3.
#8Casey Jones, Vigilante (tempo tap only)
WhyNothing to blank (the ETB is already on the stack or resolved, and the upkeep discard is a delayed trigger that exists independently of Casey). But a beginning-of-combat tap on a 4/3 saves 4 damage, more than most 2-drop blanks save, so it is a real play when the race is close and no better target is out.
WhenTheir beginning of combat, when 4 damage is the difference.
AfterTapped for the turn; no abilities lost that matter.
Traps
Any attacker: Flash Wasp after attackers are declared to tap the biggest one. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat; it still deals damage. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step (or earlier) to stop an attack. Set a stop there on Arena/MTGO.
Practiced Offense target: Wasp in response to Practiced Offense strips the double strike. If Wasp resolves before Offense, the double strike is granted AFTER Wasp's effect began and is kept (ruling 2). The tap is the payload: a tapped creature cannot be declared as an attacker. Since Offense is a sorcery it is always cast before their beginning of combat, so just Wasp at beginning of combat: tapped AND the earlier-granted double strike is removed. The +1/+1 counters on the rest of their team still happen.
Bloodghast: Blank Bloodghast. "This creature can't block" is an ability; removing it lets Bloodghast block. You also remove a haste they only have at 10 or less life. It recurs off every land anyway. Never target it.
Casey Jones, Vigilante: Wasp Casey to stop the draw three or the upkeep discard. The ETB is already on the stack when you can respond and resolves regardless; the upkeep discard is a delayed trigger that exists independently of Casey's abilities. Tidebinder is the answer to Casey's abilities; Wasp on Casey is only ever a 4-damage tempo tap (target 8).
Creature returned by Carnage, Crimson Chaos: Blank the reanimated creature. It came back with "This creature attacks each combat if able" and "When this creature deals combat damage to a player, sacrifice it" granted before Wasp's effect; blanking removes those drawbacks and helps them. Blank Carnage itself only for Trample (low value).
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares (ours): Ninjutsu Kaito in by returning the unblocked Wasp. Wasp leaving the battlefield ends the blank immediately ("for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield"). Return Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers instead; same for Tidebinder.
Voice of Victory: Wasp Voice at their beginning of combat like everything else, or Wasp it into open W/B mana on our turn as the default. You cannot cast spells on their turn while Voice's static is live. On our turn, a Hex or Erode in response to Wasp's trigger leaves Voice tapped but unblanked. Kill it with Hex/Sheriff/Bitter Triumph first; Wasp is the backup.
Marauding Mako with counters: Wasp shrinks a big Mako. Counters stay; Wasp freezes its size and stops further growth. A 6/6 Mako is still a 6/6 vanilla. Requiting Hex still kills it (Mako is MV 1; counters do not change mana value), as does Bitter Triumph; Shoot the Sheriff cannot (Shark Pirate is an outlaw).
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Practiced Offense answer: it is a sorcery, so it always resolves in their precombat main before you act. Flash Wasp at their beginning of combat on the creature that got double strike/lifelink. It is tapped (cannot be declared as an attacker) and, because Wasp's effect has the later timestamp, it loses the granted double strike too. The +1/+1 counters on the rest of their team still land. Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay! on the Offense itself (they must pay {2}, which they rarely can on curve) is the cheaper answer when you have it; Tidebinder can never touch the spell or its flashback.
  • Cool but Rude is the one engine our two cards cannot blank and our 75 has no enchantment removal, so the highest-value counter in the matchup is on the Class spell: Spell Snare (MV 2), Annul (enchantment spell), Flashfreeze (red) or Spell Pierce. Hold U open on their T2 for it when you can.
  • Default Tidebinder in a normal game: at their declare attackers, after Inti's / Cool but Rude's attack loot, the discard payoff triggers hit the stack. Counter Mako's growth trigger (blanks their win condition) or Inti's attack trigger (blanks their card engine), then block with the untapped 3/2: it eats a Mako with one counter, trades with Elf, blocks Inti. Do this rather than holding three mana for a Casey that may not come.
  • Iron-Shield Elf kill: cast Bitter Triumph / Requiting Hex / Shoot the Sheriff on Elf; when they respond with "Discard a card: This creature gains indestructible until end of turn. Tap it", flash Tidebinder and counter the activation. Elf dies, the discarded card is gone. Wasp on Elf first (our turn) does the same more cheaply: the indestructible granted in response to Wasp is removed by Wasp's later timestamp. Day of Black Sun needs neither trick: it makes each creature with MV X or less lose all abilities before destroying, so indestructible never matters (but X=2 also kills our Wasp, Drowner, Siren and Beavers; Tidebinder at MV 3 survives).
  • Voice of Victory (post-board): the turn it lands, kill it on OUR turn with Requiting Hex, Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph. Wasp it only when no removal is in hand, and expect a Hex/Erode response that leaves it tapped but unblanked. Only once Voice is gone or blanked is Tidebinder, Bitter Triumph, Spell Snare etc. live on their turn again.
  • Kaito lines: Kaito's "-2: Tap target creature. Put two stun counters on it" handles the biggest Mako for two turns while Wasp blanks the second threat (Academic/Inti). Ninjutsu Kaito by returning Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers, never Wasp or Tidebinder (returning them ends the blank). A blanked Hardened Academic cannot block flyers, so Wasp/Siren connect for the ninjutsu window and for Enduring Curiosity draws.
  • Floodpits Drowner + Wasp: Drowner's ETB ("tap target creature an opponent controls and put a stun counter on it") must resolve at their beginning of combat, before attackers are declared, to stop that attack; cast after attackers are declared the creature still deals damage and the stun counter only skips its next untap. Wasp on Mako stops it growing during the stun window, then Drowner's "{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries" (instant speed, Drowner has vigilance so it stays untapped after attacking) removes the stunned creature into their library. Drowner is a Merfolk like Tidebinder; neither is a Pirate/outlaw, so their sideboard Shoot the Sheriff kills both.
  • Removal map so Wasp/Tidebinder are held for the right things: Requiting Hex kills Mako, Inti, Elf, Academic, Moonshadow, Bloodghast (it comes back), Voice; it cannot touch Casey, Tersa, Carnage. Shoot the Sheriff CANNOT target Marauding Mako (Shark Pirate is an outlaw) but kills everything else including Casey, Tersa, Carnage, Voice; Bitter Triumph kills anything. So Tidebinder's unique jobs are Casey's ETB (Sheriff lets the draw resolve), Monument, and Carnage's ETB; when Hex is in hand for Mako, spend Wasp on Academic or Inti instead.
  • Tersa/Casey tempo: on the play, your T3 with three lands untapped is exactly their Tersa/Casey turn; hold Tidebinder for the ETB when payoffs (Mako, Class, Inti) are already out, and block with the 3/2 afterward. On the draw you cannot reach their T3 ETB; Casey's T4 upkeep discard is your window if payoffs are out. Flashfreeze (side) counters any of their red spells (Casey, Tersa, Inti, Mako, Carnage, Academic, Cool but Rude, Slagstorm); when you have it, counter the creature spell and save Tidebinder for the declare-attackers payoff trigger.
  • Graveyard hate: our Strategic Betrayal (they exile the creature of THEIR choice and their graveyard) and Ghost Vacuum turn off Bloodghast, Tersa's attack recursion, Carnage's ETB target, Mayhem and Practiced Offense flashback; after that, Tidebinder's remaining targets are just the ETBs and the discard payoffs, so fire it more freely as a body.
  • Faebloom Trick (side) is a second beginning-of-combat tap for the Offense target that also leaves two 1/1 fliers; same timing rule as Wasp (before attackers are declared).
Hold or fire
TIDEBINDER: with 3 mana open on their turn, the premium windows are Casey Jones's ETB (when Mako / a level-2 Class / Inti / Monument is on board, this is #1; with no payoff out, Shoot the Sheriff on the body is as good), Monument's first single-card discard trigger (post-board; treat as #1 when it is on the table), Tersa's ETB (unless you hold Sheriff/Triumph and no payoff is out), Carnage's ETB. If none of those is coming this turn, do NOT keep holding: at their declare attackers counter whichever discard-payoff trigger is on the stack (Mako's growth > Inti's attack trigger) and block with the 3/2. Fire it as a plain 3/2 body when (a) their hand is empty and no Inti/Monument is on board, (b) you need the blocker to survive, or (c) the block kills an attacker; never on our own main phase for tempo. On the play your first window is their T3, which is exactly their Tersa/Casey turn. On the draw the first window is their T4 (their upkeep, if Casey landed T3 and payoffs are out; otherwise their attack). If Voice of Victory is out and unblanked you cannot cast it on their turn at all: kill Voice on our turn first, or cast Tidebinder on our turn as a body if you must. WASP: their beginning of combat, every time, on the creature that would attack for the most (after a Practiced Offense that is the double-striker; on an Offense-free turn it is the biggest Mako or the haste Academic), always before attackers are declared. There is nothing to hold Wasp for: Offense is a sorcery, so any Offense they cast that turn has already resolved by beginning of combat. Our end step (blank only, no tap) is the fallback only when Voice of Victory forbids casting on their turn. Expect a Requiting Hex/Erode response when they have W or B open: the tap still happens, the blank does not. Post-board, the moment Voice of Victory resolves, remove it on our turn (Hex/Sheriff/Triumph first, Wasp as backup); nothing else matters until it is off. Never ninjutsu Wasp or Tidebinder back to hand while their blank is doing work.
How many to run
Game 1 the stock 1 Tidebinder + 2 Wasp is right. Post-board: the MTGGoldfish stock sideboard (Duress 3, Day of Black Sun 2, Annul 1, Ghost Vacuum 1, Spell Pierce 2, Strategic Betrayal 1, Vren 1, Flashfreeze 2, Faebloom Trick 2) carries zero Tidebinders; the Karsten Sept 3 aggregate carries 2. If your 75 has them, bring both in for 3 total and keep both Wasps: every copy has live targets (2 Casey, 2 Tersa, 2 Inti, 4 Mako, 1 Carnage, plus Monument/Voice if they board them), nothing has hexproof/ward, and their removal barely taxes it (Requiting Hex cannot target MV 3 Tidebinder; only 2 Erode main, 1 Shoot the Sheriff and 1 Slagstorm in the side; Deathmark is dead against blue creatures). If your sideboard lacks them, this matchup is the argument for adding 2 Tidebinder over Duress. Also bring Flashfreeze x2 (their engine is red), Annul regardless of Monument (4 Cool but Rude plus up to 2 Monument = 6 live targets, and nothing in our 75 removes a resolved Class), Strategic Betrayal / Ghost Vacuum (Bloodghast, Tersa, Mayhem, flashback), Day of Black Sun (X=2 wipes their board minus Casey/Tersa/Carnage; it also kills our Wasp, so cast it before deploying Wasp, and Tidebinder survives), and keep Spell Pierce (Class, Offense x3 plus flashback, Hex, Erode are all targets). Leave Duress out (27 of 38 nonland cards are creatures). Cut the slowest top-end (Wan Shi Tong, Librarian is the usual candidate) and trim We Say Thee Nay! if they show Voice of Victory, since counters are dead on their turn under Voice.
Review trail: confidence high, 10 disputed, 23 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Prior refuter: 'Wasp in response to Practiced Offense strips the double strike.' Rejected: Wasp's loses-all-abilities effect starts when Wasp's trigger resolves; Offense then grants double strike with a later timestamp, and Tidebinder/Wasp ruling 2 (abilities gained after the effect begins are kept) applies. The tap is what stops the attack; Wasp at beginning of combat after Offense resolved is the version that strips it.
  • Prior refuter: 'Countering a Cool but Rude trigger blanks the Class.' Rejected: Tidebinder's oracle text limits the blank to 'an artifact, creature, or planeswalker'; Cool but Rude is an Enchantment (Class).
  • Prior refuter: 'Countering Bloodghast's Landfall blanks Bloodghast.' Rejected: ruling 3 checks the source's type as the trigger resolves; a card in the graveyard is not a permanent, so nothing is lost and it retriggers on the next land.
  • Prior refuter: 'Tidebinder can counter Voice of Victory's can't-cast clause or its Mobilize on their turn.' Rejected: the clause is a static ability (not targetable) and it forbids casting Tidebinder during their turn at all; Mobilize triggers only on their turn.
  • Prior refuter: 'Tapping the attacker with Wasp after attacks stops the damage.' Rejected: tapping does not remove a creature from combat (CR 506.4); Wasp must resolve before declare attackers.
  • Prior refuter: 'Countering Casey's ETB still leaves the upkeep discard.' Rejected: the delayed trigger is created by the resolution of the ETB ('...draw three cards. At the beginning of your next upkeep...' is one ability); a countered ETB never creates it.
  • Prior refuter: 'Countering Iron-Shield Elf's activation stops the discard.' Rejected: 'Discard a card:' is a cost, paid on activation before anyone can respond; only the indestructible and the self-tap are denied.
  • Reviewer 2 (strategy): 'With no payoff out, countering Casey's ETB is close to a blank.' Partially rejected: accepted that the ETB is card-neutral in count and that its value scales with payoffs on board (ranking made conditional, Sheriff-on-the-body listed as the alternative), but not 'close to a blank': the ETB text lets them see three cards deeper for Practiced Offense/Class and puts three cards in a graveyard that fuels Tersa's attack trigger ('seven or more cards in your graveyard'), Carnage's ETB target and Hardened Academic's leave-graveyard trigger, and the random discard can be bad for them. Kept Casey's ETB as priority 1 with the condition stated.
  • Reviewer 2 (strategy): 'For Tersa, prefer Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph in response to the ETB.' Partially rejected: Sheriff in response lets 'discard up to two cards, then draw that many cards' resolve, which with Mako / level-2 Class / Inti / Moonshadow on board is two Mako counters, 4 damage, an impulse card and Moonshadow counters; Tidebinder denies all of that plus the haste attack. Made it conditional (Tidebinder with payoffs out, kill spell without) instead of demoting Tersa.
  • Reviewer 2 (strategy): 'Drop the Spell Pierce line from combined_lines or keep Pierce.' Resolved by keeping Pierce: Cool but Rude, 3 Practiced Offense plus flashback, 2 Requiting Hex and 2 Erode are all noncreature spells, so Pierce has 11+ targets in their 60 and the Pierce line stays.
Changes after review
  • Monument to Endurance: removed the false claim 'countering any one blanks the rest'; now states that triggers already on the stack resolve independently of their source (CR 113.7a), Tidebinder counters exactly one, and the best window is the first single-card discard. Added a matching trap entry.
  • Casey Jones ETB: dropped the '3-for-1' language; stated it is card-neutral in count and its value scales with payoffs on board; added the Shoot the Sheriff-on-the-body alternative and the on-the-draw note (2 lands during their T3, so the ETB is unreachable).
  • Casey Jones upkeep discard: moved from traps to a target (priority 2) as a legitimate second window when Mako / level-2 Class / Inti / Monument is on board or a blocker is needed; explicitly the only Casey window on the draw.
  • Added Marauding Mako's growth trigger as a Tidebinder target (priority 5) and made 'counter the payoff trigger at declare attackers and block' the default Tidebinder line in hold_or_fire and combined_lines.
  • Re-ranked Tidebinder targets around what Requiting Hex cannot kill (Casey, Monument, Tersa, Carnage) and demoted Inti/Moonshadow/Elf/Academic to Hex-or-Wasp jobs with Tidebinder as the opportunistic option.
  • Tidebinder hold-or-fire: removed the too-restrictive rule; added the declare-attackers default and fire condition (c) 'the block kills an attacker'.
  • Inti (both cards): corrected 'denies that turn's discard entirely' to 'denies Inti's own discard and pump; Cool but Rude's attack loot and Elf/Academic activations still allow discards'; fixed the Wasp phrasing so the blank, not the tap, is what stops the 'Whenever you attack' trigger.
  • Wasp hold-or-fire: removed the self-contradictory 'hold for Offense' instruction; Offense is a sorcery so Wasp at their beginning of combat is always after it; stated our end step is only for Voice of Victory turns; noted the team-wide +1/+1 counters still happen.
  • Voice of Victory: added that Wasp cast on our turn into open W/B mana can be killed in response (tap happens, no blank, since Wasp's duration ended before the effect would begin); made Requiting Hex / Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph the first choice and Wasp the backup; listed all three removal spells.
  • Added Casey Jones as a Wasp tempo-tap option (priority 8) instead of a pure trap.
  • Added Tidebinder trap for the Carnage-returned creature (countering its sacrifice trigger, or blanking it via another trigger, removes drawbacks that were granted earlier).
  • Added Hardened Academic lifelink-activation race case to Tidebinder target 11 and the Elf/Academic trap; added the Elf self-tap denial.
  • Fixed the garbled 'no: MV 1, so Hex does hit it' parenthetical in the Mako Wasp trap.
  • Replaced the wrong Time Ebb analogy on the level-2 activation with a plain description.
  • Deathmark: noted it only destroys green or white creatures and is dead against us.
  • how_they_win: Casey's discard now correctly attributed to the delayed upkeep trigger, not the ETB; added the Cool but Rude 'answer on the stack' note.
  • Wasp summary: 'they only have sorcery-speed pump' corrected to name Inti's attack trigger as the exception (it can only target an attacking creature, so a beginning-of-combat tap is still safe).
  • Verified Day of Black Sun oracle ('Each creature with mana value X or less loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures.'): removed it from the 'needs Elf blanked first' list, noted it ignores indestructible on its own, and added that X=2 kills our Wasp/Drowner/Siren/Beavers while Tidebinder survives.
  • Verified We Say Thee Nay! ('Counter target spell unless its controller pays {2}'): described it and Spell Pierce as tax counters.
  • Floodpits Drowner line: fixed 'sorcery speed' (the shuffle activation has no timing restriction; {1}{U}, {T}), fixed 'for good' (shuffled into their library, not exiled), and added that the ETB must resolve before attackers are declared to stop an attack.
  • Counts: stated that the stock Goldfish sideboard carries zero Tidebinders (Karsten aggregate carries 2) with advice for both cases; Annul now boarded in regardless of Monument (Cool but Rude is a 4-of enchantment); Spell Pierce kept; Duress out; Day of Black Sun sequencing note added.
  • Added Faebloom Trick as a second beginning-of-combat tap and the 'counter Cool but Rude on the stack' combined line.
  • Expanded dont_bother with the no-payoff Casey ETB case and the cards with no targetable abilities.
vs. Boros Dwarves4.1% TB: goodWasp: good▼
Cheap Dwarves plus Equipment. Leyline Axe often starts on the battlefield (turn 0). Kíli the Resourceful (T2) plus two artifacts or legendaries gives them an enduring story (Storied needs three artifacts, legendaries and/or Sagas, and Kíli himself counts), after which their first equip each turn costs {0} and Kíli draws one card per turn off the first Dwarf or Equipment that enters. Dwarven Mauler makes equips onto him {2} cheaper, so Dragonfire Blade goes onto a creature on T2 for {1} or free. The beats come from one creature stacked with Axe (double strike, trample) plus Dragonfire Blade (+2/+2, hexproof from monocolored), Doc Ock's Tentacles (+4/+4) and Lavaspur Boots (+1/+0, haste, ward {1}). Thorin, Mountain-king ({3}{R}, T4) is the payoff: his ETB moves every Equipment onto one creature (often himself, so Boots give him haste and he swings that turn as a roughly 10-power double strike, trample, hexproof-from-mono attacker) and that creature then "deals damage equal to its power to up to one target creature", their only main-deck removal; post-board expect Erode (2) and Chainsaw (3). Dragonfire Blade is the card that beats us: hexproof from monocolored blanks Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Requiting Hex, Floodpits Drowner and The Wondrous Wasp on whoever wears it; only Kaito -2 (a blue-black source), Tidebinder on the equip, Day of Black Sun and Annul-on-cast get around it. Doc Ock's Tentacles' free-attach trigger never fires in this list (highest creature mana value in the 75 is 4: Thorin, Fíli), so Tentacles only moves via an equip activation (paid {5}, {3} onto Mauler, or Kíli's {0}), Thorin's ETB or Raubahn's attack trigger.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Strong for two specific jobs and fine elsewhere. Job one: counter Thorin's ETB when its creature target is Blade-protected (Cavern of Souls stops counterspells on the creature spell, not counters on its ability). Job two: counter a Dragonfire Blade equip activation, which makes the Blade lose all abilities, hexproof included, while Tidebinder lives, reopening every mono-black spell on their best creature. It also blanks Kíli's engine and Chainsaw's ETB post-board, and ward never taxes it because it targets the ability, not the creature. Why not premium: the first Blade equip lands on their T2 before we ever have three mana; Thorin arrives on the turn we want to tap out for Kaito; almost every other target sits on a mana value 2 or less body that Requiting Hex kills for {B} permanently; and the blank ends the moment a 3/2 meets a double striker, Chainsaw or Thorin's damage trigger. Post-board with three copies it plays like a premium card.
#1Thorin, Mountain-king
When Thorin enters, attach any number of target Equipment you control to target creature you control. When one or more Equipment become attached to that creature this way, that creature deals damage equal to its power to up to one target creature.
WhyOne trigger does their whole turn: a mass re-equip at instant speed plus their only main-deck removal, aimed at Enduring Curiosity, Beavers, or a blocker. Tidebinder is the cleanest answer but not the only one: in response to the ETB you can instead kill the creature it targets (Requiting Hex on a mana value 2 or less carrier not wearing the Blade; Shoot the Sheriff or Bitter Triumph on Thorin himself when he is the target, since he has no hexproof yet). With the creature target gone, nothing attaches and the reflexive damage trigger never happens (CR 608.2b: the ability does nothing to or with an illegal target). Save Tidebinder for the case where the target already wears Dragonfire Blade, or when you have no cheap removal in hand.
WhenThorin resolves, his ETB goes on the stack (usually with Kíli's draw trigger and maybe Giott's loot). Flash Tidebinder with the ETB on the stack, target the ETB. Do NOT wait for the reflexive damage trigger: by then the Equipment has already moved. Their T4 is the normal turn. On the play you can hold three open on their T4; on the draw you have exactly four lands on their T4, so holding three for Tidebinder is possible but costs your own T4 play unless it is Enduring Curiosity (flash, castable at their end step if Thorin does not show).
AfterNo Equipment moves, no damage. Thorin stays as a 3/4 that has lost Trample. Anything equipped to him later grants normally (later timestamp). All of it comes back if Tidebinder dies.
RiskIf Kíli's trigger is also on the stack you only counter one; Thorin's is the one. Cavern of Souls does not protect abilities. Ward on the creature Thorin targets is irrelevant to us (we target the ability).
#2Dragonfire Blade (equip activation, usually a RE-equip)
Equip {4}. This ability costs {1} less to activate for each color of the creature it targets.
WhyThe Blade's hexproof from monocolored is static and cannot be countered, but its equip activation can. Countering it makes the Blade (an artifact) lose all abilities while Tidebinder lives: no +2/+2, no hexproof. That reopens Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Requiting Hex, Wasp and Drowner on their best creature. The first Blade equip normally happens on their T2 (Kíli's {0} or Mauler's discount makes it {1}), before we can have three mana, so the realistic window is a later equip: the Blade moving to a new carrier after we Kaito -2, Drowner or kill the old one, or a second Blade being cast and equipped.
WhenTheir main phase, in response to the equip activation (paid, Mauler-discounted, or Kíli's {0}). Costs stay paid; if it was Kíli's free equip, that is used up for the turn. Their T3 is the earliest turn we can do this on the play; their T4 on the draw.
AfterBlade stays attached where it already was and grants nothing. While Tidebinder lives the Blade has no Equip ability at all, so it cannot be equipped again, not even with Kíli's {0} (that is an alternative cost for an equip ability that no longer exists); only Thorin's ETB or Raubahn's attack trigger can move it, and even then it grants nothing. Its abilities, hexproof included, return the moment Tidebinder leaves.
RiskOnce Blade is blanked, kill the creature it was meant to protect in the same turn cycle. Tidebinder dying (Chainsaw, a double striker in combat) switches the Blade back on.
#3Kíli the Resourceful
Whenever another Dwarf or Equipment you control enters, draw a card. This ability triggers only once each turn.
WhyKíli is the engine: the once-per-turn draw plus the static free equip plus Storied. None of the statics can be countered, but countering the draw blanks the whole creature and takes the free equip and Storied with it. Only spend Tidebinder here when Requiting Hex is not in hand (Hex kills Kíli for {B} for good) and the draw is real.
WhenThey cast a Dwarf or Equipment with Kíli out; the draw trigger goes on the stack (once per turn). Respond with Tidebinder targeting the draw. Their own ETBs (Dáin's dig, Giott's loot) may sit alongside it; pick Kíli's.
AfterNo card drawn. Kíli is a 1/2 with no abilities while Tidebinder lives: no draws, no free equips, no Storied. If they already had an enduring story it stays for the rest of the game (it is a player designation), so Fíli's anthem still works; if they had not yet reached three artifacts/legendaries, a blank Kíli cannot grant it, and only Fíli's own Storied could. Kíli still dies to Requiting Hex as normal.
RiskA tempo play, not the only answer. Kíli also dies to Requiting Hex ({B}), Shoot the Sheriff, or gets blanked by Wasp.
#4Chainsaw (sideboard, 3)
When this Equipment enters, it deals 3 damage to up to one target creature. / Whenever one or more creatures die, put a rev counter on this Equipment.
WhyLikely to come in against us: 3 damage kills Tidebinder, Wasp, Spyglass Siren, Dream Beavers and a fresh Enduring Curiosity (4/3). Countering the ETB saves the creature and blanks Chainsaw (artifact): no rev counters, no '+X/+0', no equip while Tidebinder lives. Priority order for what to save: a Tidebinder or Wasp whose blank is holding the game together, or the unblocked flier you need for ninjutsu. Do not spend it to save Enduring Curiosity: Curiosity returns as an enchantment and keeps drawing cards (it only loses the 4/3 body).
WhenChainsaw resolves, ETB on the stack targeting our creature. Flash Tidebinder, counter it. If they cast Chainsaw with no target, let it go; the rev-counter trigger ('Whenever one or more creatures die') is a later window to blank an already-resolved Chainsaw after a trade.
AfterChainsaw sits blank until Tidebinder leaves. Kíli's draw trigger off the Equipment entering still resolves unless that was your target instead.
RiskA second Chainsaw or Thorin's damage kills Tidebinder and wakes the first one up.
#5Leyline Axe / Doc Ock's Tentacles (equip activations)
Leyline Axe: 'Equip {3}'. Doc Ock's Tentacles: 'Equip {5}' ({3} onto Dwarven Mauler, {0} via Kíli).
WhyCountering the equip blanks the Equipment (artifact): Axe stops granting '+1/+1 and has double strike and trample'; Tentacles stops granting '+4/+4'. If it was already attached to a creature, that creature loses the bonus on the spot. Whichever of the two is carrying their damage is the target.
WhenTheir main phase, in response to the equip activation. The only windows that blank Tentacles itself are its own equip activations; Thorin's ETB and Raubahn's trigger also move it, but those are abilities of Thorin/Raubahn, so countering them blanks Thorin/Raubahn, not Tentacles.
AfterEquipment stays on the battlefield, attached where it already was, granting nothing while Tidebinder lives; it has no Equip ability, so it cannot be re-equipped until Tidebinder leaves. Hone counters placed on it before the blank grant nothing; counters Dwalin adds afterward most likely grant +1/+0 normally (later timestamp, Tidebinder ruling 2). See the hone-counter note under Dwalin.
RiskTheir mana was spent (or the free equip consumed), but the tempo swing is smaller than Thorin/Blade. Only worth the one main copy if it prevents lethal or a double-strike hit that kills Curiosity.
#6Dwalin, Weaponmaster
Whenever Dwalin enters or attacks, put a hone counter on each Equipment you control. (Each hone counter on an Equipment grants +1/+0 to equipped creature.)
WhyWith 3-4 Equipment out each trigger is permanent power spread across their whole toolbox; counters already placed stay forever. Countering the trigger also strips First strike for as long as Tidebinder lives. Requiting Hex ({B}) kills him permanently, so Tidebinder here is a tempo play for when Hex is not in hand.
WhenETB trigger as Dwalin resolves, or the attack trigger right after attackers are declared (Dwalin is already attacking; countering does not remove him from combat). Prefer the attack trigger if it lets your 3/2 block a first-striker turned 2/1 vanilla.
AfterNo hone counters this time; earlier ones remain on the Equipment and keep working as long as that Equipment itself has not been blanked by a Tidebinder. Dwalin is a 2/1 with no First strike and no future triggers while Tidebinder lives. Hone-counter note: the reminder text says each counter 'grants' +1/+0, which reads as an ability of the Equipment, so a blanked Equipment's earlier counters should grant nothing and later counters should work (timestamp order). Confirm against the set's release notes; the CR text for hone counters was not in our data.
RiskOnly 2 copies; do not hold the main copy waiting for him.
#7Raubahn, Bull of Ala Mhigo (attack trigger, or his ward trigger)
Whenever Raubahn attacks, attach up to one target Equipment you control to target attacking creature. / Ward, Pay life equal to Raubahn's power.
WhyA free instant-speed equip at every attack, and his ward makes our targeted removal cost life equal to his power. Tidebinder targets the ability, so ward never triggers on it; after the counter Raubahn has lost ward, and Shoot the Sheriff / Requiting Hex hit him for free. Second line: target him with Wasp, Shoot the Sheriff or Requiting Hex, let the ward trigger go on the stack, and Tidebinder counters the ward trigger. Its source is Raubahn (a creature), so he loses all abilities, no life is paid, and your original spell resolves. That is the line when he is already big and the life tax would hurt.
WhenAttackers declared, Raubahn's trigger on the stack: counter it, then kill him with a mono-black spell in the same combat. Or: cast removal / flash Wasp at him at any time, respond to the ward trigger with Tidebinder. Both illegal only if he wears Dragonfire Blade (hexproof from monocolored blocks the mono-black spell or Wasp, though not Tidebinder itself).
AfterNo Equipment moves. Raubahn is a 2/2 with no ward and no attack trigger while Tidebinder lives. Equipment already on him keeps granting (its abilities are the Equipment's, not his), and anything equipped to him after this grants normally.
Risk2 copies only. If he is wearing the Blade, blanking Raubahn does not remove the Blade's hexproof (that ability belongs to the Blade).
#8Fíli the Pathfinder (sideboard, 1)
Whenever Fíli or another nontoken Dwarf you control enters, create a 2/2 red Dwarf creature token.
WhyCountering the token trigger blanks Fíli, which removes the static 'creatures you control get +1/+1' and his Storied while Tidebinder lives. The anthem cannot be hit directly; this is the door.
WhenFíli's own ETB (best) or any later nontoken Dwarf entering while Fíli is out.
AfterNo token this time; tokens already made stay. Fíli is a 2/2 with no anthem, no Storied, no future tokens while Tidebinder lives.
RiskOne copy in the 75; treat as opportunistic. Fíli is MV 4, so Hex does not answer him; Sheriff and Triumph do.
#9The Queen of Dale (sideboard, 2)
Whenever an opponent casts their first noncreature spell each turn, you recruit. (Draw a card, then discard a card. If you discarded a nonland card, create a 1/1 white Human Soldier creature token.)
WhyEvery removal spell we cast feeds her. Counter the recruit trigger once and she is a blank 2/1 while Tidebinder lives, so our Sheriffs and Hexes stop paying her.
WhenCast a cheap noncreature spell (Requiting Hex at Kíli is ideal), her trigger goes on the stack, flash Tidebinder targeting it. Four mana total (Hex {B} plus Tidebinder {2}{U}), so T4 at the earliest.
AfterNo recruit. Queen keeps her 2/1 body; tokens already made stay.
RiskExpensive line; usually just Requiting Hex her instead (MV 2), accepting the one recruit.
#10Lavaspur Boots (equip activation)
Equip {1}
WhyThe cheapest and most frequent equip in the deck (4 main). Countering it blanks the Boots (artifact): no +1/+0, no haste, no ward {1} on the carrier while Tidebinder lives. Real only when haste is the point (a fresh Thorin or Tentacles carrier about to swing) or when it eats Kíli's {0} equip for the turn.
WhenTheir main phase, in response to the equip activation.
AfterBoots stay attached where they were, granting nothing, and cannot be re-equipped until Tidebinder leaves.
RiskLow value: one mana of theirs for three of ours. Third-copy filler.
#11Basilisk Collar (sideboard, 1)
Equip {2}
WhyCollar on the Thorin-ETB damage dealer turns 'deals damage equal to its power' into deathtouch removal of anything, and Collar plus double strike lifelink swings races. Countering the equip blanks Collar (no deathtouch, no lifelink) while Tidebinder lives.
WhenTheir main phase, in response to the equip activation. If Collar arrives via Thorin's ETB, counter Thorin's ETB instead.
AfterCollar stays attached where it was, granting nothing, and cannot be re-equipped until Tidebinder leaves.
RiskOne copy post-board; opportunistic.
#12The Lonely Mountain
{4}{R}, {T}: Create a 2/2 red Dwarf creature token. This ability costs {1} less to activate for each Equipment you control. Activate only as a sorcery.
WhyLate-game mana sink; countering eats their mana and the land tap for the turn. Filler target when nothing better will show up this turn.
WhenTheir main phase, in response to the activation. Sorcery speed, so it happens only on their turn.
AfterMana and the tap stay paid; no token. The land is not an artifact, creature or planeswalker, so it keeps all abilities and can be activated again next turn.
RiskLow value; only fire the third Tidebinder here.
#13Giott, King of the Dwarves / Dáin's Company
Giott: 'Whenever Giott or another Dwarf you control enters and whenever an Equipment you control enters, you may discard a card. If you do, draw a card.' Dáin's: 'When this creature enters, look at the top four cards of your library. You may reveal a Dwarf or Equipment card from among them and put it into your hand.'
WhyCard-quality triggers, not card advantage (Giott loots, Dáin's digs). Countering Giott's loot does blank Double strike while Tidebinder lives, which matters if he is the Tentacles carrier; Dáin's blank only removes conditional lifelink.
WhenTheir ETBs as the creature resolves, or any later Dwarf/Equipment entering for Giott.
AfterGiott 1/1 with no Double strike and no loot; Dáin's 2/2 with no lifelink. Both keep bodies; Leyline Axe equipped after the blank grants double strike again (later timestamp).
RiskFiller. Requiting Hex handles both bodies for {B}.
#14Shattered Acolyte (sideboard, 2)
{1}, Sacrifice this creature: Destroy target artifact or enchantment.
WhyAcolyte is their only clean answer to Enduring Curiosity: Curiosity is an Enchantment Creature in both forms, so Acolyte can destroy the 4/3 (it returns as an enchantment) and a second Acolyte finishes it; it also hits Ghost Vacuum. Countering saves the permanent; the sacrifice stays paid so Acolyte is gone anyway and nothing loses abilities.
WhenIn response to the activation.
AfterAcolyte is in the graveyard; our permanent survives.
RiskRarely worth a card unless it is the second Acolyte on an enchantment-form Curiosity. Acolyte is a Warlock (outlaw), so Shoot the Sheriff cannot target it; Requiting Hex can (MV 2).
Traps
Dragonfire Blade: Tidebinder the hexproof so we can target the creature. 'Equipped creature gets +2/+2 and has hexproof from monocolored' is a static ability of the Blade; it cannot be countered. The only hook is the Equip activation; countering that blanks the whole Blade.
Thorin, Mountain-king: Let the ETB resolve and counter the damage trigger, keeping Tidebinder flexible. The reflexive trigger ('When one or more Equipment become attached to that creature this way, that creature deals damage...') fires after the Equipment has already moved. Countering it saves your creature but the mass equip stands. Counter the first trigger, or kill its creature target in response.
Leyline Axe: Counter it entering on turn 0 or counter its double strike. 'If this card is in your opening hand, you may begin the game with it on the battlefield' is a static ability that lets them take a pre-game action (CR 103.6); nothing ever uses the stack. The stat/keyword grant is static too. Only 'Equip {3}' can be countered.
Kíli the Resourceful: Counter the free equip or the Storied. 'you may pay {0} rather than pay the equip cost' is a static cost alternative and Storied is a static keyword; neither uses the stack. The equip activation itself (from the Equipment) is what you counter; doing so consumes the free equip for the turn. Blanking Kíli via his draw trigger also shuts both off.
Dwarven Mauler: Tidebinder has a target here. 'Equip abilities you activate that target this creature cost {2} less to activate' is static; Mauler has no activated or triggered ability. Only Wasp can blank him.
Doc Ock's Tentacles: Wait for the 'Whenever a creature you control with mana value 5 or greater enters, you may attach' trigger. No creature in the 75 has mana value 5 or more (Thorin and Fíli are MV 4), so that trigger never happens. Tentacles only moves via an equip activation, Thorin's ETB or Raubahn's trigger; counter those.
Cavern of Souls: Cavern-cast Dwarves are safe from Tidebinder. 'that spell can't be countered' protects the creature spell only. Its ETB and other abilities are fair game. Nothing on Cavern itself can be targeted: its two {T} abilities are mana abilities and 'As this land enters, choose a creature type' never uses the stack.
Bofur, Reliable Guardian (adventure) / Erode: Counter the hexproof-and-indestructible or the destroy effect with Tidebinder. Both are spells (the Adventure half is cast as an instant). Tidebinder counters abilities only. Note Bofur's adventure does not protect abilities from Tidebinder either (Tidebinder never targets the permanent), but it does fizzle Wasp, Drowner and mono-black removal aimed at the creature. Spell Snare (MV 2), Spell Pierce or We Say Thee Nay! (both are taxes) answer the adventure spell.
Rest in Peace (sideboard): Counter the ETB to keep our graveyard. You can counter 'When this enchantment enters, exile all graveyards', but the replacement effect 'If a card or token would be put into a graveyard from anywhere, exile it instead' is static and stays, and an enchantment never loses abilities to Tidebinder. RIP does hurt us: with it out Enduring Curiosity is exiled instead of dying, so it never returns. Answer RIP with Annul on cast, or race it.
Sacred Foundry / Multiversal Passage / Inspiring Vantage / The Lonely Mountain (enters tapped): Counter the life payment or force it in tapped. 'As this land enters...' and 'enters tapped unless...' are replacement/static effects, not triggered abilities.
Raubahn, Bull of Ala Mhigo / Lavaspur Boots ward: Tidebinder gets taxed by ward. Ward triggers when the permanent 'becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls'. Tidebinder targets the ability on the stack, never the permanent, so no ward trigger and no life or mana tax.
Lavaspur Boots ward {1}: Spend Tidebinder on the Boots' ward trigger to save {1}. Rules-legal (the ward's source is the equipped creature, so it would go blank), but a three-mana card to dodge a one-mana tax while Thorin and Blade re-equips are on the table. Pay the {1}. The ward-trigger line is for Raubahn's life-payment ward only.
Not worth it
Dáin's Company ETB: a 2-mana dig for one card, and the creature it blanks is a 2/2 with conditional lifelink; save the Tidebinder.Giott's loot: card quality, not card advantage; Requiting Hex kills the 1/1 for {B}.The Lonely Mountain token: low-stakes 2/2 at sorcery speed and the land keeps its abilities; third-copy filler only.Lavaspur Boots' ward {1}: pay the mana, never the Tidebinder.Rest in Peace ETB: the replacement effect stays and RIP does not lose abilities.Shattered Acolyte activation: the body is sacrificed either way; only worth it if it is the second Acolyte on an enchantment-form Enduring Curiosity that is drawing you cards.Kíli's draw trigger when Requiting Hex is in hand: Hex kills him for {B} permanently and keeps Tidebinder for Thorin or the Blade.
The Wondrous Wasp
Two mana at instant speed to tap their Axe or Tentacles carrier before attackers and strip every keyword the Equipment already on it grants, for as long as Wasp lives. That is the best thing Wasp does here, and it is more durable than it looks: an Equipment attached before Wasp resolved keeps granting nothing, and they cannot refresh it by re-equipping the same Equipment to the same creature (CR 701.3b: attaching to the object it is already attached to does nothing). Blanking Kíli at their upkeep is fine when Requiting Hex is not in hand. The cap is Dragonfire Blade: hexproof from monocolored means Wasp cannot target whoever wears it, and that is usually their best creature. Lavaspur Boots' ward {1} is a tax you pay; Raubahn's ward costs life equal to his power, so hit him only while small or with Tidebinder backing up the ward trigger. Wasp is a 2/1 that dies to Chainsaw and Thorin's damage, so expect the blank to end.
#1The equipped attacker that is NOT wearing Dragonfire Blade (Leyline Axe / Tentacles carrier)
WhyTapping before attackers stops the double strike, trample swing for a turn. The blank also strips every keyword granted by Equipment attached BEFORE Wasp resolved (later timestamp wins): Axe's double strike and trample, Boots' haste and ward. The +X/+X from Equipment and the +1/+0 from hone counters stay (those modify P/T, not abilities). To get double strike back on that creature they must attach an Equipment AFTER the blank; re-equipping the Axe it already wears does nothing (CR 701.3b), so they need to move it away and back (two sorcery-speed equips) or attach a different Equipment.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared, to stop the attack. If they already attacked, Wasp does not remove the creature from combat, but resolving Wasp before blockers still removes double strike and trample from an attacker whose Axe was attached earlier, so it deals normal damage once. Pay {1} extra if it wears Lavaspur Boots (ward). Illegal if it wears Dragonfire Blade.
AfterCreature tapped, no abilities from itself or from earlier-attached Equipment for as long as Wasp stays on the battlefield. Any Equipment attached AFTER the blank (Kíli free equip next turn, Raubahn's attack trigger, Thorin ETB moving Equipment onto it) grants normally again. Treat this as a one-turn tempo swing plus removal of the creature's printed abilities and its current Equipment keywords while Wasp lives.
#2Kíli the Resourceful (only when Requiting Hex is not in hand)
WhyBlanking Kíli removes the draw trigger, the static free equip and Storied, the three things that make the deck run. He is a 1/2, so the tap is irrelevant; the blank is the point. But Requiting Hex kills him for {B} permanently on sight, so Hex is the default answer and Wasp is the backup.
WhenTheir upkeep or draw step, before they can cast a Dwarf or equip: Kíli is blank for their whole main phase, so no draw and no free equip that turn. Do NOT wait until a Kíli trigger is on the stack; Wasp on Kíli then does not stop the draw (the trigger exists independently of its source). Illegal if Kíli wears Dragonfire Blade.
AfterKíli is a 1/2 with no abilities while Wasp lives. If they already had an enduring story it stays for the game (player designation), so Fíli's anthem still works; if they had not yet reached it, a blank Kíli cannot grant it. Kíli's free equip and draws are gone. Kill him with Requiting Hex when you draw it.
#3Giott, King of the Dwarves
WhyNative Double strike on a body that loves Tentacles (+4/+4 double strike is 10 damage). Blank removes Double strike and the loot trigger while Wasp lives.
WhenTheir beginning of combat or your end step. Blade-wearing Giott is untargetable.
After1/1 with no abilities. Leyline Axe equipped to him afterward gives double strike back (later timestamp), so follow up with Requiting Hex (MV 2).
#4Dwalin, Weaponmaster
WhyTapping him before attackers skips 'Whenever Dwalin enters or attacks, put a hone counter on each Equipment you control' and the blank removes First strike, which lets your 2/1 and 3/2 bodies trade.
WhenTheir beginning of combat. The ETB trigger cannot be stopped by Wasp; only Tidebinder counters it.
After2/1 vanilla while Wasp lives; hone counters already placed keep working (they sit on the Equipment, not on Dwalin).
#5Raubahn, Bull of Ala Mhigo
WhyBlank removes 'Ward, Pay life equal to Raubahn's power' and his attach trigger, so mono-black removal hits him free afterward.
WhenWhile he is a 2/2 (pay 2 life for ward), or at any size if Tidebinder is in hand to counter the ward trigger (then no life is paid and he goes blank from Tidebinder before Wasp even resolves). Once equipped and without Tidebinder, skip him.
After2/2 with no ward, no attack trigger while Wasp lives.
#6Dwarven Mauler (before he wears the Blade)
WhyBlanking Mauler removes 'Equip abilities you activate that target this creature cost {2} less', so Blade onto him goes from {1} to {3}, Axe from {1} to {3}, Tentacles from {3} to {5}. Matters on turns when Kíli is not out or the free equip is already spent; otherwise a plain tempo tap.
WhenTheir upkeep before they equip, or their beginning of combat as a tap.
After2/1 with no discount while Wasp lives.
#7Dáin's Company / Dwarf tokens / Bofur
WhyDáin's lifelink is minor; tokens and Bofur's creature half have nothing worth blanking. Pure tap-a-blocker or tap-an-attacker plays when Wasp is only a tempo body.
WhenYour turn before attacks to remove a blocker for the ninjutsu Wasp, or their beginning of combat.
AfterTapped; Dáin's loses lifelink while Wasp lives.
Traps
Any creature wearing Dragonfire Blade: Wasp the Blade carrier. 'hexproof from monocolored' makes it an illegal target for Wasp (mono-blue), Floodpits Drowner, Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph and Requiting Hex. Kaito's -2 (blue-black source) can target it; so can Tidebinder countering the Blade's equip to switch the hexproof off.
