vs. Selesnya Landfall3.5% TB: goodWasp: premium▼
Every threat is a landfall trigger: Sazh's Chocobo (+1 counter per land), Mossborn Hydra (double its counters per land, trample), Mightform Harmonizer (double target creature's power per land), Icetill Explorer (extra land drop, replay lands from graveyard, mill) and Earthbender Ascension (ramp plus quest counters). Fetch lands (Fabled Passage, Escape Tunnel) give two landfall batches per drop, Erode on their own Elves/Chocobo fetches a basic for another batch at instant speed, and Explorer lets them replay a sacrificed fetch every turn. The kill turn is T3 only if they had T1 Llanowar Elves (T2 Hydra, T3 warp Harmonizer for {2}{G}, land, crack fetch: Hydra 1 -> 2 -> 4 counters with its power doubled twice on top); otherwise it is T4. Esper Origins is a T2 sorcery they flash back on T4+ ({3}{G}) as Summon: Esper Maduin, a 4/4 Saga creature that draws a permanent, refunds GG, then gives the team +2/+2 and trample; Dyadrine is the grind piece (card plus Robot per attack).
Tishana's Tidebinder
Nearly everything this deck does is a triggered ability, so Tidebinder always has a target, and the loss-of-abilities rider is where the real value is: countering one landfall trigger turns Harmonizer into a vanilla 4/4, Hydra into a no-trample X/X that stops growing, or Explorer into a 2/4 with no extra land drop and no graveyard lands, for as long as the 3/2 lives. It is not premium because each land produces 3-5 separate triggers and you stop exactly one, and because Erode (4 main, {W} instant) and Sheltered by Ghosts (4 side) remove the 3/2 and instantly restore everything. Two things to internalise: (1) if they have {W} open they can Erode Tidebinder in response to its own trigger; the target is still countered (ruling 1) but nothing is blanked, so fire anyway when the countered trigger alone is worth 3 mana (a lethal double, a Hydra doubling at 2+ counters) and otherwise wait for the W to be tapped; (2) the blank is a window to kill the threat, not the answer.
#1Mightform Harmonizer
Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, double the power of target creature you control until end of turn.
WhyThe lethal multiplier. On a fetch turn it fires twice (fetch enters, basic enters); a Hydra that already has 4 counters becomes 16 power from Harmonizer alone, far more once Hydra's own counter-doubling in the same batches is counted (they order counters first). Two Harmonizers double twice per land. Countering one trigger blanks the Harmonizer so every later land this turn (fetch crack, Explorer's extra drop, an Erode basic) does nothing.
WhenFire the moment Harmonizer's trigger is on the stack in their main phase. If they respond by cracking an uncracked Fabled Passage/Escape Tunnel or by Eroding their own Elves/Chocobo, that new batch (including a second Harmonizer double) resolves above your trigger; you still cut the doubling in half, and waiting for a second batch that may never come is worse. On the play you have 3 mana on their T3; on the draw you do not (Wasp, see below).
AfterLoses Landfall and Warp; a vanilla 4/4. Hydra's own doubling in the same batch still resolves. A warped Harmonizer is still exiled at end step: the exile is a delayed trigger created when the warp cost was paid, not one of the permanent's own abilities, so the blank does not stop it.
RiskErode on Tidebinder (or Sheltered exiling it) restores Harmonizer immediately; with {W} open the blank may never land (ruling 1). Kill Harmonizer with Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff while it is blank.
#2Mossborn Hydra
Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, double the number of +1/+1 counters on this creature.
WhyExponential and permanent: each fetch drop doubles twice (1 -> 2 -> 4), a second fetch drop takes it to 16, with trample. Countering a doubling at 2+ counters saves that many counters for good, and the blank strips trample and all future growth. If you can survive the turn this is the better long-game target; if the Harmonizer double is lethal this turn, hit Harmonizer.
WhenLandfall batch on the stack. They will order Hydra's counter-doubling to resolve before Harmonizer's power-doubling; you can respond to the whole batch at once. Same fetch/Erode-in-response caveat as above.
AfterStays at its current counters (counters are not abilities; 0/0 base plus counters keeps it alive), loses Trample and Landfall. 'Enters with a +1/+1 counter' is a replacement effect and already happened. Result: a vanilla X/X. A 3/2 Tidebinder blocks it and soaks 2 (kills a 3/3 outright); Beavers/Siren each buy a turn; Drowner or Kaito -2 is the real lock.
RiskErode/Sheltered on Tidebinder re-enables it. Summon: Esper Maduin's chapter III and Earthbender Ascension's reflexive trigger can give it trample back (abilities gained after the blank began are kept, ruling 2); see targets 5 and 9.
#3Icetill Explorer
Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, mill a card.
WhyThe mill is low value by itself (it feeds fetch replay and Esper Origins flashback); you counter it to get the blank, which strips the two static abilities 'You may play an additional land on each of your turns' and 'You may play lands from your graveyard'. Those statics are the engine that turns one fetch into two landfall batches every turn. Statics cannot be countered, so the mill trigger is the only Tidebinder hook. Wasp does the same job for 2 with no trigger needed, so prefer Wasp here and save Tidebinder for Harmonizer/Hydra.
WhenNeeds the mill trigger on the stack, so only when a land enters under their control; there is no end-step window. Best on their first land drop of the turn, before they replay a fetch from the graveyard (their second drop becomes illegal the moment the blank lands).
AfterVanilla 2/4. Lands already played this turn stay played; a fetch already replayed from the graveyard stays on the battlefield.
RiskLow; they rarely protect a 2/4.
#4Earthbender Ascension (ETB)
When this enchantment enters, earthbend 2. Then search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle.
Why4 copies, their most common T3 play. Countering denies a 0/0 haste land creature with two +1/+1 counters, a ramp land AND the landfall batch that basic would produce (Chocobo/Hydra/Explorer all fire off the search). Real tempo when the board is only Chocobo/Elves.
WhenEnchantment resolves, ETB on the stack, their main phase T2-T3. Flashfreeze ({1}{U}) on the enchantment spell is the cheaper post-board answer and also stops the landfall counting.
AfterNothing is blanked (enchantment). It keeps 'Landfall, put a quest counter on this enchantment. When you do, if it has four or more quest counters on it, put a +1/+1 counter on target creature you control. It gains trample until end of turn.' and keeps counting.
RiskUses Tidebinder before Harmonizer/Hydra arrive; take it when you are on the play with nothing scarier on board.
#5Summon: Esper Maduin (Esper Origins flashed back)
I, Reveal the top card of your library. If it's a permanent card, put it into your hand. / II, Add {G}{G}. / III, Other creatures you control get +2/+2 and gain trample until end of turn.
WhyNot a rare line: Esper Origins (3 copies, {1}{G}) is cast T2 and flashed back T4-T6 for {3}{G}, entering transformed as a 4/4 Enchantment Creature, Saga with a finality counter. Chapters are triggered abilities; chapter II is confirmed not a mana ability by the card's own Scryfall ruling ('uses the stack and can be responded to'). Because the permanent is a creature, countering ANY chapter blanks the whole 4/4 for as long as Tidebinder lives: counter I as it enters and it never reaches II or III. Chapter III is the specific danger when your plan is a frozen Hydra: +2/+2 and trample gained after the blank are kept (ruling 2).
WhenChapter I: as it enters (lore counter added on entry). Chapters II/III: as their precombat main phase begins, before attacks. Cheaper answer: Flashfreeze on the flashback cast (green sorcery spell, MV 2, so Spell Snare also hits it).
AfterLoses all chapters; stays a 4/4 with a finality counter. A Saga with no chapter abilities is not sacrificed for lore counters and gets no new lore counters (CR 704.5s and the Saga lore-counter rule in CR 714). If Tidebinder later leaves, chapters return and lore counting resumes from where it stopped; with 3+ lore counters it is sacrificed immediately and the finality counter exiles it (Scryfall ruling: finality still exiles a permanent that has lost its abilities).
RiskOnly relevant once Origins is in the graveyard; the front-face cast and the flashback itself cannot be targeted (see traps).
#6Surrak, Elusive Hunter (sideboard, 2)
Whenever a creature you control or a creature spell you control becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, draw a card.
WhyEvery Wasp, Drowner ETB, Kaito -2, Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff aimed at their creatures draws them a card while Surrak lives. Counter the FIRST draw trigger and Surrak is blank: no more draws, no trample. Tidebinder targets a trigger, not a creature, so it does not itself feed Surrak. Flashfreeze cannot answer him ('This spell can't be countered').
WhenThe moment one of your targeted spells/abilities makes a creature of theirs a target, Surrak's trigger goes on the stack above it; flash Tidebinder then, and your original spell still resolves.
AfterVanilla 4/3. 'This spell can't be countered' is a static about the spell and never mattered to Tidebinder.
Risk5 mana total with the spell that triggered it; worth it when Surrak would otherwise draw 2+ over the game.
#7Sheltered by Ghosts (sideboard, 4)
When this Aura enters, exile target nonland permanent an opponent controls until this Aura leaves the battlefield.
WhyTheir post-board answer to Kaito (a planeswalker without hexproof on THEIR turn), Enduring Curiosity, or your Tidebinder/Wasp (exiling the blanker restores the blanked threat). Countering the ETB saves the permanent outright. This is Tidebinder's BACKUP answer: the Aura is an MV-2 noncreature spell, so Spell Snare, Spell Pierce, Annul and We Say Thee Nay! all counter the Aura itself for 1-2 mana and also deny the +1/+0 lifelink ward buff; Flashfreeze cannot (white).
WhenThe Aura resolves and attaches; its ETB goes on the stack targeting your permanent. Flash Tidebinder then. Tidebinder targets the trigger, not the enchanted creature, so ward {2} does not tax it.
AfterNothing is blanked (Aura). The Aura stays attached: 'Enchanted creature gets +1/+0 and has lifelink and ward {2}', so Wasp/Drowner/Kaito -2 on that creature now costs 2 more.
RiskFire only when the cheap counters are gone or you are tapped below them; otherwise Snare/Pierce/Annul the Aura.
#8Dyadrine, Synthesis Amalgam
Whenever you attack, you may remove a +1/+1 counter from each of two creatures you control. If you do, draw a card and create a 2/2 colorless Robot artifact creature token.
WhyTheir grind engine (card plus body every attack). Countered: no card, no Robot, and no counters are removed (the removal is part of the countered resolution). Tidebinder enters in declare attackers, before blockers, so the 3/2 can also block.
WhenDeclare attackers step, trigger on the stack. Cheaper: Flashfreeze or Annul on the spell (G/W legendary artifact creature, {X}{G}{W}).
AfterLoses Trample and the attack trigger; keeps its +1/+1 counters on a 0/1 base. 'Enters with a number of +1/+1 counters equal to the mana spent' is a replacement effect and can't be countered.
RiskA 2-of; do not hold Tidebinder specifically for it.
#9Earthbender Ascension (reflexive trample trigger)
Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, put a quest counter on this enchantment. When you do, if it has four or more quest counters on it, put a +1/+1 counter on target creature you control. It gains trample until end of turn.
WhyOnce Ascension has 3+ quest counters, every land re-arms a blanked Hydra/Chocobo with trample for the turn (abilities gained after the blank began are kept, ruling 2). If your stall is a frozen Hydra behind chump blockers, this is the trigger that breaks it. Ignore it before 3 quest counters.
WhenEither the landfall quest-counter trigger (counter it and there is no reflexive trigger) or the reflexive '+1/+1 counter and trample' trigger once it is on the stack; both are their main phase, before attacks.
AfterNothing is blanked (enchantment). Quest counters already there stay.
RiskConditional; only when the trample would let the blanked attacker connect through your blocker.
#10Fabled Passage / Escape Tunnel (fetch crack)
{T}, Sacrifice this land: Search your library for a basic land card, put it onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle. (Fabled Passage: then if you control four or more lands, untap that land.)
WhyThe land is sacrificed as a cost and stays sacrificed; countering the activation means no basic and no second landfall batch. This is NOT mana denial (26 lands plus Elves, Roots and Ascension) and it blanks nothing. Its one real use: countering the crack stops EVERY trigger in the second batch at once (no land enters), which matters when that batch would be Hydra + Harmonizer + Chocobo and you cannot afford to leave two of them. Hidden cost: it also cancels the search that would have fed your Wan Shi Tong.
WhenActivation on the stack. Only when you would otherwise waste the mana on their turn, or when the whole second batch is the problem rather than one trigger in it.
AfterNothing is blanked (land, and it is in the graveyard). With Explorer out they can replay the fetch from the graveyard later.
RiskSpending Tidebinder here on T2-3 can leave you without it for the Harmonizer turn.
#11Escape Tunnel (unblockable activation)
{T}, Sacrifice this land: Target creature with power 2 or less can't be blocked this turn.
WhyThe lethal setup: make a 1-2 power Hydra or Chocobo unblockable FIRST, then grow it with the next land (Explorer's extra drop, or an Erode/Ascension basic). Scryfall ruling: once the ability has resolved, the creature stays unblockable even if its power grows past 2. Countering the activation leaves the land sacrificed with no effect.
WhenActivation on the stack, their main phase, while they still have a land drop or a way to put a land onto the battlefield afterward. If they have no follow-up land, ignore it.
AfterNothing is blanked (land). No unblockable; the Tunnel is gone.
RiskLow frequency; if you instead can counter the doubling that follows, that is usually the better use.
#12Earthbended land (delayed return trigger)
When it dies or is exiled, return it to the battlefield tapped (the delayed triggered ability created by earthbend, per the Ba Sing Se / Earthbender Ascension rulings).
WhyWhen you kill or exile an earthbended land, it comes back tapped as a fresh land, and that re-entry is a land entering: every landfall trigger they control fires. Countering the delayed trigger is the only way to make the land stay dead, and it denies that free landfall batch. Blanking the land (Wasp or Tidebinder) does NOT remove this trigger: it is created by the earthbend effect, not an ability of the land.
WhenThe return trigger goes on the stack when the land creature dies or is exiled (e.g. after your Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff resolves, or it dies in combat). Flash Tidebinder then.
AfterNothing is blanked: per CR 603.7e the source of a delayed trigger created by an ability is that ability's source, i.e. Ba Sing Se (a land) or Earthbender Ascension (an enchantment). The land stays in the graveyard (or exile).
RiskLow value unless the landfall batch it would produce matters; mostly a warning that killing earthbended lands feeds their engine.
#13Sapling Nursery (sideboard, 4) indestructible activation
{1}{G}, Exile this enchantment: Treefolk and Forests you control gain indestructible until end of turn.
WhyIf they respond to Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff/Requiting Hex on a Treefolk token or an earthbended Forest with this, countering the activation leaves Nursery exiled (paid as a cost) with no indestructible granted, and your removal resolves. Do NOT do this to protect Day of Black Sun: Black Sun's 'loses all abilities until end of turn' has the later timestamp and strips Nursery's indestructible before the destroy, so the Treefolk die anyway.
WhenActivation on the stack in response to your single-target destroy spell.
AfterNothing is blanked (enchantment, already exiled).
RiskNiche; do not board Tidebinder in for this.
#14Ba Sing Se (earthbend activation)
{2}{G}, {T}: Earthbend 2. Activate only as a sorcery.
WhyCountered: 3 mana plus the tap wasted, no 0/0 haste land with two counters. Pure tempo, only when no creature trigger is available.
WhenTheir main phase, activation on the stack.
AfterNothing blanked (land). Its '{T}: Add {G}' was never targetable.
RiskLow value; skip unless the mana is otherwise wasted.
Traps
Icetill Explorer: Counter 'You may play an additional land' or 'You may play lands from your graveyard'. Both are static abilities and cannot be targeted. The only way to remove them is to counter its mill trigger (which blanks the creature) or Wasp it.
Mightform Harmonizer (Warp): Counter the warp cast, or counter the end-step exile to 'punish' the warp. Warp is a static ability that works while the spell is on the stack; casting for a warp cost is casting a spell, not an activated or triggered ability. The end-step exile IS a delayed triggered ability and is legally targetable, but countering it gifts them a permanent Harmonizer; whether that Harmonizer is even blanked is a genuine rules corner (source of a delayed trigger from a static ability, CR 603.7g vs the spell becoming a new object). Never do it, so the corner never matters.
Mossborn Hydra / Dyadrine: Counter 'enters with a +1/+1 counter' / 'enters with counters equal to mana spent' so it comes in as a 0/0 and dies. 'Enters with' is a replacement effect, not a trigger. Nothing to target. And blanking either later does not kill it: counters stay.
Landfall in general: Counter 'the landfall' when a land enters and shut the whole turn off. The land entering is not an ability. Each of Chocobo, Hydra, Harmonizer, Explorer, Ascension (and Nursery post-board) has its own separate trigger. Tidebinder counters exactly one; pick the creature whose trigger, and whose blank, matters most. The exception is countering a fetch-crack activation, which stops the second batch from ever existing (target 10).
Erode / Shared Roots: Counter the basic-land search when they Erode their own Llanowar Elves to ramp. Those are spell effects; Tidebinder cannot touch them (Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay! / Flashfreeze on Roots handle the spell). You can only counter the landfall triggers the fetched basic produces.
Llanowar Elves / Hushwood Verge / Ba Sing Se / Temple Garden: Counter '{T}: Add {G}'. Mana abilities can't be targeted (printed on Tidebinder). Wasp can blank Elves, Tidebinder can't touch it.
Esper Origins (front face): Counter 'Surveil 2. You gain 2 life' or the flashback. It is a sorcery spell, and flashback is a static ability that grants casting permission; Tidebinder only counters activated and triggered abilities, so neither the flashback nor the resulting spell can be targeted. Only the transformed Saga's chapter triggers are targets.
Torpor Orb (sideboard, 2): Counter Torpor Orb's effect, or flash Tidebinder/Wasp in under a resolved Orb. 'Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger' is static, and it switches off Tidebinder's and Wasp's own ETB triggers (and Drowner's, Siren's, Beavers' and Wan Shi Tong's). Under an Orb they are a 3/2 and a 2/1 flyer. Cast them while the Orb is still on the stack (they trigger normally) or answer the Orb with Annul / Spell Pierce / Spell Snare (MV 2) / Duress.
Temple Garden / Ba Sing Se (entering): Counter 'you may pay 2 life' or 'enters tapped unless you control a basic land'. Replacement effects / static, not triggers.
Sapling Nursery (Affinity for Forests) / Surrak ('This spell can't be countered'): Something Tidebinder interacts with. Cost reduction and uncounterability are statics about the spell. Tidebinder never counters spells, so Surrak's clause is irrelevant; his draw trigger is the target.
Earthbender Ascension / Sheltered by Ghosts / Rest in Peace / fetch lands / Ba Sing Se: Countering their trigger or activation blanks the permanent. Only artifacts, creatures and planeswalkers lose abilities. Enchantments and lands keep everything; you just stop the one ability. Rest in Peace's exile-instead is a replacement effect and cannot be stopped at all.
Earthbended land (0/0 haste land creature): Blanking it turns it back into a plain land, or blanking it stops it from returning when it dies. 'Becomes a 0/0 creature with haste that's still a land' is an effect on it, not an ability, so a blanked earthbended land is still a creature (it loses only haste and its mana ability). The return is 'the delayed triggered ability created by earthbend' (Scryfall ruling on Ba Sing Se and Ascension), created by the effect and not an ability of the land, so the blank does not touch it: the land still comes back tapped and triggers landfall. Tidebinder CAN counter that delayed trigger when it fires (target 12); nothing is blanked because its source is Ba Sing Se or Ascension.
Sheltered by Ghosts (static): Counter '+1/+0, lifelink and ward {2}'. Static grant. Only the exile ETB is a trigger. (The ward trigger it grants IS a trigger and can be countered when your removal targets the creature, which also blanks that creature, but paying {2} is usually cheaper than 3 mana.)
Sapling Nursery vs Day of Black Sun: Hold Tidebinder to counter Nursery's indestructible activation when you cast Day of Black Sun. Day of Black Sun reads 'Each creature with mana value X or less loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures.' Its loses-all-abilities effect is later in timestamp order than Nursery's indestructible grant (layer 6), so the indestructible is stripped before the destroy. The Treefolk die whether or not you counter the activation; they exiled their own engine for nothing. Note Black Sun also blanks and kills your own Wasp (X >= 2) and Tidebinder (X >= 3), ending every blank you set up.
Not worth it
Sazh's Chocobo landfall ('put a +1/+1 counter on this creature'): one counter is nothing and Wasp blanks it for 2.Earthbender Ascension's quest-counter trigger before it has 3 quest counters: no reflexive trigger is coming yet (see target 9 for when it starts to matter).Ward {2} trigger from Sheltered by Ghosts: pay the 2 instead of spending a 3-mana card.Rest in Peace ETB 'exile all graveyards': the ETB is not worth a Tidebinder and the ongoing replacement effect can't be stopped. Its real cost to us is that Enduring Curiosity is exiled instead of dying and never returns; answer RIP itself (Annul, Spell Pierce, Duress) if Curiosity is the game plan.Sapling Nursery token trigger: one 3/4 reach denied while Nursery keeps making more; Wasp the token instead if reach is the problem (Wasp target 8).Icetill Explorer's mill as a trigger in its own right: only counter it for the blank.Ba Sing Se earthbend / fetch crack as 'mana denial': 26 lands plus Elves, Roots and Ascension; they are never short on mana. Counter these only for the batch denial described in targets 10-14.
The Wondrous Wasp
Every creature in this deck is worth exactly its abilities: Hydra and Chocobo are 0/0 and 0/1 bodies that only matter because they grow, Harmonizer only matters for its landfall, Explorer only for its statics. A 2-mana flash 'loses all abilities' is close to a permanent Pacifism on growth, and the 2/1 flyer is unblockable in game 1 (no flyers, no reach) for Enduring Curiosity draws. Two things to internalise: (1) Wasp stops FUTURE triggers only; a landfall trigger already on the stack resolves no matter what you do to its source, so the best Wasp is cast at their upkeep, before the land drop; (2) if they have {W} open they can Erode Wasp in response to its trigger: the tap still happens but nothing is blanked (the 'for as long as' condition is already false when the effect would begin, CR 611.2b), so prefer moments when the W is tapped.
#1Mossborn Hydra
Why'Landfall, double the number of +1/+1 counters on this creature' plus Trample. Blanked, it is frozen at its current counters with no trample; Beavers/Siren each buy a turn and Drowner or Kaito -2 lock it. On the draw with T1 Elves on their side, this is your only play for their T3 Harmonizer turn (you have 2 lands).
WhenBlank: their upkeep or draw step, BEFORE the land drop; if you wait for the landfall batch, the doubling on the stack still resolves. Tap: their beginning of combat step if it would attack for lethal; tapping after attackers are declared does not remove it from combat (the blank still removes trample, which can matter for damage assignment).
AfterStays an X/X where X = its current counters (0/0 base plus counters, does not die), loses Trample and Landfall. Erode or Sheltered on Wasp restores it, so kill it while it is blank. Esper Maduin III or Ascension's reflexive trigger can give it trample back for a turn (gained after the blank, ruling 2).
#2Mightform Harmonizer
Why'Landfall, double the power of target creature you control until end of turn.' Blanked, the kill multiplier is gone for every later land. Hardcast (4 mana) Harmonizers are the real target; a warped one leaves at end step anyway and is exiled regardless of the blank (warp's exile is a delayed trigger, not one of its abilities).
WhenTheir upkeep on any turn a Harmonizer is on the battlefield. There is no window between a warped Harmonizer resolving and their land drop (active player gets priority and may play a land immediately). Against a fresh warp you can Wasp with the first landfall batch on the stack, but if they hold an uncracked Fabled Passage/Escape Tunnel (or {W} for Erode on their own creature) they will make the second batch in response to Wasp's trigger and that second Harmonizer double resolves before the blank applies. Wasp then only prevents landfall from lands entering later (Explorer's extra drop, an Erode basic).
AfterVanilla 4/4. Any doubling already resolved this turn stays until end of turn.
#3Icetill Explorer
WhyStrips both statics: 'You may play an additional land on each of your turns' and 'You may play lands from your graveyard', ending the replay-the-fetch loop and the double land drops. No trigger needed, so this is Wasp's job, not Tidebinder's.
WhenAny time; the earlier the better, ideally before their next turn so they never get the extra drop. Lands already played this turn stay played.
AfterVanilla 2/4; landfall mill also gone.
#4Sazh's Chocobo
Why'Landfall, put a +1/+1 counter on this creature.' A 1-drop that is routinely 5/6+ by turn 4 and the other favourite Harmonizer target. Freeze it only when Hydra and Harmonizer are already handled or absent: a doubled Chocobo has no trample and Beavers/Siren chump it, so do not spend Wasp on it at T3 upkeep just because no Hydra is out yet.
WhenTheir upkeep before the land drop; or beginning of combat as a tap to stop a lethal swing.
AfterFrozen at current counters on a 0/1 base, no more growth.
#5Summon: Esper Maduin (Esper Origins flashed back)
Why4/4 Enchantment Creature, Saga. Wasp blanks all remaining chapters: no 'Add {G}{G}', no team +2/+2 trample (the alpha that re-tramples a frozen Hydra). A blanked Saga is not sacrificed and gets no new lore counters (CR 704.5s and the CR 714 lore-counter rule); if Wasp leaves it resumes at its current lore count and with 3+ is sacrificed and exiled by the finality counter.
WhenIts chapter I still resolves as it enters; Wasp at their end step or upkeep before their next precombat main phase to stop II and III.
AfterAbility-less 4/4; finality counter still exiles it if it dies (Scryfall ruling).
#6Dyadrine, Synthesis Amalgam
WhyLoses Trample and 'Whenever you attack, you may remove a +1/+1 counter from each of two creatures you control. If you do, draw a card and create a 2/2 colorless Robot'. Their grind engine becomes a vanilla body with counters.
WhenBefore their declare attackers (upkeep or beginning of combat); tap at beginning of combat if the swing matters.
AfterKeeps counters (0/1 base), no trample, no cards, no Robots.
#7Surrak, Elusive Hunter (sideboard)
WhyTargeting Surrak with Wasp draws them one card ('Whenever a creature you control ... becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, draw a card'), then he is a vanilla 4/3: no more draws off your Drowner/removal/Kaito, no trample. Tidebinder on his first draw trigger is the cleaner answer; Wasp is the fallback.
WhenAny time; sooner is better since each of your targeted plays feeds him.
AfterVanilla 4/3 (his 'can't be countered' was a spell clause). They draw exactly one from the Wasp targeting.
#8Sapling Nursery's 3/4 Treefolk token (sideboard)
WhyPost-board the reach Treefolk are the only thing that blocks your air force. Wasp's Sting strips reach (and the tap at their beginning of combat or your own turn removes it as a blocker), reopening the flying lane for Wasp, Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren and Wan Shi Tong to keep Enduring Curiosity drawing. Cheaper than answering Nursery itself.
WhenYour own turn before attacks (tap plus blank) or their end step; a token, so no Erode-in-response concern beyond losing the blank.
AfterA 3/4 with no reach that stays tapped this turn if you tapped it on your turn.
#9Llanowar Elves
Why'{T}: Add {G}' is a mana ability Tidebinder can't touch but Wasp's blank removes. T1 Elves into T2 Hydra is the only line that makes T3 the kill turn. Usually still the wrong use: you want Wasp for Hydra/Harmonizer, and blanking Elves does not stop a Hydra already cast.
WhenTheir T2 upkeep (only possible on the play). Once they are past T3, ignore it.
AfterA 1/1 that taps for nothing.
#10Earthbended land creature
WhyIt is a creature, so Wasp can hit it: it loses haste and its '{T}: Add {G}' while Wasp lives, and stays a 0/0 creature with its counters. It does NOT lose the return-on-death: that is the delayed triggered ability created by earthbend (Scryfall ruling), not an ability of the land. If it later dies or is exiled it returns tapped as a normal land and fires every landfall trigger they control. Very low value; mostly a warning that killing earthbended lands feeds their engine unless Tidebinder counters the return (Tidebinder target 12).
WhenAny time; lowest priority.
AfterA 0/0 with counters that produces no mana and cannot attack the turn it was animated; if it dies it still comes back as a plain land.
Traps
Any landfall creature: Wasp in response to the landfall trigger to stop it. A triggered ability on the stack exists independently of its source; blanking the source does not counter it. Wasp before the land (upkeep), or use Tidebinder, which does counter the trigger.
Hydra / Chocobo / Dyadrine: Blanking them kills them because their base is 0/0 or 0/1. +1/+1 counters are not abilities; the creature keeps its size. Only a characteristic-defining P/T ability would be lost, and none of these have one.
Attacking Hydra: Flash Wasp after attackers are declared to tap the attacker and stop damage. Tapping does not remove a creature from combat. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step (or earlier) to prevent the attack. Once declared, Wasp still blanks (no trample), which can matter for damage assignment, but it still hits.
Torpor Orb (sideboard): Wasp still taps and blanks under Torpor Orb. 'Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger.' Wasp's Sting never triggers; it is a 2/1 flyer. Flash it while the Orb is on the stack if you must, or answer the Orb first.
Creature enchanted with Sheltered by Ghosts: Wasp it for 2. It has ward {2}: Wasp's trigger is countered unless you pay 2 more. Wasp's body still enters. Budget 4 mana or pick another target. Better: kill the enchanted creature with Shoot the Sheriff/Bitter Triumph (paying ward) and your exiled Tidebinder/Wasp returns as a new object with a fresh ETB.
Surrak, Elusive Hunter: Free targeting. Every Wasp target while Surrak is out draws them a card. Blank Surrak first (or Tidebinder his first draw trigger).
Warped Mightform Harmonizer: Wasp it to keep it from being exiled and stuck as a blank body, or expect the blank to stop the exile. The warp exile is a delayed trigger created on cast; it fires regardless of the blank. Wasp on a warped Harmonizer only matters if it stops a landfall batch THIS turn, and they can crack a fetch in response to make that batch anyway.
Earthbended land: Wasp it as a Stone Rain: blank it, kill it, it stays dead. The return is a delayed trigger from earthbend and survives the blank; the land comes back tapped and triggers landfall. Only Tidebinder countering that return trigger keeps it dead.
Wasp with {W} open on their side: The blank is guaranteed once the trigger is on the stack. Erode ({W} instant) on Wasp in response to its trigger: the tap still happens, but 'for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield' is already false when the effect would begin, so nothing is blanked (CR 611.2b). Prefer their upkeep when the W is tapped, or hold Spell Pierce.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Danger-turn rule: if they had T1 Llanowar Elves, expect Hydra on T2 and the Harmonizer warp on T3; on the draw you have 2 lands, so Wasp the Hydra at their T3 UPKEEP (Harmonizer then doubles a 1-power body). Without T1 Elves the sequence is T1 Chocobo, T2 Roots/Origins, T3 Hydra or Ascension, T4 warp Harmonizer plus land plus fetch: develop Drowner/Siren/Beavers on T2-T3, keep 3 open on their T4 for Tidebinder (live even on the draw), and never Wasp a Chocobo at T3 upkeep just because no Hydra is out yet.
  • On the play with 3 mana on their T3: hold Tidebinder for Harmonizer's landfall trigger and fire the moment it is on the stack. If they crack a fetch or Erode their own creature in response, the new batch resolves first; you still halve the doubling.
  • Drowner lock, correct timing: flash Floodpits Drowner on THEIR turn (end step is ideal) to tap and stun the blanked Hydra, then on our very next turn activate '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries' while the stun counter is still there. Their untap step removes the stun counter instead of untapping, so a Drowner cast on our own turn (summoning sick) never gets a legal target from its own stun. Kaito -2 puts TWO stun counters, so one survives their untap and gives a Drowner that has been on the battlefield since our turn began a target. The blank keeps the Hydra small in the meantime; it does not change this timing.
  • Kaito -2 (tap target creature, two stun counters) on the blanked Hydra or Chocobo locks their only real attacker while Kaito ticks. Kaito is hexproof only on OUR turn: on theirs, Erode ({W}) kills him and Sheltered by Ghosts exiles him. Do not tap out for Kaito into an open W; ninjutsu him for {1}{U}{B} off an unblocked flyer and keep {U} for Spell Pierce, or hold Tidebinder/Snare for Sheltered's ETB post-board.
  • Enduring Curiosity plus Wasp: game 1 they have zero flyers and zero reach, so a blank-then-swing Wasp draws a card every turn. Post-board Sapling Nursery's 3/4 reach Treefolk stop this; Wasp the Treefolk (strip reach, tap it) to reopen the lane.
  • Post-board Surrak: the first time Drowner/Wasp/Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff/Kaito -2 targets their creature, Surrak's draw trigger goes on the stack; Tidebinder it and Surrak is blank for the rest of the game while your spell still resolves.
  • Protect the blank rather than relying on it. Their outs are Erode (4 main; the basic-land search goes to the destroyed permanent's controller, so Eroding our Tidebinder gives US the land, but it still ends the blank) and Sheltered by Ghosts (4 side). Spell Pierce ({U}) covers BOTH unless they pay {2}; We Say Thee Nay! covers both for 2; Spell Snare and Annul also hit Sheltered (MV-2 enchantment). Kill the blanked threat with Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff/Drowner while it is blank.
  • Sheltered by Ghosts exiled your Tidebinder or Wasp: kill the enchanted creature (Shoot the Sheriff/Bitter Triumph, paying ward {2}). The Aura leaves, the blanker returns as a new object with a fresh ETB: Tidebinder counters something again, Wasp re-blanks.
  • Post-board Torpor Orb switches off every ETB in our deck (Tidebinder, Wasp, Drowner, Siren, Beavers, Wan Shi Tong). Answer it on sight: Annul, Spell Pierce, Spell Snare (MV 2), Duress (noncreature nonland card), or flash Tidebinder/Wasp in while the Orb is on the stack.
  • Post-board Flashfreeze x2 reshapes the hold decision: for 2 mana it counters Hydra, Harmonizer (including the warp cast), Icetill Explorer, Earthbender Ascension, Shared Roots, Esper Origins (both the T2 cast and the flashback), Dyadrine ({X}{G}{W}) and Sapling Nursery; not Surrak (can't be countered) and not the white cards. Hold Flashfreeze for the spell and keep Tidebinder for what has already resolved: the Harmonizer trigger off a resolved warp, Surrak's first draw trigger, Esper Maduin's chapter I.
  • Killing an earthbended land (Bitter Triumph, combat, Day of Black Sun) returns it tapped as a plain land and fires every landfall trigger they control. If that batch matters, Tidebinder the delayed return trigger (source is Ba Sing Se / Ascension, nothing blanked) or leave the land alone.
  • Wan Shi Tong: every fetch crack, Erode search, Shared Roots and Ascension search is 'Whenever an opponent searches their library' for our bird (counter plus card). Countering a fetch crack with Tidebinder cancels that draw; one more reason the fetch-crack line is a last resort.
  • Day of Black Sun sizing: X=0 (BB) kills Treefolk and Robot tokens; X=1 adds Chocobo and Elves; X=3 adds Hydra. X >= 2 also blanks and kills your own Wasp, X >= 3 your Tidebinder, ending every blank. Nursery's indestructible response does not save anything from Black Sun, so never spend Tidebinder on it there.
Hold or fire
Tidebinder: HOLD, with an explicit budget rule. Every real play of theirs is a trigger you will want to counter, so do not main-phase it as a 3/2 early. Fire on, in order: (1) a Harmonizer power-double that would connect for lethal or 6+; (2) a Hydra doubling from 2+ counters when you can survive the turn (permanent cap); (3) Esper Maduin's chapter I as it enters (blank 4/4 Saga); (4) Explorer's mill once they are replaying fetches from the graveyard; (5) post-board, Surrak's first draw trigger and Sheltered by Ghosts' ETB aimed at Kaito/Curiosity/your blanker when the cheap counters are gone. Fetch-crack, Tunnel and earthbend triggers only when the whole batch is the problem or the mana is otherwise wasted. Mana budget on T4-T5 (the decision you actually face): if their board has a Hydra at 2+ counters or a Harmonizer, hold Tidebinder (or Flashfreeze post-board) rather than tapping out for Kaito; if the board is Chocobo/Explorer only, cast Enduring Curiosity (flash, so it can also be held) or ninjutsu Kaito off an unblocked flyer and keep {U} for Spell Pierce against Erode. A bare 3/2 at their end step is fine once a hard-cast Curiosity/Kaito is already down and they show no Harmonizer. If they have {W} open, the counter is guaranteed but the blank is not (Erode in response, ruling 1): fire only when the countered trigger alone is worth it. Wasp: FIRE EARLY. Its best moment is their upkeep on the growth creature (Hydra > Harmonizer > Explorer > Chocobo) before the land drop; waiting for the perfect moment lets a trigger already on the stack through. On the play Tidebinder is live for their T3, so Wasp can wait for a hardcast Harmonizer or Explorer. On the draw with their T1 Elves, Wasp the Hydra at their T3 upkeep and hold Tidebinder for T4; without Elves, hold Wasp for the T3 Hydra/T4 Harmonizer instead of spending it on Chocobo. Never blank and forget: schedule the removal for the blanked threat before Erode/Sheltered undo the blank.
How many to run
Tidebinder: 1 main (stock) is right for game 1. The MTGGoldfish stock sideboard in the data file carries no extra copies (Duress 3, Day of Black Sun 2, Annul 1, Ghost Vacuum 1, Spell Pierce 2, Strategic Betrayal 1, Vren 1, Flashfreeze 2, Faebloom Trick 2); the Karsten aggregate does carry 2. If your 75 has them, bring both in for games 2-3 (3 total): the deck's whole plan is creature triggers, so the blank rider almost always applies, and post-board Surrak and Sheltered by Ghosts add two more targets. If your sideboard is the stock one, the post-board plan is Flashfreeze x2 (every green threat spell for 2, including the Harmonizer warp cast and the Origins flashback), Spell Pierce x2 (Erode, Sheltered, Torpor Orb, Rest in Peace, Ascension), Annul x1 (Orb, Sheltered, Nursery, Ascension, RIP, Dyadrine as an artifact spell) and Day of Black Sun x2 (tokens and Chocobo/Elves at low X; remember it kills your own blankers at X >= 2/3). Wasp: keep both (2), do not want more than the 75 offers; against 4 Erode plus 4 Sheltered a blank is a window, not an answer, so pair the blankers with hard removal and counters rather than adding a third Wasp.
Review trail: confidence high, 5 disputed, 20 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Reviewer 2 (counts): 'the stock 75 has ZERO Tidebinder in the sideboard.' Half right. The MTGGoldfish stock sideboard in the data file indeed has none, but the same file's note and the task brief record the Karsten Sept 3 aggregate and postboard lists with 2 Tidebinder in the sideboard, and the brief says the sideboard 'commonly carries 2 more'. Kept the bring-in recommendation as conditional on which 75 the pilot is running, and added the stock-sideboard alternative (Flashfreeze/Pierce/Annul/Black Sun).
  • Reviewer 1 (Saga): cites CR 714.3c for the lore-counter condition. The substance (a Saga permanent with no chapter abilities gets no lore counters and is not sacrificed) is right and is now stated as fact, but I am not certain of the sub-rule number, so the page cites CR 704.5s and 'the Saga lore-counter rule in CR 714' rather than a possibly wrong 714.3c.
  • Reviewer 1 (Hydra arithmetic '1 -> 16 across two fetch drops'): accepted, but two fetch drops in ONE turn requires Explorer's extra land drop; the page says 'each fetch drop doubles twice (1 -> 2 -> 4), a second fetch drop takes it to 16' without implying it happens in a single turn.
  • Reviewer 2 (Sheltered as backup answer): accepted, with one correction to the reviewer's own list: Flashfreeze cannot counter Sheltered by Ghosts (white), and the page says so.
  • No reviewer finding was rejected outright. Regex hint-block corrections from the draft stand except the Ba Sing Se one, which is reversed: the return clause is a delayed triggered ability created by earthbend whose source is Ba Sing Se (or Earthbender Ascension) per CR 603.7e and the Scryfall ruling 'the delayed triggered ability created by earthbend'; the hint block was closer to right than the draft.

vs. Azorius Momo2.5% TB: situationalWasp: good▼
A one-drop flier tempo deck built around Momo, Friendly Flier ({W}, 1/1 flying, static: "The first non-Lemur creature spell with flying you cast during each of your turns costs {1} less to cast"). The discount applies to warp costs too (CR 601.2f, reductions apply to the total cost including alternative costs), so with Momo out Quantum Riddler warps for {U} and Starfield Shepherd warps for {W}. Their nut sequence is turn 1 Momo, turn 2 warp Riddler for {U} plus Daydream for {W}: Daydream ("Exile target creature you control, then return that card to the battlefield ... with a +1/+1 counter") makes the Riddler a new object, the warp end-step exile never finds it, and they have a permanent 5/7 flier on turn 2 that drew two cards (ETB twice) and draws extra whenever they are hellbent ("As long as you have one or fewer cards in hand, if you would draw ... you draw that many cards plus one instead"). Springleaf Drum (turn 2 onward, tapping Momo) enables double-spell turns so Sage of the Skies' cast trigger copies it ("When you cast this spell, if you've cast another spell this turn, copy this spell"). Practiced Offense (+1/+1 counter on the team plus double strike or lifelink on their best flier, flashback {1}{W}) and Stirring Hopesinger's Repartee (a team counter per creature-targeting instant/sorcery) close in the air by turn 5-6. Glen Elendra Guardian (flash, "{1}{U}, Remove a counter: Counter target noncreature spell. Its controller draws a card") stops our removal, our counterspells and a hard-cast Kaito; Erode ({W}, 3 main + 1 side, "Destroy target creature or planeswalker") kills our Kaito and any Wasp or Tidebinder whose blank is holding their board down.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Real targets, narrow windows, and most of them open late. Their engine is static (Momo's discount), spells (Daydream, Practiced Offense, Erode), a mana ability (Springleaf Drum), or warp bodies that leave at end step anyway; Tidebinder touches none of that. Its best outcome by far is countering the re-entry draw of a Quantum Riddler that is staying (after Daydream or a 5-mana hard cast): that blanks their single best card (flying, ETB, and the extra-draw replacement all gone). Second is countering a Glen Elendra Guardian activation so our removal, counterspell or hard-cast Kaito resolves and Guardian is a vanilla 3/4 afterwards. Third and fourth are the Sage of the Skies copy trigger and Stirring Hopesinger's Repartee (Hopesinger is MV 3, so Requiting Hex cannot answer it; Tidebinder or Wasp is a real answer there). Timing is the problem: the first Daydreamed Riddler and the first Sage double-spell usually happen on their turn 2-3, before we can cast a 3-mana flash creature on their turn (their turn 3 on the play, their turn 4 on the draw). So Tidebinder lives on the second Daydream (flashback {2}{W}), the hard-cast Riddler, a Guardian activation, or a late Sage. The 3/2 ground body is poor against fliers and Erode ends every blank for {W}. One copy, used as a scalpel. Spider-Sense (1 side) is the only card in their 75 that can stop the trigger; Disdainful Stroke only hits MV 4+ and cannot touch Tidebinder or Wasp, and Guardian only counters noncreature spells, so Tidebinder itself always resolves.
#1Quantum Riddler that is staying (Daydream re-entry or 5-mana hard cast)
When this creature enters, draw a card.
WhyA permanent Riddler is the card that beats us: a 5/7 flier (4/6 if hard cast) out of Requiting Hex range, immune to Day of Black Sun X=3 (MV 5), and a two-cards-per-draw engine once they are hellbent ('you draw that many cards plus one instead', a replacement effect Tidebinder can only remove by blanking the whole permanent). Countering the ETB denies the draw, removes flying so Dream Beavers, Spyglass Siren and Wasp go over it, and removes the replacement draw. It is a creature when the trigger resolves, so it loses all abilities (ruling 3 satisfied).
WhenAfter Daydream resolves (the Riddler re-enters as a new object with a +1/+1 counter and its ETB draw goes on the stack) or after a hard cast resolves: flash Tidebinder targeting the draw trigger. Realistic from their turn 3 on the play / their turn 4 on the draw, so this is mostly the SECOND Daydream (flashback {2}{W} from the graveyard), a later Daydream from hand, or a hard-cast Riddler. The turn-2 Daydreamed Riddler is Wasp's job (see Wasp target 1). Never target a warp-cast Riddler's ETB (see traps).
AfterLoses: flying, the ETB, the extra-draw replacement effect. Keeps: 4/6 or 5/7 body and its counters. Still a ground wall; a Practiced Offense cast AFTER the blank still grants double strike or lifelink (ruling 2). To remove it for good: Bitter Triumph, or Kaito -2 / Floodpits Drowner ETB for a stun counter and then Drowner's '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it' (the stun counter is required; the blank does not enable the shuffle).
RiskTheir next Daydream (flashback counts) makes it a new object with everything back. Erode ({W}) on Tidebinder returns its abilities immediately. Spider-Sense can counter Tidebinder's trigger.
#2Glen Elendra Guardian
{1}{U}, Remove a counter from this creature: Counter target noncreature spell. Its controller draws a card.
WhyGuardian stops Bitter Triumph, Requiting Hex, Shoot the Sheriff, Spell Snare, We Say Thee Nay!, Day of Black Sun and a HARD-CAST Kaito (a planeswalker spell is a noncreature spell; a ninjutsu'd Kaito is not cast and cannot be countered). It activates in response to removal, so our spells cannot answer it while it has a counter. Tidebinder is the clean counter-the-counter: our spell resolves, they lose the {1}{U} and the counter they paid, and Guardian is a vanilla 3/4 while Tidebinder lives. Note 'Its controller draws a card' refers to the countered SPELL's controller: if Guardian's ability resolves, WE draw the consolation card; countering it with Tidebinder forfeits that draw, which is fine because the spell resolving is worth more.
WhenReactive, usually on our turn: we cast a noncreature spell with {2}{U} still open; they activate Guardian targeting it (the -1/-1 counter is removed as a cost, so Guardian is now a 3/4); we flash Tidebinder targeting the activation. Tidebinder's ETB resolves first: activation countered, our spell then resolves. Realistic from turn 5 (2-mana removal plus 3 for Tidebinder). Guardian has flash: if they flash it in during our spell and activate, the same sequence works.
AfterLoses: flying, flash (moot), and the counter ability, so it cannot counter anything while Tidebinder is on the battlefield. Keeps: 3/4 body (the -1/-1 counter is gone, spent as cost). Ammunition note: 'Remove a counter' means ANY counter. A +1/+1 counter from Practiced Offense, Repartee or Abandoned Air Temple landing on a Guardian that still has its -1/-1 counter annihilates with it (CR 704.5q), disarming Guardian; a later +1/+1 counter re-arms it.
RiskErode or Get Lost on Tidebinder returns Guardian's abilities (but not the spent counter). Spider-Sense can counter Tidebinder's trigger, and Spider-Sense itself is a noncreature spell they can protect with Guardian's second counter if it has one.
#3Sage of the Skies
When you cast this spell, if you've cast another spell this turn, copy this spell. (The copy becomes a token.)
WhyThe copy is their main two-for-one: a second 2/3 flying lifelink body that also pumps Momo. Countering the trigger costs us 3 mana to remove a 3-mana token, plus we keep a 3/2 body. The token copy has mana value 3 (copies keep the mana cost), so Requiting Hex cannot answer it afterwards; Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff can.
WhenTheir main phase, after they have already cast a spell this turn (intervening if: the trigger only goes on the stack if they cast something earlier). Sage spell on the stack, copy trigger above it; flash Tidebinder targeting the trigger before it resolves. The first Sage double-spell is usually their turn 2 with Springleaf Drum, before we can afford Tidebinder on their turn; this window is realistically their turn 4 onward.
AfterThe trigger's source is the Sage SPELL on the stack, not a permanent, so Sage itself loses nothing (ruling 3): it resolves as a normal 2/3 flying lifelink and Momo still gets +1/+1. You only deny the copy.
RiskA Sage cast as their first spell of the turn has no trigger to counter.
#4Stirring Hopesinger
Repartee, Whenever you cast an instant or sorcery spell that targets a creature, put a +1/+1 counter on each creature you control.
WhyHopesinger is {2}{W}, MV 3: Requiting Hex cannot touch it, so blanking it is a real answer, not a waste of a Hex target. Every Daydream, Practiced Offense, and any Erode or Get Lost aimed at one of our creatures becomes a team pump; lifelink on a 1/3 also skews the race. Counter one Repartee instance and Hopesinger is a 1/3 ground body with no lifelink and no Repartee while Tidebinder lives. Erode or Get Lost aimed at Kaito during THEIR turn does not trigger Repartee (Kaito is a planeswalker then, not a creature).
WhenTheir turn (Daydream and Practiced Offense are sorceries) or in response to Erode/Get Lost on one of our creatures: the spell goes on the stack, Repartee triggers above it; flash Tidebinder targeting the Repartee trigger. The spell itself still resolves (Practiced Offense's own counters still land, Erode still kills). With two Hopesingers out you counter one trigger and the other still pumps. No prior spell is needed for this window, unlike Sage.
AfterLoses: flying, lifelink, Repartee. Keeps: 1/3 body and every +1/+1 counter already on it. Counters already placed on the rest of the team stay.
RiskDaydream on Hopesinger later makes it a new object and restores everything. Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff (Bird Bard is not an outlaw) kill it permanently for 2 mana if you have them.
#5Starfield Shepherd that is staying (Daydream re-entry or 5-mana hard cast)
When this creature enters, search your library for a basic Plains card or a creature card with mana value 1 or less, reveal it, put it into your hand, then shuffle.
WhySame logic as Riddler, smaller stakes: a Shepherd kept with Daydream is a permanent 4/3 flier (3/2 base plus the Daydream counter) that just tutored Momo or Flitterwing Nuisance a second time. MV 5 (warp does not change mana value), so Day of Black Sun X=3 misses it and Requiting Hex cannot hit it. Countering the re-entry search blanks a permanent flier and denies the tutor. EXCEPTION: with Wan Shi Tong, Librarian on our battlefield, let the search resolve ('Whenever an opponent searches their library, put a +1/+1 counter on Wan Shi Tong and draw a card').
WhenAfter Daydream resolves on a Shepherd (or after a rare hard cast): flash Tidebinder targeting the search trigger. Never on a warp-cast Shepherd (leaves at end step).
AfterLoses: flying, the ETB. Keeps: 3/2 or 4/3 body and counters.
RiskTheir next Daydream resets it; Erode on Tidebinder resets it.
#6Steamcore Scholar
When this creature enters, draw two cards. Then discard two cards unless you discard an instant or sorcery card or a creature card with flying.
WhyDenies a 2-card dig and leaves a 2/2 ground body with no vigilance. Fine when the window is free; not worth holding mana for (2 copies).
WhenTheir main phase, ETB on the stack, flash Tidebinder targeting it.
AfterLoses flying, vigilance, the ETB. Keeps the 2/2 body.
RiskLow value; Daydream resets.
#7Momo, Friendly Flier
Whenever another creature you control with flying enters, Momo gets +1/+1 until end of turn.
WhyThe pump is trivial; the point is that countering it is the only way Tidebinder can switch off Momo's static discount, which is otherwise untouchable, and with the discount gone their warps cost 2 again. Usually Requiting Hex for {B} is the right answer to a 1/1 (Momo is MV 1), so only do this when Hex is not in hand and you wanted the 3/2 body anyway.
WhenTheir turn, when a flier of theirs enters and Momo's trigger is on the stack; flash Tidebinder targeting it.
AfterLoses: flying, the discount, the pump trigger. Keeps: 1/1 body.
RiskSpending 3 mana on a 1-mana creature that Hex kills outright.
#8Flitterwing Nuisance
{2}{U}, Remove a counter from this creature: Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player or planeswalker this turn, draw a card.
WhyOne copy; their 3 mana already spent and the counter gone. Countering the activation leaves a 2/2 with no flying and denies the draws. Removal mapping: Nuisance is a Faerie Rogue (an outlaw), so Shoot the Sheriff cannot target it, but Requiting Hex can (MV 1).
WhenTheir precombat main when they activate; flash Tidebinder targeting the activation.
AfterLoses flying and the activated ability. Keeps 2/2 body (counter already removed as cost).
RiskLow priority; only if you have nothing else for the mana.
#9Abandoned Air Temple
{3}{W}, {T}: Put a +1/+1 counter on each creature you control.
WhyCounterable, and 4 of their mana for 3 of ours is a fine trade late, but the source is a land so nothing loses abilities. Only when the pump would kill our blockers or swing a race.
WhenWhenever they activate (usually precombat on their turn): flash Tidebinder targeting the activation. The {3}{W} and the tap stay paid.
AfterNothing changes on the land; it can activate again next turn.
RiskYou spent your Tidebinder on a land.
Traps
Momo, Friendly Flier: Counter the cost reduction when they cast a cheap flier or a 1-mana warp. 'costs {1} less to cast' is a static ability; nothing goes on the stack. The only counterable thing on Momo is the +1/+1 trigger (target 7), which does switch the discount off as a side effect of the blank.
Springleaf Drum: Counter the Drum activation to strand their turn-2 play. '{T}, Tap an untapped creature you control: Add one mana of any color' is a mana ability; Tidebinder's own reminder text says mana abilities can't be targeted.
Daydream / Practiced Offense / Erode / Get Lost (and their flashback casts): Counter the blink, the pump, or the removal with Tidebinder. These are spells, not abilities; flashback is a static that permits casting. Tidebinder counters nothing here. The only hooks are a Repartee trigger from Stirring Hopesinger (target 4) or the re-ETB of the blinked creature after Daydream resolves (targets 1 and 5). Use Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay! / Spell Snare (Get Lost is MV 2) for the spell itself.
Quantum Riddler / Starfield Shepherd cast for warp: Counter the warp exile trigger ('Exile this creature at the beginning of the next end step') so their cheap body stays. The end-step exile is a delayed triggered ability and is targetable, but countering it is a gift: they keep the body permanently for 1-2 mana, which is exactly what they pay Daydream to achieve (and it very likely keeps its abilities too, because the trigger's source is the resolved spell, not the permanent). Never target it. Also, blanking a warped creature does not stop the exile: a delayed trigger already created is not an ability of the permanent, so a Tidebinder-blanked warped Riddler still leaves at end step.
Quantum Riddler / Starfield Shepherd cast for warp: Counter the ETB of a body they just warped in. It is exiled at end step regardless. You spent 3 mana to deny one card or one tutor from a body that was leaving anyway. Save Tidebinder for a Riddler or Shepherd that is staying (Daydream re-entry or hard cast). Recasting a warped card from exile later costs its FULL mana cost ({3}{U}{U} / {3}{W}{W}, minus Momo's {1}), so the exiled body is not coming back cheaply.
Quantum Riddler: Counter the 'draw that many cards plus one' bonus draw. It is a replacement effect ('if you would draw ... instead'), not a trigger. It can only be removed by blanking Riddler via its ETB (Tidebinder) or via Wasp.
Sage of the Skies: Counter the copy once it is on the stack, or expect Sage itself to be blanked. Once the trigger resolves the copy is a spell, and Tidebinder cannot counter spells. And because the trigger's source is a spell on the stack, not a permanent, the Sage that resolves keeps flying and lifelink (ruling 3).
Glen Elendra Guardian / Flitterwing Nuisance: Counter 'This creature enters with a -1/-1 counter on it' to make it enter bigger or counterless. 'Enters with' is a replacement effect, not a trigger. Nothing to target.
Glen Elendra Guardian: Counter the activation to deny THEM the card draw. 'Its controller draws a card' means the controller of the countered spell, i.e. us. If Guardian's ability resolves, we draw the card. Countering the activation is still right (our spell resolves and Guardian goes vanilla), but the card we forfeit is ours, not theirs.
Starfield Shepherd: Counter the tutor while Wan Shi Tong, Librarian is on our battlefield. Wan Shi Tong: 'Whenever an opponent searches their library, put a +1/+1 counter on Wan Shi Tong and draw a card.' Let it resolve. (Erode's basic-land search does NOT trigger Wan Shi Tong: 'Its controller may search' means the controller of the destroyed permanent, i.e. us.)
Morningtide's Light (sideboard): Counter the 'At the beginning of the next end step, return those cards to the battlefield' delayed trigger. They cast it as a fog on their turn, exiling OUR creatures; countering the return strands our own creatures in exile forever. The source is the sorcery, so nothing loses abilities either. Only consider it if every exiled creature was theirs (a self-blink), which is rare.
Rest in Peace (sideboard): Counter the ETB 'exile all graveyards'. The source is an enchantment, so nothing loses abilities, and the replacement effect 'If a card or token would be put into a graveyard from anywhere, exile it instead' keeps working. You save only what is in the yards right now (Enduring Curiosity's return and their own flashbacks care). They probably do not board it in against us anyway.
Multiversal Passage / Hallowed Fountain / Abandoned Air Temple / Starting Town: Counter the 'enters tapped' or 'as this land enters, choose / pay 2 life' text. All static or replacement ('As this land enters'); nothing triggers.
Not worth it
Warp-cast Starfield Shepherd or Quantum Riddler ETB: it leaves at end step, and they run 4 Momo so denying one search rarely matters; the permanent (Daydreamed) version is the target.Momo's +1/+1 trigger when you hold Requiting Hex: Hex answers Momo (MV 1) for {B} and permanently; save Tidebinder.Abandoned Air Temple activation unless the pump changes combat this turn: the land keeps its ability and you traded a card for 4 of their mana.Flitterwing Nuisance's per-hit draw triggers: after its activation resolves, each 'whenever a creature you control deals combat damage ... draw a card' is a separate delayed trigger whose source is Nuisance, so Tidebinder can legally counter one draw and blank Nuisance; one copy, one card denied, not worth planning around.Get Lost's Map tokens ('{1}, {T}, Sacrifice this token: Target creature you control explores'): a counterable activated ability, but the Map is sacrificed as a cost so nothing is blanked and you deny one explore. Never.Any ETB of a creature you are about to Day of Black Sun anyway: X=3 kills every creature in their 75 with MV 3 or less (Momo, Nuisance, Guardian, Sage and its token, Scholar, Hopesinger); only Quantum Riddler and Starfield Shepherd (both MV 5) survive, and a live Guardian can counter the sweeper.
The Wondrous Wasp
Cheap, and it fits the matchup: a 2/1 flash flier blocks and trades in a deck of 1/1 and 2/3 fliers, there is no hexproof or ward in their 75, and the blank removes static and replacement abilities that Tidebinder can only reach by catching a trigger window: Momo's discount, Riddler's extra-draw replacement, Guardian's whole counter plan (Tidebinder needs Guardian to activate first). Two things make Wasp the more important of our two cards here. First, it is the only on-curve answer to their turn-2 Daydreamed Riddler: castable on their turn 2 on the play, on our turn 2 or their turn 3 on the draw, well before Tidebinder is castable on their turn. Second, Guardian can only counter noncreature spells, so it cannot stop Wasp or Wasp's trigger; only their single Spider-Sense can. Two hard limits: The Wondrous Wasp is LEGENDARY, so only one Wasp blank can exist at a time (a second Wasp forces the legend rule before its trigger even goes on the stack; you keep one Wasp and one blank), which makes the second copy redundancy against Erode, not a second lock. And everything they do costs {W}: Erode kills Wasp and ends the blank instantly, and Daydream on the blanked creature makes it a new object with a +1/+1 counter and a fresh ETB, twice per Daydream (flashback {2}{W}). A Wasp blank is a tempo play, not a lock.
#1Quantum Riddler that is staying (after Daydream or a hard cast)
WhyTheir best card and the one Tidebinder usually cannot reach in time. Blanking removes flying (Beavers, Siren and Wasp fly over a 5/7 ground wall) and the 'draw that many cards plus one' replacement effect, so their hellbent card engine is off while Wasp lives. Tapping it in their beginning of combat also stops a 5-power (or double-strike) flying attack. Riddler is MV 5: Hex cannot touch it, Day of Black Sun X=3 misses it; the permanent answers are Bitter Triumph, or a stun counter (Kaito -2 or Floodpits Drowner ETB) followed by Drowner's shuffle ability.
WhenBest: right after Daydream resolves on their turn 2-3 (the new Riddler is on the battlefield; its ETB draw is on the stack or has resolved; Wasp taps and blanks the new object). On the draw, if you have only one land on their turn 2, cast Wasp on your own turn 2 targeting it: the tap is wasted but the blank is what matters. Later: their beginning of combat step, before attackers, to stop an attack. Never on a warp-cast Riddler (leaves at end step).
AfterLoses flying, ETB, replacement draw. Keeps body and counters. Practiced Offense cast AFTER the blank still grants double strike or lifelink (ruling 2); Offense cast BEFORE Wasp loses its grant (see trap and target 5).
#2Glen Elendra Guardian (already on the battlefield with a counter)
WhyBlank it and it cannot activate '{1}{U}, Remove a counter from this creature: Counter target noncreature spell' while Wasp lives, so Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff, our counterspells and a hard-cast Kaito all resolve. They cannot fight this with Guardian: Wasp is a creature spell and its trigger is an ability, not a spell.
WhenOur main phase, BEFORE casting the noncreature spell we want to protect, or at their end step. Wasp ETB targets Guardian; once it resolves AND Wasp is still on the battlefield, cast the spell. If they Erode Wasp with the trigger still on the stack, the tap still happens but the blank never begins (its duration has already ended, same logic as Tidebinder ruling 1), so re-check the board before casting the protected spell. Do not cast the removal first and Wasp in response: that is Tidebinder's job (countering the activation); Wasp does not counter.
AfterLoses flying, flash, the counter ability. Keeps the -1/-1 counter (2/3) and stays tapped this turn. Daydream is NOT a clean reset here: the returned Guardian enters with Daydream's +1/+1 counter and its own -1/-1 counter, they annihilate (CR 704.5q), and it is a 3/4 flier with full abilities but NO counter, so it cannot activate until Practiced Offense, Repartee or Air Temple gives it one. Same for Flitterwing Nuisance.
#3Momo, Friendly Flier
WhyWasp removes the static 'costs {1} less' that Tidebinder cannot reach directly, plus flying and the pump; blanked at their upkeep, their whole turn is a mana short and their warps cost 2 again. A tapped, blanked Momo also cannot block Siren or Beavers. Weigh against Requiting Hex: Hex for {B} is permanent and cheaper and Momo (MV 1) is one of only two Hex targets in their 75 (the other is Flitterwing Nuisance, also MV 1). Use Wasp on Momo when no Hex is in hand.
WhenTheir upkeep (after untap, before draw) if the goal is the mana tax for their turn; beginning of our combat if the goal is a free attack for Siren/Beavers.
AfterLoses flying, the discount, the +1/+1 trigger. Keeps 1/1 body and any counters.
#4Stirring Hopesinger
WhyMV 3, so Hex cannot kill it; blanking before their sorcery turn means Daydream and Practiced Offense no longer pump the team, and lifelink gone makes the race winnable. Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff kill it permanently if you have them; Wasp is the answer when you do not.
WhenTheir upkeep or our end step, so the blank is in place before their main phase. Blanking in response to a Repartee trigger does nothing to that trigger (Wasp does not counter; the trigger already exists and resolves).
AfterLoses flying, lifelink, Repartee. Keeps 1/3 body and existing counters.
#5Sage of the Skies (or its token copy), especially the one they just gave double strike or lifelink
WhyThe lifelink flier they most often point Practiced Offense at. Practiced Offense is a sorcery, so any grant meant for this combat is already on the creature by their beginning of combat. Wasp then has the LATER timestamp in layer 6, so 'loses all abilities' removes the granted double strike or lifelink along with printed flying and lifelink, and the tap keeps it out of the attack. The +1/+1 counters stay. They cannot respond with another Offense at beginning of combat.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, after Practiced Offense has resolved and before attackers are declared. Casting Wasp BEFORE the Offense is the wrong order: a grant gained after the blank began is kept (ruling 2).
AfterLoses flying, lifelink, and any earlier Offense grant. Keeps 2/3 body and counters. Sage's token copy is MV 3, so Hex is not an option for it; Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff (Human Monk) are.
#6Any single untapped flier of theirs on OUR turn
WhyPure tempo: tap their only flying blocker at our beginning of combat so Spyglass Siren / Dream Beavers connect, which is how Enduring Curiosity draws and how Kaito ninjutsus in. The Wasp cast this combat is summoning sick and cannot attack itself.
WhenOur beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared.
AfterWhatever that creature was, it is a tapped body with no abilities until Wasp dies or they Daydream it.
Traps
Any attacking creature: Flash Wasp after attackers are declared to tap the attacker. Tapping does not remove a creature from combat; it still deals damage. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step (or earlier).
The Wondrous Wasp (second copy): Cast the second Wasp while the first is on the battlefield to hold two blanks. Wasp is legendary. When the second enters, the legend rule (a state-based action) puts one of them in the graveyard BEFORE the new trigger is put on the stack. Keep the new one and the old blank ends; keep the old one and the new trigger still taps its target but the 'for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield' blank never begins (that Wasp is already gone). One Wasp, one blank. The second copy is for after the first dies to Erode, or as a tap-plus-legend-rule trick when you only want the tap.
Quantum Riddler / Starfield Shepherd cast for warp: Wasp the warped body. It is exiled at the beginning of the next end step by a delayed trigger that is not one of its abilities, so it leaves whether or not it is blanked. If they Daydream it in response, your Wasp did nothing anyway (new object). Wait until after Daydream resolves and Wasp the new object.
Daydream (main, and flashback from the graveyard): Treat a Wasp blank as permanent. 'Exile target creature you control, then return that card to the battlefield' makes a new object: the blank ends, it gets a +1/+1 counter, its ETB fires again, Momo triggers again. Each Daydream does this twice (flashback {2}{W}). If the blank matters, be ready to kill the Daydream target in response (Bitter Triumph for Riddler/Shepherd/Sage/Hopesinger/Guardian/Scholar, Requiting Hex only for Momo or Nuisance) so Daydream fizzles; Hopesinger's Repartee trigger still resolves. The exception is Guardian or Nuisance: Daydream disarms them (counters annihilate), so a blank there needs no protection from Daydream.
Practiced Offense: Wasp the creature first, then expect the double strike or lifelink to fizzle when they cast Offense. Abilities gained AFTER Wasp's effect began are kept (ruling 2), and the counters land regardless. The right order is the reverse: Offense is a sorcery, so wait for their beginning of combat; Wasp then has the later timestamp and strips the granted double strike or lifelink along with flying, and the tap keeps the creature out of the attack. Counters stay either way.
Erode / Get Lost: Assume the blank sticks through their turn, or that a resolved Wasp trigger guarantees the blank. Erode is {W} and kills Wasp; the blanked creature gets everything back immediately. Worse, Erode in response to Wasp's trigger means the creature is tapped but never loses its abilities (the blank's duration has already ended when it would begin). If the blank is load-bearing (Guardian before our removal, Riddler before our attack), sequence it on OUR turn with the follow-up in the same main phase after confirming Wasp is still on the battlefield, or hold Spell Pierce / We Say Thee Nay! for the Erode.
Spider-Sense (sideboard, 1 copy): Nothing they can do about a Wasp blank once it resolves. Web-slinging {U} lets them return a TAPPED creature they control to hand as part of the cost; the Wasp-tapped, blanked creature is perfect fodder, resetting it to a recast while countering our next triggered ability (Tidebinder, Wasp, Drowner, Curiosity draw, Beavers drain, Wan Shi Tong) for one mana.
Glen Elendra Guardian in their hand: Wasp pre-empts a Guardian they have not cast yet. Guardian has flash; they cast it in response to our removal and activate immediately. Wasp handles a Guardian already on the battlefield; a flashed-in Guardian is a Tidebinder job (counter the activation).
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Turn-2 Riddler answer: they warp Riddler for {U} under Momo and Daydream it for {W}. On the play, flash Wasp after Daydream resolves (their turn 2, our 2 lands) to tap and blank the permanent 5/7: no flying, no extra-draw engine. On the draw, do it on our turn 2 or their turn 3. Bitter Triumph it on a later turn, or stun it (Kaito -2 / Drowner ETB) and shuffle it with Floodpits Drowner. Tidebinder is for the SECOND Daydream (flashback {2}{W}) or a hard-cast Riddler, when we have {2}{U} open on their turn.
  • Wasp then remove: our main phase, Wasp targeting an on-board Glen Elendra Guardian (they cannot respond with Guardian: creature spell, ability trigger). Confirm Wasp survived (Erode in response leaves the tap but no blank), then Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff the Daydreamed Riddler or the pumped Sage, or hard-cast Kaito.
  • Counter the counter: cast our removal, counterspell or hard-cast Kaito with {2}{U} still open. If Guardian activates, flash Tidebinder targeting the activation: our spell resolves, they lose the mana and the counter, we forfeit only the consolation draw (which would have been ours), and Guardian is a vanilla 3/4 while Tidebinder lives. While an armed Guardian is on board and neither line is available, ninjutsu Kaito instead of casting him (ninjutsu is not a spell, Guardian cannot counter it).
  • Open the skies for Kaito: at our beginning of combat, Wasp taps their only untapped flier. Attack with Spyglass Siren / Dream Beavers (the Wasp cast this combat is summoning sick and stays home); with an unblocked attacker, ninjutsu Kaito, Bane of Nightmares ({1}{U}{B}) and Enduring Curiosity draws off each connect. Their blockers are all fliers, so this is usually the only way a ninjutsu window opens.
  • Blanked equals blockable: any creature Wasp or Tidebinder has blanked has no flying, so our ground bodies (Tidebinder 3/2, Enduring Curiosity 4/3 while a creature, Floodpits Drowner 2/1, Soulstone Sanctuary as a 3/3 for {4}) can block it and our fliers go over it. Kaito can never block: he is a creature only during OUR turn, and blocking happens on theirs.
  • Offense then Wasp: when they precombat Practiced Offense onto a flier, flash Wasp at their beginning of combat targeting that creature. The tap stops the attack and the later-timestamp blank strips the granted double strike or lifelink along with flying. Never Wasp first and hope the Offense does nothing (gained-after abilities are kept).
  • Wan Shi Tong farms Shepherd: with Wan Shi Tong, Librarian on the battlefield, never counter Starfield Shepherd's ETB search; it gives us a +1/+1 counter and a card. Erode's land search does not trigger him (we are the searcher).
  • Fizzle the Daydream: Daydream undoes our stun counters (Kaito -2, Drowner) and our blanks. If they Daydream a stunned or blanked creature that matters, Bitter Triumph the target in response (Requiting Hex only works on Momo or Nuisance, MV 1) and Daydream fizzles; Stirring Hopesinger's Repartee trigger still resolves, counter that with Tidebinder if the team pump would matter. A Daydreamed Guardian or Nuisance comes back counterless and disarmed (+1/+1 and -1/-1 annihilate), so do not spend a spell protecting a blank on those.
  • Sideboard shape: Day of Black Sun X=3 (5 mana) kills every creature in their 75 with MV 3 or less; Quantum Riddler and Starfield Shepherd (MV 5) survive, and an armed Guardian or Disdainful Stroke (MV 4+ on the stack) can counter it, so Wasp the Guardian first or bait the Stroke. Duress takes Daydream / Practiced Offense / Erode. Spell Pierce protects a load-bearing Wasp or Tidebinder from a {W} Erode. Spell Snare hits Get Lost, Rest in Peace, Disdainful Stroke, Spider-Sense (all MV 2). Flashfreeze does nothing (no red or green). Annul only hits Springleaf Drum and Rest in Peace: leave it out. Their Disdainful Stroke hits Kaito, Enduring Curiosity, Day of Black Sun and Wan Shi Tong (X of 2+), never Tidebinder or Wasp; Spider-Sense is the only card in their 75 that can stop our ETB triggers.
  • Their double-spell turn with {2}{U} up (turn 4+): wait for the second spell. If it is Sage of the Skies, Tidebinder the copy trigger; if it is Daydream with Hopesinger out, Tidebinder the Repartee trigger; if it is Daydream on a Riddler or Shepherd, let Daydream resolve and Tidebinder the re-entry ETB so the now-permanent flier is blanked.
Hold or fire
Tidebinder: hold {2}{U} for a window only when one of these is true: (a) a Glen Elendra Guardian with a counter is on their board and you intend to cast a noncreature spell (removal, counter, hard-cast Kaito) this turn; (b) it is their turn, Daydream is in their graveyard (flashback) or likely in hand, and a Riddler or Shepherd is on the battlefield or in exile, so a permanent-flier re-entry ETB is likely; (c) it is their turn, they have already cast one spell, and Sage of the Skies is likely (copy trigger needs a prior spell); (d) Stirring Hopesinger is on the battlefield and they are about to cast a creature-targeting instant or sorcery (no prior spell needed). Turns 2-4 against this deck are for Hex on Momo/Nuisance, Drowner, Wasp and developing, not for holding a 3-mana reactive body: the early Sage copy and the first Daydreamed Riddler happen before Tidebinder is castable on their turn. If no window is coming, cast it at their end step as a 3/2 body with no target rather than waste the mana, or spend the mana on Kaito, Drowner or removal. Never point it at a warped creature's ETB or exile trigger, a land, or a spell. Wasp: fire right after Daydream resolves on their early Riddler (their turn 2 on the play; our turn 2 or their turn 3 on the draw); fire pre-emptively on OUR turn when the blank protects a spell (Guardian) and re-check Wasp is alive before casting it; fire at THEIR upkeep when the blank taxes their turn (Momo without Hex in hand, Hopesinger before their sorceries); fire at THEIR beginning of combat, after Practiced Offense has resolved, when the goal is stopping an attack and stripping the grant (Riddler, Sage); fire at OUR beginning of combat when the goal is connecting (Curiosity draws, ninjutsu). Never fire on a warped body, never after attackers are declared, and never cast the second Wasp while the first is alive expecting two blanks (legend rule: one Wasp, one blank). On the play: you are a turn ahead; Wasp answers their turn-2 Riddler on their turn, Tidebinder is held for windows (a)/(b) from turn 5. On the draw: lifelink fliers punish slow starts; Wasp is a flash flying blocker first (trade with Momo/Nuisance, chump Sage) and a blank second, and Tidebinder goes down as a body at their end step whenever the mana would otherwise go unused. In every case, assume Erode ({W}) can end any blank at any time: never build a turn that dies to one white mana.
How many to run
