Keep both The Wondrous Wasp in the main (there is no third copy anywhere in the 75, so the question is only whether to cut one: no). Wasp is the on-curve answer to their turn-2 Daydreamed Riddler, a flash flying blocker in an all-flier matchup, immune to Guardian, and it blanks the statics (Momo discount, Riddler draw engine, Guardian counter plan) Tidebinder needs a trigger window to reach. Because Wasp is legendary the two copies are redundancy against Erode (3-4 copies in their 75), not two simultaneous blanks; that redundancy is exactly what you want against a deck full of {W} removal. Keep 1 Tishana's Tidebinder main and leave both sideboard copies out: its windows (permanent Riddler/Shepherd ETB, Guardian activation, Sage copy, Repartee) are real but open late, most of their engine is static, spell, mana ability or a warp body that leaves anyway, the 3/2 ground body is poor against fliers, and 3-mana reactive plays are slow against a deck of {W} and {1}{W} threats. Sideboard slots go to Day of Black Sun (X=3 clears everything but Riddler/Shepherd; Wasp the Guardian first), Duress (Daydream / Practiced Offense / Erode), and Spell Pierce (protects blanks and Kaito from Erode / Get Lost).
Review trail: confidence high, 5 disputed, 22 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Reviewer 2: 'Hopesinger is a 2-mana card: Requiting Hex kills it permanently for {B}' and 'Hex answers Momo, Hopesinger and Nuisance only'. Rejected: Stirring Hopesinger's mana cost in the deck file is {2}{W} (MV 3) and Requiting Hex reads 'Destroy target creature with mana value 2 or less.' Hex cannot target Hopesinger. Hopesinger therefore stays a legitimate Tidebinder/Wasp target when Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff is not in hand (reviewer 3 got this right).
  • Reviewer 2: 'Flitterwing Nuisance ... Requiting Hex (MV 2) can'. Nuisance's mana cost is {U}, MV 1. The conclusion (Hex can hit it) stands; the stated MV was wrong and is not repeated.
  • Reviewer 2: 'Starfield Shepherd is a 4-mana card ... survives X=3 (MV 4)'. Shepherd's mana cost is {3}{W}{W}, MV 5. The conclusion (survives Day of Black Sun X=3) stands, and the entry uses MV 5 (reviewer 3 got this right).
  • Reviewer 2: 'Hopesinger: Hex it. Tidebinder the Repartee trigger only when no Hex is in hand'. Rejected for the same reason: Hex is never an option on Hopesinger. The ordering change (Riddler first, Guardian second) was accepted on its merits; the Hopesinger demotion argument was not.
  • Reviewer 1: 'Momo's discount ... Tidebinder can reach it, just only via the trigger window' was accepted as a wording fix, but the Wasp summary keeps the point that Wasp reaches Guardian's counter plan without Guardian having to activate first, which Tidebinder cannot do (Guardian has no triggered abilities).
Changes after review
  • FATAL fixed: removed Kaito from the list of blockers in 'Blanked equals blockable'; Kaito is a creature only 'During your turn' and blocking happens on the opponent's turn. Added Drowner and Soulstone Sanctuary as ground blockers.
  • Fixed Glen Elendra Guardian's consolation draw: 'Its controller draws a card' is the countered spell's controller (us). Tidebinder target, trap list and combined line rewritten; added a new trap entry for it.
  • Fixed Daydream on Guardian/Nuisance: the Daydream +1/+1 counter and the 'enters with a -1/-1 counter' annihilate (CR 704.5q), so the returned Guardian is a 3/4 flier with no counter and cannot activate until re-armed. Applied in Wasp target 2, Daydream trap, and Fizzle-the-Daydream line.
  • Fixed Day of Black Sun: verified oracle 'Each creature with mana value X or less loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures.' X=3 misses both Quantum Riddler (MV 5) and Starfield Shepherd (MV 5), and an armed Guardian or Disdainful Stroke can counter it. Applied in dont_bother, combined lines and counts.
  • Fixed Requiting Hex targets: 'mana value 2 or less' hits only Momo (MV 1) and Flitterwing Nuisance (MV 1). Removed every claim that Hex answers Sage or Hopesinger; Daydream-fizzle line now names Bitter Triumph. Added Shoot the Sheriff cannot target Nuisance (Faerie Rogue = outlaw).
  • Fixed how_they_win: Momo's discount applies to warp costs (CR 601.2f), so warp Riddler costs {U} and warp Shepherd costs {W}; nut line is T1 Momo, T2 warp Riddler + Daydream = permanent 5/7 on turn 2. Removed the impossible turn-1 Momo + Springleaf Drum.
  • Re-ordered Tidebinder targets: permanent Riddler ETB is now #1, Guardian activation #2, Sage copy #3, Repartee #4; timing notes now say the early windows (their T2-3) close before Tidebinder is castable on their turn, so its realistic windows are the flashback Daydream, hard-cast Riddler, Guardian activation and late Sage.
  • Re-ordered Wasp targets: permanent Riddler #1 (with the on-play/on-draw turn math), Guardian #2, Momo #3 (when Hex is not in hand), Hopesinger #4, Sage #5, tempo tap #6.
  • Added Wasp legend rule: only one Wasp blank can exist; second Wasp forces the legend rule before its trigger is put on the stack; new trap entry; reflected in Wasp summary, hold_or_fire and counts (second copy = redundancy vs Erode).
  • Fixed 'Open the skies for Kaito' and Wasp target 6: the Wasp cast at our beginning of combat is summoning sick and cannot attack; attackers are Siren/Beavers only.
  • Rewrote the Practiced Offense trap and Wasp target 5: Offense is a sorcery, so Wasp at their beginning of combat has the later layer-6 timestamp and strips the granted double strike/lifelink; Wasp BEFORE Offense is the wrong order (ruling 2). Added a combined line for it.
  • Added Erode-in-response-to-Wasp-trigger: tap resolves, blank never begins (duration already ended, same logic as Tidebinder ruling 1); pilot must confirm Wasp is alive before casting the protected spell. Applied in Wasp target 2, Erode trap, and Wasp-then-remove line.
  • Fixed Floodpits Drowner: its shuffle requires a stun counter on the target ('target creature with a stun counter on it'); the blank does not enable it. Stun sources named (Kaito -2, Drowner ETB).
  • Added Starfield Shepherd that is staying (Daydream re-entry / hard cast) as Tidebinder target 5 and mentioned as a Wasp option; MV 5 so it survives Day of Black Sun X=3; Wan Shi Tong exception carried over.
  • Added Kaito hard-cast is Guardian-counterable (planeswalker spell is a noncreature spell) and ninjutsu is not; reflected in Guardian target, hold_or_fire window (a), and the counter-the-counter line.
  • Added Repartee exception: Erode/Get Lost aimed at Kaito on THEIR turn targets a planeswalker and does not trigger Repartee.
  • Added Disdainful Stroke (MV 4+: hits Kaito, Curiosity, Day of Black Sun, Wan Shi Tong X 2+; never Tidebinder or Wasp) and Spider-Sense as the only trigger-stopper in their 75 to the sideboard line and Tidebinder summary; added Spell Snare's MV-2 targets.
  • Added dont_bother entries for Flitterwing Nuisance's per-hit delayed draw triggers (legal Tidebinder target, not worth it) and Get Lost's Map token activations (Map sacrificed as cost, nothing blanked).
  • Hedged the warp exile trap: the warped body probably keeps its abilities when its exile trigger is countered (source is the resolved spell, not the permanent); practical advice (never target it) unchanged. Fixed 'recast from exile' to note it costs the full mana cost.
  • Split hold_or_fire window (b) into separate Riddler/Shepherd re-entry, Sage (prior spell required) and Hopesinger (no prior spell required) conditions; moved the hold-turns to 5+ and told the pilot to develop on turns 2-4.
  • Verified Wan Shi Tong, Librarian oracle ('Whenever an opponent searches their library, put a +1/+1 counter on Wan Shi Tong and draw a card'); the Shepherd line stands and is now quoted.
  • Counts recommendation rewritten: there is no third Wasp in the 75; both Wasps stay as redundancy (legend rule); 1 Tidebinder main, sideboard copies stay out.
vs. Golgari Midrange2.1% TB: goodWasp: situational▼
A near-creatureless Golgari control deck: 4 Deceit is the only maindeck creature card, backed by 13 maindeck answers (3 Requiting Hex, 2 Shoot the Sheriff, 1 Bitter Triumph, 4 Deadly Cover-Up, 1 Maelstrom Pulse, 1 Nowhere to Run, 1 Professor Dellian Fel) and 3 Duress for our counterspells; postboard adds The End, 2 Day of Black Sun, Vicious Rivalry, Strategic Betrayal and a second Nowhere to Run. Card advantage comes from Unholy Annex ("At the beginning of your end step, draw a card...") from turn 3, Together as One converge (Firdoch Core, Verges, Watery Grave let them hit X=4-5: draw, burn any target including Kaito, gain X) from turn 6, and Deceit, either evoked for 2 as targeted discard (they see our hand and take our best nonland card) or hardcast for 6 as a 5/5 with the same trigger. They close with Ritual Chamber's 6/6 flying Demon, which also flips Annex into a 2-point drain each end step, plus Fel and the manlands (Restless Cottage/Reef, Great Hall, Ba Sing Se earthbend). Watch their mana: 4 Firdoch Core ({3}, taps for any color) and The Soul Stone ({1}{B}, taps for {B}) mean three lands plus a rock is 5 mana on THEIR TURN 4, so Ritual Chamber and Deadly Cover-Up arrive a turn earlier than the land count suggests and hardcast Deceit on their turn 5. Because almost every payoff is a triggered ability from a permanent that is NOT an artifact/creature/planeswalker, Tidebinder's value here is the COUNTER half (the discard never happens, the Demon never exists); the "loses all abilities" lock is a bonus their removal usually ends within a turn.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Tidebinder is the only card in our 75 that stops Deceit's discard (nothing else in the deck does except countering a 6-mana creature spell with We Say Thee Nay!), it locks Professor Dellian Fel off any loyalty activation, and it can answer the Ritual Chamber Demon trigger when Requiting Hex or Floodpits Drowner are not in hand. Duress cannot take it and Requiting Hex (MV 2 or less) cannot kill it. Priorities 1-6 are the real list; 7-10 are take-it-if-free. Expect the 3/2 body to eat a Sheriff or Bitter Triumph; that is fine, the countered ability is gone for good.
#1Deceit (evoked for {U/B}{U/B}, paid with black)
"When this creature enters, if {B}{B} was spent to cast it, target opponent reveals their hand. You choose a nonland card from it. That player discards that card."
WhyTwo-mana targeted discard that takes our best card, and Tidebinder is our ONLY answer to the trigger (Spell Snare cannot hit Deceit, MV 6 even when evoked; Spell Pierce does not counter creatures; We Say Thee Nay! must counter the spell itself). Counter the discard and they have spent 2 mana and a card for nothing, because the evoke sacrifice still resolves. One-for-one on cards, but they get nothing and we keep a 3/2 flash body with our hand intact.
WhenDeceit enters; the discard trigger and the evoke trigger ("When this permanent enters, if its evoke cost was paid, its controller sacrifices it") both go on the stack in the order they choose. Respond with Tidebinder targeting the DISCARD trigger, never the sacrifice. They choose when to evoke, so this is not a window you can schedule: they will do it when you are tapped out. Fire at the first one you can hit.
AfterDeceit loses all abilities while Tidebinder lives, but it is sacrificed anyway, so that clause is irrelevant. Our hand is untouched.
RiskIf they paid {U}{U} instead (2 Watery Grave, 2 Restless Reef, 4 Firdoch Core and Multiversal Passage-as-Island make it possible, though they will almost always choose {B}{B}), the trigger to counter is the bounce: "return up to one other target nonland permanent to its owner's hand", aimed at Kaito or Enduring Curiosity.
#2Deceit (hardcast for {4}{U/B}{U/B})
Same ETBs. Paid with at least {B}{B}: the discard trigger. Paid with at least {U}{U}: the bounce trigger. Paid with both {U}{U} and {B}{B} among the six mana: BOTH triggers go on the stack (each has its own intervening-if clause).
WhySix mana normally buys a 5/5 plus our best card (or plus a bounced Kaito). Countering leaves them a 5/5 with no live abilities and us our hand. Tidebinder is "up to one target", so when both triggers are stacked you must pick: counter the DISCARD (permanent card loss); a bounced Kaito or Curiosity can be recast. Exception: counter the bounce if it targets a Kaito that is about to ultimate or a Curiosity that is your only engine while your hand is already empty.
WhenDeceit resolves and enters; the ETB(s) go on the stack; flash Tidebinder targeting the one you chose. Realistically their turn 5 with a Core/Stone, turn 6 on lands alone.
AfterDeceit is a creature at resolution, so it loses all abilities while Tidebinder lives; it has no on-board abilities anyway, so it remains a 5/5 attacker. Answer it with Floodpits Drowner (tap and stun, then its shuffle-away ability), Kaito's -2 (tap, two stun counters), Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff (Elemental Incarnation is not an outlaw). Requiting Hex cannot target it (MV 6).
RiskIf they remove Tidebinder nothing changes: the countered trigger is gone for good; Deceit only regains abilities it never uses on board.
#3Professor Dellian Fel
Any loyalty ability: "+2: You gain 3 life." / "0: You draw a card and lose 1 life." / "−3: Destroy target creature." / "−6: You get an emblem with 'Whenever you gain life, target opponent loses that much life.'"
WhyCountering ANY loyalty activation strips Fel of all abilities while Tidebinder lives: he cannot activate again. Take whichever activation you can afford; do not wait for a specific one. The -3 is the best value (loyalty already paid 5 to 2, our creature survives, a 2-loyalty blank walker dies to any attack). If you happen to have {2}{U} open when he goes -6, take it: the emblem can never be removed once created and it turns every lifegain (Annex-with-Demon, +2, Together as One, Hex blight) into drain, and Fel sits at 1 with nothing to show. Do not hold mana across turns specifically for the -6; a 1-of walker is better handled by attacking him or Bitter Triumph.
WhenTheir main phase, sorcery speed: as soon as they activate (loyalty counters already added or removed), respond with Tidebinder targeting the ability on the stack.
AfterFel is a planeswalker at resolution, so he loses every ability until Tidebinder leaves. Loyalty counters stay. Kill him with Kaito/Siren/Wasp attacks or Bitter Triumph before they remove Tidebinder.
RiskOnly 1 copy in the main. If they answer Tidebinder, Fel wakes up; attack him immediately.
#4Unholy Annex // Ritual Chamber (Ritual Chamber door)
"When you unlock this door, create a 6/6 black Demon creature token with flying."
WhyTheir endgame in one trigger: 5 mana for a 6/6 flier that also turns Annex from "Otherwise, you lose 2 life" into a 2-point drain each end step. Countering it wastes the 5 mana and the Demon never exists. BUT our deck has cheaper answers to the token: Requiting Hex ({B}, the token is MV 0; 4 copies and otherwise nearly dead against this deck), Floodpits Drowner, Shoot the Sheriff (Demon is not an outlaw) and Kaito's -2. Rule: if you hold Hex or Drowner, spend that on the Demon and keep Tidebinder for Deceit/Fel. Tidebinder the Demon trigger only when it is your only answer in hand, or when the 3/2 body plus Enduring Curiosity draws matters.
WhenTwo ways it arrives, both on their main phase: (a) they cast the Ritual Chamber half for {3}{B}{B}, the Room enters with that door unlocked and the trigger goes on the stack; (b) they already have Annex in play and pay {3}{B}{B} as a sorcery to unlock the Chamber door (a special action that does not use the stack), then the trigger goes on the stack. Flash Tidebinder with the trigger on the stack. Key the window off their mana, not the turn: their turn 4 if they resolved a Firdoch Core or Soul Stone on lands, their turn 5 on lands alone.
AfterSource is an enchantment (Room), so it loses nothing (ruling 3). Case (b): the Annex door is unlocked, so it still draws them a card each end step but with no Demon the same trigger makes them lose 2 life. Case (a): only the Chamber door is unlocked; a locked Annex has no end-step trigger at all until they pay another {2}{B} as a sorcery to unlock it, so the Room does nothing. No 6/6 ever exists either way.
RiskOrdinary: they Sheriff/Triumph the 3/2 afterwards. Nothing about the counter is undone.
#5Restless Cottage / Restless Reef (attack trigger, NOT the animation)
Cottage: "Whenever this land attacks, create a Food token and exile up to one target card from a graveyard." Reef: "Whenever this land attacks, target player mills four cards."
WhyCounter the attack trigger, not the animation: at resolution the land is an animated 4/4 creature, so it loses all abilities while Tidebinder lives, including "{T}: Add {B} or {G}" / "{T}: Add {U} or {B}" and the {2}{B}{G} / {2}{U}{B} animation, and the effect keeps applying after it reverts to a land (the affected object is locked in when the effect begins). A Stone Rain stapled to a 3/2, against a hand full of 5- and 6-drops. Reef also loses the deathtouch its animation gave it.
WhenDeclare attackers step, attack trigger on the stack.
AfterStill a 4/4 attacking creature this turn (the resolved animation effect persists; Tidebinder does not remove it from combat); no Food, no exile, no mill. From end of turn onward it is a land with no abilities at all until Tidebinder leaves.
RiskYou still take or block the 4/4 this turn. Wasp does the same job for 2 mana if you act in their beginning of combat instead (see Wasp #2).
#6Firdoch Core
"{4}: This artifact becomes a 4/4 artifact creature until end of turn."
WhyCountering wastes 4 mana AND, because Core is an artifact at resolution, it loses all abilities while Tidebinder lives, including "{T}: Add one mana of any color" and changeling. That shuts off their rainbow source for Together as One converge and a whole land's worth of mana. Rare (they animate late as a blocker or finisher) but excellent when it happens; if they animate to block on OUR turn, Wasp does it for 2 mana instead.
WhenIn response to the animation activation, usually their beginning of combat or when they need a blocker on our turn.
AfterNot a creature this turn; no mana, no changeling, no animation until Tidebinder leaves.
RiskLow. Do not confuse with its mana ability, which can never be targeted.
#7Nowhere to Run (ETB)
"When this enchantment enters, target creature an opponent controls gets -3/-3 until end of turn."
WhyOnly worth it to save Floodpits Drowner (our stun engine) or when the -3/-3 would break a lethal or Curiosity-fuelled attack. Do not spend it to save Enduring Curiosity's body: Curiosity comes back as an enchantment when it dies and keeps drawing cards. Kaito does not need saving either (3/4 on our turn, -3/-3 leaves him 0/1).
WhenETB trigger on the stack, at instant speed since Nowhere to Run has flash.
AfterEnchantment source: loses nothing. It keeps its static ("Creatures your opponents control can be the targets of spells and abilities as though they didn't have hexproof. Ward abilities of those creatures don't trigger."), which from then on lets Sheriff and Triumph hit creature-Kaito on our turn. Only the -3/-3 is stopped.
RiskThree of our mana into their two; fair trade at best.
#8Ghost Vacuum (sideboard) reanimation
"{6}, {T}, Sacrifice this artifact: Put each creature card exiled with this artifact onto the battlefield under your control with a flying counter on it. Each of them is a 1/1 Spirit in addition to its other types. Activate only as a sorcery."
WhyThis is how they steal our exiled Tidebinder/Wasp/Drowner/Siren. Countering it: the Vacuum is already sacrificed as a cost, 6 mana is gone, and every card exiled with it stays exiled forever. Rare, single copy, but a total blowout when it happens.
WhenTheir main phase, activation on the stack.
AfterVacuum is already in the graveyard (cost), so nothing to strip. Exiled cards stay exiled.
RiskSideboard-only; they will not fire it into open {2}{U} once they have seen Tidebinder, which is also fine.
#9Earthbend return (delayed trigger from Ba Sing Se)
"When it dies or is exiled, return it to the battlefield tapped." (a delayed triggered ability created by the earthbend effect; source is Ba Sing Se)
WhyThe ONLY way to keep an earthbended land dead. When it dies to Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph or combat, counter the return trigger and the land stays in the graveyard/exile. Blanking the land with Wasp or Tidebinder does NOT turn this off (Scryfall ruling on Ba Sing Se calls it "the delayed triggered ability created by earthbend"; it is not an ability the land has).
WhenWhen the earthbended land dies or is exiled, the return trigger goes on the stack; flash Tidebinder targeting it.
AfterSource is Ba Sing Se, a land, so nothing loses abilities. The dead land simply does not come back.
Risk1-of Ba Sing Se, 3 mana per earthbend; take it only if you are killing the land anyway.
#10Qarsi Revenant (sideboard) renew
"Renew, {2}{B}, Exile this card from your graveyard: Put a flying counter, a deathtouch counter, and a lifelink counter on target creature. Activate only as a sorcery."
WhyStops a Demon token or Deceit from picking up flying/deathtouch/lifelink; the Revenant is exiled from the graveyard as a cost either way.
WhenTheir main phase, activation on the stack.
AfterSource is a card in exile, not a permanent, so nothing loses abilities. The counters are never placed.
RiskSideboard-only, 2 copies, low frequency.
Traps
Together as One: Counter the converge draw/damage/lifegain with Tidebinder. It is a sorcery; converge and its draw/damage/gain are spell effects, not abilities. Only a real counterspell (We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Pierce postboard) stops it.
Unholy Annex // Ritual Chamber (door unlock): Counter the {3}{B}{B} unlock itself so the Chamber stays locked. Unlocking a door by paying its cost is a special action and does not use the stack; casting the half is a spell. Neither can be targeted. The only targetable piece is the "When you unlock this door" Demon trigger.
Deceit (evoke): Counter the evoke sacrifice trigger to "punish" the evoke. That hands them a 5/5 for 2 mana AND the discard still happens: the discard trigger is already on the stack and exists independently of its source, so Deceit losing all abilities does not stop it. Always target the discard/bounce trigger; the sacrifice resolves on its own.
Deceit (any): Countering the ETB removes Deceit. Countering an ETB never removes the permanent. Hardcast Deceit stays a 5/5; evoked Deceit leaves only because the separate sacrifice trigger resolves.
Deceit (hardcast, paid UU+BB): Tidebinder counters both the bounce and the discard. "Counter up to one target" ability. Pick one; default to the discard.
Restless Cottage / Restless Reef (animation): Counter the {2}{B}{G} / {2}{U}{B} animation to blank the land. When that counter resolves the land is not yet a creature, so per ruling 3 it keeps all abilities; you only waste their 4 mana. To blank the land counter the ATTACK trigger, when it is a creature.
Great Hall of the Biblioplex (animation): Counter the {5} to strip the land. Not a creature at resolution; it loses nothing. Its granted "Whenever you cast an instant or sorcery spell, this creature gets +1/+0 until end of turn." trigger IS counterable once it is a permanent 2/4 creature, and then the land does lose everything, but that is a lot of setup for a 2/4.
Ba Sing Se (earthbend activation): Counter the {2}{G}, {T}: Earthbend 2 to blank Ba Sing Se or the target land. Ba Sing Se is a land at resolution, so it loses nothing; the target land never became a creature. You only waste their 3 mana. The permanent answer is countering the RETURN trigger when the earthbended land dies (target #9).
Watery Grave / Overgrown Tomb / Multiversal Passage: Counter the "you may pay 2 life" to make it enter tapped. "As this land enters" is a replacement effect, not a triggered ability. Untargetable.
Verges, Firdoch Core, The Soul Stone, Great Hall ("{T}, Pay 1 life: Add one mana of any color"): Counter a mana activation to Stone Rain them. All are mana abilities (could add mana, no target, not loyalty), even the one with a life payment. Tidebinder's own text says mana abilities can't be targeted.
The Soul Stone (Indestructible): Target indestructible so Bitter Triumph/Pulse can kill it. Indestructible is static and untargetable. It is only lost as a side effect of countering the harness activation or the ∞ upkeep trigger.
Nowhere to Run (static): Counter the hexproof-ignoring / ward-cancelling text to protect Kaito. Static ability; only the -3/-3 ETB trigger is a target, and the enchantment keeps its static either way.
Professor Dellian Fel (emblem): Wait and counter the emblem's "Whenever you gain life, target opponent loses that much life" trigger. You can counter one instance (the emblem is not a permanent, nothing loses abilities), but the emblem itself can never be removed and triggers on every lifegain. Stop the −6 activation if you can, or Bitter Triumph Fel before he reaches 6.
Mazemind Tome (four-counter exile): Counter "When there are four or more page counters on this artifact, exile it. If you do, you gain 4 life" to deny the 4 life. It is a state-triggered ability. Countering it blanks the Tome (artifact source) while Tidebinder lives, but the moment Tidebinder leaves the Tome regains the ability, the condition is still true, it triggers again and they exile it and gain 4. The life is delayed, not denied.
The End / Deadly Cover-Up / Day of Black Sun / Vicious Rivalry / Requiting Hex (blight): Counter the cost reduction, collect-evidence, or blight. Spells and additional/alternative costs are not abilities. Use real counterspells.
Qarsi Revenant (on the battlefield): Tidebinder something to strip flying/deathtouch/lifelink. All three are static keywords; Revenant has no activated/triggered ability while on the battlefield. Wasp is the tool for that job.
Not worth it
The Soul Stone harness ({6}{B}, {T}, exile a creature you control) and its ∞ upkeep return: 7 mana plus exiling one of their four creatures, never fired into open blue mana. If it does happen while you have Tidebinder up, take it: costs stay paid, the Stone loses indestructible and its mana ability while Tidebinder lives, and a returned Deceit is a 5/5 whose ETBs do not fire ("if {B}{B} was spent to cast it" is false, it was not cast). Do not plan around it.Unholy Annex end-step trigger: with a Demon out it denies a card and a 4-point life swing, but the better use of the mana is Hex/Drowner on the Demon itself; with no Demon out you also save them 2 life. Only as an end-of-turn flash for the body when nothing better is coming.Great Hall of the Biblioplex {5} animation: 5 mana for a 2/4, let them do it (and the land is not a creature at resolution, so nothing is stripped).Restless Reef mill-four trigger for the mill alone: irrelevant to us; only counter it for the land-blanking side effect (target #5).Intimidation Tactics cycling ({3}, Discard this card: Draw a card), sideboard: the discard is a cost, so countering the cycling leaves the card discarded and denies the draw. A free bonus if you were flashing the body at their end step anyway; card in hand, nothing stripped. Never hold Tidebinder for it.Ghost Vacuum "{T}: Exile target card from a graveyard": countering it blanks the Vacuum (artifact) while Tidebinder lives, shutting off the {6} reanimation too, but a 1-mana artifact and one graveyard card are not worth the counter. The {6} reanimation activation IS worth it (target #8).Mazemind Tome draw/scry activations: denies one card and locks a 2-mana artifact; only as an end-of-turn flash when nothing else is coming. The four-counter exile trigger is a trap (see traps).Fel's emblem trigger once the emblem exists: one instance only, the emblem stays (see traps).
The Wondrous Wasp
Wasp's Sting has almost nothing to blank in this 75: the only maindeck creature card (Deceit) has no on-board abilities, and Wasp dies to Requiting Hex (MV 2), Shoot the Sheriff and Nowhere to Run. Its real jobs here are (1) a 2-mana flying flash clock against a deck with few blockers and only sorcery-speed sweepers, (2) tapping the Demon or a hardcast Deceit in THEIR beginning of combat, which skips the attack AND keeps it tapped through our whole turn (no untap until their next untap step), clearing the way for Kaito, and (3) the niche but strong play of tapping an ANIMATED manland or Firdoch Core, which stays a blank permanent (no mana, no re-animation) as long as Wasp lives. No hexproof or ward anywhere in their deck. Requiting Hex, Drowner and Sheriff all kill the Demon token outright for less, so Wasp is the fallback, not the first choice.
#1Demon token (from Ritual Chamber)
WhyTap it in their beginning of combat: it cannot attack this turn and is still tapped on our turn, so Kaito and the ground team attack freely. It also loses flying while Wasp lives, so it cannot block Siren or Wasp later. Do this only when you have no Hex, Drowner or Sheriff in hand, which kill the token for less.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared (needs {1}{U} open on their turn). On our turn, precombat, to remove it as a blocker.
AfterA 6/6 ground vanilla while Wasp lives, tapped until their next untap step. It is still a Demon (creature type is not an ability), so Annex still drains 2 each end step; Wasp does not stop that. When they Hex/Sheriff the Wasp it flies again.
#2Restless Cottage / Restless Reef / Firdoch Core, animated
WhyOnce animated it is a creature, so Wasp can target it; the lose-all-abilities effect keeps applying after it stops being a creature, so the land (or Core) loses "{T}: Add..." and its animation ability for as long as Wasp lives. A 2-mana Stone Rain that also stops the attack if you act before attackers. Best case: they pay {4} to animate Firdoch Core as a blocker on OUR turn; Wasp taps the blocker and permanently blanks the rock (no mana, no changeling, no re-animation) while Wasp lives, cheaper than the Tidebinder line for the same Core.
WhenTheir beginning of combat, immediately after the animation resolves and before attackers are declared. If they animate to block on our turn, tap it then and it cannot block.
AfterStill a 4/4 this turn (the animation effect persists), tapped, no keywords (Reef loses its granted deathtouch). From next turn: a land/artifact with no abilities at all until Wasp leaves.
#3Deceit (resolved, hardcast)
WhyNot just a fog: tap it in their beginning of combat and it stays tapped through our turn, so it neither attacks nor blocks Kaito. Nothing to blank, its ETBs already resolved.
WhenTheir beginning of combat, before attackers.
AfterTapped 5/5 until their next untap step; unchanged otherwise.
#4Qarsi Revenant (sideboard)
WhyStrips flying, deathtouch and lifelink so it neither races in the air nor trades up.
WhenTheir beginning of combat before attackers, or our precombat to clear a blocker.
AfterA 3/3 vanilla while Wasp lives. Renew counters placed BEFORE Wasp's effect are also lost; counters placed after are kept (ruling-2 logic: abilities gained later stay).
#5Great Hall of the Biblioplex (animated) / earthbended land (Ba Sing Se)
WhyBoth animations are permanent (no "until end of turn"), so Wasp can hit them any time: the land loses its mana abilities and, for the earthbended land, the haste granted by earthbend. The +1/+1 counters stay (2/2). WARNING: the earthbend return ("When it dies or is exiled, return it to the battlefield tapped") is a delayed triggered ability created by the earthbend effect, not an ability of the land, so blanking it does NOT stop the return; killing a Wasp-blanked earthbended land still brings it back tapped. Only Tidebinder on the return trigger keeps it dead.
WhenAny time you have {1}{U}; before attackers if it is attacking.
After2/4 or 2/2 body with no abilities and no mana while Wasp lives; the earthbended land still returns if killed.
Traps
Deceit (as it enters): Flash Wasp to blank Deceit and stop the discard trigger. The trigger is already on the stack and exists independently of its source; blanking Deceit does not counter it. Only Tidebinder (or a counterspell on the Deceit spell) stops it.
Earthbended land: Wasp it, then Shoot the Sheriff it to kill it for good. The return is a delayed triggered ability created by earthbend (Scryfall ruling on Ba Sing Se), not an ability the land has; "loses all abilities" does not touch it. The land comes back tapped. Counter the return trigger with Tidebinder instead.
Demon token: Blank the Demon to turn off Annex's drain. Annex checks "If you control a Demon"; Demon is a creature type, not an ability. The drain continues.
Any attacker after attackers are declared: Wasp an attacking Demon/manland to remove it from combat. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat; it still deals damage. Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step.
Firdoch Core / The Soul Stone / manlands, not animated: Wasp them to shut off mana. Wasp targets creatures only; an un-animated land or artifact is not a legal target.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Evoked Deceit (their turn 2 onward, whenever we are tapped out or they think we are): with the discard trigger and the evoke sacrifice both on the stack, Tidebinder targets the discard. They lose 2 mana and a card, we keep our hand and a 3/2. If Tidebinder is not available, accept the discard; Spell Snare cannot hit Deceit (MV 6 even when evoked) and Spell Pierce does not counter creatures.
  • Hardcast Deceit paid with UU and BB: both the bounce and the discard are on the stack. Counter the discard; recast the bounced Kaito/Curiosity. Then Floodpits Drowner taps and stuns the 5/5 and shuffles it away, Kaito -2 stuns it, or Bitter Triumph it. Requiting Hex cannot target it (MV 6); do not waste Wasp's Sting on it either.
  • Their turn 4 after a turn-3 Firdoch Core (or turn-2 Soul Stone): 5 mana means Ritual Chamber or Deadly Cover-Up now. Do not try to hold Tidebinder for this; develop Kaito or Curiosity and answer the Demon token with Requiting Hex (MV 0 token), Drowner, Sheriff or Kaito -2. Save Tidebinder for Deceit and Fel.
  • On lands alone, their turn 5 with Ritual Chamber cast or unlocked and no Hex/Drowner in our hand: flash Tidebinder, counter the Demon trigger. They are down 5 mana; if the Annex door was already unlocked their end step now reads "draw a card... Otherwise, you lose 2 life"; if they cast the Chamber half fresh, the Room does nothing until they pay another {2}{B}.
  • Professor Dellian Fel activates anything (+2, 0, -3 on Drowner/Siren, -6): Tidebinder counters, Fel is a blank walker; swing Spyglass Siren / Wasp / Kaito into him next turn. If he is at 6+ and you do not have {2}{U} up, Bitter Triumph him at end of turn rather than gambling.
  • Kaito, Bane of Nightmares against their removal: Deadly Cover-Up, Day of Black Sun and Fel's -3 miss him (he is a non-creature planeswalker on their turn), but Bitter Triumph, Maelstrom Pulse, The End and Together as One (any target) all hit him there, a blue-paid Deceit bounces him, and once Nowhere to Run is on the battlefield its static lets Shoot the Sheriff and Bitter Triumph target creature-Kaito on OUR turn despite hexproof. He is their hardest problem, not an untouchable one; Tidebinder on a Deceit bounce or Fel activation is part of keeping him alive.
  • Demon already on board and no Hex/Drowner in hand, their beginning of combat: flash Wasp, tap the Demon. It skips the 6-point attack, stays tapped through our turn (Kaito and the team attack into an empty board) and cannot block Siren/Wasp later because it lost flying. Expect Hex/Sheriff on the Wasp; the missed attack and the mana they spend on a 2-drop are the point.
  • They animate Restless Cottage ({2}{B}{G}) at beginning of combat: flash Wasp before attackers to tap it, and it is a blank land (no mana, no animation) as long as Wasp lives. If you only have Tidebinder, let it attack and counter the "Whenever this land attacks" trigger for the same land-blanking effect, blocking the 4/4 with Beavers or taking it.
  • Earthbended land dies to Sheriff/Triumph/combat: with Tidebinder up, counter the return trigger and it stays dead. Wasp on it first only strips haste and mana; it does not stop the return.
  • Hand attack: Duress cannot take Tidebinder or Wasp; only Deceit's discard (and postboard Intimidation Tactics) can. Against a Duress-heavy hand the creature-counters are the interaction most likely to survive, so expose We Say Thee Nay! and Spell Snare to Duress and keep the creatures for Deceit/Fel/Chamber.
Hold or fire
Tidebinder targets, in order: any Deceit ETB, any Fel loyalty activation, the Chamber Demon trigger (only when Hex/Drowner/Sheriff are not in hand or the 3/2 body matters), then a manland attack trigger or Core animation. Because Enduring Curiosity, Floodpits Drowner, Wasp and Tidebinder all have flash, on most turns you pass with mana up anyway, so keeping {2}{U} available costs little: if nothing counterable happens, flash Curiosity/Drowner/Wasp at their end step instead and nothing was lost. The only real conflict is a Kaito turn. Decide by their mana, not the turn number: if they have shown Firdoch Core or Soul Stone (5 mana on their turn 4), play Kaito and let Hex/Drowner/Kaito -2 handle the Demon; if they are on lands alone and you are on the play, hold. Never sit on Tidebinder waiting for an evoke: they choose when to evoke and will do it when you are tapped out, so fire at the first Deceit/Fel/Chamber trigger you can hit. Once they have seen Tidebinder in game 1 they will play around it (no Chamber unlock or Fel activation into three open blue), and that sequencing tax is real value even when it never fires. On the draw you are a mana behind on their turns, so lean harder toward developing and using Hex/Drowner for the Demon. Wasp: fire it as a 2-mana flying clock at their end step whenever you have nothing else to do with {1}{U}, because its Sting has few targets here; hold it only when a Demon, hardcast Deceit or animated manland is on board and you can tap it in their beginning of combat next turn (which also clears the blocker for our turn). Expect both bodies to eat removal; the countered trigger is the permanent gain, the lock is temporary.
How many to run
Tidebinder: bring in both sideboard copies, 3 total postboard, cutting 2 Requiting Hex (live only against the Demon token and animated lands; it cannot hit Deceit at MV 6 or Qarsi Revenant at MV 3, and 2 copies remain for the token). Keep both Shoot the Sheriff: they answer hardcast Deceit and the postboard Qarsi Revenant (Vampire, not an outlaw) that Hex cannot. Warning on going to 3: Deadly Cover-Up with collect evidence 6, The End and Ancient Vendetta all hunt copies by name, so once one Tidebinder is in our graveyard the other two can be exiled from hand and library; do not leave a dead Tidebinder in the graveyard into a 5-mana Cover-Up turn if you can avoid it (our own Ghost Vacuum sideboard copy is not for that, but Strategic Betrayal timing is). Wasp: keep 2 in game 1 (a 2-mana flash flier is a fine clock against a deck with almost no creatures and sorcery-speed sweepers) and keep 2 postboard unless the slot is needed for Duress or Spell Pierce (Spell Pierce is sideboard-only in our stock list) against Together as One / Deadly Cover-Up; if you cut a creature, Wasp is the first out because its Sting is nearly blank here and it dies to Hex, Sheriff and Nowhere to Run. Do not cut Wasp to zero.
Review trail: confidence high, 7 disputed, 24 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • No reviewer finding was rejected outright; every fatal/major was accepted after checking the deck file, and the earthbend correction was independently confirmed against Scryfall's Ba Sing Se rulings (2025-10-02: 'the delayed triggered ability created by earthbend'). Items below are the ability_tags hint-block claims rejected or corrected against the oracle text.
  • ability_tags lists Ward, Flash and Hexproof as keywords of Nowhere to Run: only Flash is its own keyword; hexproof and ward are words in its static text about OPPONENTS' creatures. Rejected as targets: static ability, not counterable.
  • ability_tags omits Deceit's evoke sacrifice trigger, The Soul Stone's ∞ upkeep trigger and Ritual Chamber's "When you unlock this door" Demon trigger; all three are triggered abilities and were added (the evoke sacrifice is a deliberate no-target).
  • ability_tags records "When it dies or is exiled, return it to the battlefield tapped" as Ba Sing Se's own trigger. It is neither Ba Sing Se's nor the earthbended land's ability: it is a delayed triggered ability created by the earthbend effect (CR 603.7, Scryfall ruling). Consequence: Wasp/Tidebinder ability-loss never turns it off; Tidebinder can counter the return itself when the land dies (source Ba Sing Se, a land, nothing stripped).
  • ability_tags treats Restless Cottage/Reef's animation activations as ordinary targets; ranked as traps for the ability-loss purpose because the land is not a creature when the counter resolves (Tidebinder ruling 3). Only the attack triggers strip the land.
  • Great Hall's "{T}, Pay 1 life: Add one mana of any color" is correctly tagged as a mana ability despite the life payment; confirmed, kept as untargetable.
  • Reviewer 2 wrote '4 Duress'; the featured list runs 3 main + 1 side. Immaterial to any claim, corrected in passing.
Changes after review
  • Re-ranked Tidebinder targets: Deceit evoke ETB #1, Deceit hardcast ETB #2, Fel #3, Chamber Demon trigger #4 (with the explicit rule to prefer Hex/Drowner/Sheriff/Kaito -2 on the token and save Tidebinder for Deceit/Fel), resolving the contradiction between the old #1 and hold_or_fire.
  • Added the hardcast-Deceit UU+BB case: both ETBs trigger; Tidebinder is up-to-one target; default counter the discard, with the stated exception.
  • Rewrote all timing to key off Firdoch Core / Soul Stone acceleration (Chamber and Cover-Up on their turn 4, hardcast Deceit turn 5) instead of raw turn numbers.
  • Rewrote hold_or_fire: our flash creatures make holding {2}{U} nearly free; only Kaito turns conflict; decide by their mana; never wait for an evoke; sequencing tax is value.
  • Corrected the earthbend claim (Wasp target, trap, disputed note): the return is a delayed triggered ability, not a granted ability; blanking does not stop it; added Tidebinder-on-the-return-trigger as target #9 (the only permanent answer) and an earthbend Wasp trap.
  • Split the Chamber after_effect into case (a) Chamber cast fresh (Annex door locked, Room does nothing) and case (b) Annex already unlocked (draw + lose 2).
  • Fixed the evoke-sacrifice trap: the discard still resolves even if the sacrifice is countered.
  • Fel: any activation (+2, 0, -3, -6) locks him; removed the 'must hold for -6' framing; -6 is take-it-if-available.
  • Kaito wording corrected everywhere: sorcery sweepers and Fel -3 miss him, but Triumph, Pulse, The End, Together as One and a blue Deceit hit him, and Nowhere to Run's static lets Sheriff/Triumph hit creature-Kaito on our turn. Added Kaito -2 as an answer to Deceit/Demon.
  • Requiting Hex cannot target Deceit (MV 6): changed 'do not waste' to 'cannot target'.
  • Blue-source list for the Deceit bounce mode now includes Restless Reef and Multiversal Passage; Great Hall's rainbow mana correctly excluded.
  • Replaced the Hymn analogy with 'targeted discard: they see our hand and take our best nonland card'. Fixed '2-for-0' to one-for-one-with-body-and-nothing-for-them.
  • Maindeck answer count corrected from '20+' to 13, enumerated; postboard additions listed.
  • Soul Stone harness/∞, Great Hall animation and Annex end-step trigger moved from ranked targets to dont_bother with take-it-if-free notes; Soul Stone-returned Deceit reworded from 'vanilla' to 'ETBs do not fire because it was not cast'.
  • Mazemind Tome four-counter exile moved to traps with the state-trigger explanation (life delayed until Tidebinder leaves, not denied).
  • Added Ghost Vacuum {6} reanimation as Tidebinder target #8 (Vacuum sacrificed as cost, exiled creatures stay exiled) and the {T} exile blank as dont_bother.
  • Added Intimidation Tactics cycling to dont_bother with the cost-stays-paid note.
  • Added Ba Sing Se earthbend activation as an explicit Tidebinder trap.
  • Nowhere to Run target narrowed: save Drowner or a lethal/Curiosity attack; do not save Curiosity's body (returns as an enchantment).
  • Wasp: Demon tap now framed as their-combat tap that persists through our turn and as a fallback to Hex/Drowner/Sheriff; Kaito 'gets over it' removed (no flying). Deceit tap upgraded from 'fog only' to blocker-clearing. Added our-turn Wasp on an animated Firdoch Core blocker.
  • Great Hall granted trigger quote now includes 'until end of turn'.
  • Duress-proofing line reworded: Deceit's discard and postboard Intimidation Tactics can take the creatures; 'safe' softened to 'most likely to survive hand attack'.
  • Counts: cut 2 Hex not Sheriff (Qarsi Revenant MV 3 Vampire, hardcast Deceit); added the name-exile warning (Cover-Up evidence, The End, Vendetta) for running 3 Tidebinder; noted Spell Pierce is sideboard-only.
  • Confidence raised to high: no fatal findings, all majors fixed and oracle/ruling-backed (earthbend ruling fetched from Scryfall in this pass).
vs. Boros Tokens2.1% TB: situationalWasp: good▼
Turn 1 Stadium Headliner or Political Triumph, turn 2 Voice of Victory or Belladonna Took, turn 3 Warleader's Call or Hop to It, then Song of Totentanz for X with haste, with Frontline Rush and a hardcast The Last Ronin's Technique (instant, {3}{W}) as the instant-speed token makers. Every token that enters fires three engines at once: Warleader's Call ("Whenever a creature you control enters, this enchantment deals 1 damage to each opponent"), Belladonna Took ("Whenever a token you control enters... gain 1 life / draw a card / +1/+1 counter on each creature") and Political Triumph ("scry 1 and put a plan counter"; the fourth counter draws and gives the team a counter). Mobilize on Voice/Headliner and Dalkovan Encampment make the attack step itself produce tokens, so the engines fire on THEIR turn. The card that shapes the matchup is Voice of Victory's static "Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn": while an un-blanked Voice is on the battlefield we cannot flash in Tidebinder or Wasp, or cast We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Snare, Requiting Hex or Bitter Triumph, on their turn at all. Our activated abilities (Restless Reef, Soulstone Sanctuary, Floodpits Drowner's shuffle) still work on their turn; only spells are locked.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Most of their triggers (Took, Political Triumph, Mobilize) happen on THEIR turn behind Voice of Victory's lockout, where Tidebinder cannot be cast. On OUR turn, the Took trigger it could counter is usually better answered by Requiting Hex on Took itself (1 mana, permanent, and cast in response to Frontline Rush / Last Ronin's Technique before any token enters, so zero triggers ever happen). What Tidebinder does that nothing else in our 75 does: (1) save a creature from Stadium Headliner's sac-ping, (2) stop The Queen of Dale's recruit trigger that our own removal spell causes and blank her, (3) permanently deny Political Triumph's fourth-counter payoff (an enchantment we cannot otherwise remove), and (4) work through Dawn's Truce, because it targets an ability, not a hexproof permanent. Otherwise it is a 3/2 flash body. Never hold 3 mana for Tidebinder alone in this matchup. Keep the main copy, do not board in more.
#1Stadium Headliner
{1}{R}, Sacrifice this creature: It deals damage equal to the number of creatures you control to target creature.
WhyTheir only creature-based removal, and the one job removal cannot do for us: killing Headliner in response does nothing (it is already sacrificed) and the damage still happens; only countering the ability saves our creature. Realistic aim points on our turn: an attacking Wasp (killing Wasp restores Voice's lockout and wakes Took), Floodpits Drowner (in response to its ETB or its shuffle activation), Dream Beavers or Spyglass Siren. They rarely aim it at Enduring Curiosity, which just returns as an enchantment; their Curiosity answer is bargained Torch the Tower (3 damage plus 'exile it instead', so no return). Sacrifice is a cost, so they have already lost Headliner: a clean 1-for-1 plus a 3/2 body.
WhenIn response to the activation. On OUR turn always available; on their turn only if no un-blanked Voice is on the battlefield. Headliner is in the graveyard when Tidebinder's trigger resolves, so nothing is blanked (ruling 3).
AfterNothing on the battlefield changes except our creature lives. Headliner stays sacrificed. If the target was Drowner mid-shuffle, note the shuffle ability would have resolved on its target anyway (abilities exist independently of their source once on the stack); Tidebinder saves Drowner for reuse, it does not change whether Voice gets shuffled.
RiskNone beyond Torch later killing the 3/2; the countered ping stays countered.
#2The Queen of Dale (sideboard, expect it post-board vs Dimir)
Whenever an opponent casts their first noncreature spell each turn, you recruit. (Draw a card, then discard a card. If you discarded a nonland card, create a 1/1 white Human Soldier creature token.)
WhyQueen punishes our removal and counters: every first noncreature spell each turn is a loot and often a Soldier token that then fires Took, Call and Triumph. Killing her with Requiting Hex does not stop that Hex's own recruit trigger (it is already on the stack above the Hex). Only Wasp-first or Tidebinder on the trigger stops it, and Tidebinder also blanks her while it lives. Dawn's Truce (hexproof) does not protect the trigger from Tidebinder.
WhenCast the turn's first noncreature spell (Hex, Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff, Duress), let the recruit trigger go on the stack, flash Tidebinder targeting it. Tidebinder and Wasp are creature spells and do not trigger her. On our turn always; on THEIR turn too once Voice is dead or blanked, because our instant-speed Spell Snare / We Say Thee Nay! / removal is also a 'first noncreature spell'. Without Tidebinder or Wasp, lead with the cheapest noncreature spell you were casting anyway (recruit is once per turn) so the real removal does not feed her.
AfterQueen is a 2/1 (3/2 with Call) with no text while Tidebinder lives. The loot and token never happen.
RiskTorch the Tower on Tidebinder wakes her; the trigger you countered stays countered.
#3Political Triumph
When the fourth plan counter is put on this enchantment, sacrifice it, draw a card, and put a +1/+1 counter on each creature you control.
WhyUnique Tidebinder job: it is an enchantment, so none of our creature removal touches it (only Annul on the stack). The sacrifice is part of the effect, not a cost, so countering the payoff leaves Triumph on the battlefield holding four counters; the trigger condition is 'the fourth plan counter is put', and a fifth counter is not the fourth, so by the oracle text the payoff should never happen again (no Scryfall ruling on file confirms this, but nothing contradicts it). Nothing is blanked (enchantment source, ruling 3). Rare: the fourth counter must land on OUR turn or with Voice handled.
WhenPolitical Triumph has TWO triggers. The first, 'Whenever a creature you control enters, scry 1 and put a plan counter', resolves and places the fourth counter; THEN the fourth-counter payoff triggers. Countering the first only delays the payoff (the next counter becomes the fourth). Target the second one. Windows: their instant-speed token (Frontline Rush, Last Ronin's Technique, Fountainport Fish) on our turn, or any token on their turn once Voice is blanked/gone.
AfterTriumph remains with 4 plan counters and still scrys 1 per creature entering; it never draws the card or hands out counters.
RiskLow. Only a second Political Triumph gets that value back.
#4Belladonna Took
Whenever a token you control enters, you gain 1 life if this is the first time this ability has resolved this turn. If it's the second time, draw a card. If it's the third time, put a +1/+1 counter on each creature you control.
WhyTook is their card-advantage and anthem engine and a creature, so countering one trigger blanks her while Tidebinder lives. A countered trigger did not 'resolve' and does not advance the first/second/third count: when three tokens enter at once (hardcast Last Ronin's Technique, Hop to It, Song X=3), counter any one Took trigger and only two resolve (life, card), so the team +1/+1 is denied regardless of which you pick. BUT if you hold Requiting Hex or Shoot the Sheriff, cast it on Took in response to the token spell instead: 1-2 mana, permanent, zero triggers. Tidebinder is the Took answer only when you have no removal, or post-board when Dawn's Truce is protecting her (hexproof does not stop Tidebinder countering the trigger, and the blank is not targeted).
WhenWith a Took trigger on the stack after the token(s) enter. On OUR turn: they hardcast The Last Ronin's Technique ({3}{W} instant, three tokens = three triggers, the biggest window Voice cannot close), Frontline Rush (two Goblins), or a Fountainport Fish at our end step. On THEIR turn: only if Voice is blanked, gone, or never landed (Mobilize tokens in declare attackers, Hop to It / Song tokens in their main phase).
AfterNo life, no cards, no counters from tokens entering while Tidebinder is on the battlefield. Keeps her 2/2 body, keeps Warleader's Call's +1/+1 (an external static), keeps counters already placed; cards already drawn stay drawn. Other Took triggers already on the stack still resolve, which is why the count-denial trick works.
RiskTorch the Tower or Erode on the 3/2 wakes her at once, and you spent 3 held mana that could have been Curiosity or Kaito.
#5Captain Marvel, Earth's Protector (sideboard, 1 copy)
Power-up, {5}{W}{W}: Put a +1/+1 counter and an indestructible counter on Captain Marvel. (Activate each power-up ability only once. Reduce the cost by her mana cost if she entered this turn.)
WhyFootnote-tier: the discounted {2} power-up still needs 7 total mana the turn she enters (5 to cast plus 2), a turn-7+ play in a 22-land deck, and she is a one-of. If it ever happens on our turn (she has flash, so she may come down at our end step), counter it: no counters, and she loses flying, lifelink and the power-up while Tidebinder lives. 'Activate only once' counts the countered activation, so she can never power up again even after Tidebinder leaves.
WhenIn response to the same-turn discounted power-up, at our end step after she flashes in. If she is cast or powered up on their turn under Voice you cannot respond.
AfterA vanilla 5/4 (6/5 with Call) that Wan Shi Tong, Dream Beavers or a Restless Reef Shark can block; no lifelink racing; no counters ever. A Marvel that already carries an indestructible counter has no remaining ability for Tidebinder to counter, so Tidebinder can never blank that Marvel (Wasp can).
RiskShe still hits for 5 on the ground if Tidebinder dies; the counters are gone for good either way.
#6Fountainport
{2}, {T}, Sacrifice a token: Draw a card.
WhyFiller when Tidebinder has nothing better to do at your end step: they sacrifice a token and pay {2} and a tap for nothing. Land source, so no blank. One of the few abilities they routinely activate on OUR turn.
WhenYour end step, in response to the activation. The token is already sacrificed (cost).
AfterNo card drawn; token stays gone; Fountainport unchanged.
RiskYou spent your only Tidebinder to deny one card.
#7Warleader's Call
Whenever a creature you control enters, this enchantment deals 1 damage to each opponent.
WhyOnly when that single point is lethal or decides an exact race. Enchantment source, nothing blanked, every other token's ping still resolves.
WhenAny token entry you can respond to (our turn, or their turn after Voice is handled). One trigger per creature; you counter exactly one.
AfterOne damage prevented; Call keeps its anthem and future pings.
RiskAlmost always a wasted card.
Traps
Voice of Victory: Flash in Tidebinder during their attack to counter Mobilize 2 ('Whenever this creature attacks, create two tapped and attacking 1/1 red Warrior creature tokens'). Impossible in every game state. If Voice's Mobilize trigger exists, Voice has its abilities, so 'Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn' is live and Tidebinder (a spell, flash or not) cannot be cast. If Voice is blanked, stunned or gone, there is no Mobilize trigger. A second un-blanked Voice attacking re-imposes the lockout too. The only abilities-loss answers to Voice are Wasp and Day of Black Sun; the removal answers are Requiting Hex / Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph / Drowner's shuffle on OUR turn, or Spell Snare / We Say Thee Nay! on the Voice spell.
Voice of Victory / Stadium Headliner / Dalkovan Encampment: Counter the 'Sacrifice them at the beginning of the next end step' delayed trigger on the Mobilize tokens to hurt them. It is a counterable triggered ability, but countering it means they KEEP the Warrior tokens permanently. Because a delayed trigger shares the source of the ability that created it (CR 603.7), countering the Voice/Headliner version would also blank that creature while Tidebinder lives; against Voice you can never be casting Tidebinder at that moment (see above), and against Headliner it is a marginal line: they keep a 1/1 Warrior, Headliner loses its sac-ping and Mobilize. Encampment's version is land-sourced, no blank. Default: do not target it.
Warleader's Call: Counter the +1/+1 anthem. 'Creatures you control get +1/+1' is a static ability; only the 1-damage trigger can be targeted, and Call is an enchantment so it never loses abilities.
Song of Totentanz / Hop to It / Frontline Rush / The Last Ronin's Technique: Tidebinder the token creation, the haste, or the sneak. These are spells resolving, not abilities. 'Creatures you control gain haste' and 'they enter tapped and attacking' are spell effects; Sneak is an alternative cost paid while casting. Answer them with We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Snare (Frontline Rush is MV 2), Spell Pierce or Flashfreeze (Song, Rush, Call, Torch, Headliner are red), on our turn if Voice is out, on theirs if it is not.
Stadium Headliner: Countering the sac-ping 'locks' Headliner. Sacrifice is a cost, so Headliner is in the graveyard when Tidebinder's trigger resolves; ruling 3 says a source that is not an artifact/creature/planeswalker at resolution loses nothing. You counter the damage only. (Headliner CAN be blanked via its Mobilize 1 attack trigger or that trigger's end-step sacrifice, because it is still on the battlefield then; see dont_bother.)
Political Triumph / Warleader's Call / Rest in Peace / Fountainport / Dalkovan Encampment: Countering one of their triggers or activations shuts the permanent off. Enchantments and lands are not artifacts, creatures or planeswalkers; the ability is countered but the permanent keeps every ability. Blankable sources in their 75: Took, Queen, Marvel, Headliner (via Mobilize/its delayed trigger only) and, in theory only, Voice.
Political Triumph: Counter the Triumph trigger that is about to place the fourth counter. That is the scry/plan-counter trigger; countering it only delays the payoff, because the next counter becomes the fourth. Let it resolve, then counter the separate 'When the fourth plan counter is put on this enchantment' trigger, which denies the payoff for good.
Sacred Foundry / Inspiring Vantage / Dalkovan Encampment: Respond to 'As this land enters, you may pay 2 life' or 'enters tapped unless'. Replacement effects and static abilities; they never use the stack.
Torch the Tower: Counter the Bargain sacrifice or the 'exile it instead' clause. Bargain is part of casting; the exile clause is a replacement effect created by the resolving spell. Neither is an ability on the stack.
Fountainport: '{4}, {T}: Create a Treasure token' is a mana ability and cannot be targeted. It is NOT a mana ability (creating a Treasure does not add mana), so it CAN be targeted, but it is worthless to counter. The '{T}: Add {C}' line and sacrificing the Treasure itself are the mana abilities.
Dawn's Truce (sideboard): Their hexproof instant fizzles Tidebinder's trigger. Tidebinder targets an ability on the stack, not a permanent; 'you and permanents you control gain hexproof' does not protect abilities, and the resulting blank is not targeted. Dawn's Truce DOES fizzle Wasp's Sting and our Hex/Sheriff/Bitter Triumph, which makes Tidebinder relatively better post-board.
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares (ours): Kaito's −2 ('Tap target creature. Put two stun counters on it') on Voice of Victory turns off the lockout. Static abilities work while tapped or stunned. Only losing abilities (Wasp, Day of Black Sun) or removing Voice from the battlefield (Hex, Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Drowner's shuffle) ends the restriction.
Not worth it
Stadium Headliner's Mobilize 1 attack trigger and its end-step sacrifice, both DO blank Headliner (creature on the battlefield at resolution), but they only ever appear on their turn with no un-blanked Voice out, and in that spot Took's trigger off the same token or holding mana for We Say Thee Nay! is worth more; the end-step version also leaves them a permanent 1/1.Dalkovan Encampment, both the '{2}{W}, {T}' activation and the delayed 'Whenever you attack this turn, create two 1/1 red Warrior creature tokens' trigger it creates are legal targets, but land source, no blank, two temporary tokens, and it happens on their turn.Warleader's Call ping, 1 damage per counter, nothing blanked; only when it is the exact lethal point.Fountainport Fish ('{3}, {T}, Pay 1 life: Create a 1/1 blue Fish') and Treasure abilities, legal, worthless; the Fish entering is more useful as the token that opens a Took-trigger or fourth-counter window on our turn.Rest in Peace ETB ('When this enchantment enters, exile all graveyards'), enchantment source, one-shot, nothing blanked. RIP does hurt us (its replacement effect exiles Enduring Curiosity when it dies so it never returns, and leaves Ghost Vacuum nothing to target), so expect it post-board; answer it with Annul or Spell Snare (MV 2) on the stack, not Tidebinder.Get Lost (sideboard), it targets OUR permanent and gives US the two Map tokens; nothing of theirs to counter, and the Map's explore is an activated ability with sacrifice as a cost anyway.Captain Marvel's ETB, she has none; flash, flying and lifelink are keywords, only the power-up is a target.
The Wondrous Wasp
Wasp does two real jobs here, blanking Voice of Victory's lockout or Belladonna Took's engine on OUR main phase, and its 2/1 flyer connects every turn for Enduring Curiosity because their main deck has no flyers. It is not premium, for three reasons. (1) Allocation: Voice, Took, Queen and Headliner all die to Requiting Hex (MV 2 or less) and Shoot the Sheriff (none are outlaws), and Bitter Triumph and Floodpits Drowner also answer them, all castable on our turn under Voice. Point permanent removal at Voice (the lockout matters every turn) and Wasp at Took; if you hold only Wasp, Wasp the Voice. (2) Fragility: 3 Torch the Tower, 1 Erode main (+3 Erode, 2 Get Lost side) and Headliner's sac-ping all kill a 2/1, and a Torch or ping cast in response to Wasp's Sting means the tap happens but the blank never begins (Wasp is not on the battlefield when the 'for as long as' effect would start, CR 611.2b; the same logic as Tidebinder ruling 1). (3) Post-board Dawn's Truce fizzles the Sting. The 'tap before attackers' mode is mostly unavailable because Voice stops us casting Wasp on their turn; the value is the blank. Use the window the turn you open it.
#1Voice of Victory
WhyBlanks both 'Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn' and Mobilize 2. With the static gone we can flash Tidebinder into their token entries, counter Song of Totentanz or Hop to It with We Say Thee Nay!, and Hex/Bitter Triumph at instant speed on their turn. Prefer Requiting Hex / Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph on Voice when you have them (1-2 mana, permanent, no Torch-in-response problem for the body); Wasp is the answer when removal is not in hand, when it is the second Voice, or when removal is earmarked for Took.
WhenOUR turn, main phase 1 (we cannot cast it on theirs while Voice is live). Best when they are tapped out: with {R} or {1}{R} open they can Torch or Headliner-ping Wasp with the Sting on the stack, and then Voice is never blanked and you get no instant-speed window that turn. Do it before anything else that turn so a response does not strand your sequencing. On the draw: Spell Snare (one blue source) can still counter the turn-2 Voice on the stack before the lockout exists; without it, Wasp or Hex the Voice on our turn 2 or 3. On the play: their turn 2 Voice, our turn 3 Wasp; or Spell Snare / We Say Thee Nay! on the Voice spell on their turn 2.
AfterVoice is a 1/3 (2/4 with Call) with no text while Wasp lives; a second Voice still locks us out. Tokens already created stay. If they Torch Wasp on THEIR turn after the blank is established, Voice is still blank while Torch is on the stack, so respond with any instant right then; the lockout returns when Torch resolves.
#2Belladonna Took
WhyBlanks the token trigger ('Whenever a token you control enters, you gain 1 life ... draw a card ... put a +1/+1 counter on each creature you control') without needing a trigger window, which Tidebinder does need. This is the default Wasp target when Voice has been handled by removal, because Took's engine only matters when tokens enter and a temporary blank covers the turns that matter.
WhenAny time on our turn (or their turn if no live Voice: their end step is fine since her triggers happen in their main/combat phases). Do it before their next turn's token spells, not after. Same Torch/ping-in-response caveat: if they respond by killing Wasp, no blank.
After2/2 (3/3 with Call) body with no text while Wasp lives; counters already on their team stay, cards already drawn stay drawn.
#3The Queen of Dale (sideboard)
WhyBlanks 'Whenever an opponent casts their first noncreature spell each turn, you recruit', so our removal and counters stop feeding them loots and Soldier tokens. Killing her with Hex still gives them that Hex's recruit; Wasp first does not (Wasp is a creature spell).
WhenOur main phase BEFORE casting the turn's first noncreature spell.
After2/1 (3/2 with Call) vanilla. She does not fly, so Wasp blocks her, and trades with her if she attacks into it.
#4Captain Marvel, Earth's Protector (sideboard, 1 copy)
WhyRemoves flying and lifelink (and the power-up if unused), turning a 5/4 flying lifelinker into a ground body that Wan Shi Tong, Dream Beavers or Restless Reef's 4/4 deathtouch Shark can block. If Voice is gone, Wasp at their beginning of combat also taps her so she cannot attack that turn.
WhenTap-before-attackers only works at their beginning of combat, which requires no live Voice. Otherwise on our turn, purely for the blank. An indestructible counter placed BEFORE Wasp's effect began loses its keyword while Wasp lives (the counter itself stays); one placed AFTER is kept (ruling 2).
After5/4 (6/5 with Call) with no abilities while Wasp lives. She can still be powered up after Wasp dies if the ability was never activated.
#5Stadium Headliner
WhyBlanks '{1}{R}, Sacrifice this creature: It deals damage equal to the number of creatures you control to target creature' and Mobilize 1, protecting Drowner/Beavers/Siren/Wasp itself from their only creature-based removal.
WhenOur turn, when nothing better is on their board (no Voice, no Took, no Queen).
After1/1 (2/2 with Call) that can still attack and block but cannot snipe.
Traps
Voice of Victory / Belladonna Took: Once Wasp is cast the blank is guaranteed, so firing into open Torch mana is fine. If they Torch the Tower (2 damage) or Headliner-ping Wasp with the Sting on the stack, Wasp is gone before the trigger resolves: the tap still happens, but 'loses all abilities for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield' does nothing because the duration condition is already false when the effect would begin (CR 611.2b; same logic as Tidebinder ruling 1). No blank, no instant-speed window, and they traded a 1-mana spell for our 2-drop. Fire into open R only when the body-for-Torch trade is acceptable on its own, or when the Torch drawn away from Kaito/Curiosity is the point.
Any Mobilize or Song of Totentanz attacker: Flash Wasp in during their attack to tap the attacker. Two problems: Voice's static stops you casting Wasp on their turn at all, and even without Voice, tapping an attacking creature after attackers are declared does not remove it from combat. Tap-before-attackers only works in their beginning of combat step.
Rat tokens from Song of Totentanz: Blank a Rat to shrink their board. Its only ability is 'This token can't block'; removing it lets the Rat block your Kaito/Curiosity.
Dawn's Truce (sideboard, 3 copies): Wasp's Sting cannot be stopped. 'You and permanents you control gain hexproof until end of turn' cast in response makes the targeted creature an illegal target and the Sting fizzles; Wasp still enters as a 2/1 flyer. They can cast it on our turn with {1}{W} open, and it fizzles our Hex/Sheriff/Bitter Triumph the same way. No native hexproof or ward in their 75 otherwise.
Voice of Victory: Once Voice is blanked the lockout is gone for the game. It returns the moment Wasp leaves the battlefield (Torch, Erode, Get Lost, Headliner ping, a chump block) and a second Voice is unaffected. Use the window that turn; if they Torch Wasp on their turn, cast your instants while Torch is still on the stack.
Kaito ninjutsu returning Wasp: Free value: ninjutsu Kaito off an unblocked Wasp and replay Wasp for a second Sting. It works mechanically, but returning Wasp to hand ends the blank on Voice/Took immediately. Only do it if the Wasp target is already dead or you are replaying Wasp onto a better target this turn.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • ALLOCATION RULE for Voice of Victory and Belladonna Took (both MV 2, neither an outlaw): Requiting Hex (4), Shoot the Sheriff (2), Bitter Triumph (2) and Floodpits Drowner's stun-then-shuffle (4) all work on our turn under Voice. Point permanent removal at Voice, because the lockout costs us every turn; point Wasp at Took. If you hold only Wasp, Wasp the Voice (when they are tapped out if possible). If you hold only removal, kill Voice.
  • Stop the first Voice before it exists: Spell Snare (MV 2) on the Voice spell on their turn 2 works even on the draw with one blue source; on the play We Say Thee Nay! is also available. The restriction is a static on the battlefield and does nothing while Voice is a spell. Spell Snare also hits Took, Queen, Frontline Rush, Rest in Peace, Get Lost and Dawn's Truce; it misses Political Triumph (MV 1) and Warleader's Call (MV 3), which Annul (side, 1) counters as enchantment spells.
  • THE Wasp line when it is the answer: our main phase, Wasp on Voice (before casting anything else). Their turn is now open: hold We Say Thee Nay! (Teamwork: tap Wasp alone, power 2, for the {4} tax) for Song of Totentanz or Hop to It; flash Tidebinder when Mobilize/Hop tokens enter to counter Took's trigger and blank her; Hex/Bitter Triumph on Took at instant speed. If they Torch Wasp on their upkeep, respond while Torch is on the stack, Voice is still blank until it resolves. If they Torch or Headliner-ping Wasp in response to the Sting on OUR turn, the blank never starts; plan the turn as if the Voice is still live.
  • Drowner on Voice: Floodpits Drowner ETB ('tap target creature an opponent controls and put a stun counter on it'; the stun does NOT turn off the static) on our turn, then '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries', an activated ability that is legal on THEIR turn even under Voice. The Voice goes back into their library (it can be redrawn) and the lockout is gone until they replay one. Wasp suspends Voice; Drowner removes it.
  • Queen of Dale post-board: Wasp her precombat, THEN cast Hex/Bitter Triumph freely. Without Wasp: cast the removal, let the recruit trigger go on the stack, flash Tidebinder to counter it and blank her. With neither, lead each turn with the cheapest noncreature spell you were casting anyway; recruit is once per turn.
  • Tidebinder as a bodyguard: when Stadium Headliner is sacrificed at an attacking Wasp (to end the Voice/Took blank), at Drowner, or at Beavers/Siren, flash Tidebinder and counter the damage; Headliner is already gone, our creature lives, and we have a 3/2 body. Curiosity does not need this protection (it returns as an enchantment); their Curiosity answer is bargained Torch, which exiles.
  • Their token entries on OUR turn are the Tidebinder chances: a hardcast The Last Ronin's Technique ({3}{W} instant, three tokens = three Took triggers, three Call pings, three Triumph counters), Frontline Rush (two Goblins), or a Fountainport Fish at our end step. If you have Hex or Sheriff, kill Took in response to the token spell instead (zero triggers). If not, counter one Took trigger (denies the third-resolution team counter and blanks her); if Political Triumph is on 3 counters, let the scry trigger resolve and counter the fourth-counter payoff instead.
  • Wasp + Enduring Curiosity: their main deck has no flyers (only sideboard Captain Marvel), so the 2/1 Wasp hits every turn and 'Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card' triggers. Ninjutsu Kaito off the unblocked Wasp only if the Wasp blank no longer matters.
  • Day of Black Sun X=2 ('Each creature with mana value X or less loses all abilities until end of turn. Destroy those creatures') kills every token, Voice, Took, Headliner and Queen at sorcery speed on our turn, immune to Voice. It is SYMMETRICAL: it also destroys our Wasp (2), Drowner (2), Beavers (1), Siren (1), Wan Shi Tong (MV 2 on the battlefield) and Faerie tokens. Curiosity (4), Tidebinder (3) and a creature-Kaito (4) survive. Sweep first, deploy 1- and 2-drops afterward. With Vren, the Relentless out (MV 4, survives), every creature of theirs that would die is exiled instead and Vren makes a Rat per exiled creature at end step.
  • Activated abilities work on their turn under Voice, only spells are locked: Restless Reef '{2}{U}{B}: Until end of turn, this land becomes a 4/4 blue and black Shark creature with deathtouch' is a deathtouch blocker for anthem'd tokens, Voice, or a Frontline-Rush-pumped attacker (NOT for Captain Marvel unless she has already lost flying to Wasp or Tidebinder); Soulstone Sanctuary '{4}: becomes a 3/3 creature with vigilance' is another blocker; Drowner's shuffle removes a stunned creature.
  • Post-board Dawn's Truce (3) protects their permanents at instant speed on our turn: it fizzles Hex/Sheriff/Bitter Triumph on Voice or Took and fizzles Wasp's Sting, but not Tidebinder (it targets an ability). Against a Truce-heavy opponent, bait it with the cheapest removal first, and value Tidebinder's Took/Queen windows higher.
Hold or fire
WASP: it is an allocation decision, not a hold decision. If you have Hex/Sheriff/Bitter Triumph for Voice, use that on Voice and fire Wasp at Took (or the second Voice) on our main phase, ideally when they are tapped out; if Wasp is your only Voice answer, Wasp the Voice on our main phase and accept that Torch or a Headliner ping in response cancels the blank (you still get the tap and a 2/1 flyer, and a Torch spent on Wasp is not spent on Curiosity). Post-board, Queen of Dale before your first noncreature spell. Never hold Wasp for a their-turn combat tap in this matchup: Voice makes that mode unavailable, and without Voice the only creature worth tapping pre-attack is Captain Marvel. From turn 4 with none of Voice/Took/Queen on board, flash it at the end of their turn (only if no live Voice) as a Curiosity clock rather than sitting on it. TIDEBINDER: never hold 3 mana for it alone; hold only when the same mana also backs We Say Thee Nay! or Bitter Triumph in hand, otherwise develop Curiosity (4/3 blocks a 2/2 and survives, and returns if killed). Its live windows are on our turn: Headliner's sac-ping at Wasp/Drowner/Beavers, Queen's recruit trigger off our removal, an instant-speed token entry (Last Ronin's Technique, Frontline Rush, Fountainport Fish) when you have no Hex for Took or Political Triumph is on three counters, and Marvel's same-turn power-up (rare). While an un-blanked Voice is on the battlefield you cannot cast it on their turn at all, so never plan around a their-turn trigger. If no window has appeared by turn 4-5, cast it main phase as a 3/2 with no target; blocking a 2/2 anthem'd token with it is a card-for-token trade (3/2 dies to 2 damage), acceptable only to protect life total. Once Voice is dead or blanked, Tidebinder is a real card again: Took's trigger on Mobilize/Hop tokens, Queen's recruit off our instant-speed spells, Triumph's fourth counter. On the draw: Spell Snare on the turn-2 Voice if you have it, else Hex/Wasp it on our turn 2/3 main. On the play: hold Spell Snare / We Say Thee Nay! for the turn-2 Voice spell.
How many to run
Wasp: keep both main copies, never side them out. It is a good, not premium, card here: one of ~14 answers to the four 2-drops we must kill or blank (4 Voice, 4 Took, 3 Queen post-board, 4 Headliner), plus the only flying Curiosity attacker that costs 2. Tidebinder: keep the 1 main copy (Headliner bodyguard, Queen recruit, Triumph fourth counter, Truce-immune) but do NOT bring in the 2 sideboard copies; its engine-countering windows fall on their turn behind Voice, and on our turn Hex on Took dominates it. Sideboard plan (judgment, not rules): IN 2 Day of Black Sun, 3 Duress (takes Song, Hop, Call, Triumph, Rush, Torch at sorcery speed, immune to Voice), 1 Flashfreeze (Song, Rush, Call, Torch, Headliner are red; dead on their turn under Voice, so only one), 1 Vren, the Relentless (3/4 ward 2 walls 2/2 tokens, exiles their dying creatures, turns Day of Black Sun into a Rat army). OUT 4 Kaito, Bane of Nightmares (a 4-5 loyalty planeswalker on their turn against hasty anthem'd tokens plus Call pings dies the turn he lands), 2 Wan Shi Tong (dies to Day of Black Sun X=2, slow), 1 Hidden Lair (curve drops to 4 without Kaito/WST; keep 22 lands; cut the Tidebinder instead if you prefer 23). Skip Faebloom Trick (3 mana for two 1/1s against 2/2 tokens, uncastable on their turn under Voice) and Spell Pierce (same Voice problem). Bring Annul in for Flashfreeze if they show Rest in Peace or Warleader's Call is beating you. Net: 2 Wasp, 1 Tidebinder in all 60s.
Review trail: confidence high, 6 disputed, 16 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Reviewer 3's Restless Reef correction ('a 4/4 deathtouch blocker for Captain Marvel') is wrong: Captain Marvel's oracle text reads 'Flying, lifelink' and the Shark has deathtouch only, no flying or reach, so it cannot block her until Wasp or Tidebinder has stripped flying. Reviewers 1 and 2 had this right; I used their version.
