  • Reviewer 2's Headliner bodyguard framing ('Drowner-in-response-to-shuffle' as if Tidebinder saves the Voice shuffle) is refined, not adopted wholesale: once Drowner's '{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it' is on the stack, killing Drowner does not stop it resolving on its target (abilities on the stack exist independently of their source, the same principle the rules brief uses for Took's stacked triggers), so the Voice is shuffled either way; Tidebinder on the ping saves Drowner for reuse, nothing more. Kept as a bodyguard line with that wording.
  • Reviewer 2's sideboard plan (IN 2 Day of Black Sun, 3 Duress, 2 Flashfreeze, 1 Vren = 8; OUT 7) does not balance and brings two copies of a counterspell that is dead on their turn under Voice. Adjusted to 1 Flashfreeze so IN 7 = OUT 7; the Kaito/Wan Shi Tong/land cuts are accepted. This is strategy judgment, flagged as such in the page.
  • Reviewer 1's citation letter 'CR 603.7e' for delayed triggers sharing their creator's source: the substance is accepted (it is the standard rule for delayed triggered abilities under CR 603.7) but I cite the section, not the sub-letter, because I could not confirm the letter against the current CR text.
  • Reviewer 1's Political Triumph caution is accepted as phrasing only: the claim that a fifth counter does not re-trigger 'When the fourth plan counter is put on this enchantment' follows directly from the oracle text and no reviewer produced a contrary ruling; the page now says 'should not recur, no ruling on file'.
  • Two rules claims rest on the Comprehensive Rules rather than oracle text and are labelled as such on the page: (a) a 'for as long as' duration whose condition is already false when the effect would begin does nothing (CR 611.2b), which is also the exact logic of Tidebinder ruling 1 in the brief, applied here to Wasp dying in response to its own Sting; (b) delayed triggered abilities share the source of the ability that created them (CR 603.7). I am confident in both; neither changes a recommended play.

vs. Jeskai Control1.9% TB: goodWasp: situational▼
Draw-go control with almost no permanents: 12 cheap instant-speed burn/removal spells (4 Sear, 4 Firebending Lesson, 2 Broadside Barrage, 2 Get Lost), 2 Spell Snare and 1 Three Steps Ahead, plus card-flow (4 Stock Up, 3 Consult the Star Charts, Prismari Charm, Traumatic Critique). Turn 3 Tablet of Discovery ramps them (it enters untapped and taps for {R} or {R}{R}-for-instants at once) and mills a card they may play that turn. From turn 5-6 they cast Thor, God of Thunder (5/5 flier: "Whenever you cast a noncreature spell, Thor deals damage equal to that spell's mana value to any target", turning every removal spell into extra burn at Kaito or our 2/1s) and 3 Jeskai Revelation (7 mana: bounce, 4 damage, two prowess Monks, draw two, gain 4). Their lands are engines too: Cori Mountain Monastery ({3}{R},{T}: impulse a card), Mistrise Village ({U},{T}: next spell uncounterable), Great Hall of the Biblioplex ({5}: permanent 2/4), Petrified Hamlet (locks a named land). Sideboard (15): Wan Shi Tong 2, Emeritus of Ideation 2 (comes with a {U} Ancestral Recall castable from exile), Torpor Orb 1, Disdainful Stroke 1, Return the Favor 1, Slagstorm 1 (their only sorcery-speed sweeper), Ghost Vacuum 1, Flashfreeze 3 (dead against us: we cast no red or green spells), Get Lost 1, Broadside Barrage 1, Petrified Hamlet 1.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Governing rule for this matchup: Tidebinder must SURVIVE until its own trigger resolves for the blank to happen. Against 4 Firebending Lesson ({R}, 2 damage), 4 Sear, 2 Barrage and Get Lost at instant speed, they can kill the 3/2 in response to its ETB; by Tidebinder ruling 1 the targeted ability is still countered but the permanent keeps its abilities. So price every target by the countered ability ALONE unless they are tapped below {R} (and, once Thor is online, remember any response spell also fires a Thor trigger for its MV at Tidebinder). On that pricing the main-deck copy is Thor insurance: counter a 5+ MV cast trigger (Revelation 7, Critique X+2) and you save that damage no matter what they do; counter Thor's ETB when he is cast on exactly five tapped-out lands and Thor is blanked from the start. Postboard, Wan Shi Tong's ETB is the best target in the 75 (no counters, no cards, for X+2 mana, and that loss is permanent even if they Lesson Tidebinder next turn). Tablet of Discovery is real but situational: Tablet itself gives them {R}/{R}{R} to answer Tidebinder in response, so the blank sticks only when they hold no Lesson/Sear. Everything else on their side is spells (never Tidebinder targets), statics, or lands (never blanked, tempo only). The 3/2 flash body is independently good here: MV 3 dodges Spell Snare (MV 2) and Disdainful Stroke (MV 4+), and it forces one of their burn spells.
#1Thor, God of Thunder (2 main), the cast-damage trigger
"Whenever you cast a noncreature spell, Thor deals damage equal to that spell's mana value to any target."
WhyThe one target whose countered ability is worth 3 mana by itself: a Revelation trigger is 7 damage (plus Revelation's own 4 = 11), Critique X+2, Barrage 3. Countering it saves that damage whatever they do afterwards, and if Tidebinder survives, Thor is blanked (no flying, no future triggers) while it lives. Also the answer when Thor aims a trigger at your Wasp/Siren/Beavers/Drowner (any spell of MV 1-2 kills them): counter the trigger, the creature lives, Thor is blanked. The spell that caused the trigger STILL RESOLVES, Tidebinder counters abilities, not spells.
WhenThey cast a noncreature spell; the Thor trigger goes on the stack ABOVE the spell and they choose its target then. With the trigger on the stack, flash Tidebinder targeting it. Tidebinder's ETB resolves first (above both the trigger and the spell): trigger countered, Thor blanked, then the spell resolves. Fire on the biggest MV you expect (Revelation 7, Critique, Barrage 3) or on a trigger that kills a creature/Kaito; do not spend it on a Sear (MV 2) trigger aimed at your face.
AfterLoses all abilities (flying, both triggers) while Tidebinder lives. The underlying spell resolves normally. If they respond to Tidebinder's ETB with another instant (Lesson {R} or Sear), that cast fires a NEW Thor trigger for its MV; if it or the spell kills Tidebinder first, the targeted trigger is still countered (ruling 1) but Thor is NOT blanked.
RiskYou counter only one trigger; a second spell in response makes a second trigger. Expect the blank to stick only when they are tapped out after the big spell (common after a 7-mana Revelation). Return the Favor (side, 1) can copy the trigger or move Tidebinder's trigger onto a throwaway ability.
#2Wan Shi Tong, Librarian (2 side), the ETB
"When Wan Shi Tong enters, put X +1/+1 counters on him. Then draw half X cards, rounded down."
WhyHighest permanent value in the 75 once boarded: they paid X+2 mana and get a printed 1/1 with no counters and no cards, and the counters/cards never come back even if Tidebinder dies next turn. If Tidebinder survives, he also loses flying, vigilance and the search trigger. Bonus: X is 0 on the battlefield so his MV is 2 and Requiting Hex kills him regardless of the X paid.
WhenUsually cast with flash at YOUR end step for most of their mana. Trigger goes on the stack; respond with Tidebinder (you need 3 untapped at your own end step: postboard, hold it when they pass with 5+ open). Also fine at sorcery speed on their turn.
AfterETB never resolves (no counters, no cards). While Tidebinder lives: no flying, vigilance or search trigger. If Tidebinder later dies he regains flying/vigilance but stays a 1/1.
RiskThey can hold {R} back for Lesson in response; the countered ETB alone still pays for the card. Not worth it if they pay X=0-1.
#3Thor, God of Thunder, the ETB
"When Thor enters, exile target Equipment, instant, or sorcery card from your graveyard. Until the end of your next turn, you may play that card."
WhyCountering the ETB denies the graveyard re-buy for good (often a Revelation or Sear), and if Tidebinder survives, Thor is blanked before any cast trigger can ever fire. Both windows (ETB and cast trigger) blank Thor fully; the ETB window's advantage is that no damage ever gets through and a mute ground 5/5 is easy for Kaito -2 stun, Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff (God Warrior Hero, not an outlaw) or Drowner stun-and-shuffle. Its disadvantage: when they have {R} open (Tablet, spare land) the blank is fragile and the re-buy alone is often just a Sear.
WhenTheir main phase (Thor is sorcery-speed): Thor resolves, ETB goes on the stack targeting a graveyard card. Flash Tidebinder on the ETB. Take it when Thor is cast on exactly five lands with everything tapped; with red open, prefer to hold for the first big cast trigger. If their graveyard has no instant/sorcery/Equipment the ETB is removed for lack of targets and cannot be targeted (rare after turn 5). With 5 mana you may instead We Say Thee Nay! the Thor spell (unless Mistrise Village resolved first).
AfterLoses flying, the re-buy and the cast trigger while Tidebinder lives. Keeps 5/5 body, legendary, types. The ETB stays countered forever; cast triggers resume the moment Tidebinder leaves.
RiskA Lesson in response kills Tidebinder before the blank (ruling 1); Thor then attacks as a full 5/5 flier and pings off every spell. Have a real Thor answer in hand before you commit the only main-deck copy this way.
#4Cori Mountain Monastery (2 main)
"{3}{R}, {T}: Exile the top card of your library. Until the end of your next turn, you may play that card."
WhyThe one spot where the fragility problem mostly disappears: they activate at YOUR end step with 4 of their mana, so they are usually below {R} and cannot kill Tidebinder in response. 4 mana wasted, card denied, and a 3/2 attacks into an empty board next turn. A land, so nothing is blanked and it works again next turn; this is a body-plus-bonus play, not a lock.
WhenIn response to the activation on the stack (typically your end step or their main phase with spare mana). Check they cannot still tap Tablet for {R}{R} (instants only) before you commit.
AfterNo card exiled; the 4 mana and the land tap stay spent. Land keeps all abilities.
RiskSkip it if you would rather keep that 3 mana for We Say Thee Nay! on a Revelation you expect next turn.
#5Tablet of Discovery (4 main), the ETB (situational, best postboard with 2+ copies)
"When this artifact enters, mill a card. You may play that card this turn." (its "{T}: Add {R}." and "{T}: Add {R}{R}. Spend this mana only to cast instant and sorcery spells." are mana abilities and cannot be targeted)
WhyTablet is an ARTIFACT, so if Tidebinder survives, Tablet loses ALL abilities including both mana abilities while Tidebinder lives, and the milled card is denied. But Tablet enters untapped: they can tap it for {R} (Lesson) or {R}{R} (Sear) in response to Tidebinder's trigger, and then the mill is denied but the rock stays online (ruling 1). Even a successful blank usually lasts until their next {R}. Not worth the single main-deck copy (that is Thor insurance) or a passed turn 3 on the play; fine postboard when a Thor answer is already in hand and you have nothing else for the mana.
WhenTheir main phase, Tablet resolves, the ETB goes on the stack; flash Tidebinder on it. Turn 3 on the play only (3 open mana on their turn 3); on the draw you have 2 lands. Copies 2-4 later in the game are worth less since they are already ramped. Postboard Annul ({U}, counters the artifact spell) is the cleaner answer.
AfterIf it sticks: no mill, no {T}: Add {R}, no {R}{R}; still an artifact (a Three Steps Ahead token copy has printed abilities and its own counterable ETB). Ends when Tidebinder dies or is bounced (Sear, Lesson, Prismari Charm, Revelation).
RiskLesson off Tablet's own mana in response: Tidebinder dead, Tablet fully online, you are down a card and your turn 3.
#6Emeritus of Ideation (2 side), the attack trigger, and its ward trigger
"Whenever this creature attacks, you may exile eight cards from your graveyard. If you do, this creature becomes prepared." and its ward: "Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter it unless that player pays {2}."
WhyTidebinder is NEVER taxed by ward: its trigger targets an ability on the stack, not the creature (CR 702.21b). Countering the attack trigger for a flat 3 mana stops the second Ancestral Recall and, if Tidebinder survives, Emeritus (a creature) loses flying AND ward, so Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff (Human Wizard, non-outlaw) then costs 2 with no tax. Second window: cast Triumph/Sheriff on Emeritus, ward triggers, flash Tidebinder on the WARD trigger; your removal resolves untaxed and you keep a 3/2. That costs 3 instead of paying {2}, so take it only when the body matters.
WhenAttack trigger: their declare-attackers step, trigger on the stack. Ward trigger: in response to it, after your own removal spell targets Emeritus.
AfterAttack trigger countered (no exile, no new prepared status). If Tidebinder survives: no flying, no ward, no attack trigger while it lives. Emeritus STAYS prepared and the Recall copy in exile stays castable for {U} (Scryfall ruling 2026-03-20).
Risk5/5 attacker still hits you this combat; blanking does not stop the attack already declared.
#7Mistrise Village (1 main)
"{U}, {T}: The next spell you cast this turn can't be countered." (not a mana ability)
WhyA delay play, not a counter. The Village ability must resolve BEFORE they cast the protected spell, so when Tidebinder counters it they know before committing and simply do not cast Revelation/Thor that turn. Worth 3 mana only when the spell they wanted to force through wins them the game this turn, or when you want the 3/2 body anyway and also hold a counterspell (Tidebinder 3 + We Say Thee Nay! 2 or Flashfreeze 2).
WhenIn response to the activation while it is on the stack. If they cast the spell in response to Tidebinder's trigger, the Village ability has not resolved and the spell is counterable.
AfterNothing blanked. Land tapped and {U} spent; their next spell is counterable again; Village works again next turn.
RiskIf you cannot also counter the spell, skip it.
#8Petrified Hamlet (2 main, 1 side)
"When this land enters, choose a land card name." (the lock, "Activated abilities of sources with the chosen name can't be activated unless they're mana abilities.", is static and cannot be countered)
WhyThe ETB is the only window. If countered no name is ever chosen, so the statics do nothing all game; Hamlet is a colorless land. Against us they name Restless Reef or Soulstone Sanctuary. Only worth it in a game where Reef beats (4/4 deathtouch, mills 4 on attack) are your realistic path through their removal.
WhenTheir main phase when Hamlet is their land drop; the choose-name trigger goes on the stack. Land: nothing blanked.
AfterHamlet keeps "{T}: Add {C}."; the other abilities are dead text forever.
RiskUsually low value; they may name a land you never animate.
#9Great Hall of the Biblioplex (4 main), once animated
The granted "Whenever you cast an instant or sorcery spell, this creature gets +1/+0 until end of turn." (exists only after the {5} animation has resolved)
WhyOnce Great Hall is a 2/4 creature, countering its pump trigger blanks the whole permanent, mana abilities included, so it cannot tap for mana while Tidebinder lives. Cute; Requiting Hex (MV 0) or Drowner stun-and-shuffle handle the animated land better.
WhenOnly after the {5} animation has resolved, when they cast an instant/sorcery and the +1/+0 trigger is on the stack. Countering the {5} animation itself blanks nothing (see traps).
AfterLoses pump trigger, "{T}: Add {C}." and the pay-1-life mana ability. Keeps 2/4 Wizard creature-land status.
RiskRequires them to have spent 5 mana first; rarely matters.
#10Ghost Vacuum (1 side)
"{T}: Exile target card from a graveyard."
WhyIf they aim Vacuum at Enduring Curiosity in your graveyard while its "When Enduring Curiosity dies, if it was a creature, return it to the battlefield" trigger is on the stack, countering the activation saves Curiosity, and the artifact loses all abilities while Tidebinder lives.
WhenIn response to the Vacuum activation on the stack.
AfterVacuum is a blank artifact while Tidebinder lives. Its {6} ability is not worth countering: sacrifice is a cost, nothing remains to blank.
RiskOne sideboard copy that may not come in against us.
Traps
Emeritus of Ideation: Ward {2} taxes Tidebinder's trigger when you counter Emeritus's attack trigger. Ward (CR 702.21b) triggers only when the warded PERMANENT becomes the target. Tidebinder's ETB targets an ability on the stack, never a permanent, so ward never triggers on Tidebinder. Only Wasp's Sting, Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff, Kaito -2 and Drowner's ETB (which target Emeritus himself) pay ward.
Any target while they hold {R}: Countering the ability guarantees the permanent is blanked. Tidebinder ruling 1: if Tidebinder leaves the battlefield before its trigger resolves, the ability is still countered but the source keeps its abilities. Lesson ({R}) or Sear in response kills the 3/2 first. Fire for the blank only when they are tapped below {R} (Tablet counts: it taps for {R} or {R}{R} for instants at once).
Great Hall of the Biblioplex: Counter the {5} animation and the land loses all abilities. Tidebinder checks whether the source is an artifact, creature or planeswalker AS ITS TRIGGER RESOLVES (ruling 3). The animation has not resolved, so Great Hall is still just a land: their 5 mana is wasted but nothing is blanked, and they can activate it again as soon as they have {5} to spare.
Tablet of Discovery: Shut off the mana by targeting "{T}: Add {R}{R}". Both tap abilities are mana abilities (add mana, no target, not loyalty) and cannot be targeted. The ETB is the ONLY window.
Emeritus of Ideation (side): Counter or blank the 'enters prepared' to stop Ancestral Recall. "This creature enters prepared." is not a triggered ability, and the Recall copy is created in exile as it enters, nothing on the stack. Casting that copy (for {U}) is casting a spell: answer it with Spell Pierce/We Say Thee Nay!. Scryfall ruling 2026-03-20: a prepared creature that loses all abilities stays prepared and the copy stays castable.
Torpor Orb (side): Tidebinder something about it. "Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger." is static, nothing to target. With Orb out Tidebinder, Wasp, Drowner, Beavers, Siren and our Wan Shi Tong all enter with NO trigger. Kill it on the stack: Spell Snare (MV 2), Annul, Spell Pierce, We Say Thee Nay!, or Duress it first.
Petrified Hamlet: Counter the lock once it names Restless Reef. The lock is a static ability. Once the ETB has resolved there is nothing left to counter.
Thor, God of Thunder: Countering Thor's trigger also stops the spell, or countering the spell stops the trigger. Two separate objects. The trigger fires on CAST, so it is already on the stack even if you then We Say Thee Nay! the spell; Tidebinder countering the trigger leaves the spell to resolve. You need both answers to stop both.
Thor, God of Thunder: The spell that made the trigger can be the Sear that kills Tidebinder. Targets are chosen when the spell is cast, before Tidebinder was on the battlefield (CR 601.2c); and Tidebinder's ETB sits on top of the stack and resolves first, so Thor IS blanked before that spell resolves. Ruling 1 bites only if they cast a SECOND instant in response to Tidebinder's trigger.
Consult the Star Charts / Firebending Lesson kicker, Three Steps Ahead / Return the Favor spree, Flashback (the card): Counter the kicker, the spree modes, or the flashback cast. Kicker and spree are additional costs of casting; flashback is a way to cast a spell; casting a spell is not an ability. Flashback (the card) targets a graveyard card, so nothing to blank either.
Hallowed Fountain / Steam Vents: Counter the 'pay 2 life or enters tapped'. "As this land enters, you may pay 2 life. If you don't, it enters tapped." is a replacement effect, not a trigger.
Return the Favor (side): They can redirect Tidebinder's trigger onto Kaito or Curiosity at will. The new target must be legal for Tidebinder's trigger: another activated or triggered ability ON THE STACK at that moment. The common use is moving it onto one of THEIR throwaway triggers (a Monk prowess trigger, a Thundering Falls surveil) so Thor/Tablet is spared. It hits YOUR permanent only if one of your abilities is on the stack at the same time (you flash Tidebinder on top of your own Drowner ETB or Kaito -2). Same limit for the copy mode: a copy of Tidebinder's trigger with a new target can counter one of your stacked abilities, and 'this creature' still means your Tidebinder, so your Kaito/Drowner would lose abilities while your own Tidebinder lives. Postboard, do not stack Tidebinder on top of your own abilities while they have {R}{R}+{1} open.
Jeskai Revelation Monk tokens: Prowess is a trigger, blank a Monk. Legal but worthless: a blanked Monk is still a 1/1 body and could never block a flying Wasp anyway.
Not worth it
Thundering Falls "When this land enters, surveil 1.": counterable but a land is never blanked and surveil 1 is nothing.Monk token prowess: a 1/1 with no abilities is still a 1/1.Great Hall's {5} animation as a 'mana waste' play: costs you 3 and a card to waste 5 they had spare; it never blanks the land.Ghost Vacuum's {6} return ability: sacrifice is a cost, so nothing remains to blank; they never activate it into open mana anyway.Wan Shi Tong's second trigger "Whenever an opponent searches their library...": counterable, but our 75 has no library search (Map explore does not search), so it never fires. Do not wait for it.Get Lost's Map tokens are created for OUR side ("Its controller creates two Map tokens"); their explore activation is ours, not an opposing ability.Petrified Hamlet before turn 5: the lock only matters in the long Reef/Sanctuary game.Tablet of Discovery with the single main-deck copy: it is Thor insurance; Tablet is a postboard luxury.
The Wondrous Wasp
Jeskai Control has two creatures in the main 60 (Thor x2) and a handful postboard, so Wasp's Sting is usually 'up to one target' with nothing to hit. Wasp's job here is ~90% the body: a 2-mana flash evasive attacker that draws off Enduring Curiosity and, above all, a ninjutsu carrier for Kaito past Spell Snare, Disdainful Stroke and Three Steps Ahead. The Sting matters in two spots: their beginning of combat when they are tapped below {R} (tap + blank Thor so he neither attacks nor pings), and OUR precombat to tap their lone blocker for an alpha plus ninjutsu. Into open red or any held instant with Thor online, expect tap-only: their response spell fires a Thor trigger for its MV, the 2/1 dies before the Sting resolves, and the tap still happens but the blank never begins. All their main-deck removal is instant-speed (Sear, Lesson, Barrage, Get Lost, Prismari Charm, Revelation), so no window is safe; cast Wasp at their end step after they have spent mana in their turn to minimise open Snare/Lesson mana. Postboard Slagstorm (sorcery, 3 to each creature) is their one sweeper.
#1Thor, God of Thunder
WhyTap + blank: Thor does not attack this turn and, while Wasp lives, loses flying and "Whenever you cast a noncreature spell, Thor deals damage equal to that spell's mana value to any target". Two mana to switch off their only main-deck threat; every burn spell aimed at Wasp is one not aimed at Kaito.
WhenBEFORE attackers: their beginning of combat (or upkeep). Tapping after attackers are declared does not remove Thor from combat. Prefer it when they are tapped below {R}: if they respond with any instant, the Thor trigger it creates kills the 2/1 before the Sting resolves. Wasp never counters a trigger already on the stack; that is Tidebinder's job.
AfterThor: tapped, no flying, no cast trigger while Wasp lives; keeps 5/5 body and legendary. If Wasp dies in response, Thor is still tapped but NOT blanked (the duration ended before the effect began). If Wasp dies later, Thor comes back online at once.
#2Thor / Wan Shi Tong / Emeritus as their lone BLOCKER on our turn
WhyPrecombat Wasp taps the only creature that could block; Kaito (3/4 hexproof Ninja creature on your turn), Siren, Beavers and Wasp all attack, Curiosity draws a card per connect, and Kaito ninjutsu off the unblocked Wasp lands a planeswalker their counterspells can never touch. The tap sticks even if Wasp dies in response.
WhenYour precombat main phase (Wasp is not summoning-sick-relevant for the Sting; it only needs to resolve before blockers). Pay ward {2} if the blocker is Emeritus.
AfterBlocker tapped for the turn; blanked while Wasp lives. Ninjutsu returning Wasp to hand ends the blank, so do it when the tap alone was the point.
#3Wan Shi Tong, Librarian (side)
WhyThe tap removes him as a blocker this turn only; the blank removes flying (Kaito/ground creatures can now block him), vigilance (attacking taps him) and the search trigger. +1/+1 counters STAY, so he is still a big body. Wasp cannot stop the ETB; that is Tidebinder's job. Requiting Hex (MV 2 on the battlefield whatever X was) is usually the real answer.
WhenTheir beginning of combat to stop an attack, or your precombat to remove a flying blocker.
AfterTapped (1+X)/(1+X) body with all counters intact; no flying, vigilance or search trigger while Wasp lives.
#4Emeritus of Ideation (side)
WhyWasp must pay ward {2} (4 mana total) or its Sting is countered. Blank removes flying, ward and the attack trigger (no second Recall) but does NOT unprepare him: the first Recall copy stays castable (ruling 2026-03-20). Wasp-then-Triumph costs 6 total vs 4 for Triumph straight through ward, so only take the Wasp route when you also want the flying body or must stop the attack trigger this combat.
WhenTheir beginning of combat (stops the attack and the attack trigger). Any other time is just ward-removal at a premium.
AfterTapped 5/5 with no abilities while Wasp lives; still prepared; Recall still castable by them for {U}.
#5Great Hall of the Biblioplex (animated 2/4)
WhyA blanked creature-land loses its mana abilities too: while Wasp lives it cannot tap for mana and has no pump. Tapping it also removes a blocker for the turn.
WhenAfter the {5} animation has resolved; ideally their upkeep so it is tapped and mute for their whole turn.
AfterTapped 2/4 Wizard land with no abilities while Wasp lives.
Traps
Thor, God of Thunder: Flash Wasp in response to Thor's damage trigger to stop it. Wasp's Sting taps and blanks; it counters nothing. A trigger already on the stack resolves. Only Tidebinder counters it.
Thor, God of Thunder: Tap Thor after he is declared as an attacker. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat. Wasp must resolve at their beginning of combat or earlier.
Thor, God of Thunder: Sting Thor into open mana and expect the blank. Any instant cast in response fires a Thor trigger for its MV (Consult 2, Lesson 1, Charm 2) at the 2/1; Wasp dies before the Sting resolves, so Thor is tapped but keeps all abilities (CR 611.2b: a duration that has already ended means no continuous effect).
Emeritus of Ideation: Blank Emeritus so they cannot cast Ancestral Recall. Prepared status and the exiled Recall copy survive 'loses all abilities' (ruling 2026-03-20). Blanking stops only the attack trigger (second Recall), flying and ward.
Wan Shi Tong, Librarian: Blank him to shrink him, or blank him so he cannot block. +1/+1 counters are not abilities and stay; losing flying does not stop a creature from blocking ground attackers. Only the tap removes him as a blocker, and only for that turn.
Torpor Orb (side): Wasp still Stings with Orb out. "Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger.": Wasp's Sting is an ETB trigger and never happens. Wasp is a 2/1 flash flier and nothing more until Orb is gone.
Return the Favor (side): Wasp's Sting can only hurt them. Any creature on the battlefield is a legal new target, so they can move the Sting onto your Enduring Curiosity or Kaito-as-creature (your turn only). Kaito's hexproof does not protect him because the Sting is an ability YOU control (CR 702.11b). Your creature is tapped and blanked while Wasp lives.
Monk tokens (Jeskai Revelation): Blank a prowess Monk. Legal but worthless: a blanked Monk is still a 1/1 body, and it could never block the flying Wasp in the first place.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Revelation with Thor online: they cast Jeskai Revelation (MV 7); the Thor trigger for 7 goes on the stack. With 5 mana: Tidebinder counters the Thor trigger AND We Say Thee Nay! counters the Revelation (they must pay {2}, or {4} if you tap creatures with total power 2+ for teamwork; the just-cast Tidebinder, power 3, can be tapped for it, since summoning sickness only restricts {T} abilities and attacking). Their 7 vs your 5; they need 9 open to pay, 11 with teamwork. With 3 mana: Tidebinder on the trigger only; the Revelation resolves but you saved 7 damage.
  • Thor's trigger at your creature: they Sear Kaito, Thor trigger (2) aimed at your Wasp/Siren/Drowner. Tidebinder counters the trigger: creature lives, Thor blanked while Tidebinder survives, Sear still resolves.
  • Thor answered in one turn (they are tapped out): Tidebinder counters the ETB, Thor is a mute ground 5/5; on your turn Kaito -2 ("Tap target creature. Put two stun counters on it.") or Bitter Triumph/Shoot the Sheriff removes him. With only Drowner: Drowner ETB stuns Thor, next turn "{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries" removes Thor for good with no removal spent.
  • Postboard Wan Shi Tong at your end step: Tidebinder counters the ETB (1/1, no counters, no cards; no flying while Tidebinder lives). Then Requiting Hex ("Destroy target creature with mana value 2 or less") kills it: X is 0 on the battlefield so MV is 2 whatever they paid. Without Tidebinder, Wasp tap+blank plus Hex still kills it.
  • Our-turn alpha: Wasp precombat taps their only creature (Thor, or a boarded Wan Shi Tong/Emeritus, paying ward {2} for Emeritus), Kaito 3/4 + fliers attack, Curiosity draws, and Kaito ninjutsu off the unblocked Wasp lands a walker that Spell Snare/Disdainful Stroke/Three Steps Ahead never see. Ninjutsu ends Wasp's blank, so do this when the tap alone was the point (Thor already stunned or dead).
  • Wasp beginning-of-combat lock on Thor when they are tapped below {R}: tap + blank, then attack with Wasp in the air; every connect draws off Enduring Curiosity ("Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card").
  • Emeritus postboard: do not fight the first Ancestral Recall with Tidebinder/Wasp; Spell Pierce or We Say Thee Nay! the {U} copy when they are tapped low after paying 5 for Emeritus. When he attacks, Tidebinder counters the attack trigger untaxed (ward never triggers on Tidebinder); if Tidebinder survives, Emeritus has no flying and no ward, so Triumph/Sheriff costs 2 flat. Or: Triumph Emeritus, ward triggers, Tidebinder counters the ward trigger and you keep a 3/2 instead of paying {2}.
  • Cori Mountain Monastery at your end step: they tap 4 for the impulse; Tidebinder counters it while they are below {R}. Card denied, 4 mana wasted, 3/2 attacks next turn.
  • Mistrise Village before Revelation/Thor: Tidebinder counters the activation only if you also hold Flashfreeze (side; Revelation, Thor, Sear, Lesson, Barrage, Tablet and Critique are all red spells) or We Say Thee Nay! and the mana for both. Expect them to simply not cast the spell that turn; it is a delay, not a counter.
  • Tablet on the play, postboard only: pass turn 3 with 3 open; Tablet resolves, ETB on the stack, Tidebinder on it. If they hold no Lesson/Sear, Tablet is a blank rock and the mill is denied; if they Lesson off Tablet's own {R}, the mill is still denied but Tablet stays online (ruling 1). Do this only with a second Tidebinder in the 75 and a Thor answer in hand.
  • Animated Great Hall: Requiting Hex kills it (MV 0), or Drowner stun then shuffle it into their library. Wasp on it shuts off its mana abilities while Wasp lives.
  • Protecting Curiosity's return from Ghost Vacuum: Curiosity dies, its return trigger is on the stack, they tap Vacuum at Curiosity in your graveyard; Tidebinder counters the activation, Vacuum is blanked, Curiosity returns as an enchantment.
  • Baiting Spell Snare: they hold Snare for your MV-2 plays (Wasp, Drowner, We Say Thee Nay!, Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph). Lead with Drowner at their end step; Tidebinder (MV 3) and Siren/Beavers (MV 1) are Snare-proof; Kaito/Curiosity (MV 4) are the Disdainful Stroke targets postboard.
Hold or fire
Top rule: fire Tidebinder for the BLANK only when they are tapped below {R} (Tablet counts as {R} open); otherwise fire only for a countered ability worth 3 mana on its own (Thor trigger of 5+ MV or one that kills your creature/Kaito, Wan Shi Tong ETB, a Monastery impulse they already paid 4 for). The single main-deck copy is Thor insurance. ON THE PLAY: develop normally (Siren/Drowner/Wasp), keep Tidebinder for Thor: ETB if he lands on exactly five tapped-out lands, else the first big cast trigger. Do not pass turn 3 for Tablet with your only copy. ON THE DRAW: cast Kaito turn 4 (or ninjutsu him off an unblocked flier) and accept Thor's ETB resolving; Tidebinder becomes cast-trigger insurance for the first Revelation/Critique trigger. Hold 3 open on their turn 5 only if Kaito is already on board. Fire as a bare 3/2 body when (a) you are on a clock and they are tapped out at their end step, or (b) it is turn 7+ and no Thor has appeared; never keep Tidebinder in hand while Thor is already pinging, the next 5+ MV trigger is the target. POSTBOARD: with 2 copies, one is Wan Shi Tong insurance at your end step when they pass with 5+ open; the other follows the Thor rule; Tablet is a bonus only when a Thor answer is already in hand. WASP: end-step flier for Curiosity in the main; upgrade to a beginning-of-combat Sting on Thor only when they are below {R}; use the precombat tap-the-blocker line on your turn whenever it enables an alpha or ninjutsu. Never Sting something just because you can; the trigger is 'up to one'. If you have seen Return the Favor, cast Tidebinder/Wasp when they are tapped below {R}{R}+{1} and never stack them on top of your own abilities.
How many to run
Main 1 Tidebinder + 2 Wasp is right; do not add Wasp (Spell Snare, Sear, Lesson, Prismari Charm's 1 damage and Slagstorm all kill it, and their main has two creatures to Sting). Note the featured Dimir 15 (Duress 3, Day of Black Sun 2, Annul, Ghost Vacuum, Spell Pierce 2, Strategic Betrayal, Vren, Flashfreeze 2, Faebloom Trick 2) carries NO extra Tidebinder; the 2 sideboard copies exist only in the Karsten aggregate. If your 75 has them, bring in ONE (2 total): the Snare/Stroke-proof 3/2 body is a good clock and the Thor/Wan Shi Tong lines are real, but few permanents plus 12 instant burn make a third copy worse than the counterspell suite. Complete plan, IN (9): Duress 3, Spell Pierce 2, Flashfreeze 2 (Thor, Revelation, Sear, Lesson, Barrage, Tablet, Critique, Charm are all red spells), Annul 1 (Tablet of Discovery and Torpor Orb are artifact spells), Tidebinder 1. OUT (9): Requiting Hex 4 (targets are Monks, Wan Shi Tong at MV 2 and an animated Great Hall only), Shoot the Sheriff 1, Floodpits Drowner 3 (its ETB has no target vs a 2-creature main deck and the 2/1 dies to Lesson/Charm/Slagstorm; keep 1 for the Thor stun-and-shuffle line), and the 4th Drowner (or 1 Wasp if you prefer the Drowner shuffle redundancy). Without a sideboard Tidebinder the plan is 8 in / 8 out, keeping the 4th Drowner. Keep both Bitter Triumph and 1 Sheriff for Thor/Emeritus/Wan Shi Tong; keep our 2 Wan Shi Tong (flash card draw is excellent against draw-go).
Review trail: confidence medium, 10 disputed, 17 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Rules brief line 'Ward taxes Wasp's trigger and Tidebinder's trigger', rejected for Tidebinder: CR 702.21b ward triggers only when the warded PERMANENT becomes the target of an opponent's spell or ability. Tidebinder's ETB targets an ability on the stack, never a permanent, so ward never triggers on it. Wasp's Sting does target the creature and IS taxed. All three reviewers agree; the correction is applied throughout.
  • Reviewer 2's 'demote Tablet of Discovery to dont_bother', partially rejected: kept as a situational priority-5 target because the blank does stick when they hold no Lesson/Sear (Tablet oracle gives them {R}/{R}{R} at once, so it is conditional, not impossible), and the mill denial is permanent either way. Accepted the core point: it is not worth the single main-deck copy or a passed turn 3; postboard-only with a Thor answer in hand.
  • Reviewer 2's 'they cast Wan Shi Tong at our end step with ALL their mana (so they cannot respond)', partially rejected: X is their choice and they can hold {R} for Lesson; the entry says so. Accepted the ranking: the countered ETB (no counters, no cards) pays for the card even without the blank, so it is the top postboard target.
  • 'Blank Emeritus of Ideation to stop Ancestral Recall', rejected: Scryfall ruling 2026-03-20: 'If a prepared creature loses all abilities, it won't stop being prepared, and nothing will happen to its alternative characteristics or to the copy of its prepare spell in exile.' 'This creature enters prepared.' is also not a triggered ability.
  • 'Countering Great Hall of the Biblioplex's {5} animation blanks the land', rejected: Tidebinder ruling 3 checks the source's type as the trigger RESOLVES; the animation has not resolved, so Great Hall is a land, not a creature.
  • 'Tidebinder can turn off Tablet of Discovery's mana at any time', rejected: both tap abilities are mana abilities (CR 605.1a) and cannot be targeted; only the ETB window blanks it, and 'loses all abilities' (layer 6) does then remove mana abilities.
  • 'Wasp can be flashed in to stop Thor's cast-damage trigger', rejected: Wasp's Sting taps and removes abilities; it does not counter an ability on the stack.
  • 'Kicker/spree/flashback casts are counterable abilities', rejected: additional costs and alternative casting methods are part of casting a spell, which is not an activated or triggered ability.
  • 'Wan Shi Tong's MV changes with X on the battlefield', rejected: CR 202.3b, X is 0 for a permanent on the battlefield, so his MV is 2 and Requiting Hex hits him.
  • 'Blanking Wan Shi Tong removes his counters', rejected: counters are not abilities; layer-6 ability removal leaves +1/+1 counters in place.

vs. Mono-Black Aggro1.7% TB: situationalWasp: situational▼
Twenty-three cheap creatures: twelve one-drops (4 Forsaken Miner, 4 Iridescent Vinelasher, 4 Nighthowl Pursuer) and eleven two-drops (4 Desolation Prowler, 4 Sunset Saboteur, 3 Gollum), backed by 10 one-to-two-mana instants (4 Shoot the Sheriff, 4 Dissection Practice, 2 Requiting Hex) and 4 Corpses of the Lost; they aim to kill on turn 4-5. The engine is crime-driven recursion: Vinelasher's landfall ping, Dissection Practice, Requiting Hex, Shoot the Sheriff and Saboteur's attack trigger all target us, and each crime lets Forsaken Miner return from the graveyard for {B}, as a 3/2 haste once Corpses of the Lost is out ("Skeletons you control get +1/+0 and have haste"). Damage comes from Prowler's 2-life pump, Saboteur's 4/1 menace, Vinelasher pings on every land, hasty Skeleton Pirate tokens from Corpses (rebought by its end-step bounce), and Gollum's three one-shot payoffs (a +1/+1 counter, drain 2, draw a card; each mode fires at most once per Gollum) when we cast spells of the named parity. Four of their creature types are outlaws (Miner and Saboteur are Rogues, Vinelasher an Assassin, the Corpses token a Pirate, and an animated Soulstone Sanctuary has all creature types), so our Shoot the Sheriff only hits Pursuer, Prowler, Gollum and sideboard Elegy Acolyte.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Every ability in this deck is small and 8 of their 10 instants end the blank on the spot, so Tidebinder is mostly a 3/2 flash body here; cast it for the body and take whichever counter is on the stack. The counters that are genuinely worth waiting a step for: Corpses of the Lost's ETB token (their whole turn-3 mana for nothing, and it lands exactly when we have 3 open), Sunset Saboteur's attack trigger (targets the ability, so no ward discard; the blank strips menace and a single blocker kills the 4/1), and Desolation Prowler's pump in a combat we are already in (the 2 life stays paid, the pump is gone, Prowler dies as a 2/2). Gollum's cast trigger is a real but expensive target: it only exists when we cast a spell, so the line costs 4-5 mana and Gollum's lifetime output is capped at three modes anyway.
#1Corpses of the Lost
When this enchantment enters, create a 2/2 black Skeleton Pirate creature token.
WhyThe on-curve Tidebinder line in this matchup. Corpses is their turn-3 play; countering the token trigger denies a 3/2 haste attacker, wastes their entire turn of mana, and leaves us a 3/2 blocker. Nothing is blanked (Corpses is an enchantment) but the tempo swing is the largest any single counter produces against this deck.
WhenTheir main phase, usually turn 3: Corpses resolves, the token trigger goes on the stack, flash Tidebinder targeting it. Requires 3 untapped mana on their turn, so on our turn 3 hold mana rather than tapping out (on the draw this is their turn 3 = our 3 lands). The end-step bounce ('if you descended this turn, you may pay 1 life. If you do, return this enchantment to its owner's hand') is a second, weaker window: it strands Corpses so they cannot recast it for another token that turn; the 1 life is paid on resolution so countering costs them nothing.
AfterNothing lost: Corpses is an enchantment, so as Tidebinder's trigger resolves the source is not an artifact, creature or planeswalker (ruling 3). It keeps the Skeleton anthem, the haste grant and both triggers. Only that one token (or that one bounce) is denied. Miner and existing tokens keep +1/+0 and haste.
RiskThey can bounce and recast Corpses later for the token anyway (3 more mana plus a descend). Tidebinder is then a 3/2 that trades with a 3/2 Miner or a 3/1 Gollum and loses to a pumped Prowler.
#2Sunset Saboteur
Whenever this creature attacks, put a +1/+1 counter on target creature an opponent controls.
WhyA 4/1 menace with Ward, Discard a card is otherwise miserable: Wasp, Drowner, Requiting Hex, Bitter Triumph and Kaito -2 all cost a discard and Shoot the Sheriff cannot target a Rogue. Tidebinder targets the ability on the stack, not Saboteur, so ward never triggers. Blanked, Saboteur has no menace and no ward, and any single blocker (Spyglass Siren 1/1 is enough) kills the 1-toughness body. We forgo the +1/+1 counter the trigger would have given us, and the crime already happened when the trigger was put on the stack, so Miner's recursion is not undone.
WhenTheir declare attackers step: Saboteur attacks, its trigger goes on the stack targeting one of our creatures (the trigger only exists if we control a creature; with no legal target it is removed from the stack and cannot be countered). Flash Tidebinder, counter it. In declare blockers, block Saboteur with any creature; menace is only checked as blockers are declared, so the block stands even if Tidebinder dies later. Blocking with Tidebinder itself works too (4 damage kills it, so prefer a 1/1).
AfterLoses menace, ward and the attack trigger while Tidebinder lives; keeps its 4/1 body and any counters. Once Tidebinder leaves it regains ward, so kill it in the same combat.
RiskCosts us the free +1/+1 counter. If they respond by killing Tidebinder before its ETB resolves (Shoot the Sheriff), the trigger is still countered but Saboteur keeps menace and ward (ruling 1).
#3Desolation Prowler
Pay 2 life: This creature gets +2/+2 until end of turn. Activate only once each turn.
WhyTheir best attacker, and the pump is how it wins blocks or turns on Pursuer's ferocious. It is an activated non-mana ability; the 2 life is a cost and stays paid; the once-per-turn clause is spent. Countering it in a block converts a 4/4 into a dead 2/2.
WhenTheir combat. Block Prowler, they activate the pump (before or after blockers), we flash Tidebinder in response and its ETB targets the pump. Blocker math: Enduring Curiosity (4/3) kills the 2/2 and survives, the only clean version; Floodpits Drowner, Wasp or an earlier Tidebinder kill the 2/2 but die to its 2 damage, so the counter preserves a trade rather than a blowout. Kaito cannot block: he is a creature only during our turn. If they pump before blockers are declared, the freshly flashed Tidebinder can itself block the now-2/2 Prowler (a trade). Countering a lethal or near-lethal pump on an unblocked Prowler is also legitimate: 2 damage saved, 2 life they paid for nothing.
AfterLoses the pump while Tidebinder lives: a vanilla 2/2. Keeps the body. The 2 life they paid is gone, and even if Tidebinder dies later this turn they cannot pump again this turn because the activation already happened.
RiskRealistically a turn-4+ line; before that, Requiting Hex (1 mana) is the answer to Prowler. If they kill Tidebinder before its ETB resolves the pump is still countered but Prowler keeps the ability (ruling 1).
#4Gollum, Riddle Master
Whenever an opponent casts a spell with mana value of the chosen quality, choose one that hasn't been chosen, put a +1/+1 counter on Gollum / each opponent loses 2 life and you gain 2 life / draw a card.
WhyGollum's payoff is capped: each mode can be chosen once, so a Gollum delivers at most one counter, one drain and one card over the whole game. Countering a trigger and blanking Gollum denies whichever modes remain and turns a 3/1 into a textless body. The catch is cost: the trigger only exists when we cast a spell, so the cheapest execution is a 2-mana even spell plus {2}{U} (5 mana) if they named even, or a 1-mana odd spell plus Tidebinder (4 mana, but Tidebinder itself then triggers Gollum once) if they named odd. Do it when it rides along with a spell we were casting anyway, not as a plan.
WhenWe cast a spell of the chosen parity; Gollum's trigger goes on the stack above it and we receive priority. Cast Tidebinder (MV 3, odd) with the trigger still on the stack; its ETB targets Gollum's trigger. If they named even, Tidebinder does not trigger Gollum. If they named odd, casting Tidebinder adds a second Gollum trigger that resolves first (they get one mode if any remain); Tidebinder then counters the original and Gollum is blanked anyway. Works on their turn too: Shoot the Sheriff (MV 2) on their creature, Gollum triggers, Tidebinder counters. Our parities: even = Kaito, Enduring Curiosity, Floodpits Drowner, Wasp, Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, We Say Thee Nay!; odd = Spyglass Siren, Requiting Hex, Dream Beavers ({B}), Spell Snare, Tidebinder; Wan Shi Tong is X+2 on the stack, so size X to dodge. Expect them to name even most games.
AfterLoses the cast trigger while Tidebinder lives; a 3/1 with no text. Keeps its body and any +1/+1 counter already placed. Cards already drawn and life already drained stay. When Tidebinder leaves, the trigger returns with the original odd/even choice and any modes still unchosen.
RiskShoot the Sheriff kills the 3/2 Tidebinder at instant speed and turns Gollum back on (Dissection Practice's -1/-1 does not kill a 3/2 by itself; it only finishes Tidebinder after combat damage or kills Wasp). Day of Black Sun X=3 postboard does the same. Only Gollum's abilities return, not the countered trigger.
#5Iridescent Vinelasher
Offspring {2}, when this creature enters, create a 1/1 token copy of it. Also: Landfall, Whenever a land you control enters, this creature deals 1 damage to target opponent.
WhyThe offspring token trigger is the only part of the offspring package that is a triggered ability (the extra {2} is paid as a cost of casting and is never refunded). Countering it on a 3-mana Vinelasher denies the copy and blanks the original: no landfall pings and one fewer recurring crime to bring Miner back, for their whole turn's mana. Countering a landfall ping instead saves only 1 damage but still blanks the creature; take it when Tidebinder is coming down anyway.
WhenOffspring: turn 3 or later when they pay {B}+{2}; Vinelasher resolves, the offspring ETB goes on the stack; flash Tidebinder in their main phase targeting it. Landfall: whenever they play a land with Vinelasher out, the ping trigger goes on the stack; respond with Tidebinder. Both windows leave Tidebinder untapped as a blocker on their turn.
AfterLoses landfall (and offspring, irrelevant once on the battlefield) while Tidebinder lives; a vanilla 1/2. Keeps the body. A token copy already made keeps its own landfall; blanking one Vinelasher does not blank the others. The countered ping's crime already happened.
RiskLow; the offspring version is a fine on-curve play, the landfall version is filler.
#6Elegy Acolyte (sideboard)
Whenever one or more creatures you control deal combat damage to a player, you draw a card and lose 1 life. / Void, At the beginning of your end step, if a nonland permanent left the battlefield this turn or a spell was warped this turn, create a 2/2 colorless Robot artifact creature token.
WhyA 4/4 lifelinker with a draw engine is their only real top-end postboard. Countering either trigger blanks it: no lifelink, no draws, no Robots while Tidebinder lives. The combat-damage trigger goes on the stack after damage, so countering it stops the draw, not the hit.
WhenCombat damage step after their creatures connect (draw trigger on the stack), or their end step with the Void trigger on the stack (this is the only way to stop that turn's Robot once the end step has begun). End step is the better window: Tidebinder untaps with us.
AfterLoses lifelink, the draw trigger and Void while Tidebinder lives; keeps its 4/4 body and any Robot tokens already made.
RiskIt still swings as a 4/4, which our 3/2 does not block well.
#7Nighthowl Pursuer
Ferocious, Whenever this creature attacks while you control a creature with power 4 or greater, this creature gets +2/+2 until end of turn.
WhyOnly if the 3/3 menace swing is the lethal one. Countering leaves a 1/1 with no menace that one creature can block and kill. Saboteur (power 4) turns ferocious on permanently; so does a pumped Prowler or Gollum with a counter.
WhenTheir declare attackers step, ferocious trigger on the stack; then single-block it.
AfterLoses menace and ferocious while Tidebinder lives; keeps the 1/1 body.
RiskAlmost always a waste of the 3-mana card on a 1-drop.
#8Soulstone Sanctuary
{4}: This land becomes a 3/3 creature with vigilance and all creature types. It's still a land.
WhyCountering costs them a whole turn of mana (4) for nothing. The animation has no duration, so once it resolves the land is a creature permanently and, with Corpses out, a 4/3 vigilance haste Skeleton that is also an outlaw (all creature types), so Shoot the Sheriff cannot touch it; Requiting Hex (MV 0), Bitter Triumph and Day of Black Sun can. Only worth countering when they tap out for it and we have nothing better.
WhenWhenever they activate; the ability is on the stack, respond with Tidebinder.
AfterNothing lost: as Tidebinder's trigger resolves the source is still a land, not a creature, so it keeps all abilities (ruling 3) and they can pay {4} again later. Its mana ability was never targetable.
RiskLow impact; they rarely have 4 spare mana before turn 5.
Traps
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares (ours): Block Prowler with Kaito as a 3/4 and counter the pump. Kaito is a 3/4 Ninja creature only 'during your turn'. On their turn he is a noncreature planeswalker and cannot be declared as a blocker. Prowler blocks must come from real creatures (Curiosity for a clean kill; Drowner, Wasp or Tidebinder for a trade).
Forsaken Miner: Counter the crime-return trigger to shut off the recursion. The trigger comes from a card in their graveyard, which is not a permanent, so Miner does not lose abilities (ruling 3) and comes back on the very next crime. The {B} is paid on resolution, so countering it does not even cost them mana. 'This creature can't block' is static and untargetable.
Sunset Saboteur: Ward, Discard a card taxes Tidebinder when it counters the attack trigger. Ward triggers only when the permanent itself becomes the target. Tidebinder targets the ability on the stack, so no ward trigger and no discard. Menace is a static keyword and cannot be countered either. Countering the attack trigger also does not undo the crime: it was committed when the trigger was put on the stack with a target, so Miner still triggers.
Corpses of the Lost: Countering the ETB token trigger blanks the anthem or the haste. 'Skeletons you control get +1/+0 and have haste' is a static ability, and Corpses is an enchantment, so it keeps every ability when its trigger is countered (ruling 3). Only the one token is denied.
Gollum, Riddle Master: Counter the odd/even choice as Gollum enters, or treat Gollum as an unbounded engine worth a 5-mana line. 'As Gollum enters, choose odd or even' is a replacement-style static effect that never uses the stack; only the cast trigger is targetable. And the trigger reads 'choose one that hasn't been chosen': three modes total per Gollum, so a counter on the second or third trigger saves one leftover mode, often just a +1/+1 counter.
Shoot the Sheriff / Requiting Hex / Dissection Practice / Duress / Strategic Betrayal / Day of Black Sun: Counter the removal or the drain with Tidebinder. Casting a spell is not an ability and a spell's effect is not an ability. Tidebinder cannot touch any of the deck's 10 main-deck instants or its sideboard sorceries; that is Spell Snare / We Say Thee Nay! work. Requiting Hex's optional blight is an additional cost, also untargetable.
Iridescent Vinelasher: Countering the offspring trigger refunds or wastes the extra {2}, or blanks the token copy too. The {2} is an additional cost paid while casting and stays paid regardless. Countering the ETB stops only that token; any copy already on the battlefield is a separate object with its own landfall.
Mudflat Village: Counter the sacrifice-to-return ability for value. Legal (a non-mana activated ability; the sacrificed land stays sacrificed), but it costs them a land and 2 mana to recover a 1-drop Lizard; spending a 3-mana card to stop it is nearly always worse than any other use. Its two mana abilities cannot be targeted at all.
Desolation Prowler: A Tidebinder already on the battlefield blocks Prowler and then counters the pump. Tidebinder only counters on entering. A Tidebinder already in play has no answer to the pump; the line requires flashing a new copy in with the pump on the stack.
Any 3/2 or 3/1 on their side: An end-step Tidebinder is a profitable blocker. Tidebinder 3/2 trades with a Corpses-pumped Miner 3/2 (and Miner recurs), trades with Gollum 3/1 (Gollum deals 3 to its 2 toughness), and loses outright to Prowler once they pay 2 life. It blocks Vinelasher and an un-pumped Pursuer cleanly, but Pursuer has menace. Treat it as a stop-gap body, not a wall.
Not worth it
Forsaken Miner's graveyard return: a graveyard card keeps its abilities and returns on the next crime, which this deck commits nearly every turn.Skeleton Pirate token: it has no abilities of its own (haste and +1/+0 come from Corpses), so there is never a trigger to counter; do not hunt for one.Nighthowl Pursuer's ferocious: saving 2 damage on a 1-drop is not what the 3-mana card is for unless it is the lethal swing.Mudflat Village's Lizard recursion: 2 mana and a land for a 1/2 is a fine trade for us already.Soulstone Sanctuary's {4} animation unless they tap out for it: the land keeps its abilities and can re-animate.Elegy Acolyte's combat-damage draw when the damage is what matters: the trigger resolves after damage, so it only saves them a card, not us life.Corpses of the Lost's end-step bounce in the early game: it costs them nothing if countered and they descend again next turn; take it only when Tidebinder is coming down as a blocker anyway.
The Wondrous Wasp
The 2/1 flash flying body is the reason to cast Wasp here: they have no fliers and no reach, so it is an evasive clock and an Enduring Curiosity draw every turn, and as a flash blocker it kills Gollum (3/1) or a blanked Saboteur (4/1) in a trade. The blanks are fragile (4 Dissection Practice kill Wasp for {B}, 4 Shoot the Sheriff hit a Human Hero) and small. The one blank worth planning around is Desolation Prowler at their beginning of combat: tapped, it does not attack; blanked, it cannot pump while Wasp lives. Do not spend Wasp on Gollum on our own turn: the tap is wasted and, if they named even, casting Wasp hands Gollum a mode. Sunset Saboteur is the ward problem (discard a card); nothing in the 75 has hexproof.
#1Desolation Prowler
WhyTheir biggest attacker and the only creature whose ability is what makes it scary. Tapped at their beginning of combat it does not attack this turn; blanked it cannot pump (no 4/4, no ferocious enabler for Pursuer) while Wasp lives.
WhenTheir beginning of combat step, before attackers are declared. Tapping after it attacks does nothing to the attack; blanking after blocks still stops the pump if we resolve Wasp before they activate, but once the pump is on the stack only Tidebinder counters it. Floodpits Drowner's ETB (tap plus stun counter) is the cheaper default for this job; use Wasp when Drowner is not available or both attackers need stopping.
AfterLoses the pump; a tapped vanilla 2/2 while Wasp lives. Keeps its body. The once-per-turn clause is irrelevant while blanked.
#2Gollum, Riddle Master
WhyA 3/1 body with up to three remaining one-shot modes. At their beginning of combat, Wasp taps it (no 3-damage attack) and blanks it (no further modes while Wasp lives). Alternatively flash Wasp in at declare attackers and block Gollum: Wasp's 2 damage kills the 3/1 and Wasp dies, a fair 2-for-2. Never cast Wasp for Gollum on our own turn: the tap is wasted on their untap step and, if they named even, casting Wasp (MV 2) itself triggers Gollum before Wasp resolves.
WhenTheir beginning of combat (tap + blank), or their declare attackers step as a flash blocker. If they named even, casting Wasp triggers Gollum once regardless of when; if odd, Wasp is free. Prefer Tidebinder for the Gollum blank when Gollum is even and we have the mana.
AfterLoses the cast trigger while Wasp lives; keeps its 3/1 body and any counters already placed. Abilities and any unchosen modes return the moment Wasp dies to Shoot the Sheriff, Dissection Practice or Day of Black Sun.
#3Elegy Acolyte (sideboard)
WhyA 4/4 lifelinker with a draw trigger and Void; Wasp can only chump-block it (2/1 into a 4/4), so tap it before attackers and strip lifelink, the draw and future Robot tokens for as long as Wasp lives.
WhenTheir beginning of combat. Note Wasp cast in their end step does NOT stop that turn's Robot: the Void trigger is already on the stack and exists independently of its source (CR 113.7a), and its 'if a nonland permanent left the battlefield this turn' clause is game history; only Tidebinder countering the trigger stops it. To turn off Void with Wasp, resolve Wasp before the end step begins (beginning of combat, or their second main phase).
AfterLoses lifelink, the combat-damage draw and Void while Wasp lives; keeps its 4/4 body and any Robots already made.
#4Nighthowl Pursuer
WhyOnce Saboteur (power 4), a pumped Prowler or a countered Gollum is on their board, Pursuer attacks as a 3/3 menace for one mana. Tapped at their beginning of combat it does not attack; blanked it loses menace and ferocious and a single 1/1 blocks and kills the 1/1.
WhenTheir beginning of combat when ferocious is online and Prowler is not the bigger problem. Post-attack blank (declare attackers) also works: menace is gone before blockers are declared, so one creature blocks the now 1/1.
AfterLoses menace and ferocious while Wasp lives; a 1/1 with no text.
#5Iridescent Vinelasher (or its token copy)
WhyStops the landfall pings and removes one recurring crime that keeps bringing Forsaken Miner back. Only worth it in a longer game or when the 1-damage clock is what kills us; the 1/2 body is irrelevant.
WhenAny time; their end step is fine, since tapping a 1/2 rarely matters.
AfterLoses landfall while Wasp lives; a vanilla 1/2. Other Vinelasher copies keep their own landfall.
#6Sunset Saboteur
WhyBlanked, the 4/1 has no menace and dies to any blocker, and tapped at beginning of combat it does not attack. But Ward, Discard a card triggers on Wasp's ETB targeting it: pay the discard or Wasp's trigger is countered. Prefer Tidebinder on its attack trigger (no ward), a Tidebinder on the ward trigger itself, or just double-block.
WhenTheir beginning of combat (tap = no attack) or their declare attackers step after it attacks (blank = lose menace, then single-block and kill it). Either way ward asks for the discard first.
AfterLoses menace, ward and the attack trigger while Wasp lives; keeps the 4/1 body. Ward is gone while blanked, so a follow-up Requiting Hex or Drowner on it is free until Wasp leaves.
#7Skeleton Pirate token / animated Soulstone Sanctuary
WhyTap-before-attackers only. Blanking the token removes the haste Corpses granted (Wasp's later timestamp wins in layer 6) but not the +1/+0, which is Corpses' own static effect in layer 7c. Blanking an animated Sanctuary strips its mana ability, vigilance and the {4} ability; it stays a 3/3 creature with all creature types because 'becomes a creature' is the resolved effect, not an ability.
WhenTheir beginning of combat.
AfterToken: 3/2 with no haste (if they recast Corpses after Wasp entered, the new haste grant is kept, ruling 2). Sanctuary: 3/3 creature-land with no mana ability, no vigilance and no re-animate ability.
Traps
Any attacking creature: Flash Wasp after attackers are declared to tap the attacker and stop the damage. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat. To stop an attack, Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step or earlier. Post-declaration Wasp is only for the blank (removing menace before we declare blockers) or to block with the 2/1 flier.
Gollum, Riddle Master: Cast Wasp on our turn to blank Gollum before our even spells. If they named even, casting Wasp (MV 2) triggers Gollum immediately; that trigger sits above Wasp on the stack and resolves first, so they get a mode. The tap is wasted on our turn, and a 2/1 sits in front of four Dissection Practice for a full turn. Wasp's Gollum play is at their beginning of combat or as a flash blocker.
Elegy Acolyte: Wasp at their end step blanks Acolyte before Void checks, so no Robot this turn. Void triggers at the beginning of the end step; by the time we can cast Wasp the trigger is already on the stack and resolves regardless of its source losing abilities (CR 113.7a). Only Tidebinder counters that Robot. Wasp must resolve before the end step to turn Void off.
Forsaken Miner: Blank Miner to stop the recursion. The recursion works from the graveyard. Once Miner dies it is a new object with all its abilities and returns on the next crime; Wasp's blank ends with the old object. Miner also can't block, so the tap only matters at beginning of combat.
Sunset Saboteur: Wasp is a free answer to the 4/1 menace. Ward, Discard a card triggers when Wasp's ETB targets it; refuse the discard and the trigger is countered, leaving Saboteur untouched and Wasp a plain 2/1 flier.
Skeleton Pirate token: Blanking the token removes Corpses' +1/+0. The bonus comes from Corpses' static ability, not the token's; 'loses all abilities' only strips the token's own abilities and any granted before Wasp entered (haste). It stays a 3/2.
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Turn-3 Corpses: hold 3 mana on their turn 3, and when Corpses of the Lost's token trigger goes on the stack, flash Tidebinder countering it. Their whole turn produces an enchantment with no token; we have a 3/2. If they play a 3-mana offspring Vinelasher instead, counter that ETB (Vinelasher is blanked too).
  • Saboteur without paying ward, version A: at their declare attackers, Tidebinder counters Saboteur's +1/+1 counter trigger (targets the ability, no ward). Saboteur loses menace; block it with Spyglass Siren, Dream Beavers or any creature and the 4/1 dies.
  • Saboteur without paying ward, version B: point Requiting Hex, Floodpits Drowner's ETB, Bitter Triumph, Kaito -2 or Wasp at Saboteur; ward triggers ('counter it unless you discard'); flash Tidebinder countering the WARD trigger. Saboteur is a creature, so it loses all abilities from Tidebinder as well, and our original spell or ability resolves with no discard. Hex + Tidebinder is 4 mana and leaves a 3/2 behind.
  • Prowler kill: block Prowler with Enduring Curiosity (4/3) for a clean kill, or with Drowner, Wasp or an earlier Tidebinder for a trade; when they pay 2 life to pump, flash a new Tidebinder countering it. Prowler dies as a 2/2, they are down 2 life, and the pump is gone while Tidebinder lives. Kaito cannot block on their turn (creature only during our turn), so he is never part of this line.
  • Gollum parity: if they named even, bait with Floodpits Drowner or Shoot the Sheriff (MV 2), Gollum triggers, flash Tidebinder (MV 3, no trigger) countering it; Gollum is a textless 3/1 while Tidebinder lives and Kaito, Curiosity, Drowner and Wasp casts stop feeding it. If they named odd, our even spells are already free; Tidebinder, Siren, Hex, Beavers ({B}) and Spell Snare trigger it. Wan Shi Tong is X+2 on the stack, so choose X to land on the parity they did not name. Only run the counter line when the mana is spare; Gollum's payoff is capped at three modes.
  • Outlaw gap: Shoot the Sheriff cannot target Miner (Rogue), Vinelasher (Assassin), Saboteur (Rogue), the Skeleton Pirate token, or an animated Soulstone Sanctuary (all creature types). Requiting Hex (MV 2 or less, so MV 0 tokens and lands too) hits every creature in their main deck; Bitter Triumph hits anything. Save Sheriff for Prowler, Gollum, Pursuer and Elegy Acolyte.
  • End-step Tidebinder: with 3 open on their turn and no better target, take a free Corpses bounce or Vinelasher landfall counter and untap with a 3/2. Be honest about what it blocks: it trades with a 3/2 Miner (who recurs) and with Gollum 3/1, loses to a pumped Prowler, and only eats Vinelasher cleanly. It is a stop-gap body, not a wall.
  • Tapper roles: Floodpits Drowner's ETB (tap plus stun counter) at their beginning of combat is the default way to stop Prowler or Gollum from attacking and costs 2 mana with a vigilance 2/1 attached; Wasp does the same with a blank and flying. Hold whichever one is not needed as a flash blocker. Both cost a discard if pointed at Saboteur.
  • Wasp as clock: they have no fliers and no reach. Wasp flashed at their end step attacks for 2 every turn, and with Enduring Curiosity that is a card per turn. Kaito's -2 (tap, two stun counters) on Prowler plus Wasp tapping Gollum at their beginning of combat shuts off both relevant two-drops for a turn.
  • Our Day of Black Sun (sideboard, 2): X=2 destroys every creature they can present except Elegy Acolyte (MV 4), including the Skeleton Pirate token and an animated Soulstone Sanctuary (both MV 0); Corpses itself is an enchantment and survives. On our side it kills Siren, Beavers, Drowner, Wasp and Wan Shi Tong; Tidebinder (MV 3), Curiosity (MV 4) and Kaito (MV 4, a creature on our turn) survive. Miner still comes back on their next crime. Their own Day of Black Sun (3 in their side) at X=2 kills our Wasp but not Tidebinder.
Hold or fire
Tidebinder: this is a body-first matchup, so fire it at the first of: Corpses of the Lost's ETB token trigger (usually their turn 3, worth their whole turn of mana), Sunset Saboteur's attack trigger when we have a creature to block with (kills a 4/1 menace with no ward discard), a Desolation Prowler pump in a combat where the counter saves our creature or kills Prowler, or a 3-mana offspring Vinelasher ETB. If none of those has appeared by their turn-3 or turn-4 end step, flash it in as a 3/2 blocker and take whatever incidental trigger is on the stack (Corpses bounce, Vinelasher ping). Never hold it for Gollum past turn 3; take the Gollum counter only when it rides along with a spell we were casting anyway. Never hold it for a Forsaken Miner return. On the play we naturally have 3 open on their turn 3 for the Corpses line; on the draw their curve is a turn ahead, so treat Tidebinder purely as a flash blocker and lean on Requiting Hex and Drowner for Prowler and Saboteur. Wasp: fire at their beginning of combat on Prowler (or Elegy Acolyte postboard) to stop the attack and the pump, or on Gollum to stop its attack and blank it. Against a hand of 1-drops with no Prowler, Gollum or Acolyte, flash it at their end step as a 2/1 flier and start the Curiosity clock, or flash it in at declare attackers to block and trade with Gollum. Never cast Wasp on our own turn for a blank, and never target Saboteur with it unless the discard is a land we do not need. Both cards: expect the blank to be temporary; 4 Shoot the Sheriff and 4 Dissection Practice (the latter kills Wasp but not a 3/2 Tidebinder on its own) mean the body and the countered trigger are the value you can count on, and the blank is a bonus.
How many to run
Keep the 2 Wondrous Wasp in the main (stock): flash flying body, Prowler tapper, Curiosity trigger; the blanks are a bonus. Keep the 1 Tishana's Tidebinder main and bring in 0 more from the sideboard in either seat (stock has 2 in the side). Reasoning: the matchup is decided on turns 1-4 by one- and two-mana interaction, every ability in their deck is small, and 8 of their 10 instants end a blank on the spot; a second or third 3-mana conditional body is dead against a hand of Miners, Vinelashers and instants, and on the draw is exactly where the card is worst. The one main copy earns its slot as a flash 3/2 that also takes a Corpses token or a Saboteur trigger; if you need a cut for the second Day of Black Sun, it is the marginal one. Sideboard priorities are Day of Black Sun (X=2 wipes their whole main-deck board except sideboard Acolyte) and cheap removal; do not bring in Duress (it hits only their 10 instants and Corpses against 23 main-deck creatures).
Review trail: confidence high, 11 disputed, 17 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • 'Dream Beavers is MV 4 and even' and 'Dream Beavers is a legal 3+-power Prowler blocker' (reviewer 3). Rejected: the deck file lists Dream Beavers as {B}, a 1/1 flier; it is MV 1 (odd) and cannot kill a 2/2.
  • 'Only the Gollum block is clearly profitable for an end-step Tidebinder' (reviewer 1). Rejected: Gollum is 3/1 and deals 3 damage to Tidebinder's 2 toughness, so that block is a trade, not a profitable block (reviewer 2 had this right).
  • 'Block Prowler with Drowner or Wasp and the 2/2 dies to 2 damage' framed as a kill (reviewer 2). Accepted as legal but reframed: Prowler deals 2 to a 2/1, so those blocks are trades even with the pump countered; only Enduring Curiosity (4/3) kills and survives.
  • 'Cut Tidebinder from the main / board it out' (reviewer 2, implied). Partially rejected: kept the 1 main copy as a flash 3/2 with real on-curve targets (Corpses token, Saboteur trigger); accepted bringing in 0 more.
  • 'Ward, Discard a card taxes Tidebinder when it counters Saboteur's attack trigger.' Rejected: ward (CR 702.21b) triggers when the permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability; Tidebinder's trigger targets the ability on the stack, not Saboteur.
  • 'Countering Forsaken Miner's return blanks Miner.' Rejected: ruling 3, the source is a card in the graveyard, not an artifact/creature/planeswalker, so it keeps its abilities; the {B} is paid on resolution so nothing is wasted.
  • 'Countering Saboteur's attack trigger undoes the crime so Miner does not trigger.' Rejected: a crime is committed when the ability is put on the stack with a target (OTJ release notes); countering it afterward does not undo it.
  • 'Countering Corpses of the Lost's ETB token strips the anthem/haste.' Rejected: Corpses is an enchantment (ruling 3) and the anthem is a static ability; only the token is denied.
  • 'Countering Soulstone Sanctuary's {4} animation blanks the land.' Rejected: as Tidebinder's trigger resolves the source is still a land (ruling 3), so it keeps its abilities and can re-animate.
  • 'Wasp on the Skeleton Pirate token removes the +1/+0.' Rejected: the bonus is Corpses' static ability applying in layer 7c; loses-all-abilities in layer 6 strips only the token's own and previously granted abilities (haste), and a Corpses recast after Wasp entered regrants haste (ruling 2).
  • 'A Tidebinder already on the battlefield can block Prowler and then counter the pump.' Rejected: Tidebinder counters only on entering.
Changes after review
  • Removed Kaito from every Prowler blocker list (fatal): Kaito is a creature only 'during your turn' per oracle and cannot block on theirs; added a trap entry saying so. Blocker math rewritten: Curiosity kills cleanly, Drowner/Wasp/Tidebinder trade.
  • Fixed Day of Black Sun X=2: it destroys the animated Soulstone Sanctuary and the Skeleton Pirate token (both MV 0); only Elegy Acolyte (MV 4) survives. Added our survivors (Tidebinder MV 3, Curiosity, Kaito) and noted their own 3 Day of Black Sun.
  • Fixed Elegy Acolyte Wasp timing: Wasp in the end step does not stop that turn's Void Robot because the trigger is already on the stack (CR 113.7a); only Tidebinder counters it. Added as a Wasp trap.
  • Corrected counts everywhere: 11 two-drops (4 Prowler, 4 Saboteur, 3 Gollum), 10 main-deck instants, 4 Corpses, 23 main-deck creatures.
  • Reframed Gollum: three lifetime modes ('choose one that hasn't been chosen'), not an ongoing tax or engine; demoted from Tidebinder #1 to #4 with the 4-5 mana execution cost stated; demoted from Wasp #1 to #2 and removed the cast-Wasp-on-our-turn advice.
  • Fixed our parity list: Dream Beavers is {B} (MV 1, odd), stock runs 4 Floodpits Drowner, added Bitter Triumph/We Say Thee Nay! to even and Spell Snare to odd; added Wan Shi Tong X+2 parity trick.
  • Corrected end-step Tidebinder block claims: trades with Miner 3/2 and Gollum 3/1, loses to pumped Prowler; no longer called profitable. Added as a Tidebinder trap.
  • Corrected 'Wasp cannot block Acolyte' to 'can only chump-block'.
  • Fixed Duress note: it is in our sideboard (cannot 'come out'); now says do not bring it in; 23 creatures.
  • Self-caught error not raised by reviewers: Dissection Practice's -1/-1 does not kill a 3/2 Tidebinder on its own; it kills Wasp or finishes Tidebinder after combat damage. Fixed in Gollum risk and hold_or_fire.
  • Re-ranked Tidebinder targets for the actual curve: Corpses of the Lost ETB token #1 (on-curve turn 3), Sunset Saboteur attack trigger #2, Prowler pump #3, Gollum #4, Vinelasher offspring #5, Acolyte #6, Pursuer #7, Sanctuary #8.
  • Added missed targets: Nighthowl Pursuer to Wasp list (#4); Skeleton Pirate token to Tidebinder dont_bother (no abilities of its own); animated Soulstone Sanctuary is an outlaw so Shoot the Sheriff cannot target it (Hex MV 0 / Bitter Triumph / Day of Black Sun can).
  • Added combined line: counter Saboteur's WARD trigger with Tidebinder when Hex/Drowner/Bitter Triumph/Kaito -2/Wasp target it, so our effect resolves with no discard and Saboteur is blanked.
  • Verdicts changed from good/good to situational/situational with the body-first reasoning stated in both summaries.
  • Rewrote hold_or_fire: fire on the first of Corpses ETB / Saboteur attack trigger / relevant Prowler pump / offspring Vinelasher, else flash as a blocker by their turn-3/4 end step; never hold past turn 3 for Gollum; play/draw split made consistent with counts_recommendation.
  • Counts recommendation changed to keep 1 Tidebinder main and board in 0 in either seat (was: up to 1 more on the draw or on seeing Gollum), removing the contradiction with hold_or_fire.
  • Prowler timing cleaned up: removed the confusing 'also fine at their end step' phrasing; stated pump-before-blockers lets the flashed Tidebinder block the 2/2.
vs. Boros Dragons1.6% TB: situationalWasp: good▼
Curve of hasty fliers: Momo T1, Voice of Victory or Sarkhan T2, a warped Nova Hellkite T2-T3 (Momo makes the {2}{R} warp cost {1}{R}), Magmatic Hellkite / Smaug / Clarion Conqueror T3-T4, Lorehold or Dragonhawk T5, with Sarkhan and Smaug Treasures and Maelstrom of the Spirit Dragon fetching the next Dragon. The two cards that actually beat us are STATIC abilities: Voice of Victory ("Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn") switches off every flash creature and counterspell we own on their turn, and Clarion Conqueror ("Activated abilities of artifacts, creatures, and planeswalkers can't be activated") turns off Kaito's loyalty abilities, Floodpits Drowner's shuffle and our Map tokens (not our lands: Restless Reef and Soulstone Sanctuary still animate, and ninjutsu from hand still works). Cavern of Souls (3 copies, naming Dragon) makes every Dragon spell uncounterable, including Clarion Conqueror, which is itself a Dragon. Neither Tidebinder nor a counterspell touches a static, so the matchup is: kill Voice on OUR turn (Requiting Hex is the cheapest and permanent, then Shoot the Sheriff / Bitter Triumph, then Wasp), blank or kill Clarion (Wasp, Shoot, Triumph; Hex cannot, MV 3), then play the instant-speed game. Their answers to our locks are cheap and asymmetric: Burst Lightning (2 dmg) and Erode kill either Wasp or Tidebinder; Nova Hellkite's 1-damage ETB kills the 2/1 Wasp (and Drowner, Siren, Beavers) but not the 3/2 Tidebinder; Charring Bite (Twinmaw's omen half, {1}{R}, "5 damage to target creature without flying") kills Tidebinder, Drowner and Curiosity but can never target the flying Wasp, and being a sorcery it can never hit Kaito (a creature only on our turn).
Tishana's Tidebinder
Tidebinder has real targets (Magmatic Hellkite's land destruction, Lorehold's loot, Smaug's attack trigger, Dragonhawk), but the two cards that beat us, Voice of Victory and Clarion Conqueror, are statics it cannot touch, and Voice also stops Tidebinder from being cast on their turn, which is when almost every Dragon trigger happens. While an unblanked Voice is out the only live windows are on OUR turn: Lorehold's loot in our upkeep, a Maelstrom activation at our end step, Monica's counter trigger when they cast an instant on our turn, or a plain 3/2 flash body. Once Voice is dead or blanked it becomes decent, and it is our only 'counter' that works through Cavern of Souls, because Cavern protects the spell, not its ETB trigger. Its ceiling is capped because countering a Hellkite ETB still leaves a 4/5 body while Bitter Triumph or We Say Thee Nay! removes it, and Burst Lightning / Erode / Charring Bite end the blank for 1-2 mana.
#1Magmatic Hellkite
"When this creature enters, destroy target nonbasic land an opponent controls. Its controller searches their library for a basic land card, puts it onto the battlefield tapped with a stun counter on it, then shuffles."
