  • Verified every added card (Requiting Hex, We Say Thee Nay!, Restless Reef, Soulstone Sanctuary, Wan Shi Tong, Twinmaw/Charring Bite, Monica, Kaito, Curiosity, Drowner) against the oracle text in opponents.json / the deck file before citing it.
vs. Azorius Tempo1.5% TB: goodWasp: good▼
A flash-tempo deck that plays almost entirely on OUR turn. Turn 2 it drops a lock piece: most often High Noon (4 copies, "Each player can't cast more than one spell each turn", on the battlefield by turn 4 in roughly half of games) or a creature lock, Voice of Victory (2) / Jennifer Walters (1), "Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn". Turn 3 it adds the draw engine Enduring Innocence ({1}{W}{W}), then holds mana and flashes in 3-drop flyers that answer whatever we cast: Aven Interrupter ("When this creature enters, exile target spell. It becomes plotted"), Aang ("When Aang enters, airbend up to one other target creature or spell"), Floodpits Drowner (stun), plus Bilbo's Gambit and Spell Pierce. Skycoach Conductor enters prepared with a {U} instant copy in exile ("Exile target non-Pilot creature you control, then return that card to the battlefield") that re-buys those ETBs, erases our stun counters, and resets any creature our Wasp/Tidebinder has blanked. The real win engine is Enduring Curiosity plus Voice's Mobilize: Voice and two Warrior tokens connecting draws three cards a combat. It closes with 2-power evasive bodies and 4/4 Bird tokens from The Eagles Are Coming!, with Settle the Wreckage / Avatar's Wrath as resets. 21 creatures, 16 with flash; only 10 (Aang x3, Interrupter x4, Drowner x2, their Wasp x1) carry an ETB trigger Tidebinder can counter; Conductor has no counterable ability at all.
Tishana's Tidebinder
Ten of their creatures are ETB-trigger flash bodies, Innocence draws whenever they flash one in on our turn, and Eagles is a single upkeep trigger, so Tidebinder always has something to hit. Two things blunt it. Voice of Victory / Jennifer Walters mean we cannot cast Tidebinder on their turn at all (their attack triggers, Drowner activations and an Eagles trigger in THEIR upkeep are untouchable while an unblanked lock creature lives), and High Noon means that once we cast a spell on our turn we cannot respond to the Interrupter that answers it. The reliable window costs no extra mana: pass with {2}{U} up on a turn we have nothing to cast and counter whatever they flash in at our end step (Innocence draw, Drowner stun, their Wasp's Sting, Aang/Interrupter ETB). That is legal under High Noon (our one spell) and under Voice (it is our turn). The highest-value line, saving our Kaito/Curiosity from Interrupter, needs 7 mana and no High Noon, so it is opportunistic, not the default. Nothing in the 75 has hexproof or ward. Their answers to the Tidebinder SPELL: Bilbo's Gambit, a second Aven Interrupter (plots it), Aang (airbends it). Spell Pierce, Disdainful Stroke and Flashfreeze cannot touch a 3-mana blue creature. Once it resolves, Erode and Pyrrhic Strike (creature mode: MV 3 or greater) kill it and end every blank; Seam Rip cannot (MV 3).
#1Aven Interrupter
"When this creature enters, exile target spell. It becomes plotted."
WhyFour copies, their main answer to the Kaito / Enduring Curiosity / Wan Shi Tong spell. Countering the trigger means our spell resolves normally, and Interrupter becomes a 2/2 with no flying and no "Spells your opponents cast from graveyards or from exile cost {2} more" tax. Highest value per event, but it is a 7-mana play (4-drop plus Tidebinder) with no High Noon out, so treat it as a late-game bonus, not a plan to sit on mana for.
WhenOur turn, our main phase. They flash Interrupter in response to our spell; its ETB targets our spell; we flash Tidebinder targeting that trigger. Tidebinder's ETB resolves first, counters the exile, then our spell resolves. Requires 3 spare mana beyond the spell we cast. Dead under High Noon (our cast spell was our one spell that turn). Works while Voice/Walters is out because it is OUR turn. Also a cheap version: when they Interrupt our Wasp or Spell Snare, 3 mana saves it.
AfterInterrupter stays on the battlefield as a 2/2 with no abilities while Tidebinder lives: no flying, no {2} exile/graveyard tax. It keeps its body and any counters. If Tidebinder dies or is blinked/bounced, flying and the tax return.
RiskOur original spell is STILL on the stack under the first Interrupter's trigger. A second Interrupter (or a Conductor blink of the first, which gives it a fresh ETB) can target that original spell again, and if cast while the Tidebinder spell is on the stack it can plot Tidebinder instead. Aang can airbend either spell; Bilbo's Gambit returns Tidebinder to hand. The line is safe only when they are tapped out, hold one answer, or High Noon caps them at one spell (which also caps us).
#2Enduring Innocence
"Whenever one or more other creatures you control with power 2 or less enter, draw a card. This ability triggers only once each turn."
WhyThe most frequent realistic target. Every creature they flash in except Enduring Curiosity (4/3) triggers it, and they flash on OUR turn, so this trigger lands where the Voice lock does not apply and where a 3-mana Tidebinder is affordable. Countering it blanks Innocence: no lifelink, no draws, and no "When Enduring Innocence dies, if it was a creature, return it to the battlefield" while Tidebinder lives, so Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff (Sheep Glimmer, not an outlaw) then kills it for good.
WhenWhen the draw trigger goes on the stack, typically at our end step alongside an Interrupter/Conductor/Drowner/Aang ETB. If both the Innocence draw and an Interrupter ETB aimed at our spell are on the stack, counter the Interrupter ETB unless the spell is cheap and the Innocence engine is the real problem. Not reachable on their turn while a lock creature is unblanked.
AfterInnocence remains a 2/1 with no abilities. Cards already drawn stay drawn. While blanked, if it dies it does NOT return (Day of Black Sun works on exactly this principle: a creature with no abilities when it dies has no death trigger). Effect ends if Tidebinder leaves.
RiskCountering stops only this turn's draw unless Tidebinder stays. Conductor blink resets Innocence as a new object.
#3Aang, Swift Savior
"When Aang enters, airbend up to one other target creature or spell. (Exile it. While it's exiled, its owner may cast it for {2} rather than its mana cost.)"
WhyThree copies; airbend on our Kaito spell, Curiosity spell, or a resolved creature of ours is a tempo swing (we can recast for {2}, so it is softer than Interrupter's plot). Countering it saves the target and turns Aang into a 2/3 ground body with no "Waterbend {8}: Transform Aang."
WhenSame shape as Interrupter: Aang's ETB on the stack, Tidebinder in response, resolve first. Usually on our turn or at our end step, so the Voice lock does not stop us; High Noon does if we already cast a spell that turn. Kaito on the battlefield is never a legal airbend target: on our turn he is a hexproof creature (while he has a loyalty counter), on their turn a planeswalker, not a creature. Only the Kaito SPELL is exposed.
AfterAang stays a 2/3 with no flash, flying, airbend or waterbend, so he cannot transform into Aang and La. Counters stay. Ends the moment Tidebinder leaves.
RiskConductor blink: fresh object, fresh airbend, and the blink also ends the blank. Avatar's Wrath airbends our Tidebinder along with everything else.
#4The Eagles Are Coming!
Delayed trigger: "At the beginning of the next upkeep, create a 4/4 white Bird Soldier creature token with flying for each creature returned to your hand this way."
WhyONE triggered ability makes all the tokens, so a single Tidebinder stops every 4/4 (huge when kicked for 2-3 creatures). Unkicked it is still our 3/2 for their 4/4 flyer. The source is a spell card in the graveyard, so nothing loses abilities.
WhenThe next upkeep after Eagles resolves. Cast during THEIR turn (e.g. in response to our end-step removal when no lock creature is live), the next upkeep is OURS and we respond freely even under a lock. Cast on OUR turn (kicked at our end step returning Interrupter/Aang/Conductor for re-buys, or saving a creature from our removal), the next upkeep is THEIRS: with an unblanked Voice/Walters out the trigger is untouchable, unless the returned creatures included the lock piece itself, in which case no lock is on the battlefield during their upkeep and Tidebinder counters it as our one spell. Without a lock creature, it is fair game either way.
AfterNo tokens are created; the bounced creatures are already in hand and stay there. Tidebinder is a 3/2 body.
RiskThey can Gambit/Interrupt the Tidebinder spell, but in an upkeep they usually have nothing else to spend mana on.
#5Enduring Curiosity (theirs)
"Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card."
WhyTheir card-advantage engine and biggest body (4/3), and Requiting Hex cannot reach it (MV 4). Countering one draw trigger blanks the whole card: no more draws and no dies-return, so Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff (Cat Glimmer, not an outlaw) then leaves it in the graveyard permanently. Without the blank, killing it just gives them an enchantment that still draws.
WhenTheir combat damage step, when the draw trigger(s) go on the stack. ONLY possible if no unblanked Voice/Walters is on the battlefield, and under High Noon this is our one spell for their turn. One Tidebinder counters one trigger; with several unblocked attackers the other draws still resolve.
AfterCuriosity stays a 4/3 with no abilities. Cards already drawn stay. While blanked, killing it is permanent. If Tidebinder leaves first, the return trigger is back.
RiskConductor blink or their own Eagles return-to-hand resets it. Erode / Pyrrhic Strike on Tidebinder at instant speed re-enables everything.
#6Floodpits Drowner (theirs)
Either "When this creature enters, tap target creature an opponent controls and put a stun counter on it." or "{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries."
WhyThe shuffle activation is a hard, permanent answer to our Curiosity / Beavers / Wan Shi Tong, and to the Wasp or Tidebinder holding a lock piece down. Countering it: Drowner stays tapped (cost paid), our creature stays (still stunned), and Drowner is blanked so it can never re-activate while Tidebinder lives. Countering the ETB only saves a stun. If Requiting Hex is in hand, Hex the Drowner (MV 2) instead and keep Tidebinder.
WhenShuffle: any time they have {1}{U} and an untapped Drowner facing a stunned creature; a good pilot fires it on their own turn under the lock, so our real window is when no lock creature is live, or on our turn if they activate then. ETB: on our turn when they flash Drowner in. Kaito is never a legal Drowner target (hexproof creature on our turn while he has loyalty, planeswalker on theirs).
AfterDrowner remains a 2/1, tapped if it activated, with no flash, vigilance, ETB or shuffle. The stun counter already on our creature stays and expires normally. Ends if Tidebinder leaves.
RiskThey have 2 Drowners; the stun counter remains a target for the second copy.
#7Re-entry ETB after a Skycoach Conductor blink
The fresh ETB of the returned creature: Interrupter's exile, Aang's airbend, Drowner's stun, or their Wasp's Sting.
WhyAll Aboard returns the creature as a new object with a new ETB. Tidebinder cannot stop the blink (it is a spell), but the re-entry trigger is an ordinary triggered ability: counter it and the returned creature is blanked as a new object. This is the second-most common way their ETBs recur.
WhenWhenever the blink resolves and the new ETB goes on the stack: on our turn (always legal) or on theirs (only when no unblanked lock creature is out). Under High Noon, All Aboard's copy was their one spell that turn.
AfterThe returned creature stays blanked while Tidebinder lives; any stun counter it shed to the blink is gone regardless.
RiskTheir Conductors are 4 copies; each blink is {U}.
#8The Wondrous Wasp (theirs, 1 main)
"Wasp's Sting: When The Wondrous Wasp enters, tap up to one target creature. It loses all abilities for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield."
WhyTheir Wasp on our Enduring Curiosity or Wan Shi Tong shuts our engine off. Counter it and their Wasp is a 2/1 with no flying and no blank. Requiting Hex (MV 2) is the cheaper answer after the fact, but it does not undo a Sting that already resolved, so the counter is worth it when the target is Curiosity / Wan Shi Tong or the Wasp/Tidebinder holding their lock down.
WhenWhenever they flash it in (usually our turn or our end step). Their Wasp blanking OUR Tidebinder or Wasp does NOT turn off our effects (ours last as long as our creature remains on the battlefield, not as long as it has abilities), so a Sting aimed at our Wasp is mostly a tap.
AfterTheir Wasp stays a vanilla 2/1 ground creature while Tidebinder lives; nothing of ours is tapped or blanked.
RiskLow; it is a 1-of.
#9Ghost Vacuum
"{T}: Exile target card from a graveyard." and "{6}, {T}, Sacrifice this artifact: Put each creature card exiled with this artifact onto the battlefield under your control..."
WhyThe free {T} exile (Vacuum costs {1} to cast, the activation costs no mana) is how they beat Enduring Curiosity's return: exile it from our graveyard in response to the return trigger. Countering that saves the return and blanks Vacuum (artifact) for as long as Tidebinder lives. The 6-mana ability, if ever activated with our Beavers/Curiosity/Wan Shi Tong underneath, is a blowout to counter: Vacuum is already sacrificed and the cards stay exiled forever.
WhenExile activation: in response, typically the moment our Curiosity dies. 6-mana ability: sorcery speed on their turn, so only reachable when no lock creature is live.
AfterExile mode countered: Vacuum stays with no abilities until Tidebinder leaves. Sac mode countered: Vacuum is gone, exiled cards stay exiled.
Risk1-of main, 1 more in side; only relevant in long games.
#10Enduring Curiosity / Enduring Innocence (return trigger)
"When Enduring Curiosity dies, if it was a creature, return it to the battlefield under its owner's control. It's an enchantment." (Innocence has the identical clause.)
WhyCountering the return leaves the card in the graveyard for good with no dependence on Tidebinder surviving. A fair alternative when we killed it before blanking it. Our sideboard Ghost Vacuum does the same for {1} cast + {T}.
WhenAfter the creature dies (Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff / combat), with the return trigger on the stack. Source is a card in the graveyard, so nothing loses abilities (ruling 3), but the counter is permanent. Kill on their turn only when no lock creature is live; otherwise kill on our turn and respond on our turn.
AfterThe Enduring card stays in the graveyard. Tidebinder is a 3/2 body.
RiskCosts 3 mana on top of the removal spell.
#11Restless Anchorage
"{1}{W}{U}: Until end of turn, this land becomes a 2/3 white and blue Bird creature with flying." and "Whenever this land attacks, create a Map token."
WhyOpportunistic. Countering the animate ability wastes their 3 mana but the land is not a creature as Tidebinder's trigger resolves, so it keeps its abilities (ruling 3). Countering the ATTACK trigger is better: it is a creature at that moment, so the permanent loses ALL abilities for as long as Tidebinder lives, including its mana ability and animate, even after it turns back into a land (the animation is an effect, not an ability, so it still ends at end of turn; the blank stays on the permanent).
WhenDeclare attackers on their turn, so only when no lock creature is live. 1-of.
AfterAttack trigger countered: no Map token, the 2/3 loses flying until end of turn, and afterward Anchorage is a land that cannot tap for mana or animate while Tidebinder lives. Animate countered: land untouched, 3 mana wasted.
RiskRarely worth holding Tidebinder for; take it if it is the only thing on offer.
#12Abandoned Air Temple
"{3}{W}, {T}: Put a +1/+1 counter on each creature you control."
WhyFour mana plus tapping the Temple versus three of ours, and it stops a lethal alpha. The land is not a creature so nothing is blanked.
WhenTheir precombat main or combat, so only without a live lock creature. 2 copies.
AfterNo counters placed; Air Temple keeps its mana ability. Tidebinder is a 3/2 body.
RiskRare activation in a tempo deck.
#13Seam Rip (side)
"When this enchantment enters, exile target nonland permanent an opponent controls with mana value 2 or less until this enchantment leaves the battlefield."
WhyOnly worth countering when the target is the Wasp holding Voice/Walters blanked (losing it re-locks their turn) or a Floodpits Drowner mid-line. Enchantment source: nothing loses abilities, the exile simply does not happen. Tidebinder itself (MV 3) is not a Seam Rip target.
WhenTheir main phase, so we need no unblanked lock creature, which is exactly when Wasp is holding one down. Post-board only.
AfterOur permanent stays; Seam Rip stays on the battlefield doing nothing.
RiskThree mana to protect a 1-2 drop is a poor trade unless that drop is the lock-breaker.
Traps
Voice of Victory / Jennifer Walters: Tidebinder counters "Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn", or counters Voice's Mobilize attack trigger. The lock is a static ability; only activated and triggered abilities can be targeted. Mobilize 2 IS a trigger, but any unblanked Voice on the battlefield during their turn means we cannot cast Tidebinder at all, and a blanked Voice has no Mobilize, so the trigger is never reachable. Remove the lock with Requiting Hex ({B}, MV 2 or less), Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff (Human Bard / Human Advisor Hero, not outlaws), Spell Snare on the cast (MV 2, cast on their turn before the lock exists), or a Wasp blank on OUR turn.
High Noon: Counter High Noon or its ability, or answer it with Wasp/Tidebinder at all. "Each player can't cast more than one spell each turn" is static. The "{4}{R}, Sacrifice" ability needs red they do not have, and High Noon is an enchantment so it would not lose abilities anyway. Answers: Spell Snare (MV 2) on the cast, Duress / Annul / Spell Pierce from the side, or ignore it via Kaito's ninjutsu (an activated ability, not a spell). Sequencing under High Noon: ninjutsu Kaito without casting a spell, then hold Wasp or Tidebinder as our single spell for whatever they flash at our end step. It also caps THEM at one spell, so they cannot Interrupt our spell and Gambit our response in the same turn.
Aven Interrupter: Counter the {2} tax, or expect the countered ETB to remove Interrupter. "Spells your opponents cast from graveyards or from exile cost {2} more to cast" is static; Tidebinder removes it only as a side effect of blanking the creature after countering its ETB. Countering an ETB never removes the permanent. Also: Interrupter is a Bird Rogue, an outlaw, so Shoot the Sheriff cannot kill it; hard answers to a resolved one are Bitter Triumph, Kaito's -2 stun, or our Drowner stun-and-shuffle.
Skycoach Conductor: Tidebinder stops the blink, or stops it entering prepared. "This creature enters prepared" is a replacement effect, not a trigger. Casting the prepared copy ("Exile target non-Pilot creature you control, then return that card to the battlefield") is casting a SPELL, so Tidebinder cannot touch it; only We Say Thee Nay! or side Spell Pierce can (not Spell Snare: the copy's mana value is 1). Scryfall ruling 2026-03-20: the copy "remains in exile for as long as that permanent is on the battlefield and is prepared", so killing Conductor (Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff: Bird Pilot is not an outlaw) deletes the blink outright.
Bilbo's Gambit: Counter the Gift Treasure or the bounce. Gambit is an instant; "Return target spell to its owner's hand" and the Gift Treasure creation are spell effects. If the gift was promised, "players can't cast spells this turn" then shuts off our flash for the rest of that turn.
Settle the Wreckage / Erode / Avatar's Wrath / Pyrrhic Strike / Spell Pierce / Disdainful Stroke / Flashfreeze: Counter the basic-land search, the airbend, or the exile. All spell effects. Tidebinder never interacts with a spell. Note Erode kills Tidebinder or Kaito-as-planeswalker, and Pyrrhic Strike's creature mode (MV 3 or greater) kills Tidebinder.
Clarion Conqueror (side): Counter "Activated abilities of artifacts, creatures, and planeswalkers can't be activated." Static. Tidebinder cannot target it. Kill it (Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff: Dragon is not an outlaw, Day of Black Sun X=3) or blank it with Wasp. Kaito's ninjutsu still works either way: a card in hand is not a permanent.
Mobilize Warrior tokens: Counter the tokens' "Sacrifice them at the beginning of the next end step" delayed trigger. It is a counterable trigger, but countering it GIFTS them two permanent 1/1s. And it lands on their turn, where an unblanked Voice locks us out anyway.
Hallowed Fountain / Multiversal Passage / Abandoned Air Temple / Temple of Enlightenment (enters tapped): Counter the shock-land life payment or the basic-type choice. "As this land enters" clauses are replacement effects; "enters tapped" is a static/replacement. Temple's "When this land enters, scry 1" IS a trigger but is not worth 3 mana.
Restless Anchorage (animate): Countering the animate ability blanks the land. Tidebinder checks the source's type as its trigger resolves (ruling 3). The land has not become a creature yet, so it keeps all abilities; they only lose the 3 mana. The attack trigger, by contrast, does blank it.
Any mana ability (lands, Gambit's Treasure): Target a mana ability. "(Mana abilities can't be targeted.)" is printed on Tidebinder.
Not worth it
Temple of Enlightenment scry 1: 3 mana for a scry is never right.Aang's "Waterbend {8}: Transform Aang": counterable (Aang stays tapped, mana stays spent), but they almost never activate it; do not hold for it.Aang and La's attack trigger: only exists after an 8-mana transform.Jennifer Walters' "{3}{G}{W}{W}: Transform" (sorcery speed): reachable only by naming Forest on Multiversal Passage; if it ever happens on an open turn it is a counterable activated ability that blanks Walters, lock included, but do not plan around it.Petrified Hamlet ETB ("choose a land card name"): countering it leaves Hamlet nameless so its shutdown clause never applies (aimed at our Restless Reef at best), but it still taps for {C}; 1-of sideboard land.Restless Anchorage's Map token by itself: only take it when the blank-the-land bonus matters or nothing else is coming.Floodpits Drowner's ETB stun on a non-essential creature: save Tidebinder for the shuffle activation, or Hex the Drowner.Voice of Victory #2's Mobilize trigger: unreachable in practice (see traps), so never hold for it.Multiple Enduring Curiosity draw triggers in one combat: one Tidebinder counters one; the blank is the point, not the single card.
The Wondrous Wasp
Wasp does two jobs here, one unique and one shared. UNIQUE: blanking Enduring Curiosity (MV 4) and Enduring Innocence (MV 3), which Requiting Hex cannot reach, so that Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff kills them for good instead of returning an enchantment that still draws. SHARED: switching off Voice of Victory / Jennifer Walters ("Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn" is static, gone under "loses all abilities"), which reopens their turn to Tidebinder, We Say Thee Nay!, Spell Snare and Bitter Triumph. But the stock list runs 4 Requiting Hex ({B}: destroy creature with MV 2 or less), and Voice, Walters, their Drowner and their Wasp are all MV 2, so Hex is the first answer to every MV-2 creature: permanent, one mana, and not undone by their 4 Skycoach Conductor blinks, 1+2 Erode, 2 Eagles bounces, or post-board Seam Rip (MV 2 hits Wasp). Wasp is the answer when Hex/Triumph is not in hand or is needed for a Drowner, and it leaves a 2/1 flash flyer that enables Kaito's ninjutsu. Post-board it also turns off Clarion Conqueror. No creature in the 75 has hexproof or ward. Their answers to a Wasp blank: Erode, Conductor's copy blinking the blanked creature, Eagles returning it to hand (recast = new object), Seam Rip (side), Avatar's Wrath, Settle the Wreckage if Wasp attacks, Bilbo's Gambit on the Wasp spell, and Drowner stun-then-shuffle. Pyrrhic Strike cannot hit Wasp (MV 2).
#1Voice of Victory / Jennifer Walters (when no Requiting Hex or Bitter Triumph is in hand)
WhyThe lock shields everything else in their deck. Voice's blank also deletes Mobilize 2 ("Whenever this creature attacks, create two tapped and attacking 1/1 red Warrior creature tokens"), the engine that feeds Curiosity three draws a combat; Walters' "{3}{G}{W}{W}: Transform" is unreachable without Multiversal Passage naming Forest, so blanked she is a vanilla 2/3. WITH Hex in hand, Hex the lock piece and send Wasp to Curiosity/Innocence instead: Hex is permanent, Wasp's blank dies to Erode / Conductor blink / Eagles.
WhenOUR turn only (main phase or our end step), because while an unblanked lock creature is out we cannot cast Wasp on theirs. The tap is irrelevant (it untaps in their untap step); the blank is everything. If both Voice and Walters are out, one Wasp is not enough; pair with Hex/Triumph on the other.
AfterVoice stays a 1/3 (Walters a 2/3) with no abilities while our Wasp remains on the battlefield: no lock, no Mobilize tokens. Their turn is fully open to our instants and flash. Ends immediately if Wasp is killed, exiled, bounced, or ninjutsu'd away; Conductor blinking or Eagles bouncing the lock creature also resets it.
#2Enduring Curiosity (theirs)
WhyWasp's unique job: Hex cannot reach MV 4. Blanked, it stops drawing ("Whenever a creature you control deals combat damage to a player, draw a card") and loses its dies-return clause, so our next Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff (Cat Glimmer, not an outlaw) kills it permanently. Unblanked, killing it returns an enchantment that keeps drawing. As their biggest body (4/3), it is also the best tap-before-attackers target when no lock creature is out.
WhenBlank: any time on our turn, or at their end step only when no unblanked Voice/Walters is out. Tap-before-attackers: their beginning of combat, same condition; a tap after attackers are declared does not remove it from combat. Then kill it while blanked, same turn if possible (Conductor blink resets it for {U}).
After4/3 with no abilities: no draws, and if it dies while Wasp is on the battlefield it stays dead. Cards already drawn stay drawn. If Wasp leaves before it dies, the return clause is back.
#3Enduring Innocence
WhyTheir other engine ("Whenever one or more other creatures you control with power 2 or less enter, draw a card"), fed by every creature they flash except Curiosity, plus lifelink. MV 3, so Hex cannot reach it either. Blank then kill = permanent. Only a 2/1, so Wasp trades with it in combat too, but blank-then-kill is worth more than the trade.
WhenOur turn, ideally before their next flash creature enters (the draw fires when they flash in on our turn).
After2/1 with no abilities: no lifelink, no draws, no return while Wasp lives.
#4Clarion Conqueror (side)
Why"Activated abilities of artifacts, creatures, and planeswalkers can't be activated" shuts off Kaito's loyalty abilities (creature on our turn, planeswalker on theirs, covered either way) and our Floodpits Drowner's shuffle. Killing it is better and permanent (Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff: Dragon is not an outlaw, Day of Black Sun X=3); Wasp is the play when no removal is in hand, or when we need the 3/3 flyer tapped this turn for a ninjutsu attack.
WhenOur turn, before we want to activate Kaito. Post-board only. Kaito's ninjutsu is unaffected either way (a card in hand is not a permanent).
After3/3 with no flying and no lock on activated abilities while Wasp lives. Kaito's +1/0/-2 and our Drowner's shuffle work again.
#54/4 Bird Soldier tokens (The Eagles Are Coming!)
WhyOnce they exist they are the biggest bodies in their deck and they fly. Tapping one at their beginning of combat (no lock creature out) stops 4 damage; blanking removes flying so Dream Beavers / Spyglass Siren are not forced to chump and our ground creatures can block it.
WhenTheir beginning of combat when no unblanked Voice/Walters is out, or our turn purely for the blank.
AfterA 4/4 with no abilities (no flying) while Wasp lives; tapped this turn.
#6Aven Interrupter
WhyIts ETB already happened, so the blank only removes flying and the static "Spells your opponents cast from graveyards or from exile cost {2} more to cast". If they plotted our Kaito/Curiosity, blanking Interrupter lets us cast the plotted card from exile for free (plot's permission is a rules designation, unaffected). Also clears a flying blocker for Wasp/Siren attacks. Remember Shoot the Sheriff cannot kill it (Rogue).
WhenOur main phase, the turn we want to cast the plotted card (sorcery-speed, a later turn). Check no second Interrupter is out; each one taxes separately.
After2/2 with no abilities: no flying, no tax. Casting the plotted card still requires that Avatar's Wrath has not been cast this cycle ("opponents can't cast spells from anywhere other than their hands" until their next turn) and, under High Noon, is our one spell.
#7Aang, Swift Savior
WhyAirbend already resolved by the time we can act; blanking removes flying and "Waterbend {8}", making him a 2/3 ground body our Beavers/Curiosity can wall. Best as a tap-before-attackers target when no lock is out, or a blocker-tap on our turn.
WhenTheir beginning of combat (no lock creature) to keep him from attacking; or our beginning of combat to remove a flying blocker so Spyglass Siren connects for a ninjutsu.
After2/3 with no flash/flying/airbend/waterbend while Wasp lives. Anything already airbended stays exiled (we still recast it for {2}).
#8Floodpits Drowner (theirs)
WhyIf they have stunned one of our creatures and hold {1}{U}, blanking Drowner removes "{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle this creature and target creature with a stun counter on it into their owners' libraries" before they can fire it. Requiting Hex first (MV 2, permanent, {B}), Bitter Triumph second; Wasp only if neither is in hand.
WhenOur turn, before they untap with the mana to activate. Only when they actually have a stunned target of ours (Kaito can never be one).
After2/1 with no abilities: no vigilance, no shuffle. The stun counter on our creature stays and still expires normally.
#9Skycoach Conductor
WhyTap value only: a 2/3 flying vigilance blocker that walls Wasp and Spyglass Siren. Tapping it at our beginning of combat lets a flyer through for a Kaito ninjutsu. The blank does NOT stop the prepared copy (Scryfall ruling 2026-03-20: "If a prepared creature loses all abilities, it won't stop being prepared"); to remove the blink, kill Conductor.
WhenOur beginning of combat, the turn we want an unblocked flyer.
AfterTapped this turn; loses flying and vigilance while Wasp lives. Its All Aboard copy stays castable from exile.
#10Restless Anchorage (while animated)
WhyWasp must target a creature, and the animated land is one. The blank sticks to the permanent after the animation ends at end of turn, so Anchorage becomes a land that cannot tap for mana or re-animate while Wasp remains. Tapping the attacking 2/3 does not remove it from combat, so this is about the mana denial, not the attack.
WhenTheir turn while it is animated (requires no unblanked lock creature), or if they animate it on our turn as a blocker.
AfterA land with no mana ability, no animate and no attack trigger while Wasp lives. 1-of, so opportunistic.
Traps
The Wondrous Wasp (theirs): Blank their Wasp to free the creature of ours it blanked. Their effect lasts "for as long as The Wondrous Wasp remains on the battlefield", not for as long as it has abilities. Removing its abilities changes nothing. Kill it (Requiting Hex, Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff) or stun-and-shuffle it with our own Floodpits Drowner; our stock 75 has no bounce effect. The same is true in reverse: their Wasp on our Tidebinder or Wasp does not turn off our locks.
Voice of Victory / Jennifer Walters: Flash Wasp on their turn to tap Voice before attacks, or treat Wasp as the only lock-breaker. While the lock piece is on the battlefield we cannot cast any spell on their turn. Wasp must be cast on OUR turn; the tap is irrelevant then. And Requiting Hex ({B}, MV 2 or less) kills either lock creature permanently for less; Bitter Triumph and Shoot the Sheriff also work, and Spell Snare counters the MV-2 spell on their turn before the lock exists.
Any attacker: Wasp after attackers are declared to stop the attack. Tapping an attacking creature does not remove it from combat. To stop an attack Wasp must resolve in their beginning of combat step (or earlier), which is only possible when no unblanked lock creature is live.
Skycoach Conductor: Blank Conductor to stop the blink. Confirmed by Scryfall ruling 2026-03-20: a prepared creature that loses all abilities stays prepared and the copy of its spell in exile is unaffected. The copy exists only "for as long as that permanent is on the battlefield and is prepared", so the removal that works is killing Conductor (Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff) or countering the {U} copy with We Say Thee Nay! / side Spell Pierce (not Spell Snare, MV 1). The blink also erases our stun counters on the blinked creature and returns it as a new object, ending any blank on it.
High Noon: Wasp answers the one-spell lock. High Noon is an enchantment; Wasp only targets creatures. Under High Noon, Wasp + Tidebinder in the same turn is impossible. Kaito's ninjutsu is an activated ability, not a spell, so it does not count toward the one spell.
Aang / Aven Interrupter: Blanking undoes the airbend or the plot. Effects of an ability that already resolved stay. Our airbended card stays exiled (recast for {2}); our plotted card stays plotted (castable free as a sorcery on a later turn; blanking Interrupter only removes the {2} tax).
Kaito, Bane of Nightmares (ours): Use Wasp as the ninjutsu enabler while it holds a lock or an Enduring card down; or treat Kaito as safe once resolved. Ninjutsu returns the unblocked attacker to hand; Wasp leaving the battlefield ends the blank instantly. Enable ninjutsu with Spyglass Siren or Dream Beavers and leave Wasp home. Kaito on the battlefield dodges every targeted creature effect (hexproof creature on our turn while he has a loyalty counter, planeswalker on theirs) but NOT Erode on their turn ("Destroy target creature or planeswalker", 1 main + 2 side) or Settle the Wreckage when he attacks (it targets the player, so hexproof is irrelevant).
Lines with the rest of the deck
  • Unlock then punish: on our turn, Requiting Hex the Voice of Victory / Jennifer Walters (MV 2) if we hold it; otherwise Wasp blanks it. Their next turn is now open: Tidebinder on Enduring Curiosity's combat-damage draw or Drowner's shuffle activation, We Say Thee Nay! / Spell Snare on their spells, Bitter Triumph at instant speed. If Wasp is the lock-breaker, keep it out of combat and expect Erode / Conductor blink / Eagles to test it; hold the second Wasp or a removal spell for the re-locked creature.
  • Blank-then-kill on the Enduring cards: Wasp (or a Tidebinder counter of the draw trigger) on Enduring Curiosity / Innocence, then Bitter Triumph or Shoot the Sheriff while blanked, ideally the same turn so a {U} Conductor blink cannot reset it first. Its "When ... dies, if it was a creature, return it to the battlefield" no longer exists, so it stays dead. If we killed it first instead, Tidebinder counters the return trigger (no blank, but permanent), or side Ghost Vacuum exiles it from the graveyard in response.
  • Pass with three up: on a turn we have nothing worth casting (or under High Noon, having cast nothing), hold {2}{U} and Tidebinder whatever they flash at our end step: Enduring Innocence's draw, Drowner's stun ETB, their Wasp's Sting, an Aang airbend. Legal under Voice because it is our turn, legal under High Noon as our one spell. This is the everyday Tidebinder line; the 7-mana Interrupter save is the bonus.
  • Bait the Interrupter (late game): with 3 spare mana and no High Noon, cast the spell we are willing to see Interrupted/airbended; when the Aven Interrupter or Aang ETB targets it, flash Tidebinder on that trigger. Our spell resolves and their 3-drop is a vanilla ground body. Only do it when they are tapped low: a second Interrupter or Aang can re-target our still-pending spell, or hit the Tidebinder spell itself.
  • High Noon sequencing: ninjutsu Kaito precombat off an unblocked Spyglass Siren / Dream Beavers without casting a spell (ninjutsu is an activated ability, so Interrupter, Aang, Gambit, Disdainful Stroke and High Noon's count cannot touch it), then hold Wasp or Tidebinder as our single spell for their end-step flash play. They are capped at one spell too, so our We Say Thee Nay! / Spell Snare on it cannot be baited out by a second spell.
  • Eagles timing: when no unblanked Voice/Walters is out, cast our removal at THEIR end step (Bitter Triumph is an instant). If they respond with The Eagles Are Coming!, the 4/4 token trigger lands in OUR upkeep, where Tidebinder counters every token at once. Under a live creature lock our removal is main-phase only and any Eagles response (or a kicked Eagles at our end step re-buying Interrupter/Aang/Conductor) lands its trigger in THEIR upkeep, untouchable, unless the returned creatures included the lock piece itself.
  • Kill the Conductor, not the blink: Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff on Skycoach Conductor deletes the All Aboard copy (it exists only while Conductor is on the battlefield and prepared). If they cast the copy, We Say Thee Nay! or side Spell Pierce counters it; Wasp/Tidebinder blanks do nothing to it.
  • Free the stunned creature: when their Floodpits Drowner has stunned our Curiosity/Beavers and they activate "{1}{U}, {T}: Shuffle...", Tidebinder counters it (Drowner stays tapped and blanked). Cheaper pre-emptive version: Requiting Hex the Drowner on our turn, or Wasp it if Hex is not in hand.
  • Answer their Wasp the right way: their Wondrous Wasp blanking our Curiosity / Wan Shi Tong is fixed by Requiting Hex, Bitter Triumph, Shoot the Sheriff, or our Floodpits Drowner (stun, then shuffle it away), never by our Wasp or Tidebinder blanking it.
  • Kaito exposure: on the battlefield he dodges Aang's airbend, Drowner's stun, their Wasp and Pyrrhic Strike (hexproof creature on our turn while he has loyalty; planeswalker, not a creature, on theirs), but Erode kills him on their turn and Settle the Wreckage exiles him if he attacks into open {2}{W}{W}. Attack with Kaito into a tapped-out opponent, or after Duress / Spell Pierce has handled Settle.
  • Post-board: kill Clarion Conqueror (Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff / Day of Black Sun X=3) to reopen Kaito's loyalty abilities and our Drowner's shuffle; Wasp on it only when no removal is in hand. Tidebinder on Seam Rip's ETB is worth it only when Seam Rip is aimed at the Wasp holding down Voice/Walters.
Hold or fire
WASP: versus Voice of Victory / Jennifer Walters on our turn, Requiting Hex first, Bitter Triumph second, Wasp only if neither is in hand or Hex is needed for a Drowner; Wasp's blank is undone by Erode, a {U} Conductor blink, an Eagles bounce or post-board Seam Rip, while Hex is permanent. Without a lock creature to break, Wasp's default job is Enduring Curiosity / Innocence: blank, then kill with Triumph/Sheriff so the return clause is gone (Hex cannot reach either). Third choice: their beginning of combat, when no lock creature is out, to tap the biggest flyer (Curiosity, a 4/4 Bird, Aang). A bare 2/1 flash flyer as a ninjutsu enabler is the last resort, and never with the Wasp that is holding a blank. TIDEBINDER: default is DEVELOP, not hold. Cast Kaito by ninjutsu and run Curiosity out at their end step when no lock creature is out. Tidebinder's everyday window is free: on a turn we have nothing to cast, pass with {2}{U} up and counter whatever they flash at our end step (Innocence draw first, Drowner stun, their Wasp's Sting, Aang/Interrupter ETB), legal under both High Noon (our one spell) and Voice (our turn). Fire it on sight at an Eagles upkeep trigger in our upkeep, a Drowner shuffle activation aimed at our engine, or a Ghost Vacuum activation. The 7-mana Interrupter save is a bonus when we have the mana, no High Noon is out, and they are tapped low enough that a second Interrupter/Aang/Gambit cannot answer it. Under a live creature lock with no Hex/Wasp coming, Tidebinder is castable only on our turn: use it on whatever they flash in on our turn, or as a 3/2 body that blocks Warrior tokens and trades with their 2/3s (it cannot block their flyers). ON THE PLAY: our turn 2 comes before their lock, so keep Hex/Wasp for when Voice/Walters shows; leave {U} up on their turn 2 for Spell Snare on Voice, Walters or High Noon (all MV 2) if the hand allows. ON THE DRAW: expect a turn-2 lock piece before our turn 2; Hex the creature lock (or Spell Snare it on their turn), develop Siren/Beavers with the spare mana, and save Wasp for the Enduring cards; if it is High Noon, plan the ninjutsu-plus-one-spell pattern from turn 3 on.
How many to run
Keep stock for the 75: 2 Wondrous Wasp main, 1 Tishana's Tidebinder main + 2 in the side. Wasp is good here (two Enduring engines Hex cannot reach, two lock creatures as a backup answer, Conqueror post-board, none with hexproof/ward), and 2 is the right count for a 1.5% deck; a third would be a liability elsewhere. Tidebinder is good but blunted by the creature lock and High Noon, so no maindeck bump. POST-BOARD vs this deck, bring in (in this order): Duress x3 (High Noon, Bilbo's Gambit, Eagles, Erode, Settle, Avatar's Wrath, Spell Pierce), Annul x1 (4 High Noon, 2 Curiosity, 2 Innocence as enchantment creature spells, Ghost Vacuum, Seam Rip), Spell Pierce x2 (High Noon, Gambit, Eagles, Erode, Settle, Wrath, and the {U} All Aboard copy that Spell Snare cannot hit), then ONE extra Tidebinder (2 total): its real targets are 10 ETB creatures plus Innocence's draw and the Eagles trigger, and its 3/2 ground body cannot block their flyers, so the third copy is only worth it if they show heavy Enduring/Eagles. Cut Shoot the Sheriff x2 first (Aven Interrupter is a Rogue, an outlaw, so Sheriff misses their most common 3-drop), then trim Dream Beavers; keep every Requiting Hex and Spell Snare (their MV-2 lock pieces are the whole matchup). Ghost Vacuum is a reasonable 1-mana answer to the Enduring return triggers if room exists; Day of Black Sun stays out (X=2 kills our own Wasp/Siren/Beavers and their 2-drops only).
Review trail: confidence medium, 6 disputed, 16 changes
Disputed reviewer points (kept as written)
  • Reviewer 1 missed target 'Voice of Victory #2 Mobilize 2 trigger becomes reachable once Wasp has blanked Voice #1' rejected: an unblanked Voice on the battlefield during their turn still has 'Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn' (oracle), so we cannot cast Tidebinder while Voice #2 attacks; a blanked Voice has no Mobilize trigger. Voice has no flash, so it is never cast on our turn either. The trigger is listed in dont_bother as unreachable, not added as a target.
  • Reviewer 2 claim 'Conductor's 2/3 flying vigilance blocks Tidebinder profitably every turn' softened: Tidebinder is 3/2, Conductor 2/3 (Scryfall), so the block is a trade, not a profitable block. The count correction (10 ETB creatures, 16 flash, 21 creatures) is accepted; the body claim in counts_recommendation now says it cannot block flyers and trades with their 2/3s.
  • Reviewer 2 'Downgrade Wasp to good' accepted, but the 'Wasp is the best card' framing is replaced rather than inverted: Wasp keeps a unique job Hex cannot do (blanking Enduring Curiosity MV 4 / Innocence MV 3 so the kill is permanent; Enduring Curiosity oracle: returns as an enchantment that still has the draw trigger), so it stays a priority card, ranked behind Hex only on the MV-2 targets.
  • Reviewer 2 'bring in at most one extra Tidebinder' accepted as the default, with the third copy kept as a conditional option; this is a judgment call, not a rules point.
  • Reviewer 3 'do not imply a P/T the source does not give' is moot: Aang, Swift Savior 2/3 and Jennifer Walters 2/3 were pulled from raw Scryfall JSON (api.scryfall.com/cards/named) on 2026-09-11 and are stated as fact.
  • Draft claim kept: countering Restless Anchorage's ATTACK trigger blanks the permanent including its mana ability after it stops being a creature. Tidebinder ruling 3 fixes the type check at resolution; the duration is 'for as long as this creature remains on the battlefield' and the animation is an effect, not an ability, so it ends at end of turn while the blank persists (CR 611.2c: a continuous effect from a resolved ability affects only the objects it affected at resolution). Same logic now applied to Wasp on an animated Anchorage.
Changes after review
  • Re-read /Users/emersonspartz/dimir-tidebinder-wasp-guide/data/decks/standard-azorius-tempo-woe.json (main/side counts, every oracle text, our stock list) and pulled raw Scryfall JSON for Skycoach Conductor (16 rulings), Aang, Jennifer Walters, Requiting Hex, Shoot the Sheriff, Bitter Triumph, Faebloom Trick, Kaito, Day of Black Sun, Annul, Strategic Betrayal, Spell Snare, We Say Thee Nay!, Dream Beavers, Wan Shi Tong, Spyglass Siren, plus rulings for Aven Interrupter, Voice of Victory, High Noon, Kaito, Enduring Curiosity, Eagles, Floodpits Drowner. No project files modified; scratch JSON only.

Wasp verdict downgraded premium -> good; hold_or_fire and on-the-draw plan rewritten around Hex; Wasp's unique job (Curiosity MV 4 / Innocence MV 3) stated.
  • MAJOR fixed: second-Interrupter risk rewritten (our original spell is still on the stack and can be re-targeted; the Tidebinder spell itself can be plotted/airbended/Gambited).
  • MAJOR fixed: Eagles end-step line now carries the lock condition; added the kicked-Eagles-at-our-end-step case and the lock-piece-returned exception.
  • MAJOR fixed: Tidebinder default changed from HOLD to DEVELOP; added the 'pass with three up at our end step' window as the everyday line; Interrupter save kept at priority 1 as highest value per event but explicitly conditional; Innocence draw moved to priority 2 as the most frequent realistic target.
  • MAJOR fixed: Clarion Conqueror now says kill it first (Bitter Triumph / Shoot the Sheriff, Dragon not an outlaw / Day of Black Sun X=3), Wasp as fallback.
  • MAJOR fixed: High Noon moved to the front of how_they_win with its rough turn-4 frequency; added sequencing rule (ninjutsu without a spell, hold one flash spell; they are capped too) in traps and combined_lines.
  • MAJOR fixed: sideboard plan now brings Duress, Annul (9 main targets incl. enchantment creature spells), Spell Pierce before one extra Tidebinder; Shoot the Sheriff cut first because Aven Interrupter is a Bird Rogue (outlaw).
  • MAJOR fixed: creature counts corrected to 21 creatures / 16 flash / 10 ETB-trigger creatures; Conductor noted as having no counterable ability.
  • Verified and de-hedged: Scryfall ruling 2026-03-20 confirms a prepared creature that loses all abilities stays prepared; the copy exists only while Conductor is on the battlefield and prepared, so killing Conductor removes the blink. Removed all 'unverified' language.
  • Filled in Aang 2/3 and Jennifer Walters 2/3 from Scryfall; removed 'bear'.
  • Added Kaito's loyalty-counter condition to every hexproof-creature statement (oracle: 'as long as Kaito has one or more loyalty counters on him').
  • Added Kaito exposure to Erode (their turn) and Settle the Wreckage (targets the player).
  • Minor fixes: Ghost Vacuum exile is {T} with no mana; Abandoned Air Temple is {3}{W} plus tap, not five mana; Enduring Innocence is a turn-3 play; Innocence triggers off every creature except Curiosity 4/3, Conqueror 3/3 and 4/4 Bird tokens; Eagles-under-lock statement made consistent (their upkeep untouchable, ours not); Curiosity blank at their end step carries the lock condition; Petrified Hamlet still taps for {C}; Walters' transform is reachable via Multiversal Passage naming Forest (dont_bother); 'blanks a resolved creature' changed to 'exiles'; no bounce in our 75, Hex added as their-Wasp answer; answers to a Wasp blank now include Eagles, Settle the Wreckage, Bilbo's Gambit; Pyrrhic Strike noted as killing Tidebinder (MV 3) but not Wasp.
  • Added targets: re-entry ETB after a Conductor blink (Tidebinder), 4/4 Bird Soldier tokens (Wasp), animated Restless Anchorage (Wasp), Mobilize token sacrifice trigger (trap: countering gifts them 1/1s). Added Curiosity + Mobilize as the real win engine in how_they_win.
  • Confidence set to medium: every rules claim is now backed by oracle text or a cited Scryfall ruling, but the strategy weightings (Hex-vs-Wasp priority, sideboard order, one vs two extra Tidebinders) are judgment calls, and a fatal strategy error in the first draft was caught by review rather than by me.
Built 2026-09-10 from MTGGoldfish featured lists (one per archetype) and Scryfall oracle text. 16 decks, 169 Tidebinder targets, 116 Wasp targets. Art: Tishana's Tidebinder, The Wondrous Wasp (Scryfall).
